# KurtShape improvement backlog — Fable deep review, 2026-10-17

Companion to [`FABLE_REVIEW.md`](FABLE_REVIEW.md). Checkout `f57d592` (`main`); Kurt's running application predates `24bab02`, `1f0001e` and `f57d592`. Nothing here has been implemented; this is the ordered plan.

## How to read this

- **IDs** are `<area>-<nn>`: A shell and visual, B viewport, C modeling and interaction, D architecture, E responsiveness, F reliability/bridge/data safety, G testing/packaging/maintenance, H assembly, import and document lifecycle. The number is the finder's order, so IDs are stable even after re-ranking.
- **Classification** is strict: *verified defect* (reproduced in an isolated experiment or unambiguous in source), *design weakness* (observed quality judgment), *hypothesis* (plausible; the verification needed is named), *opportunity* (missing capability or improvement). 79 of the 118 active findings were then independently refuted-or-confirmed against source, tests, docs and git history and re-rated by a judge from Kurt's and a newcomer's viewpoint; the Verification column shows *confirmed*, *downgraded* (with the corrected rating) or *not independently verified* (the pass was stopped to limit usage). The **Evidence** column is separate and says what the finding rests on: *reproduced* (an experiment, measurement or probe run in this review), *captures* (a dated screenshot in the repository), *source* (code reading only) or *preference* (a missing capability or design judgment). A finding can be reproduced and still not independently verified. 6 duplicates were folded into their primary finding and 1 claim was refuted; both are listed at the end and are not backlog items. The 61 claims found already solved at HEAD are in `FABLE_REVIEW.md` §12.6.
- **Severity** is impact if true and is deliberately separate from **priority**: the phase tables below rank by user impact × confidence ÷ effort with dependency order; several medium-severity items come first because they are one-day changes Kurt meets hourly.
- **Effort**: S < 1 day, M 1–3 days, L 1–2 weeks, XL > 2 weeks, for one person familiar with the code.
- **User value** is the judge's rating (very high / high / medium / low) across both personas.
- Line numbers are at `f57d592`; where the finding touches code added after Kurt's running build it says so.

## Phase 1 — Quick wins (about one week; all S; no architecture change)

See `FABLE_REVIEW.md` §10 for the one-line success measure of each. In recommended order:

1. G-01 build identity everywhere (title, Messages header, `capabilities.build`, `diagnostics`, session file, export provenance, launcher warning; F-10 merged).
2. F-03, F-04, F-09 bridge JSON errors with codes, client reconciliation of `outcome_unknown`, required-field table.
3. C-03 error details composed into status + Messages with the failing feature selected.
4. A-10 status wording for empty, sketch-only and editing states (C-13 merged).
5. A-05 hover labels show single-key shortcuts from the router.
6. A-08 Messages dot counts severity, ignores the startup backlog.
7. E-02 one managed recompute per Finish sketch.
8. E-05 / E-08 one inspection per action; no refresh for bridge reads.
9. F-05 `LastFailure` write preserves the Modified flag.
10. F-06 locked-destination error naming the file; stage files cleaned; brief retry.
11. F-07 bridge `save` refuses another project's file without `overwrite:true`.
12. D-01 / D-02 three-phase dispatch (`inspection_failed { applied:true }`) and tick backoff with log rate limit.
13. C-04 Enter / Shift+Enter in every owned editor's quantity field.
14. C-01 `add_line` opt-in `join:true`; `inspect` flags coordinate-matched junctions; `set_parameter` warns on them.
15. E-11 grayscale icon cache keyed by a stable signature and bounded; row icons set only on change.
16. H-01 / H-05 workspace mode reset after Create Assembly; Rebuild on the Assembly toolbar.
17. H-02 assembly error details through the C-03 path.
18. H-06 recovered documents keep their name and propose their original path on Save.
19. Small fixes with one-line gates: C-07 one shared expression validator that surfaces the engine's own parser error; C-11 live Sweep/Split pickers; C-16 `hide_internal` predicate for Shift+H/Y; C-17 export default name, exports out of Recent, close-with-sketch prompt; C-18 picked-edge highlight survives history clicks; A-02 Help/Repair icons; A-03 duplicate Boolean button and shared glyphs; A-14 chevron padding; B-07 session-only Shift+T; B-11 region-fill token and docs; B-13 constraint glyph colour; G-10 tolerant shortcut overrides; D-11 per-document state keyed and reset; D-14 runtime fail-fast check.

Phase gate: `run-checks.ps1` green with the new native and bridge tests listed per finding; messages, everyday-ui, sketch-behavior, dress-up-edit and step-export recipes green.

## Phase 3 — Longer-term experiments (each needs its own evidence before promotion)

C-15 feature suppression (native-Body features first) · F-16 typed `create_helix`, `add_constraint`, `add_spline`, `nth` selector, Hole-aware `created_by` · H-04 phase 2 `replace_assembly_source` (re-link occurrences, report broken joint references by name) · D-09 `SketchSession`/`ModalStack`/`SketchLease` (pure refactor behind recipes, high risk if rushed) · D-10 `studio_forms.py` beyond the Hole spec · E-14 affected-consumer selector scheduling (oracle kept) · E-01 `SaveBinaryBrep`/`CompressionLevel` after archive-equivalence proofs · E-06 Escape cancellation recipe · B-10 capped sections and the ambitious visual direction (right rail, part cards, state chips, inline dimension entry, constraint badges) · the private authority/recompute experiments stay deferred.

## After contract 3.2 (added 2026-10-19)

1. **First experiment: batch steps without per-step display tessellation** (Fable independent review of 3.2, R4, `runtime/contract-3.2/fable-independent-review/REVIEW.md`). Each batch step runs `Controller._recompute` (`core.py` `_model_step`), and in the app every recompute re-tessellates the changed Body for display. Headless, six sketch-level steps take 184 ms as one batch; in the app a `sketch_circle` + `pocket` step on the U10 smoke plate costs about 1.1 s, so only 17–18 steps fit the 20 s batch budget. FreeCAD defers tessellation of hidden objects until they are shown. Try: hide the document's Body view objects for the duration of a batch and restore their visibility after the commit or the abort; if it holds, do the same for `edit_parameter` rebuilds. **Bench recipe:** `tools/run_gui_recipe.ps1 -Recipe bench-gui -Holes 20 -Repeat 3` with `"batch"` added to the `run_sections` list in `tools/perf/bench_gui.FCMacro` and a pocket variant of `bench_core.Benchmark.batch` (six `sketch_circle` + `pocket` pairs on the plate; today's six steps are sketch-only and barely tessellate), before and after the change; headless `tools/perf/bench_core.py --sections batch --holes 20 --repeat 3` must not move; in-app confirmation with `runtime/contract-3.2/u10/private_smoke.py` (`expected_rejections[].steps_completed` of the `slow_holes_*` chunks). **Acceptance:** the GUI batch metric drops, the batch-undo recipe still passes, and visibility plus the GUI Modified flag are exactly restored after a commit and after an abort.

## Finding cards

## A. Shell and visual consistency

### A-01 Icons mix four vocabularies (originals, grey native, colour native in history, Fusion standard); fallback weight is inconsistent (0.7–4.5x comparators, ~15x spread overall), not uniformly heavier

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort L** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/presentation_icons.py` · `icon_for / grayscale_icon / _gray_image` · lines src/kurtshape/presentation_icons.py 87-118, 132-150; src/kurtshape/ui.py 1681, 1698, 1726, 1737, 1796, 1813; src/kurtshape/feature_panel.py 15-25, 241-245, 318; src/kurtshape/theme.py 301
- **Evidence:** grayscale_icon rasterises the native QIcon and _gray_image keeps luminance only ('result=source.convertToFormat(Format_Grayscale8)'). Experiment: 63 of 104 installed catalog tools resolve to the fallback (out/runtime_catalog_icon_resolution.json). Ink density at 20 px: Hole 0.520 vs original remove 0.188; Use 0.308 vs pierce 0.068; Shape binder 0.382 vs plane 0.119; Coincident 0.206 vs point 0.058; history Fillet 0.412 vs fillet 0.219 (out/native_gray_metrics.json, out/native_gray_vs_original_2x.png). Originals themselves split: solids 0.15-0.23 (fillet 0.219, shell 0.215, modify-menu 0.234) vs hairlines 0.035-0.065 (construction 0.035, axis 0.044, line 0.054) (out/icon_metrics_20px.json). Parts list uses icon_for('Part', native=body.ViewObject.Icon) -> grayscale (ui.py 1726, 1737) while feature rows use obj.ViewObject.Icon in color (ui.py 1698); sweep-dialog.png (Oct 5) shows red/yellow/blue feature rows above gray body rows. feature_panel.py 241-245 uses Fusion standard icons (SP_ArrowDown renders as a green circle) in the otherwise monochrome editor: fillet-dialog-controls.png (Oct 5).
- **Scenario:** Opening the Constraints or Geometry menu in a sketch, the More menu in Part Studio, or scanning the sketch bar where Use sits among originals.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Kurt sees a toolbar where some glyphs are dark blobs and others nearly vanish, menus that look like two products spliced together, and a left dock where the same object type is colored in one list and gray in the other; the editor's only colored glyph is an accidental green arrow.
- **A newcomer would notice:** An Onshape/SolidWorks user clicks Constraints in a sketch and gets a column of FreeCAD's shaded raster icons under a KurtShape chevron; the sketch bar's Use button is a dark shaded block sitting between hairline Construction (ink 0.035) and the Geometry menu glyph. More > Hole, Helical sweep, Shape binder, Part tools look the same way. The feature history being colored while Parts is gray reads as two products stitched together. First-impression damage only: every button still has a usable icon and a name on hover, so they would not be blocked or misled.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Reproduced headlessly: see experiments 1-2. In the app: sketch -> Constraints menu (all 18 entries are fallbacks), sketch bar 'Use' button, More -> Cut -> Hole.
- **Proposed change:** One rule per surface. Command surfaces monochrome ink; object surfaces (history + parts) native colored type glyphs on BOTH lists (drop icon_for('Part', ...) in refresh 1726/1737 or, if monochrome is preferred, apply the same tint to feature rows at 1698). Fallback: replace _gray_image with alpha-mask tinting (QPainter CompositionMode_SourceIn fill with COLORS icon_ink #51575d over the alpha, then paint into a 20 px canvas with a 2 px inset) so weight matches originals; keep RASTER_SIZES. Generator: raise stroke-width 0.95 -> 1.4 for line-art assets, minimum feature 2 px at 20 px. Author originals for the ~25 fallbacks users meet (constraints family, slot/polygon, Hole, helix, binders, Use, Help). Editor: replace SP_ArrowDown/SP_DialogCloseButton with original 'more'/'cancel' SVGs in feature_panel._icon_button.
- **Acceptance:** Contact sheet (reuse scratch icons_sheet.py + native_gray.py): max/min ink ratio across toolbar+menu glyphs <= 2.5 (today ~15), no fallback above 0.30; design-surface recipe asserts every QAction icon in model/sketch bars is non-null and not the 'tools' asset; both history lists use the same icon policy in a GUI check.
- **Dependencies:** Finding on ambiguous silhouettes (same generator pass); DESIGN.md 'Shapes' section update.
- **Risk:** Tinting hides native icon shading; verify legibility of constraint glyphs at 150% with the contact-sheet script.
- **Adversarial check:** The structural claims hold at HEAD f57d592 and nothing has fixed them: (1) presentation_icons.py 87-118 rasterises the native QIcon at RASTER_SIZES and keeps luminance only (Format_Grayscale8 + restored alpha); icon_for at 132-150 falls through to that for any label/command absent from LABEL_ICONS/COMMAND_ICONS, and to the generic 'tools' glyph when no native icon exists (the Help menu button hits this path: no 'help' key). (2) The finder's runtime resolution file (scratchpad area-a-shell/out/runtime_catalog_icon_resolution.json) lists 104 installed catalog tools with 63 unmapped; every listed fallback label (Hole, Use, Shape binder, all constraints except Dimension, slots/polygons, etc.) is indeed absent from both maps in the source. (3) ui.py at HEAD: feature rows take obj.ViewObject.Icon in colour (1698, and 1796 for assembly), part rows take icon_for('Part', native=...) which greys them (1726, 1737, 1813), and origin planes take the original 'plane' glyph (1681) — three policies in one dock; git blame puts all these lines at 4681ddf (2026-10-13) with no later change and no working-tree diff; validation/sketch-presentation/part-studio/sweep-dialog.png (Oct 5) shows exactly this (red/yellow/blue feature rows, grey cube part rows). (4) feature_panel.py 241-245 (and 318) use style().standardIcon(SP_DialogCloseButton / SP_ArrowDown); theme.py:301 sets Fusion, and fillet-dialog-controls.png (Oct 5) shows the green circled arrow beside a grey X. (5) DESIGN.md 'Shapes' documents the grayscale native fallback as the intended policy, so this is a documented design choice, not a defect — design_weakness is the right class.

What does not hold as stated is the headline weight claim. The finder's own native_gray_metrics.json gives fallback/comparator ink ratios from 0.72x (Sketch fillet vs fillet) and 1.04x (Offset surface vs shell) up to 4.53x (Use vs pierce), not '1.5-4.5x'. The top of the range comes from choosing the two lightest originals (pierce 0.068, point 0.058) as comparators. Against the originals' median ink (0.133, n=74), fallbacks span 0.54x (Horizontal) to 3.9x (Hole): the heavy solid-type native glyphs (Hole, Shape binder, Intersect shapes, Regular polygon, native Fillet) are 2-4x heavier, while several constraint fallbacks (Horizontal, Equal, Extend) are lighter than the typical original. The real problem is weight inconsistency (~15x spread, Hole 0.520 vs construction 0.035), to which the originals' own hairline-vs-solid split contributes as much as the fallbacks. Two smaller corrections: the Constraints menu has 19 entries of which 18 are fallbacks (Dimension maps to the original 'dimension' glyph); and there are four vocabularies, not three (originals, grey native, colour native in history, Fusion standard icons). Severity medium stands: persistent bars are almost entirely originals (only 'Use' on the sketch bar and the generic Help glyph break them), so the mixing is met mainly in overflow menus and in the history/parts dock on every document.

### A-02 Help button and the 'Repair leftover helpers' entry fall back to the generic cube-and-wrench 'tools' glyph (Recent projects has no icon at all, not the fallback)

**verified defect · severity low · confidence high · user value low · effort S** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `make_toolbars / bind_tool_menu` · lines src/kurtshape/ui.py 472-483 (478-479), 505, 512-513, 528-532; src/kurtshape/presentation_icons.py 20-48, 132-150; tools/generate-presentation-icons.py ASSETS (recent at 102, tools at 108)
- **Evidence:** bind_tool_menu: 'if menu.menuAction().icon().isNull(): menu.menuAction().setIcon(icon_for(menu.title()))' with title 'Help'; LABEL_ICONS has no 'help' key and resources/icons has no help.svg, so icon_for falls through to 'return _original("tools")'. Experiment table (out/icon_for_resolution.json) lists Help, Recent projects, Repair leftover helpers as fallbacks. validation/onshape-theme-repair/model-workspace-1440.png (Oct 4, current theme) shows the cube+wrench glyph with a chevron at the Help position (x~1925).
- **Scenario:** Looking for help/shortcuts in the document row.
- **What Kurt would notice:** The one button a new user needs reads as a 'tools' or 'settings' control; two gear-like glyphs sit side by side.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Looking for a '?' they see a cube-and-wrench next to a gear and may read it as a second settings/tools control, delaying their discovery of the shortcut help.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Hover the third-from-right button in the document row: label says 'Help', glyph is a cube with a wrench.
- **Proposed change:** Add 'help' (circled question mark), 'recent' is already an asset, 'repair' (wrench on document) to generate-presentation-icons.py ASSETS and LABEL_ICONS; keep 'tools' only as the last-resort fallback.
- **Acceptance:** icon_for('Help') returns help.svg; design-surface recipe asserts the Help menu action icon cacheKey != tools asset.
- **Risk:** None.
- **Adversarial check:** Help holds: make_toolbars creates QMenu('Help') at ui.py 528 with no icon and bind_tool_menu (472-483) sets icon_for('Help') at 478-479; presentation_icons.LABEL_ICONS (20-48) has no 'help' key, resources/icons has no help.svg, command is None so _command_native returns None, and icon_for falls to _original('tools') at 150. model-workspace-1440.png (Oct 4, current theme) shows the cube-and-wrench glyph with a chevron immediately left of the Settings gear. 'Repair leftover helpers' holds too: self.tool(document_menu, ...) at 512-513 goes through tool_icon -> icon_for with no mapping. 'Recent projects' does NOT hold: the submenu is created at 505 and added with document_menu.addMenu(recent) with no icon set at all, and fill_recent (2706-2714) never sets one, so it shows no glyph rather than the tools glyph; the finder's claim came from its own icon_for('Recent projects') experiment table (docs/fable-deep-review/visual-proposals/icon-audit/icon_for_resolution.json), not from the UI. The fallback is documented as being for 'rare unmapped commands' (presentation_icons.py 133-137), so using it for a primary document-row button is a genuine defect. Unchanged since 4681ddf; not fixed at HEAD. Severity low is right.

### A-03 Identical glyphs on different commands, including a redundant Boolean button in the model bar

**verified defect · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/presentation_icons.py` · `LABEL_ICONS` · lines src/kurtshape/presentation_icons.py 28, 30, 36, 45-47; ui.py 553-560, 572, 588, 597, 2043-2050; feature_tools.py 177-178; tools/generate-presentation-icons.py 122-128
- **Evidence:** 'plane': 'plane' and 'show planes': 'plane' (lines 30/36); 'boolean' and 'boolean parts' both -> 'boolean' (28); 'fixed' and 'joints' -> 'fixed-joint' (45-47). ui.py 553-555 adds catalog primary 'Boolean' and 556-560 adds 'Boolean parts'; primary_feature_tool maps 'boolean' -> studio_tool('boolean_parts') (2046-2051), so both buttons run the same command. Generator: ASSETS['new-part'] == ASSETS['build-menu'] (122/128), 'patterns-menu' = linear-pattern (125), 'shape-tools-menu' = boolean (127). Experiment SHARED list: 11 assets shared across distinct labels. model-workspace-1440.png shows two two-cube buttons (x~881 and x~978) and two identical parallelograms (x~930 Plane, x~1440 Show planes).
- **Scenario:** Scanning the model bar to find Boolean, Plane or the view toggle; assembly bar Fixed vs Joints.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Kurt clicks the wrong parallelogram (creates a datum plane instead of toggling plane visibility) and sees a duplicated button, which reads as unfinished.
- **A newcomer would notice:** An Onshape user expects Plane under reference tools and plane visibility on the view cube; two identical parallelograms in one row means a wrong click until positions are memorised, and two identical Boolean buttons reads as a bug in the product.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Hover both parallelograms in the model bar (labels 'Plane' and 'Show planes'); click both two-cube buttons: both open the Boolean parts editor.
- **Proposed change:** Remove the explicit ('Boolean parts', ...) entry at ui.py 556-560 or drop 'boolean' from PRIMARY_TOOLS (feature_tools.py 177-179). Give view toggles their own family (plane + eye for Show planes), joints a chain glyph, menu triggers a variant (cube + chevron) instead of reusing the member asset; add a generator assertion that no two ASSETS strings are equal and no two LABEL_ICONS commands in the same bar share an asset.
- **Acceptance:** Generator uniqueness check passes; per-toolbar icon cacheKeys are unique in the design-surface recipe.
- **Risk:** Removing a button changes the design-surface recipe's expected action list.
- **Adversarial check:** Read presentation_icons.py LABEL_ICONS (lines 28, 30, 36, 45-47): 'boolean' and 'boolean parts' both map to 'boolean'; 'plane' and 'show planes' both map to 'plane'; 'fixed' and 'joints' both map to 'fixed-joint'. Read ui.py 553-560 and 572: the loop over catalog['primary'] adds the catalog 'Boolean' tool (PRIMARY_TOOLS has contained 'boolean' since cb96ddf, 2026-10-02) and the next loop adds an explicit 'Boolean parts' action (df26c91, 2026-10-03); primary_feature_tool (2043-2050) maps tool.key 'boolean' to studio_tool('boolean_parts'), the same call the explicit button makes, so two buttons in one bar run one command. 'Plane' (primary datum_plane, label 'Plane') and 'Show planes' (line 572) sit in the same model bar with the same SVG; 'Fixed' (588) and 'Joints' (597) share the assembly bar. Generator lines 122/128, 125 and 127 reuse assets verbatim, and an md5 pass over resources/icons found three byte-identical pairs among 75 files (boolean==shape-tools-menu, linear-pattern==patterns-menu, build-menu==new-part). validation/onshape-theme-repair/model-workspace-1440.png (Oct 4, current shell) shows the two identical two-cube buttons and the two identical parallelograms. Nothing in git after Oct 13 touches these files, and no test or validation macro asserts icon uniqueness (design-surface-validation only compares Move face vs Extrude). What softens it: the 350 ms hover label and tooltips disambiguate, and the menu-glyph duplicates live inside the More submenu rather than beside their member buttons, so the two in-bar pairs (Boolean/Boolean parts, Plane/Show planes) are the real problem; the Fixed/Joints pair is on the hidden assembly bar.

### A-04 Near-duplicate silhouettes (fillet/chamfer, loft/draft, point/pierce, cube+arrow trio) at 20 px; the hairline-stroke claim holds only at 100 % scaling, not at Kurt's DPR 1.5

**design weakness · severity low · confidence medium · user value medium · effort M** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `tools/generate-presentation-icons.py` · `ASSETS / main` · lines 45, 50-53, 56-57, 61, 63, 66, 98, 103, 127, 137
- **Evidence:** Stroke is 'stroke-width="0.95"' in a 24 px viewBox (line 137), i.e. 0.79 px at 20 px, so line-art renders as anti-aliased mid-gray. Experiment image out/icons_20px_zoom3x.png (alphabetical grid) and out/toolbar_strip_3x.png (model and sketch bars in app order): fillet vs chamfer differ by one corner; loft vs draft are both tapered tents; extrude, move-face, transform and export are all 'cube/box + arrow'; pierce (cross + circle) vs point (cross + circle); assembly, boolean, example, insert, shape-tools are all 'two cubes'. Ink: construction 0.035, axis 0.044, line 0.054 vs fillet 0.219. In model-workspace-1440.png the fillet/chamfer pair (x~491/540) is indistinguishable at a glance.
- **Scenario:** Picking Fillet vs Chamfer, Loft vs Draft, Point vs Pierce from the toolbars without waiting 350 ms for the label.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Kurt relies on hover labels for routine tools, which defeats the icon-only toolbar's purpose and makes the shell feel like a guessing game.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Immediately. An Onshape/SolidWorks user scans by silhouette, and in those apps Fillet (rounded edge on a block) and Chamfer (clipped corner) are strongly differentiated, as are Loft (two stacked sections) and Draft (slanted face with an angle). Here they would misclick Fillet/Chamfer and Loft/Draft, cancel the wrong dialog, and then hover every button waiting the 350 ms for the label (toolbar_labels.py line 15). The sketch bar's hairline glyphs (0.79 px strokes in #51575d gray) would look like disabled tools to them.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** View the two experiment images; compare 7th/8th and 6th/10th model-bar glyphs.
- **Proposed change:** Generator pass: stroke 1.4 at 24 (1.17 px at 20); minimum feature size 2 px at 20 px; differentiate pairs by composition not detail (fillet: single large-radius corner with arc highlight, chamfer: 45-degree cut with a flat facet; loft: two stacked sections joined, draft: a slanted face with angle mark; pierce: curve piercing a point not a plain cross; extrude keeps the up-arrow, move-face gets a flat face with normal arrow, transform gets a dashed ghost cube). Re-run the contact sheet at 20/40 and at QT_SCALE_FACTOR=1.5.
- **Acceptance:** Blind sort test: Kurt names 20 random 20 px glyphs with >= 90% accuracy; ink spread across originals <= 3x; design-surface recipe renders every bar icon at 20 px and asserts bbox >= 14 px and mean alpha-weighted darkness >= 0.08.
- **Dependencies:** Finding 1 (shared weight rule).
- **Risk:** Changing well-known glyphs mid-project; mitigate by keeping families (cubes for solids, strokes for sketch).
- **Adversarial check:** Confirmed parts (from the generator at HEAD f57d592): stroke-width 0.95 on a 24 px viewBox is at tools/generate-presentation-icons.py:137; fillet (51-52) and chamfer (53) are the same box and differ only in the top-left corner (Q curve vs L) and the inner line; loft (50) and draft (56) are both tapered trapezoids with a light cap; point (66) and pierce (98) are both cross+circle (pierce adds one diagonal and uses an open ring); extrude/move-face/transform (45/57/63) are all one cube plus an arrow; boolean/example/shape-tools-menu (61/103/127) are two cubes, shape-tools-menu being literally the boolean asset. These glyphs really do share the persistent bars at HEAD: PRIMARY_TOOLS in src/kurtshape/feature_tools.py:176-178 puts revolve, sweep, loft, fillet, chamfer, shell, draft, move_face side by side at QSize(20,20) (ui.py:539), and the sketch bar carries Point and Pierce (ui.py:605-616). The 350 ms label delay is real (src/kurtshape/toolbar_labels.py:15, DESIGN.md:214). Nothing guards legibility: tools/design-surface-validation.FCMacro:199-200 and :345 only assert non-null pixmaps. No commit since 4681ddf (2026-10-13) touched the icons, and docs/fable-deep-review/visual-proposals/README.md:118 (untracked) only proposes a silhouette pass, so this is not already solved. The real Oct 4 capture (validation/onshape-theme-repair/model-workspace-1440.png, enlarged 2x) does show fillet/chamfer and loft/draft as near-duplicates at a glance.

Refuted part: the "0.79 px hairline, anti-aliased mid-gray" strand and the quoted ink numbers assume device pixel ratio 1.0, which is what the finder's offscreen audit ran at (icon-audit/native_gray.log: "dpr 1.0"). Kurt's actual environment records device_pixel_ratio 1.5 (validation/onshape-theme-repair/design-surface.json and theme-startup.json, screen NE180QDM-NZ2; the "1440" captures are 2160 px wide). A one-shot offscreen probe with the bundled interpreter shows QIcon's SVG engine rasterizes a 20 px request at DPR 1.5 as a 30x30 device-pixel pixmap, so the stroke is 1.19 device px, and every sampled glyph reaches the full ink darkness (peak 0.66 = #51575d): full-ink pixel counts go construction 0 -> 49, axis 3 -> 52, line 14 -> 42, point 8 -> 20 between DPR 1.0 and 1.5. On Kurt's display the line-art is not mid-gray. The line-art vs shaded-solid weight gap persists at 1.5 (construction 0.045 / axis 0.058 / line 0.057 vs fillet 0.224, about 5x), but that is the shared-weight question the finding itself defers to Finding 1, not a hairline defect. Minor overstatements: export (101) is a page plus arrow, not a cube; insert (110) is one cube plus a bent arrow, not two cubes; assembly (109) is two cubes with a connector.

What survives is a recognition weakness for three pairs and one cube+arrow trio on icon-only bars, judged visually rather than by any user test, in a shell whose Icon Row Rule (DESIGN.md:180) deliberately routes names to delayed labels, menus and search. That is a low-severity design weakness, medium confidence; it would rise toward the finder's medium only if Kurt works at 100% scaling, which the validation evidence does not show.

### A-05 Hover labels drop every single-key shortcut because Panel.tool keeps a tip suffix only when it contains '+' (Fit F, Normal N, Planes P, Insert I, Fixed M, Line L, Rectangle G, Circle C, Arc A, Dimension D, Trim M, Construction Q)

**verified defect · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.tool` · lines src/kurtshape/ui.py 445 (tips at 549-550, 572, 586-588, 596-598, 607-608, 615, 620); src/kurtshape/toolbar_labels.py 72-75, 123, 187-190
- **Evidence:** ui.py 445: 'self.toolbar_labels.register(button, text, tip.split(" · ")[-1] if " · " in tip and "+" in tip.split(" · ")[-1] else "")' - a shortcut is kept only when it contains '+'. QActions carry no QKeySequence (the router owns keys), so _scan_toolbar's 'action.shortcut().toString()' is empty and falls back to the same stored value (toolbar_labels.py 72-75). Native tooltips are swallowed for registered buttons ('event.accept(); return True' at 187-190), so the tip text 'Fit · F' is never shown anywhere on hover. validation/onshape-theme-repair/hover-label-below-1440.png (Oct 4, current mechanism) shows the label 'Fit' with no '(F)'.
- **Scenario:** Learning shortcuts by hovering: Fit, Normal, Show planes (P), Insert (I), Fixed (M), every sketch drawing tool.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Kurt sees '(Shift+S)' on Sketch but nothing on Circle or Fit and concludes those have no shortcut; the README's keyboard-first workflow is undiscoverable from the toolbar.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Onshape users learn keys from hover tooltips; here they conclude Fit, Normal and every sketch tool have no shortcut and keep clicking the toolbar.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Hover Fit: label reads 'Fit'. Hover Sketch: label reads 'Sketch  (Shift+S)'.
- **Proposed change:** Stop parsing tips. Pass the shortcut explicitly from the router registry: in Panel.tool add a shortcut_id/keys parameter resolved via self.router.available_actions() (key for 'view.fit' etc.) or accept the last ' · ' segment when it matches r'^(\S+\+)?[A-Za-z0-9\[\]`/]$'. Also set QAction.setShortcut with a disabled context (Qt.WidgetShortcut) purely for display so menus and search show keys from one source.
- **Acceptance:** tools/design-surface-validation.FCMacro asserts popup text == 'Fit  (F)', 'Circle  (C)', 'Show planes  (P)'; tests/test_shortcuts QtRoutingTests gains a label-text case.
- **Risk:** Menus would start displaying keys; ensure the router remains the only handler (WidgetShortcut context).
- **Adversarial check:** ui.py 445 reads exactly as cited: the shortcut passed to toolbar_labels.register is tip.split(' · ')[-1] only when that segment contains '+', else ''. Tool definitions confirm the consequence: 'Fit · F' (572, 597), 'Normal · N' (572), 'Show / hide planes · P' (572), 'Insert ... · I' (586), 'Fixed ... · M' (588), sketch tools 'Line · L', 'Rectangle · G', 'Circle · C', 'Arc · A', 'Dimension · D', 'Trim · M', 'Construction · Q' (607-608), 'Normal to sketch · N' (620) all lose their key, while 'Create sketch · Shift+S' (549), 'Isometric · Shift+7' and 'Save project · Ctrl+S' keep theirs. toolbar_labels.py 72-75: no QAction anywhere in src calls setShortcut (grep), so action.shortcut() is always empty and the scan falls back to the stored value; 123 formats 'label  (shortcut)'; 187-190 swallow QEvent.ToolTip for registered buttons so the tip string 'Fit · F' is never shown by Qt either. hover-label-below-1440.png (Oct 4, current mechanism) shows 'Fit' with no key. DESIGN.md 'Delayed command labels' ('show the command name and available shortcut') and docs/onshape-design-audit/EXECUTION.md:8 ('Labels include shortcuts when available') make this a spec violation, not a design choice; the '+' test is a crude way of filtering descriptive suffixes like 'mouse navigation' or 'STEP / STL'. Unchanged since 4681ddf (Oct 13); not fixed at HEAD. Partial mitigations exist (sketch footer lists L/G/C/N, the Profile box button says 'Normal to sketch N', Shift+/ opens help), so the consequence 'keyboard-first workflow is undiscoverable' is somewhat overstated, but severity medium remains fair for ~15 everyday tools.

### A-06 Native Sketcher Tasks dock is unmanaged during managed sketch edits: clipped solver verdict, duplicated accept/close controls (Close commits, floating X cancels), dead title bar, ~25% of the canvas lost

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value high · effort M** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: captures · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `hide_native_panels / sync_edit_ui / tick` · lines src/kurtshape/ui.py 96-108 (101-105), 1246-1247, 1267-1268, 3309, 3376-3383, 3385-3421 (3409-3419)
- **Evidence:** hide_native_panels only moves/hides the dock: 'elif dock.windowTitle() == "Tasks": if main.dockWidgetArea(dock) != RightDockWidgetArea: main.addDockWidget(...)' (101-105); no resizeDocks, title bar, label or button handling anywhere. docs/sketch-interaction-reliability/evidence/screenshots/circle-drag-after.png (Oct 6): solver line reads 'Under-constrainec 3 Degrees of Free' (clipped both ends), Constraints section ~400 px of white, dock ~330 logical px wide at 150%. validation/onshape-theme-repair/sketch-editor-1440.png (Oct 4): three ways to end the sketch are visible at once: toolbar check/cross, floating 'Profile' box check/cross, Tasks 'Close'; the dock title 'Tasks' shows float/close as two near-invisible dots (x~1958/1985). Source: Tasks 'Close' ends native edit, then tick() sees sketch_lease_needs_finish() and calls finish_sketch() (1246-1247, 1267-1268, 3376-3383), i.e. Close silently commits.
- **Scenario:** Every sketch edit; also native feature dialogs (Hole, helix, primitives) that use Tasks OK/Cancel.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Kurt cannot read the solver verdict he needs most, loses a quarter of the canvas to empty panel, and has a 'Close' button whose effect (commit) differs from the floating box's cross (cancel) with no cue.
- **A newcomer would notice:** An Onshape/SolidWorks user sees three ways to end the sketch, a 'Close' that keeps changes while the Profile box's X discards them (Onshape's X discards), foreign 'Constraints'/'Elements' boxes, a title bar with two near-invisible dots and an unreadable solver verdict; the sketch workflow feels unfinished.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Start a sketch, draw a circle: read the Sketch Edit line in the Tasks dock; count accept/close controls on screen.
- **Proposed change:** In sync_edit_ui/hide_native_panels when a managed sketch is active and the Tasks dock becomes visible: dock.setTitleBarWidget(QWidget()) (drop the dead bar); main.resizeDocks([dock], [max(340, dock.minimumSizeHint().width())], Qt.Horizontal) once per show; find the solver QLabel (Sketcher TaskSketcherMessages, objectName to verify at runtime) and setWordWrap(True) + setToolTip(text), or mirror its text into KurtShape's sketch box verdict (editor.set_status) so the native line becomes redundant; hide the native TaskView control box buttons (dock.findChildren(QDialogButtonBox)/QPushButton 'Close') only while self.active_sketch() and the lease exists, so Finish/Cancel live in one place; collapse the Elements TaskBox by default. Phase 2: main.tabifyDockWidget(self, tasks_dock) or FreeCAD's overlay docking (Std_DockOverlayAll / View/DockOverlay prefs) so the viewport keeps its width; Phase 3: a KurtShape-owned constraints list in the sketch box.
- **Acceptance:** GUI recipe: during sketch edit, solver label sizeHint().width() <= label.width(); exactly one visible accept/cancel pair outside the toolbar; MDI viewport width >= 60% of the window at 1440; docs/sketch-interaction-reliability recipes still pass.
- **Dependencies:** Verify widget names in the live app (offscreen sketch edit crashes on QOpenGLWidget, see experiment 3). Keep native OK/Cancel for non-sketch Tasks dialogs.
- **Risk:** Hiding native Close must not strand native dialogs; guard on active_sketch() and restore on leave. Overlay docks change picking geometry; validate with the sketch recipes.
- **Adversarial check:** Read ui.py 96-108: hide_native_panels only re-parents the Tasks dock to the right area and hides it when no Gui.Control dialog is active; no resizeDocks, setTitleBarWidget, label or button handling exists anywhere in src (grep for resizeDocks/setTitleBarWidget/TaskView hits only the history dock at 4324 and the FeaturePanel header). tick() 1246-1247 and 1267-1268 call finish_sketch() whenever sketch_lease_needs_finish() (3376-3383) is true, and finish_sketch (3309) defaults cancel=False, so the native Tasks 'Close' does commit; docs/M1_PROGRESS.md:207 records this as intended ('Native task Close also finalizes the sketch lease'), and it matches stock FreeCAD, where Close leaves edit mode keeping changes. Screenshots hold: circle-drag-after.png (Oct 6, current shell) shows 'Under-constrainec 3 Degrees of Free' clipped at both ends, an empty Constraints box, and the dock occupying ~490 of 2000 displayed px; sketch-editor-1440.png (Oct 4) shows toolbar check/cross, the floating Profile box check/cross and Tasks 'Close' at once, with two faint float/close dots in the dock title. The solver verdict is not mirrored anywhere in KurtShape (grep 'constrained' in src hits only core/sketch_colors, not any label), so the clipped line is the only on-screen DOF readout. What did not hold: 'high' severity. The dock is an ordinary QDockWidget the user can widen with the splitter, Close-commits is undoable and consistent with FreeCAD, and the finder itself rates confidence medium. git log -S shows this code unchanged since the Oct 2 baseline / Oct 13 refactor; commits 24bab02/1f0001e/f57d592 do not touch it, so it is not already solved.

### A-07 Feature editors are re-floated to the canvas corner on every open and the shell layout (editor placement, history width, window/monitor) is rebuilt from constants each launch

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort M** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/feature_panel.py` · `FeaturePanel.show_compact / Panel.start` · lines src/kurtshape/feature_panel.py 35-38, 123-155 (147-153); src/kurtshape/ui.py 2585-2586, 2678, 3235, 3417-3419, 3473, 3585, 4314-4324; src/kurtshape/startup.py 5-11
- **Evidence:** show_compact: 'self.setFloating(True); self.resize(width, height); ... self.move(x, y)' is called by extrude() 3473, edit_extrusion() 3585, start_sketch() 3235, sync_edit_ui 3417-3419 and every studio_tool dialog (2585-2586) after complete_editor() hid it (2678). start(): maximize_on_primary_screen(main) then 'main.resizeDocks([panel], [250], Qt.Horizontal)' (4320-4324); no saveState/restoreState/restoreDockWidget or QSettings anywhere in src (grep). DESIGN.md Layout says editors 'can move or dock left/right'.
- **Scenario:** Kurt docks the editor to the right (or drags it off the model) and starts the next Extrude; or resizes the history dock and restarts.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Every feature opens on top of the geometry at the top-left of the canvas again; the dock width, splitter, Messages height and monitor choice reset each launch, so the workspace never becomes 'his'.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Onshape/SolidWorks users expect the feature dialog in one stable place; a box that floats over the geometry on each open and ignores where it was dragged reads as unfinished, though they do not expect per-launch layout memory as strongly as desktop CAD users.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Open Extrude, drag the editor to the right dock area, Confirm, open Extrude again: it floats at the canvas corner.
- **Proposed change:** Remember editor placement: in show_compact, only float+place when the panel has never been shown or was floating last time; if not isFloating() at hide, re-show in its dock area (store area via main.dockWidgetArea). Persist layout with QSettings('KurtShape','shell') in the session dir: main.saveState() on close (objectNames already exist: KurtShapeHistory, KurtShapeEditor, KurtShapeMessages, toolbars) and main.restoreState() + restoreDockWidget(editor) after the docks are created in start(); keep maximize_on_primary_screen only when no saved geometry exists or the saved screen is gone.
- **Acceptance:** GUI recipe: dock editor -> Confirm -> reopen Extrude -> editor still docked; restart isolated session -> history dock width and splitter sizes equal the saved values.
- **Dependencies:** startup.py mixed-DPI note (fetch screen after state changes) must be preserved.
- **Risk:** Restoring a floating editor onto a disconnected monitor; clamp with the existing available-geometry code.
- **Adversarial check:** feature_panel.py 123-155: show_compact unconditionally calls setFloating(True) (147), resize (148) and move (153) to the canvas's top-left, with no check of isFloating() or the previous dock area. Callers verified at the cited lines: studio_tool dialogs 2585-2586 (addDockWidget then show_compact), start_sketch 3235, sync_edit_ui 3417-3419 (show_compact whenever the editor is hidden or the mode changes), extrude 3473, edit_extrusion 3585; complete_editor hides the editor at 2678, so the next tool always re-floats it. The reproduction is valid because the panel explicitly allows Left|Right docking (feature_panel.py 35-38) and DESIGN.md:188 promises editors 'can move or dock left/right'. grep for saveState/restoreState/restoreDockWidget/QSettings/saveGeometry across src and tools returns nothing; start() (4314-4324) calls maximize_on_primary_screen and resizeDocks([panel],[250]) every launch, and startup.py 5-11 documents opening on the primary monitor each time as intended. KurtShape's docks are created inside start(), after FreeCAD has already applied its own saved window state, so FreeCAD's native persistence cannot cover them. Nothing in 24bab02/1f0001e/f57d592 touches this. Classification design_weakness and severity medium are honest: nothing is lost, the editor is movable each time, but the DESIGN.md promise of a dockable editor is hollow without memory.

### A-08 Messages unread dot counts the startup Report backlog and every native line regardless of severity, so it is already lit at launch

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `append_message / make_messages` · lines src/kurtshape/ui.py 702-718 (append_message), 736-787 (make_messages; startup backlog at 787, contentsChange hook 776-784), 656-666 (notify error channel), 4311-4324 (start() order: maximize_on_primary_screen before Panel); src/kurtshape/startup.py maximize_on_primary_screen (setGeometry); tools/messages-validation.FCMacro 174-200
- **Evidence:** append_message: 'if text.strip() and not self.messages_dock.isVisible(): self.unread_messages += 1' for every native=True line; make_messages appends the whole startup report once ('self.append_message(widget.toPlainText(), native=True)' 786-787) and connects every contentsChange (776-785). startup-origin.png (Oct 6) shows 'Messages •' lit right after 'New complete' on an empty document; model-workspace-1440.png the same. Report content in native-report-readable.png (Oct 4) is QIODevice thumbnail chatter.
- **Scenario:** Any session: the dot is already on before Kurt does anything; an actual geometry error later changes nothing visible.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Kurt learns to ignore the one quiet error channel; real failures (invalid geometry, failed Finish) are not distinguishable at the status bar.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Yes, on first launch. Onshape/SolidWorks users expect an unread/alert badge to mean something went wrong (Onshape paints the failed feature red and toasts it). They click "Messages •" on an empty document, find Qt thumbnail-cache chatter (QIODevice::read ... device not open, Start workbench migration), and file the panel as a verbose log rather than an alert channel. After that they rely on the status bar alone and miss native-only errors, exactly the failure mode the finding describes.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Launch; look at the status bar: 'Messages •'. Open Messages: startup log lines, no error.
- **Proposed change:** Count severity, not lines: in the contentsChange hook classify each added block by its char-format foreground (theme already maps native colorError/colorWarning tokens, theme.py 292-299) and only increment for KurtShape notify(..., 'error') and native error/warning blocks; do not count the startup backlog. Render severity in the button: 'Messages' plain, 'Messages • 2' with error color when errors are unread; keep info lines copyable but uncounted.
- **Acceptance:** messages recipe (tools/messages-validation.FCMacro, 34 checks) gains: dot off after startup; dot on after notify('x','error'); dot stays off after an info-only native line.
- **Risk:** Native highlighter formats are applied asynchronously; classify on the block after rehighlight or on the text prefix as a fallback.
- **Adversarial check:** The factual mechanism holds at HEAD f57d592 and the cited lines are correct: append_message (ui.py 702-718) increments unread_messages for any non-blank text while the dock is hidden, make_messages hides the dock (761) and then appends the whole Report view backlog once (787) and every later contentsChange batch (776-784), with no severity test anywhere. It is lit at startup in practice: start() (ui.py 4311-4324) calls maximize_on_primary_screen (startup.py, setGeometry) before Panel() is built, and the real session log runtime/freecad-launch.log line 220 shows "Wrn: QWindowsWindow::setGeometry: Unable to set geometry 2160x1299..." emitted at exactly that point, so the Report view already holds a warning when line 787 captures it; validation profiles additionally carry "Migrating Start Workbench..." plus seven QIODevice::read thumbnail lines (validation/onshape-design/messages.json messages_text; runtime/messages-behavior-audit/report-probe.json, all #000000 info fragments). The screenshots confirm: docs/sketch-interaction-reliability/evidence/screenshots/startup-origin.png (Oct 6, not under validation/ as cited) shows "Messages •" beside "New complete" on an empty Untitled part, and validation/onshape-design/model-workspace-1440.png (Oct 4) shows it after "Extrude complete". Nothing resets the count except opening the dock (723), no test covers unread after startup, and git log shows only eb3baf1/69dc29c/4681ddf touched this code, so it is not already solved. However, "verified_defect" and the consequence are overstated. Counting every native line is the documented and tested design, not a deviation: docs/workspace-behavior/IMPLEMENTATION.md line 7 describes the button as indicating "unread entries" while native output is retained "without interpreting every information/warning/log fragment as an application error", and tools/messages-validation.FCMacro lines 194-200 explicitly assert unread_messages > before_unread for PrintMessage, PrintWarning, PrintError and PrintLog and call the dot a "quiet indicator". The finding's own acceptance ("dot stays off after an info-only native line") would fail four of the existing 34 checks. And the dot is not "the one quiet error channel": notify(msg, "error") (ui.py 656-666) paints the status label red with the error text and does not start the 4.5 s restore timer, so it persists until replaced; _present_geometry_check (1624) notifies "Invalid geometry — see Messages" in red, and the recipe checks "A rejected controller operation shows red readable status". Real failures remain distinguishable at the status bar via that red label; the startup-lit dot is a low-signal UX weakness (startup backlog counted as unread, no severity in the count), not a defect that hides errors. Hence downgraded to design_weakness, low severity, high confidence in the facts.

### A-09 Status bar mixes KurtShape text with unexplained native widgets and a second notification channel

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: captures · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `make_toolbars (status widgets) / make_messages` · lines src/kurtshape/ui.py 629-632, 764-768 (also 771-787 Report-view mirror, 184 statusBar().show()); src/kurtshape/workspace_preferences.py 15-23; src/kurtshape/theme.py 140-141
- **Evidence:** KurtShape adds self.message and the Messages button as permanent widgets (632, 768) and hides only NavigationIndicator. Probe (experiment 3) lists the native status bar children still present: Gui::StatusBarLabel, Gui::DimensionWidget, Gui::InputHintWidget 'hintLabel', QPushButton 'notificationArea', Gui::ProgressBar. hover-label-below-1440.png shows 'Area: 2.40 cm^2' at far left (native preselection in a non-mm unit), a blue-square dropdown (notificationArea) and '57.24 mm x 36.93 mm' (DimensionWidget) next to 'Extrude complete'. docs/ui-refinement/IMPLEMENTATION.md:13 records that these were intentionally retained.
- **Scenario:** Hovering a face, or any time Kurt reads the footer.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Two notification systems (bell + Messages), a view-size readout nobody asked for, and area in cm^2 beside a shell that says 'mm' everywhere; the footer reads as FreeCAD's, not KurtShape's.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Onshape has no footer at all, so everything in this strip is new; a SolidWorks user expects the status bar to carry the document unit system and selection measurements in that unit. Hovering a face and reading "Area: 2.40 cm^2" beside an "mm" shell reads as a mis-set unit system. The bigger trap is "57.24 mm x 36.93 mm": in SolidWorks the status bar reports the size of what you selected, so a newcomer hovering the plate would plausibly take the viewport-extent readout for the face's dimensions. The blue-square dropdown with a count looks like an unrelated app's notification center and opens an unstyled FreeCAD menu, which is the one place the shell visibly stops being KurtShape.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Hover a face: left footer shows 'Area: x cm^2'; click the blue square: FreeCAD notification menu.
- **Proposed change:** Decide one footer: hide notificationArea (pref User parameter:BaseApp/Preferences/NotificationArea NotificationAreaEnabled=false, or findChild(QPushButton,'notificationArea').hide() in the same Show/Polish filter that hides NavigationIndicator) and route native notifications into Messages with severity; hide or move DimensionWidget; set Units/UserSchema to the mm-based schema so native readouts use mm^2; keep InputHintWidget but confirm it is visible beside the permanent message (stretch 1 may starve it).
- **Acceptance:** Footer at 1440 shows: KurtShape message, optional hint, Messages button; no native bell; a hovered face reports mm^2.
- **Dependencies:** Finding on Messages severity (same channel).
- **Risk:** Hiding the notification area hides native modal-free warnings; only do it once Messages shows severity.
- **Adversarial check:** Every factual claim holds at HEAD f57d592. src/kurtshape/ui.py:629-632 adds self.message (KurtShapeStatusMessage) with addPermanentWidget(..., 1) and ui.py:764-768 adds the Messages QToolButton as a permanent widget; src/kurtshape/workspace_preferences.py:15-23 hides only the widget named "NavigationIndicator" (initial hide plus Show/Polish event filter). Nothing in src/ references notificationArea, DimensionWidget, InputHintWidget or StatusBarLabel, and theme.py only styles QStatusBar borders (lines 140-141), so none of the native children are hidden or restyled. The validation probe validation/onshape-design/ui-refinement.json:307-400 (produced by tools/ui-refinement-validation.FCMacro:266-269 settings_gate) lists the visible status bar children exactly as the finder says: Gui::StatusBarLabel (x=2, w=608), Gui::DimensionWidget "35.61 mm x 22.36 mm", Gui::InputHintWidget "hintLabel", Gui::StatusBarLabel "rightSideLabel", QPushButton "notificationArea" (text "7"), and QLabel "KurtShapeStatusMessage" at x=872 -- the native label, not KurtShape's, owns the left of the footer. The cited screenshot (validation/onshape-theme-repair/hover-label-below-1440.png, Oct 4 23:29, current light shell; identical copy under onshape-design/) shows "Area: 2.40 cm^2" far left, a blue-square dropdown, "57.24 mm x 36.93 mm", "Extrude complete" and "Messages •". "Area:" appears nowhere in KurtShape source, so it is native preselection text. docs/ui-refinement/IMPLEMENTATION.md:13 states "Native view dimensions and notification controls remain in the status bar", confirming deliberate retention. Nothing fixes it later: git blame dates all cited lines to Oct 2-3 (b65f066, 69dc29c, eb3baf1, cb96ddf); the 13 commits since Oct 4 touching ui.py/theme.py/workspace_preferences.py do not touch the status bar; git status shows no uncommitted edits to those files; the live launcher profile runtime/user-data/user.cfg:17 has UserSchema=0 and no NotificationArea override. The duplication claim is strengthened by ui.py:771-787, which mirrors the native Report view into Messages, so native warnings already reach both the bell and Messages. The docs themselves treat this as open: docs/onshape-design-audit/PLAN.md:95 and docs/fable-deep-review/visual-proposals/README.md:51 propose a zoned footer that was not implemented. Classification (design_weakness), severity (low: cosmetic/consistency, no functional loss) and confidence (high) are appropriate. One correction to the proposed_change: an isolated headless probe of the bundled FreeCAD 1.1.4 shows the default schema 0 ("Internal"/Standard) is already the mm-based schema and still prints 240 mm^2 as "2.40 cm^2" (cascades to cm^2 at >=100 mm^2); only schema 6 (MmMin, "Metric small parts & CNC") or 8 (FEM) always report mm^2 (and mm). So "set UserSchema to the mm-based schema" must name schema 6 or 8 specifically.

### A-10 Status label copy lacks an empty-document state and shouts 'Rebuild required' while a sketch is being edited

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: captures · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `status_detail` · lines src/kurtshape/ui.py 1388-1410 (status_detail, rebuild_reason_text), 299-300 (word-wrapped self.status), 1745-1753 (refresh applies it); core.py 1322-1324, 1341 (sketch_only / needs_rebuild derivation)
- **Evidence:** 'measured = self.state.get("measurements"); if not measured: return "Sketches only · mm"' (1402-1404) fires for a new empty part: startup-origin.png (Oct 6) shows 'Sketches only · mm' with 'Features (0)'. 'if status == "needs_rebuild": return "Rebuild required · " + rebuild_reason_text(...)' (1400-1401) with default 'pending model changes' shows during an active sketch: circle-drag-after.png (Oct 6) left dock reads 'Rebuild required · pending model changes' wrapped over two lines while a circle is being drawn.
- **Scenario:** Fresh document; any sketch edit.
- **What Kurt would notice:** The first thing a new document says is wrong, and the dock shouts 'Rebuild required' during the most common action, training Kurt to ignore the status label.
- **A newcomer would notice:** A SolidWorks user knows 'rebuild required' as the traffic-light after an edit and would half-expect it, but an Onshape user never rebuilds and would look for a problem; both would find 'Sketches only' on a document with no sketch odd before anything else is read.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** New -> read the left status; Shift+S, pick Top, draw anything -> read it again.
- **Proposed change:** status_detail: distinguish empty ('No geometry yet · mm · Shift+S to sketch'), sketch_only ('Sketches only'), and suppress the needs_rebuild wording while self.active_sketch() or self.task ('Editing sketch · rebuild on Finish'); reserve 'Rebuild required' for the engine/build-stamp reasons.
- **Acceptance:** GUI recipe asserts the left status text for the three states.
- **Risk:** None.
- **Adversarial check:** Read ui.py status_detail (1396-1410) and rebuild_reason_text (1388-1394), plus core._build_status (1310-1345) and the per-object status at 1387. The strings are exactly what the finder quotes. 'Sketches only · mm' is the fallback for any state without measurements; core assigns 'sketch_only' to a document with no solid, including an empty one (1341: 'valid' if solid else 'sketch_only'), so a fresh New document prints it: startup-origin.png (Oct 6) shows it with Features (0) / Parts (0). 'needs_rebuild' is set whenever any active Sketcher/PartDesign/Part object is Touched (1322-1324); an in-progress sketch is Touched, so the dock prints 'Rebuild required · pending model changes' (the default reason text at 1394) during every sketch: circle-drag-after.png (Oct 6) shows it wrapped on two lines (self.status has word wrap, 299-300). finish_sketch_edit recomputes and re-evaluates status (core 1636-1638), so the message clears on Finish; it is transient but appears during the most common activity. Not changed by any commit through f57d592; docs/2026-10-03-claude-review/CODEX_PROMPT.md 886 specified the 'Rebuild required · <reason>' wording, and no test or macro asserts either label (tests check build_status values only). Why downgraded: the labels faithfully render the core state machine (the model does need a rebuild after the sketch changes; the bridge reports the same 'needs_rebuild'), so this is misleading copy and a missing 'editing sketch' state in the mapping, not wrong state. That is a design weakness, not a verified defect; severity low because the label is advisory and nothing downstream acts on it.
- **Merged duplicate C-13:** 'Rebuild required · pending model changes' is shown in the feature panel while a sketch is being edited (the model bar, with Rebuild, is hidden at that point) — design weakness, low, captures · verified. Evidence: docs/sketch-interaction-reliability/evidence/screenshots/circle-drag-after.png (Oct 6, current light shell) bottom-left reads 'Rebuild required · pending model changes' while 'DragCircle' is in edit and the status bar says 'Sketch editing …'. core.py:1325-1326 marks any Touched Sketcher object as pending → status needs_rebuild; ui.py:1400-1401 renders that as 'Rebuild required'.

### A-11 Clicking a selectable status/verdict label parks focus and silences every single-key shortcut

**verified defect · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `make_message_copyable` · lines src/kurtshape/ui.py 634-639, 631, 366, 2339; shortcuts.py 225-226, 271-280; tools/messages-validation.FCMacro 286-291 (validated StrongFocus contract)
- **Evidence:** make_message_copyable: 'label.setTextInteractionFlags(TextSelectableByMouse | TextSelectableByKeyboard); label.setFocusPolicy(QtCore.Qt.StrongFocus)' applied to the footer message and both preview verdicts (631, 366, 2339). shortcuts.py _text_input returns True for 'isinstance(widget, QLabel) and widget.textInteractionFlags() & (TextSelectableByMouse|TextSelectableByKeyboard)' (276-277) and the filter then returns False for every key except Escape and Ctrl+S/N/O (226-227). README explicitly invites clicking these messages to copy them.
- **Scenario:** Kurt clicks the footer message to copy an error, then presses N, F, L or Shift+S.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Nothing happens until he clicks the viewport; it looks like shortcuts broke.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Onshape and SolidWorks have no focusable status text, so a user who clicked the bottom message to read it would not suspect it and would assume the keyboard shortcuts are unreliable.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Click the footer text, press F: no fit. Click the canvas, press F: fit.
- **Proposed change:** Keep labels selectable but give them Qt.NoFocus (mouse selection and the context-menu Copy still work) or, if Ctrl+C must work, make _text_input treat QLabel as text input only for Ctrl/Shift chords (letters still route). Also add a visible focus cue for panes (QTreeWidget:focus { border-left: 2px solid accent }) since QAbstractItemView has border 0 (theme.py 113-116) and Tab passes ~28 toolbar stops before reaching the tree.
- **Acceptance:** tests/test_shortcuts QtRoutingTests: focus on a selectable QLabel, key 'F' -> fit callback invoked; Ctrl+C still copies.
- **Risk:** Keyboard-only copy from the label changes; document Ctrl+C via context menu.
- **Adversarial check:** Read ui.py make_message_copyable (634-639): TextSelectableByMouse | TextSelectableByKeyboard plus StrongFocus, applied to the footer message (631), the extrude preview hint (366) and the studio preview hint (2339). Read shortcuts.py: _text_input (271-280) returns True for any QLabel with selectable flags, and the filter (225-226) then returns False for every key except Escape and local.save/new/open, so neither the KurtShape callbacks nor the 'native' entries (view.fit = F) are invoked. No KurtShape action carries a QAction shortcut (no setShortcut anywhere in ui.py), so there is no second path. Both the StrongFocus and the QLabel branch were added together in 69dc29c (Oct 3) and are unchanged at HEAD. README.md 75 invites clicking/right-clicking these messages. I settled the behavioural claim with a headless probe under the bundled pythonw (offscreen, isolated profile): a QLabel configured exactly as make_message_copyable does takes focus on a left click (focusWidget == QLabel); with it focused, F, N and Shift+S fire nothing through an installed ShortcutRouter while Ctrl+S still fires local.save; focus stays on the label after the pointer leaves; clicking the central widget restores F. The same probe shows the proposed remedy works: a NoFocus label keeps focus on the viewport stand-in, F fires, and a mouse drag still selects the label text. One correction to the proposal: tools/messages-validation.FCMacro 286-291 explicitly asserts focusPolicy == StrongFocus and keyboard selection + Ctrl+C on the focused status label, so NoFocus would need that recipe revised; narrowing the QLabel branch in _text_input to Ctrl/Shift chords and navigation keys keeps that contract and fixes the routing.
- **Merged duplicate C-09:** Read-only search field and selectable status labels take keyboard focus and swallow single-key tools — hypothesis, medium, source · not independently verified. Evidence: SearchField is a QLineEdit(readOnly) with default StrongFocus; make_message_copyable gives the status message and the preview verdict TextSelectableByMouse|Keyboard plus StrongFocus. shortcuts.py:271-281 treats any QLineEdit (read-only included) and any selectable QLabel as a text input, and :226 then returns False for every key except Escape and Ctrl+S/N/O.

### A-12 Left dock paints an inert "Feature history" title bar above the filter and "Features (N)" label; parts block repeats the active part in a combo and a bold row; marker caption wraps

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.__init__ / make_history` · lines src/kurtshape/ui.py 131-133, 240-251, 262-268, 275-281, 1658, 1710-1715, 1724-1727, 1747, 1763; src/kurtshape/theme.py 75, 81; src/kurtshape/rollback_history.py 78-88
- **Evidence:** QDockWidget('Feature history') with NoDockWidgetFeatures still paints its title bar (theme.py 81 styles it); below it 'Filter features' then QLabel 'Features (N)' #SectionTitle; model-workspace-1440.png shows 'Feature history' / filter / 'Features (2)' stacked, then after the tree 'Rollback · 2 / 2' (now the marker description), 'Parts (1)', a 'Body' combo and the bold body row. The proposed-shell-layout.png direction uses one 'Features (3)' header and an 'Active' tag.
- **Scenario:** Every session; most visible on small windows where 90 px of header precede the first feature.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Hierarchy reads muddled: two 'feature' headers, a part chooser that duplicates the bold row, and a marker caption that wraps to two lines.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Immediately. An Onshape/SolidWorks user expects one "Features (N)" header with the filter beside it; three label rows that all say some variant of "feature" read as unfinished, and a "Body" dropdown under "Parts (1)" with a single bold "Body" row raises "what does this choose?" The schematic the team already drew (docs/onshape-design-audit/proposed-shell-layout.png) shows the single-header, "Active"-tag form they would expect.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Count header rows above 'Origin'.
- **Proposed change:** self.setTitleBarWidget(QWidget()) to drop the dock bar; one header row 'Features (N)' with the filter as an inline icon-field; show the active part as a right-aligned 'Active' tag in the parts row and demote body_choice to a context menu or keep it only when >1 owner; single-line marker caption with elision + tooltip.
- **Acceptance:** At 1024x700 the first feature row sits within 60 px of the toolbar; design-surface recipe checks header count.
- **Risk:** Dock cannot be dragged (already NoDockWidgetFeatures), so no loss.
- **Adversarial check:** Holds as stated at HEAD f57d592 (working tree clean apart from untracked docs/fable-deep-review). Source: `Panel.__init__` (ui.py:131-133) builds the dock as `QDockWidget("Feature history")` with `NoDockWidgetFeatures` and never calls `setTitleBarWidget` (grep: only feature_panel.py:70 does, for the editor; `git log -S setTitleBarWidget -- ui.py` is empty), so Qt still paints the title bar, styled by theme.py:81 (`QDockWidget::title { padding: 5px 8px; border-bottom }`). `make_history` then stacks the filter field (240-248) and the `#SectionTitle` label (249-251, theme.py:75 `padding: 5px 8px`) above the tree: three chrome rows before "Origin". Below the tree `rollback_label` is word-wrapped (262-265) and receives `RollbackMarker.description()` (268, 1715; rollback_history.py:78-88). The parts block adds "Parts (N)" (275-277, 1747) plus `body_choice` (278-281), which is shown unconditionally in part-studio refresh (1658) even with a single owner, while the same active part is already bolded in the tree (1724, 1763) and named in its tooltip (1727). Captures from the current era confirm it: validation/sketch-edit-history/onshape-workflows/compact-workspace.png (2026-10-05, 1200x800 logical at DPR 1.5) shows "Feature history" / "Filter features" / "Features (4)" above Origin, ~87 logical px from toolbar bottom to the first row, and "Parts (2)" + a "UseBody" combo over rows "Body"/"UseBody"; validation/sketch-conversion-rollback/rollback/between-extrusion-and-hole-history.png (2026-10-05) shows the caption "After Plate extrusion · before Hole position" wrapped onto two lines at the dock's width. The finder's cited model-workspace-1440.png is 2026-10-04 and still shows the older "Rollback · 2 / 2" slider, but the finder already noted that; the Oct 5 captures show the same header stack with the current marker. The proposed direction matches docs/onshape-design-audit/proposed-shell-layout.png (2026-10-04): one "Features (3)" header, filter beneath, "Parts (1)" with a "Plate  Active" row and an "Active target for new features" caption; PLAN.md:178-182 asks that the active target be shown "distinctly from whichever row or geometry is selected". Nothing in git log or docs shows this was addressed. Classification design_weakness / low / high is right: it is cosmetic hierarchy, not a defect, and the dock is already undraggable so dropping the bar loses nothing. Implementation caveats the finding omits: tools/rollback-history-validation.FCMacro:211,240,260 assert exact `rollback_label.text()` strings, so single-line elision must be paint-level (elided display + tooltip) or the recipe updated; tools/assembly-gui-validation.FCMacro:216 asserts `features_title.text()` equals the assembly name in assembly mode (1804), so a merged header must keep that label; tools/history-lifecycle-validation.FCMacro:192-238 drives `body_choice` via itemData/findData, so demoting the combo should keep the widget (hidden) rather than remove it.

### A-13 Document tabs leak FreeCAD MDI titles (': 1*') and a red close glyph

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value low · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: captures · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/theme.py` · `STYLESHEET QTabBar rules / Panel.refresh` · lines src/kurtshape/theme.py 137-139 (QTabBar rules), apply_theme (clears the native stylesheet, sets Fusion); ui.py 1756, 1828 (main window title only), 1766-1772 (document-row Unsaved/Modified marker)
- **Evidence:** Only QTabBar::tab colors are styled; close buttons and titles are native. model-workspace-1440.png: tabs read 'Untitled part : 1*' and 'Design acceptance plate : 1*' with a red X on the active tab while the document row already says '· Unsaved'. refresh sets only main.setWindowTitle (1756).
- **Scenario:** Two or more open documents.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Tabs show view numbers and a star that duplicate the document row, plus the only saturated red in the shell on a non-destructive control.
- **A newcomer would notice:** An Onshape user would read ' : 1' as an instance or version count and the saturated red X as a destructive control; it is the one place the shell visibly stops being KurtShape.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Open two documents; read the tab strip.
- **Proposed change:** Style QTabBar::close-button { image: url(resources/icons/cancel.svg) } with hover state; install an event filter on QMdiSubWindow WindowTitleChange that rewrites ' : N' and '*' to the document label (hypothesis: FreeCAD MDIView::onRelabel re-applies, so rewrite in the filter rather than once); or leave the star and drop the suffix in the document row to avoid duplication.
- **Acceptance:** Tab text == document label (+ ' *' at most); no red close icon in a 1440 capture.
- **Risk:** Fighting native title updates; verify no flicker.
- **Adversarial check:** Read theme.py: the only QTabBar rules are 137-139 (tab colours, selected accent underline, disabled); there is no QTabBar::close-button rule. apply_theme clears FreeCAD's generated stylesheet (main.setStyleSheet('')), installs KurtShape's own and sets Fusion, so the close glyph is Qt's common-style default (normal red X on the selected tab, disabled gray on others), which is exactly what model-workspace-1440.png (Oct 4) shows next to 'Untitled part : 1*' and 'Design acceptance plate : 1*'. Searched src/kurtshape for setWindowTitle/setTabText/tabBar: only the main window title is set (ui.py 1756, 1828) plus dialogs; nothing touches QMdiSubWindow titles, so the tab text is FreeCAD's 'Label : viewNumber[*]'. update_document_identity (1766-1772) already renders '· Unsaved' / '· Modified' in the document row, so the star duplicates it. Nothing in the log through f57d592 changes this, and DESIGN.md 240 ('Local document tabs use an accent active indicator and native local-file identity') shows the native title was accepted knowingly, which is why design_weakness is the honest class. The finder's severity (low) and effort stand for the stylesheet part; rewriting titles is the part that needs verification because FreeCAD re-applies them on relabel and activation.

### A-14 Menu-trigger padding rule targets MenuButtonPopup while every trigger uses InstantPopup, so the chevron overlaps the 20 px glyph corner

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value low · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/theme.py` · `STYLESHEET QToolButton rules` · lines src/kurtshape/theme.py 95-99 (indicator/padding and inert ::menu-button rules), 163, 179; src/kurtshape/ui.py 472-481 (bind_tool_menu, setPopupMode at 477), callers 466/499/532/568; feature_panel.py 252
- **Evidence:** 'QToolButton::menu-indicator { subcontrol-position: right bottom; } QToolButton[popupMode="1"] { padding-right: 20px; }' but bind_tool_menu uses 'button.setPopupMode(QtGui.QToolButton.InstantPopup)' (value 2), so no menu button gets the padding and the indicator is drawn inside the 4 px padding over the glyph's corner. model-workspace-1440.png: hamburger, Help, More, and in sketch-editor-1440.png the Geometry/Constraints/Edit triggers show a chevron tucked under the bottom-right of the symbol.
- **Scenario:** Any menu trigger in the toolbars.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Menu buttons look cramped and inconsistent with the proposed layout's clean chevron spacing.
- **A newcomer would notice:** An Onshape user expects the small chevron beside the icon; the tucked-under arrow (touching the Geometry glyph's circle) reads as cramped and makes it marginally less obvious which buttons open a dropdown, but hover labels and tooltips keep discovery intact.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Zoom the toolbar: chevrons touch the glyphs.
- **Proposed change:** Use QToolButton[popupMode="2"] { padding-right: 12px; } and ::menu-indicator { subcontrol-position: right center; width: 8px; height: 5px; } (arrow image already supplied by _native_arrow_stylesheet).
- **Acceptance:** Design-surface capture shows chevrons right of the glyph with >= 2 px clearance.
- **Risk:** Toolbar grows ~8 px per menu button; overflow recipe at 800 px still passes.
- **Adversarial check:** Read src/kurtshape/theme.py 85-100 and 160-191 and ui.py 430-481 plus every bind_tool_menu caller (466, 499, 532, 568) and feature_panel.py 252. Facts that held: (1) theme.py 96 'QToolButton[popupMode="1"] { padding-right: 20px; }' is the Qt-docs boilerplate for MenuButtonPopup (=1); every production menu trigger is set to InstantPopup (=2) in bind_tool_menu (ui.py 477), and grep finds no MenuButtonPopup anywhere under src/ (only the synthetic split button in tools/theme-validation.FCMacro:172), so the padding rule and the ::menu-button rules at 97-99 are inert in the app. (2) theme.py 95 only restates Qt's default (menu-indicator at bottom-right of the padding box), and the ::menu-arrow image at 179 applies to MenuButtonPopup only, so InstantPopup triggers get the base style's PE_IndicatorArrowDown inside the 4 px padding, over the glyph corner. Line 163 (image:none for KurtShapeReferenceMenu) shows the author knows the indicator draws on InstantPopup buttons. (3) I cropped and 4x-enlarged the Oct 4 captures (validation/onshape-design/model-workspace-1440.png: hamburger and More trigger; sketch-editor-1440.png: Geometry/Constraints/Edit triggers): the arrow sits under the glyph's bottom-right and touches the Geometry glyph's circle, as described. (4) Not fixed at HEAD: git blame shows lines 95-96 unchanged since b65f066 (Oct 2); 4681ddf (Oct 13) only recolored ::menu-button hover/disabled; git diff HEAD on theme.py/ui.py is empty; InstantPopup has been used since b65f066, so the Oct 4 captures reflect current code. What did not fully hold: the causal framing is loose — nothing is 'wrong' in Qt terms (this is the standard Qt/FreeCAD InstantPopup corner arrow); the deviation is from the design target in docs/onshape-design-audit/PLAN.md:42,158,233 ('small chevrons' like Onshape) and proposed-shell-layout.png, which draws chevrons beside the glyph. Line refs are accurate. Classification design_weakness / low is honest.

### A-16 Empty new document offers no in-canvas guidance

**opportunity · severity low · confidence high · user value low · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: captures · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.new / start` · lines src/kurtshape/ui.py 2922-2927 (Panel.new), 4329-4335 (start fallback), 629 and 655-690 (footer text lifecycle), 549 and 1679 (existing tooltip hints), 3214-3234 (guidance that appears only after Shift+S); docs/onshape-design-audit/PLAN.md:194
- **Evidence:** start() calls panel.new() which shows an axis cross only; startup-origin.png (Oct 6): blank white canvas, 'Features (0)', 'Parts (0)', 'Sketches only · mm', footer 'New complete'. The only hint is the mouse-navigation sentence in the idle footer (629).
- **Scenario:** First launch, or after New.
- **What Kurt would notice:** A professional first impression is a blank page; Kurt (or a collaborator) must know Shift+S and the plane picking rule from the README.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Mildly. An Onshape/SolidWorks user is used to a blank studio with planes in the tree and finds the Sketch glyph (tooltip 'Create sketch · Shift+S') and the 'Search tools… Alt+C' field without help; the one real stumble, picking a plane after Shift+S, is already explained the moment they press it. A one-line muted caption would shave the first minute, not more.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Launch the app.
- **Proposed change:** A dismissible QLabel overlay parented to the MDI viewport (hidden once the document has a feature): 'Shift+S to sketch · click Top/Front/Right · Alt+C to search tools', styled with muted ink, plus an 'Example' link; consistent with the proposed layout's quiet canvas caption.
- **Acceptance:** Overlay visible on an empty document, gone after the first sketch, absent in validation captures of modeled parts.
- **Risk:** Must not intercept picks: WA_TransparentForMouseEvents.
- **Adversarial check:** Read ui.py start() 4318-4341 and Panel.new 2922-2927: the non-demo path calls panel.new(), which runs the 'new' operation, resets the view and turns on the axis cross only. docs/sketch-interaction-reliability/evidence/screenshots/startup-origin.png (Oct 6, current light shell) shows exactly that: blank white canvas, axis cross, 'Features (0)', 'Parts (0)', 'Sketches only · mm', footer 'New complete'. grep for overlay/caption/empty-state text in src finds nothing, and no module parents a widget to the MDI view for this purpose. git log shows no commit since Oct 6 touching this. So the core claim holds. The finder's evidence sentence is inaccurate in one detail: the mouse-navigation sentence at 629 is not an 'idle' hint — notify() (655-665) replaces it with 'New complete' and restore_status (668-690) then shows 'Local document · mm · ready', so the idle footer carries no sketch hint at all; the hints that do exist are the Sketch button tooltip 'Create sketch · Shift+S' (549), the plane-row tooltip 'select after Shift+S to start a sketch' (1679), Help > Shortcuts/Example (526-528) and the visible 'Search tools… Alt+C' placeholder (71). Also, once Shift+S is pressed the app does guide: editor text 'Click a planar face in the model, or Top, Front or Right in the history. One click starts the sketch.' and footer 'New sketch · select a plane or planar face' (3231-3234). The gap is therefore only the pre-action blank state.
