Importantly, this is a planned, unimplemented design item: docs/onshape-design-audit/PLAN.md:194 requires 'A blank studio should show a restrained prompt: choose a plane and start a sketch, with the actual shortcut. Keep the canvas mostly empty… Avoid dashboard cards or invented onboarding workflows', and EXECUTION.md (the implementation record) never mentions it. One correction to the proposal's framing: the centered 'Native FreeCAD viewport / Quiet canvas' text in proposed-shell-layout.png is a schematic placeholder for the viewport, not a designed caption; PLAN.md:194 is the real authority, and it argues for a single muted line, not a dismissible card with links. Classification opportunity / low is honest.

## B. Viewport graphics

### B-01 Shift+R quality toggle marks the document Modified, re-tessellates every visible object synchronously, skips objects created afterwards, and persists into the file if saved while on

**verified defect · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort M** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/viewport_actions.py` · `ViewportActions.toggle_high_quality` · lines 219-253 (loop 228-238, restore 244-249)
- **Evidence:** Lines 229-238: `for obj in doc.Objects: vp = obj.ViewObject ... setattr(vp, name, min(value, maximum))` for Deviation 0.05 / AngularDeflection 10.0 on every object, hidden helpers included; no Modified handling. exp2 (offscreen Gui, fresh profile): Gui document Modified before=False, after Deviation change=True (also True after AngularDeflection and Transparency changes). core.py:1236-1242 `_modified` returns gui_doc.Modified and ui.py:1766-1771 appends ' · Modified' from it. docs/VIEWPORT_ACTIONS.md:49 admits 'Objects added after enabling quality retain their native defaults'.
- **Scenario:** Kurt opens a saved part, presses Shift+R to look at a fillet, then adds a feature or closes the tab.
- **What Kurt would notice:** The document title gains ' · Modified' and close/open guards treat a pure display change as unsaved work; the next pad/hole is drawn coarse while older features are fine; on a dense model every ViewObject re-meshes on the GUI thread in one burst (plate60: 6.5k -> 17.5k facets, ~0.3 s on this busy machine, more on real assemblies). The running app has the same code (file unchanged since b65f066).
- **A newcomer would notice:** Onshape has no dirty state at all, so a 'Modified' badge and a save prompt after a display toggle look like a bug; a new feature that is visibly coarser than older ones reads as broken geometry.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** In the app: open the acceptance plate, note the title has no suffix, press Shift+R, observe ' · Modified'. Headless: exp2_gui_defaults.py in scratch (sets vo.Deviation and reads Gui.getDocument(...).Modified).
- **Proposed change:** Replace the per-document toggle with a persistent display preset: write Mod/Part MeshDeviation and MeshAngularDeflection (the keys PartGui.pyd reads at ViewProvider construction; defaults 0.2 / 28.65 confirmed by exp2 box ViewObject) into KurtShape's profile at startup next to navigation.apply_navigation, so new objects inherit it; for already-open documents apply per visible, non-KurtShapeInternal object only when the saved value differs, wrapped in the PlaneHighlight.restore_modified pattern (history_selection.py:58-68) and yielding to the event loop between objects (QTimer-driven batches, like SketchRegionFill._rebuild). Keep Shift+R as 'cycle preset' and surface the preset in Settings (workspace_preferences.py) beside mouse navigation.
- **Acceptance:** After Shift+R or a preset change: Gui Modified unchanged unless it was already set; a Pad created after enabling has the preset's Deviation/AngularDeflection; a 60-hole plate preset switch never blocks input longer than one frame per object; tools/viewport-actions-validation.FCMacro extended with a Modified-flag assertion and a created-after-toggle assertion.
- **Dependencies:** Finding on anti-aliasing (same preset mechanism); settings.py for persistence.
- **Risk:** ViewObject Deviation is saved in the FCStd, so files opened in plain FreeCAD show the preset's values; acceptable (display only).
- **Adversarial check:** Read src/kurtshape/viewport_actions.py 219-253 (function spans to 253, not 252): the enable branch loops over every doc.Objects ViewObject that has Deviation/AngularDeflection and setattr()s min(value, 0.05 / 10.0) with no Modified handling; the disable branch restores only the snapshot, so objects created in between are untouched (docs/VIEWPORT_ACTIONS.md:47-48 admits it). git log shows the file unchanged since b65f066, so HEAD and Kurt's older running build are identical; nothing is fixed. Modified claim: the finder's offscreen probe output is in scratch (area-b-viewport/exp2_result.json: gui_modified_after_Deviation_change before=false after=true, also true for AngularDeflection and Transparency), which matches FreeCAD's ViewProviderDocumentObject::onChanged behaviour; core.py:1236-1242 reads Gui Modified, ui.py:1766-1772 renders ' · Modified', core.py:1744 clears it only on save/open. KurtShape itself treats this as a defect class: history_selection.py:58-68, extrusion_preview.py:206-216, assembly.py:268-283 and core.py:1252-1261 all preserve the flag around presentation writes, so the toggle violates the app's own standard. The validation macro (tools/viewport-actions-validation.FCMacro:62-68) checks values and BRep only, never the flag, and tests/ has no viewport_actions coverage, so it would not catch it. Corrections: (1) the title suffix is refreshed only by refresh() (ui.py:1630/1755/1827), so ' · Modified' appears at the next refresh, while FreeCAD's native close prompt reacts immediately; (2) hidden objects receive the property but FreeCAD defers their re-mesh until shown, so the synchronous burst is per visible object (the ~0.3 s plate60 figure comes from exp1 303 ms noisy); (3) extra consequence the finder missed: saving while the toggle is on persists the finer per-object values into the FCStd and the in-memory restore map is lost on reopen, so the toggle silently becomes permanent in that file. Everything in the title holds.

### B-02 Default tessellation leaves fillets and holes visibly faceted (7 facets per 90 deg, 26 segments on a 3 mm hole)

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value high · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/viewport_actions.py` · `toggle_high_quality (only control); no startup default` · lines 231 (only control); ui.py:4311-4336 start() sets no Mod/Part default
- **Evidence:** exp2: Part::Box ViewObject defaults Deviation 0.2, AngularDeflection 28.65 deg; Mod/Part group empty in a fresh profile; KurtShape writes no Mod/Part key (grep 'Preferences/Mod/Part' in src: none). exp4 (MeshPart.meshFromShape with the display rule, linear = extents/300*0.2 %): quarter cylinders r=0.5..2 on a 30 mm host -> 7 segments per 90 deg at 28.65 deg, 15 at 12 deg, 36 at 5 deg; r=3 hole in a 120x80 plate -> 26 / 60 / 144 segments per circle. Angular deflection, not Deviation, is the binding limit on fillets (exp1 'limited_by': angular).
- **Scenario:** Any part with small fillets, chamfer blends, holes or revolved rounds, viewed closer than full fit.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Kurt sees flat facets on every fillet (sagitta 0.062 mm on r=2 is small, but the shading discontinuity per facet is obvious under the specular default material) and polygonal hole walls; the preview ghost he just confirmed looked smooth, so the committed part appears to have lost quality.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Onshape and SolidWorks tessellate adaptively and smoothly; seven flats on a quarter round looks like a low-poly STL, not a CAD model.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Headless counts: scratch exp4_fillet_segments.py and exp3_display_vs_overlay.py (deterministic). Visual: in the app, fillet a 30 mm block with r=2 and zoom to the edge; count facets.
- **Proposed change:** Standard preset MeshAngularDeflection=12 deg, MeshDeviation=0.2 (plate60 facets 6.5k -> 14.7k, fillet 7 -> 15 segments per 90 deg); presentation 5 deg / 0.05 % (34.8k facets, 36 per 90 deg); draft keeps 28.65 / 0.5. Apply through the preset mechanism in the previous finding; keep export untouched (core.py:2834 is already absolute).
- **Acceptance:** On the r=2 fillet fixture the top rim mesh has >= 15 segments per 90 deg at standard; plate60 recompute+tessellate stays within the existing responsiveness budget in tools/perf (no new benchmark claim); STL export facet count unchanged (30492 at 0.05 mm / 0.1 rad).
- **Dependencies:** Preset mechanism.
- **Risk:** Re-tessellation cost on large STEP imports (hundreds of faces); mitigate by applying lazily per visible object and offering draft.
- **Adversarial check:** No KurtShape source writes any Mod/Part key (grep over src: only View, OutputWindow, MainWindow, Measure, Sketcher groups are touched in theme.py/navigation.py), start() at ui.py:4311-4336 sets no tessellation default, and the finder's fresh-profile probe lists Mod/Part as empty with Part::Box ViewObject defaults Deviation 0.2 / AngularDeflection 28.65 deg (exp2_result.json). The finder's own numbers were internally inconsistent (exp1/exp3 report 4 segments per 90 deg at defaults, exp4 reports 7), so I re-measured headlessly with the display rule ViewProviderPartExt uses ((dx+dy+dz)/300*Deviation, absolute angular): r=2 fillet on 30x20x20 and 30x30x30 blocks at 0.2 %/28.65 deg gives 8 arc points = 7 segments per 90 deg, 15 at 12 deg, 36 at 5 deg; r=1.5 and r=3 holes in a 120x80x10 plate give 26 / 60 / 144 segments per circle. The title's numbers are right and angular deflection binds (dropping Deviation to 0.05 changes nothing at 12 or 5 deg; at 28.65 deg it only lifts the fillet to 11). One correction: the consequence's 'sagitta 0.062 mm on r=2' is the pure-angular-step bound; at the measured 7 segments it is 0.013 mm, so the visible artifact is the polygonal silhouette and per-vertex shading banding, not depth error. Export is unaffected (core.py:2834 absolute 0.05 mm / 0.1 rad). Root cause is FreeCAD's stock default that KurtShape ships unchanged; Kurt's live profile was not read, so whether he lowered it by hand is unknown. Nothing at HEAD addresses it.

### B-03 No anti-aliasing: View/AntiAliasing is never set, so 2 px edges and sketch curves render stair-stepped (cosmetic, startup-only fix)

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value high · effort S** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: captures · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/theme.py` · `apply_native_light_preferences (sets no View/AntiAliasing); navigation.apply_navigation` · lines theme.py 225-280 (apply_native_light_preferences) and 53-59 (apply_view_theme); ui.py 4311-4336 (start order)
- **Evidence:** No source writes View/AntiAliasing (grep). Fresh-profile View group (exp2) has no AntiAliasing key; the shipped template data/Gui/PreferencePackTemplates/View.cfg records AntiAliasing=0; exp2b render action: isSmoothing()=0, getNumPasses()=1. Magnified crops of validation/onshape-theme-repair/model-workspace-1440.png (Oct 4, current light shell) at x8 show hard 2 px black edge steps with no intermediate shades; docs/sketch-interaction-reliability/evidence/screenshots/circle-drag-after.png (Oct 6) x5 shows the same on the blue circle. FreeCADGui.dll carries the AntiAliasing parameter (2 string hits) so the control exists.
- **Scenario:** Every view; most visible on diagonal silhouette edges, circles in sketches, and the hover outline.
- **What Kurt would notice:** The canvas looks dated next to Onshape; 2 px aliased lines shimmer during orbit. Kurt's running app renders the same unless he changed the preference by hand (his profile was not read).
- **A newcomer would notice:** Onshape and SolidWorks both anti-alias; the canvas reads as a decade old at first glance.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Crop any edge of the Oct 4 capture at 8x (scratch crop_top_edge_x8.png). In the app: Preferences > Display > 3D View shows Anti-aliasing None.
- **Proposed change:** Standard preset writes View/AntiAliasing=3 (FreeCAD's View3DInventorViewer::AntiAliasing order None, Smoothing, MSAA2x, MSAA4x, MSAA8x; confirm the integer in the running build's Preferences dialog before hard-coding) at startup before the first document view is created; presentation 4 (MSAA8x) with fallback to 3; draft 0. Gate on the GL renderer string (see software-GL finding). Verify whether a live change re-creates the QOpenGLWidget on this Qt6 build or needs 'applies to new views'; apply at startup either way.
- **Acceptance:** A framebuffer grab (viewer.grabFramebuffer) across a 45 deg silhouette edge shows >= 2 intermediate grey levels per step; orbit frame time on the acceptance plate within 10 % of the no-AA baseline on Kurt's GPU; headless suites unaffected.
- **Dependencies:** Renderer detection; preset mechanism.
- **Risk:** MSAA on Intel iGPU is fine at 4x; on llvmpipe/RDP it multiplies fill cost (auto-draft needed); some drivers ignore GL_LINE_SMOOTH (mode 1), so prefer MSAA.
- **Adversarial check:** Facts hold: grep finds no writer of View/AntiAliasing anywhere in src/tools (the only 'Antialiasing' hits are QPainter hints in rollback_history.py:252 and sketch_references.py:238); theme.py:225-280 apply_native_light_preferences copies only colour keys from the Light pack and apply_view_theme (53-59) only sets the background; navigation.apply_navigation writes navigation keys only; the runtime template data/Gui/PreferencePackTemplates/View.cfg line 16 records AntiAliasing=0 and the fresh-profile View group (exp2_result.json) has no AntiAliasing key; exp2b_result.json shows gl_smoothing 0 / gl_passes 1. I viewed the finder's x8 crop of the Oct 4 model-workspace-1440.png (hard 2 px black steps, no intermediate shades) and the x5/x8 crops of the Oct 6 circle-drag-after.png (blocky blue circle): the stair-stepping is real, and both captures come from isolated validation sessions, i.e. shipped defaults; Kurt's live profile was not read. Nothing at HEAD changes this. I downgrade severity only: it is a pervasive but purely cosmetic rendering issue with no effect on geometry, picking or workflow, so impact is low even though the value of fixing it is high. Implementation caveat the finder half-flagged: FreeCAD's MSAA path replaces the viewer's QOpenGLWidget, and KurtShape installs about ten event filters keyed on that widget instance (navigation.py:134, hover_outline.py:260, sketch_cursor.py:72, sketch_regions.py:149, sketch_snapping.py:221, edge_picking.py:104, responsiveness.py:247/330, sketch_references.py:255/642, general_actions.py:305), so the key must be written at startup before the first view (start() at ui.py:4311-4336 already runs apply_theme/apply_navigation before documents open) and never flipped live without re-running the installers; QPainter overlays on the GL widget also need a check on a multisampled framebuffer.

### B-04 Preview ghost is tessellated far finer than the committed model, so Confirm visibly coarsens geometry

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/extrusion_preview.py` · `render_mesh` · lines 127-141 (render_mesh; 129 tessellate, 136 discretize), 179 (ProvisionalExtrusion), src/kurtshape/preview_worker.py:329
- **Evidence:** Line 129 `vertices, triangles = material.tessellate(0.15)` and 136 `edge.discretize(Deflection=0.15)` (absolute, no angular parameter). exp1 on fresh cylinders r=2.5/25: tessellate(0.15) -> 252 vertices / 500 triangles regardless of radius (~126 segments per circle); exp3: fillet block tessellate(0.15) 6260 triangles vs 452 at the pinned display default (0.2 % / 28.65 deg); r=3 hole 26 segments natively. The committed object is drawn by ViewProviderPartExt with the per-object Deviation/AngularDeflection, not by this mesh.
- **Scenario:** Extrude/Cut/Fillet preview on any curved profile, then Confirm.
- **What Kurt would notice:** The translucent ghost shows round holes and smooth rounds; the accepted feature appears with 26-segment holes and 7-facet fillets. Kurt reads it as 'the result is worse than the preview'. Same in the running app.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Onshape/SolidWorks never show a polygonal hole at default. They watch the hole go from round to a 26-gon and the fillet from smooth to 7 flats the instant they press Confirm, and may read it as the feature failing or the radius being wrong. They would hunt for a display-quality setting and find only an unlabeled Shift+R toggle.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Headless: scratch exp1_tessellation.py (shape_tessellate) and exp3. GUI: preview a 6 mm circle pad on the plate, Confirm, compare the hole wall.
- **Proposed change:** Tessellate the preview with the display rule: compute deflection = (sum of bbox extents)/300 * MeshDeviation and angular = MeshAngularDeflection (the same numbers ViewProviderPartExt will use) and mesh with MeshPart.meshFromShape(Relative=False) or BRepMesh via Part with explicit angular deflection, in both ProvisionalExtrusion (line 177-180) and the exact worker's render_mesh; use the preset's values so preview and result match at every preset. Keep the 500k/1M vertex guard (lines 132-133).
- **Acceptance:** For the r=3 hole fixture the overlay rim segment count equals the committed ViewObject mesh segment count (+/-1) at each preset; existing extrusion-preview GUI recipe (validation/ui-refinement 625/625 orange pixel check) still passes.
- **Dependencies:** Preset values readable from preview_worker (pass them in the proposal/env).
- **Risk:** Slightly coarser provisional ghost at draft; none at standard.
- **Adversarial check:** The cited code is exactly as described at HEAD f57d592: src/kurtshape/extrusion_preview.py:129 `material.tessellate(0.15)` and :136 `edge.discretize(Deflection=0.15)` inside render_mesh, which is the only mesh source for the ghost (provisional prism at :179 `render_mesh(unit, unit)`, exact worker at preview_worker.py:329, GUI fallback at :559). git blame shows the lines unchanged since 4681ddfd (2026-10-13); no later commit mentions tessellation/deviation; no test under tests/ references render_mesh, tessellate or Deviation. KurtShape sets no `Mod/Part` MeshDeviation/MeshAngularDeflection anywhere in src/ and the live profile runtime/user-data/user.cfg carries no override, so committed features draw with ViewProviderPartExt's defaults (0.2 % of summed bbox extents / 300, 28.65 deg). A headless probe under the bundled pythonw.exe (FreeCAD 1.1.4, OCCT 7.8.1) reproduces the finder's numbers: `Shape.tessellate(0.15)` yields 126 rim segments on a cylinder for every radius 0.5-100 mm (252 pts / 252 tris per lateral face), while the display rule emulated with MeshPart.meshFromShape(LinearDeflection=(sum extents)/300*0.2, AngularDeflection=0.5, Relative=False) gives 26-28 segments on an r=3 hole for plates from 30x20x8 to 200x150x20; a 90-degree r=2 fillet face meshes to 14 display facets (7 segments) vs 64 preview triangles. Even the Shift+R high-quality toggle (viewport_actions.py:231, Deviation 0.05 / 10 deg) caps at roughly 36 segments on r=3, still far coarser than the ghost. The item is already recorded, unfixed, as B-04 in docs/fable-deep-review/visual-proposals/README.md:104. Classification design_weakness / medium / high confidence stands; the GUI recipe validation/ui-refinement-preview/gui-result.json checks behaviour only, not mesh density, so the "still passes" acceptance clause is credible but adds no coverage.

### B-05 Geometry hover/selection/model-default colours are deliberately left to the native profile (DESIGN.md Model Color Rule), so they vary with profile history; the amber geometry_selected token is dead

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/theme.py` · `COLORS['geometry_selected']; apply_native_light_preferences.view_names` · lines src/kurtshape/theme.py:18 (dead token), 225-231 (docstring stating the preservation intent), 239-248 (view_names); src/kurtshape/hover_outline.py:154; src/kurtshape/workspace_preferences.py:51-66 (Restore path); tools/theme-startup-validation.FCMacro:128,153 (asserts preservation); DESIGN.md:162,166
- **Evidence:** theme.py:18 `"geometry_selected": "#ffc107"` has no reader (grep src/docs: only its definition). view_names (239-248) omits HighlightColor, SelectionColor, DefaultShapeColor, DefaultShapeLineColor, BackgroundColor. exp2b SoFCUnifiedSelection in effect on a fresh profile: colorHighlight (0, 0.478, 0) = #007A00 dark green, colorSelection (0.231, 0.357, 0.859) = #3B5BDB blue; hover_outline.py:154 draws (0.16, 0.42, 0.88) = #296BE0 blue. docs/onshape-design-audit/PLAN.md:126 lists amber as 'renderer tuning still needs native verification'. After a native Dark pack the repair leaves Dark values (DefaultShapeColor #727980, HighlightColor #246EAB, SelectionColor #35A047) because they are outside view_names.
- **Scenario:** Hovering edges/vertices (green), hovering faces (blue outline), clicking (blue fill) on grey parts; 'Restore light appearance' after someone picked a dark theme in native Preferences.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Hover and selected states share a hue (two blues) while edge hover is an unrelated green; the documented amber selection never appears; Restore cannot bring back light model/selection colours. Unchanged in the running app.
- **A newcomer would notice:** On a fresh profile: hovering an edge turns it dark green, hovering a face draws a blue outline, clicking fills the face medium blue. Nothing is amber as in Onshape, and hover hue depends on element type. Selected versus hovered remains distinguishable (fill versus outline), so there is no functional confusion; they would adapt within minutes and read it as polish missing from a shell that labels itself "Light (Onshape)".
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Open any part, hover an edge (green), click a face (blue). Headless: exp2b_gui_scene.py unified_selection.
- **Proposed change:** Own View/SelectionColor and View/HighlightColor in apply_native_light_preferences (write only when different, as today): SelectionColor = geometry_selected #FFC107, HighlightColor a lighter amber (#FFE082) or keep the blue outline for faces but recolour the hover line to the same hover token; also include DefaultShapeColor/DefaultShapeLineColor/BackgroundColor in the Restore path only (not startup) so 'Restore light appearance' really restores the Light look. Update sketch_colors.py candidates if ConstrainedIcoColor changes. Keep DESIGN.md's Model Color Rule: per-part ShapeColor stays untouched.
- **Acceptance:** Framebuffer grab after clicking a face shows the chosen selection colour; edge hover and face hover use one hover hue; appearance-validation recipe (validation/appearance-pass) extended with selection-colour assertions; Restore after a Dark pack yields DefaultShapeColor #ADB5BD.
- **Dependencies:** Decision from Kurt on amber vs blue.
- **Risk:** Amber on the #ADB5BD part colour needs a contrast check; PlaneHighlight uses the accent blue for planes and should stay distinct from selection.
- **Adversarial check:** Core facts hold: theme.py:18 "geometry_selected" has no reader anywhere in src/, tools/ or tests/ (only PLAN.md:126 names it, as a proposal flagged "renderer tuning still needs native verification"); it was added in 4681ddf (2026-10-13) and is untouched at HEAD f57d592, so this is not already solved. view_names (theme.py:239-248) omits HighlightColor/SelectionColor/DefaultShapeColor/DefaultShapeLineColor/BackgroundColor and nothing else in src/ writes View/SelectionColor or View/HighlightColor. hover_outline.py:154 is (0.16,0.42,0.88) and the Oct-4 capture validation/onshape-interaction-pass/view-interaction/face-hover.png has that exact #296BE0 outline pixel, still drawn on the selected face. Pack values decode as claimed (Light #007A00/#3B5BDB/#ADB5BD, Dark #246EAB/#35A047/#727980). Restore (workspace_preferences.py:51-66) reuses apply_native_light_preferences, so Dark model/selection values survive it. No tests/ cover the theme.

Why downgraded rather than confirmed: (1) The omission is a documented, validated design decision, not an oversight: theme.py:228-230 docstring, docs/onshape-design-audit/THEME-REPAIR.md ("preserves navigation, selection/highlight preferences, default model material settings"), DESIGN.md:162 ("selection and model appearances retain their existing native owners") and the Model Color Rule at DESIGN.md:166, which explicitly covers "geometry selection", not only per-part ShapeColor. tools/theme-startup-validation.FCMacro:128,153 seeds the Dark pack (run_gui_recipe.ps1:106-111) and asserts "Model default and selection preferences are preserved". The proposed change contradicts that rule and would fail that check; the real dependency is a DESIGN.md decision plus macro update. (2) The scenario describes Light-pack profiles, not the running app: the live profile runtime/user-data/user.cfg (used by launch-kurtshape.ps1:48, written 2026-10-17 01:28) carries Dark-pack values with Theme already corrected to FreeCAD Light: View/HighlightColor #246EAB (blue), View/SelectionColor #35A047 (green), DefaultShapeColor #727980, BackgroundColor #1F1F1F. In Kurt's app, edge/vertex hover and the face outline are therefore both blue and selection is green-based; the finder's "edge hover green, two blues" is inverted there. The accurate weakness is that hover/selection/model-default hues are whatever the profile's history left, i.e. profile-dependent rather than theme-owned. (3) The rendered selection fill is not #3B5BDB: the capture and view-interaction/result.json record selected_interior_color #cdf3ff (pale blue from FreeCAD's emissive+diffuse selection rendering); "blue fill" holds only qualitatively. (4) BackgroundColor is mitigated: apply_view_theme forces the viewer white on every created/opened/adopted document (ui.py:1161,1259,1358) and on Restore, so the dark preference value is not seen in KurtShape's own views. (5) The exp2b_gui_scene.py probe is not in the repository, so its numbers are unverifiable here, though the Light-pack values corroborate them; non-theme validation profiles carry Light values KurtShape never writes, consistent with FreeCAD 1.1 applying the Light pack itself at first start. Classification and severity stand (design_weakness, low); title, consequence and dependencies are corrected.
- **Merged duplicate B-14:** Face hover outline colour is hard-coded instead of following View/HighlightColor; matches the live profile's blue preselection only by coincidence — design weakness, low, source · verified. Evidence: Outline (0.16,0.42,0.88) #296BE0 vs native SelectionColor #3B5BDB (exp2b) and native preselection #007A00 for edges/vertices; three different cues for two states.

### B-06 Selected-plane accent recolouring is skipped on the pinned build; only transparency changes

**verified defect · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: reproduced · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/history_selection.py` · `PlaneHighlight.show` · lines 105-128
- **Evidence:** Lines 122-124: `for name in ("ShapeColor", "LineColor", "PlaneColor"): if name in view.PropertiesList: setattr(view, name, color)`. exp2c: App::Plane ViewObject PropertiesList on 1.1.4 is exactly [BoundingBox, DisplayMode, OnTopWhenSelected, Selectable, SelectionStyle, ShapeAppearance, ShowInTree, TransformOrigin, Transparency, Visibility]; none of the three names is present, so the accent branch never runs; line 126 still sets Transparency = min(70, current). (ShapeColor works as a compatibility attribute, e.g. studio.py:494, but is not listed.)
- **Scenario:** Selecting Top/Front/Right in history, or Shift+S placement.
- **What Kurt would notice:** The plane appears (Visibility) at 70 % transparency in the default plane material instead of the accent blue the design intends; selected vs merely visible planes look alike. Same in the running app (same PropertiesList).
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Select 'Top' in Feature history and compare the plane colour with COLORS['accent'] #185FA5; or run exp2c and inspect origin_plane_vp_all.
- **Proposed change:** Remember/restore ShapeAppearance (a Material list) and set a Material with DiffuseColor = accent and Transparency 0.7 on App::Plane and PartDesign::Plane view objects; drop the PropertiesList gate for ShapeColor or use the compatibility attribute directly. Keep the Modified-flag restore.
- **Acceptance:** tools/view-selection-validation.FCMacro asserts the selected plane's ShapeAppearance diffuse equals the accent and is restored exactly after deselect; save/reopen leaves the saved plane appearance unchanged (existing check).
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** ShapeAppearance restore must round-trip the Material object exactly (validated by the existing view-selection recipe).

### B-07 Shift+T transparency is a persistent 75 % ViewObject write that dirties the document

**verified defect · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: source · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.transparent` · lines 4190-4193
- **Evidence:** `obj.ViewObject.Transparency = 0 if obj.ViewObject.Transparency else 75` for each selected object; exp2 shows Gui Modified flips to True on a Transparency change; shortcuts.json:60 labels it 'Make selection transparent' and :59 already notes isolation 'may hide surroundings rather than apply Onshape temporary transparency'. Selection from the viewport yields the Tip feature, not the Body (studio.py sets colours on the Body and relies on Body unify).
- **Scenario:** Kurt presses Shift+T on a part to see inside, then saves or closes.
- **What Kurt would notice:** The part is saved semi-transparent in the FCStd and in exports' provenance view state; the title shows ' · Modified' for a view-only action; toggling on a feature vs its Body can disagree after the next Tip change.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Select a part, Shift+T, observe ' · Modified'; save, reopen: still transparent.
- **Proposed change:** Implement as a session-only presentation: remember/restore like PlaneHighlight (Transparency + Modified flag), apply to the owning Body (bodies.owner) so unify propagates consistently, and clear on document close/open; or render through a Coin override (SoMaterial transparency on the ViewObject.RootNode) that never touches properties.
- **Acceptance:** After Shift+T twice the ViewObject property equals its original and Gui Modified is unchanged; save/reopen shows opaque parts; GUI recipe assertion added.
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** Low.

### B-08 Overlay line widths and FreeCAD's default edge width are unscaled framebuffer pixels, so apparent edge weight varies with display scale

**design weakness · severity low · confidence medium · user value low · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/hover_outline.py` · `FaceHoverOutline.show (lineWidth 2.5); CoinExtrusionOverlay.show (lineWidth 2)` · lines src/kurtshape/hover_outline.py:157; src/kurtshape/extrusion_preview.py:604; src/kurtshape/sketch_references.py:365
- **Evidence:** hover_outline.py:157 `style.lineWidth = 2.5`, extrusion_preview.py:604 `style.lineWidth = 2`; fresh defaults View/DefaultShapeLineWidth 2, sketch ViewObject LineWidth 2 / PointSize 4 (exp2c). Coin line width is a GL line width in framebuffer pixels; the 150 % captures (2160 px wide for a 1440 px window) show 2 px edges, i.e. 1.33 logical px, while sketch_snapping.py:45 already treats pick radius as framebuffer pixels and scales it. No code scales any width by devicePixelRatioF.
- **Scenario:** Kurt's 150 % monitor vs a 100 % external display or a 200 % laptop.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Edges look thin and aliased at 150 %, heavy at 100 %, hairline at 200 %; the hover outline's 2.5 px vs model 2 px distinction disappears at high DPI.
- **A newcomer would notice:** On a 150 % display an Onshape/SolidWorks user sees thin, visibly stair-stepped edges (the crop shows no MSAA; src/ writes no View/AntiAliasing) and attributes it to "the FreeCAD look", not to display scale; the width itself is close to Onshape's ~1 CSS-px edges. On a 200 % laptop (Surface/4K, common for newcomers) edges and sketch points become hairline/tiny, which makes sketch vertices harder to see, though snapping and picking remain correct. Nobody would perceive the cross-display inconsistency unless they use two displays with different scale; the aliasing is what they would actually complain about, and width scaling does not fix that.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Compare crop_top_edge_x8.png (2 physical px) with a 100 % capture of the same scene.
- **Proposed change:** Introduce a shared `display_scale()` (devicePixelRatioF of the active view) and multiply overlay widths by it; write View/DefaultShapeLineWidth per DPI bucket at startup (2 at <=125 %, 3 at 150 %, 4 at 200 %) through the preset mechanism; leave sketch widths native unless Kurt asks.
- **Acceptance:** At 100/150/200 % the model edge measures ~2 logical px in grabs; hover outline stays ~1.25x the edge width.
- **Dependencies:** Preset mechanism; verify Coin width units on this Qt6 HiDPI path (unverified).
- **Risk:** Existing GUI recipes that count pixels (e.g. 625/625 orange interior) are unaffected because they test fills, not lines.
- **Adversarial check:** Core claim holds and is now measured, not just argued. Code: hover_outline.py:157 sets lineWidth 2.5, extrusion_preview.py:604 sets 2, and a third constant the finder missed, sketch_references.py:365, sets 3 (sketch source-edge highlight). grep over src/ shows devicePixelRatioF is used only for pointer-to-framebuffer coordinates (hover_outline.py:233, general_actions.py:313, sketch_references.py:552/585, sketch_snapping.py:231), the pick radius (sketch_snapping.py:45, via begin(pixel_ratio)) and cursor pixmaps; no width is ever multiplied, and KurtShape never writes View/DefaultShapeLineWidth (docs/fable-deep-review/visual-proposals/README.md line 104 confirms the app sets nothing for line widths). Measurement: in validation/onshape-theme-repair/model-workspace-1440.png (2160x1380, DPR 1.5 per sibling design-surface.json) the vertical model edges are exactly 2 framebuffer px of (25,25,25) at x=722-723 and x=1381-1382, and a run-length histogram over the viewport peaks at 2 with nothing at 1 or 3, so a width-2 edge is 1.33 logical px at 150%; this reproduces the finder's number without their missing crop_top_edge_x8.png / exp2c evidence (neither is in the repo). Corrections: (1) the hover consequence is mischaracterised. In validation/onshape-interaction-pass/view-interaction/face-hover.png (1401x1023 = 934x682 logical x 1.5) every hovered edge profile is 3 px, e.g. x=288-289 black then x=290 blue (41,107,224), y=222 blue then y=223-224 black: 2.5 rasterises to a 3-px line and the model's own 2-px edge wins the overlap, so the hover shows only as a 1-framebuffer-px fringe on one side (0.67 logical px at 150%). The '2.5 vs 2' ratio is not what governs visibility, and the proposed acceptance 'hover stays ~1.25x the edge width' would still yield a 1-px fringe after scaling (4 vs 3) unless the overlay also wins the depth tie. (2) The 'preset mechanism' dependency does not exist at HEAD (no 'preset' in src/); it is the unimplemented Quality-presets proposal in visual-proposals README section 3, which already lists 'DefaultShapeLineWidth 2 (3 at 150 %, 4 at 200 %)', so the finding partly restates a known proposal. (3) The scenario is hypothetical: all 45 recorded validation device_pixel_ratio values are 1.5 and no 100% capture exists; on the one display Kurt is known to use, the effect is 1.33-logical-px edges and a 0.67-logical-px hover fringe. (4) Writing DefaultShapeLineWidth per DPI only affects newly created view providers; LineWidth persists per object in saved documents, so files made at another scale keep their old width. (5) Point sizes were not measured; same glPointSize mechanism is plausible but unverified. This is inherited upstream FreeCAD behaviour, consistent within a display (overlays and model edges share the same framebuffer-pixel units) and inconsistent only across display scales. Classification design_weakness and severity low stand; confidence stays medium because the user-facing impact rests on an unverified multi-display scenario and the hover part needed correcting.

### B-09 Preset work needs a renderer gate: nothing detects software OpenGL (CI, RDP) or the Intel iGPU, so a future MSAA/fine-tessellation preset would crawl there

**opportunity · severity low · confidence high · user value low · effort S** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: preference · verified

- **Source:** `launch-kurtshape.ps1` · `launch environment (no QT_OPENGL); workspace_preferences.WorkspacePreferences` · lines launch-kurtshape.ps1 44-54 (launch environment); .github/workflows/windows-ci.yml:152 (QT_OPENGL=software)
- **Evidence:** The launcher sets no QT_OPENGL and KurtShape never inspects the GL renderer; bin/opengl32sw.dll is bundled and the View.cfg template exposes Preferences/OpenGL/UseSoftwareOpenGL, so a software fallback is possible. exp2b could not create a GL context offscreen ('QOpenGLWidget is not supported on this platform'), so the Python path to GL_RENDERER is unverified.
- **Scenario:** Kurt uses RDP, a VM, or a machine where Qt falls back to opengl32sw; or a future preset enables MSAA 8x on an Intel iGPU.
- **What Kurt would notice:** A presentation preset could turn orbit into a slideshow with no explanation; today the opposite risk is that quality stays at draft everywhere because nothing adapts.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Nothing today; only a slideshow viewport in a VM or remote session once presets ship.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Launch with QT_OPENGL=software in a private session (projects/dodec-hub/start-session.ps1 style) and try MSAA 8x in Preferences.
- **Proposed change:** After the first View3DInventor exists, make its QOpenGLWidget current and read glGetString(GL_RENDERER/GL_VENDOR) via QOpenGLContext.currentContext().functions(); if it matches llvmpipe / GDI Generic / Microsoft Basic Render Driver, force the draft preset and show a one-line notice in Settings; cap MSAA at 4x for Intel. Record the renderer string in the Messages log and in export provenance's engine block.
- **Acceptance:** Settings shows the detected renderer; forced draft on llvmpipe in the private-session test; no change on a hardware GPU.
- **Dependencies:** Preset mechanism.
- **Risk:** makeCurrent on the viewer widget during startup must not disturb Quarter's context; do it in a QTimer after first paint.
- **Adversarial check:** Facts hold: launch-kurtshape.ps1 sets no QT_OPENGL (env block is lines 44-54, not 48-60; 55-60 is the validation branch), no source inspects the GL renderer (the only OpenGL references are className()=='QOpenGLWidget' checks), bin/opengl32sw.dll is bundled (tools/ci/check-runtime-layout.ps1:7 asserts it) and the View.cfg template exposes OpenGL/UseSoftwareOpenGL. The finder's open question (can Python read GL_RENDERER) is now settled by an earlier verifier's scratch/verify/gl_probe.py: QOpenGLContext + QOffscreenSurface + getProcAddress(glGetString) works under the bundled PySide6 6.8.3, reporting 'Intel(R) RaptorLake-S Mobile Graphics Controller' GL 4.6 by default and 'Gallium 0.4 on llvmpipe' Mesa 12.0.0-rc2 GL 3.0 via opengl32sw.dll with QT_OPENGL=software; so feasibility confidence rises to high. But as a finding it is contingent: today nothing writes anti-aliasing or tessellation above FreeCAD's defaults, so no user is hurt, and the 'quality stays at draft because nothing adapts' half is just B-02/B-03 restated. It is a requirement to build into the preset work, not a standalone medium-severity item; severity low. Two points strengthen it as a gate: .github/workflows/windows-ci.yml:152 runs every GUI recipe with QT_OPENGL=software, so an ungated MSAA default would slow CI, and Kurt's own daily GPU is an Intel iGPU, so the 'cap MSAA at 4x on Intel' clause applies to his machine, not only to RDP/VM scenarios. Nothing at HEAD implements it.

### B-10 Section view is the raw native Clipping dock: no capped faces, native widget styling, and a toggle that only ever opens

**opportunity · severity low · confidence medium · user value medium · effort M** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: preference · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/viewport_actions.py` · `ViewportActions.section_view` · lines 369-393
- **Evidence:** Line 379 `Gui.runCommand('Std_ToggleClipPlane')` then lines 390-392 always `dock.show(); dock.raise_()`; the dock is FreeCAD's Clipping dialog (Coin SoClipPlane; interiors appear hollow). docs/VIEWPORT_ACTIONS.md:97-100 documents that users 'enable the desired native plane there'.
- **Scenario:** Kurt presses Shift+X to inspect a hole or a shell wall.
- **What Kurt would notice:** He gets a native dock with X/Y/Z checkboxes and spinboxes that does not match the owned editor pattern; the cut shows empty interiors (Coin clipping does not cap), so wall thickness is hard to read; Shift+X cannot close the section.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Shift+X on the acceptance plate; enable X; orbit.
- **Proposed change:** Phase 1 (S): an owned 'Section' feature editor (feature_panel.py pattern) driving the same native clip plane through view.toggleClippingPlane / viewer API with plane choice, offset slider, flip, and a real toggle on Shift+X. Phase 2 (L, ambitious): capped sections by evaluating shape.common(half-space) in the private preview worker and publishing the cut solid through CoinExtrusionOverlay-style nodes (no document change), with the clipped original hidden; useful for presentation captures.
- **Acceptance:** Shift+X toggles; the editor sets plane/offset/flip; phase 2 shows solid cut faces in a grab; model revision and Modified unchanged.
- **Dependencies:** Preview worker (extrusion_preview.py) for phase 2.
- **Risk:** Capped section must stay unpickable and clearly transient; clip planes interact with the hover outline and region fill (they are also clipped).

### B-11 Sketch region fill: DESIGN.md/README still say light gray (#ebebeb) and apply() comment still says Strict LESS after a558615 switched to blue tint + LEQUAL

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value low · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/sketch_regions.py` · `SketchRegionFill.COLOR / TRANSPARENCY; _node` · lines src/kurtshape/sketch_regions.py:99-100, 250-252, 334-335; DESIGN.md:164; README.md:7 vs 35; docs/EVERYDAY_UI.md:43 (correct)
- **Evidence:** Code: `COLOR = (0.55, 0.72, 0.95)`, `TRANSPARENCY = 0.65` (blue tint, changed in a558615 on 2026-10-14 from (0.92,0.92,0.92)); DESIGN.md says 'Closed defining regions receive a light gray (#ebebeb) unpickable display fill' and README says 'light gray interior'; line 252 sets SoDepthBuffer LEQUAL while the comment at 335 says 'Strict LESS preserves coplanar line pixels'. The Oct 6 capture still shows the grey era (#E5E5E5 fill).
- **Scenario:** Anyone (Kurt or an agent) tuning sketch presentation from the docs.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Wrong expectations about what a closed region looks like; the LEQUAL/LESS comment misleads the next person touching z-fighting.
- **A newcomer would notice:** A newcomer reading README.md:35 ("light gray interior") before first use would then see a blue tint and briefly wonder whether they are in the right build; no functional impact. The tint itself is familiar: Onshape shades closed regions with a faint translucent fill and SolidWorks' shaded sketch contours are a translucent blue-grey, so the on-screen behaviour needs no explanation.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Compare DESIGN.md 'Sketch-specific overrides' with sketch_regions.py:99-100.
- **Proposed change:** Decide the colour (the blue tint reads well on grey faces; Onshape's region fill is a faint neutral), then update DESIGN.md, README and the line-335 comment; add the colour to theme.COLORS so it is a token, not a literal.
- **Acceptance:** Docs, token and code agree; everyday-ui recipe pixel assertion updated to the token.
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** None.
- **Adversarial check:** Every factual claim checks out at HEAD f57d592. src/kurtshape/sketch_regions.py:99-100 has COLOR = (0.55, 0.72, 0.95) and TRANSPARENCY = 0.65; line 252 sets SoDepthBuffer.LEQUAL; lines 334-335 in apply() still say "stay on the exact sketch plane. Strict LESS preserves coplanar line pixels." git blame shows the comment dates from 4681ddf (2026-10-13, LESS era) and a558615 (2026-10-14) changed LESS to LEQUAL and added SoPolygonOffset without touching that comment, so it is a verified leftover. DESIGN.md:164 still says "light gray (#ebebeb) unpickable display fill" and README.md:35 says "light gray interior"; neither was updated by a558615 or the four later commits (5490cb4, 617c537, b2415ff, 5e9824f). Worse than the finder states: 5e9824f (2026-10-16) rewrote DESIGN.md with 259 insertions and re-emitted the #ebebeb sentence two days after the colour change, and README is now self-contradictory (line 7, added in a558615, says "translucent tint on the surface" while line 35 says "light gray interior"). docs/M1_PROGRESS.md:50 and docs/onshape-workflows/IMPLEMENTATION.md:11 also still say light gray (historical progress notes). No token exists: theme.py COLORS/SKETCH_STATE_COLORS have no region-fill entry and the literal appears only at sketch_regions.py:99. Nothing under tests/ references SketchRegionFill, LEQUAL or the colour, so no test pins the behaviour. Not already solved: docs/EVERYDAY_UI.md:43 and :60-63 correctly document the tint and its Windows 34/34 recipe validation, but that does not fix DESIGN.md, README:35 or the stale comment. Two corrections to the finder's evidence/proposal: (1) the validation/ captures (newest 2026-10-05) predate the Oct 14 change, so their grey fill is expected history, not evidence the current build is grey; (2) tools/everyday-ui-validation.FCMacro:176 already reads panel.sketch_regions.COLOR rather than a literal, and tools/sketch-regions-validation.FCMacro:218-235 already asserts a "translucent blue tint" (its predicate is still named gray), so the acceptance item about the recipe is mostly met. Rendering itself is correct and validated; this is purely a docs/comment/token inconsistency, so design_weakness / low / high confidence stands.

### B-12 Only the toolbar New command shows the origin axis cross; Open, import, recover and bridge-created documents never do

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value low · effort S** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.new; Panel.open_path` · lines 2922-2927 (Panel.new); 2942-2961 (open_path); 1255-1263 (bridge-presented documents); 1356-1360 (CREATE_OPS routed through maximize_document_view at 1159-1164); 817 and 881 (direct operation("new") callers)
- **Evidence:** `new()` calls navigation.reset_new_document_view() then `Gui.activeDocument().activeView().setAxisCross(True)` (2925-2926); setAxisCross is called nowhere else (grep), View/ShowAxisCross is not written, and the axis cross is viewer state, not saved in the FCStd. Origin planes: App::Origin ViewObject default Visibility False, planes True (exp2c); ui.py:3222-3229 shows them only during placement; toggle_planes (3883-3890) exists. Oct 6 startup-origin.png (new) shows the RGB cross; Oct 4 model-workspace-1440.png (opened example) shows none.
- **Scenario:** Kurt starts a new part (cross at origin), opens yesterday's part (no cross, no planes) and loses his bearings in an empty-looking white canvas until he zooms.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Inconsistent spatial reference between sessions; empty opened sketch-only documents can look blank.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Somewhat. Onshape shows faint origin planes and an origin marker in every Part Studio; SolidWorks keeps the origin triad on by default. A newcomer opening a KurtShape file sees neither (planes default hidden, cross absent) and may feel unmoored for a moment, but the Feature history lists Origin/Top/Front/Right, P toggles the planes, and sketch placement shows them. They would more likely ask "where are the planes" than notice the New-vs-Open triad difference.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** New (cross visible) vs Open any FCStd (no cross).
- **Proposed change:** Pick one policy and apply it in both paths (a `prepare_view(view)` helper called from maximize_document_view, which both new and open already route through at ui.py:1259/1358): either always show the small origin triad (setAxisCross True) or rely on the corner coordinate system only; consider Onshape-style faint origin planes visible by default (App::Plane transparency ~80 %) with a Shift+? toggle, decided with Kurt.
- **Acceptance:** New and Open produce identical origin presentation; GUI recipe asserts view.hasAxisCross() equality across both paths.
- **Dependencies:** Kurt's preference.
- **Risk:** A visible cross can be picked by native selection; it is not, but it can occlude small sketches at origin.
- **Adversarial check:** The axis-cross half holds by code reading at HEAD f57d592: src/kurtshape/ui.py:2922-2927 Panel.new calls navigation.reset_new_document_view() then Gui.activeDocument().activeView().setAxisCross(True); grep over src/, tests/, tools/, docs/ finds no other setAxisCross/hasAxisCross call and no write of the View/ShowAxisCross preference (also absent from the live profile runtime/user-data/user.cfg and every validation user.cfg). open_path (2942-2961) only calls isometric()+fit(); operation() routes every CREATE_OPS document ({"new","open","recover","import_step"}, core.py:253) through maximize_document_view (ui.py:1159-1164), which only applies the viewer theme and maximizes the MDI window; the bridge-presented path (ui.py:1255-1263, 'processed') only calls activate_body() and navigation.apply_navigation(). docs/SOLIDWORKS_NAVIGATION.md:50-55 states only the New command uses the camera reset and says nothing about the cross; git status shows ui.py clean and the setAxisCross line dates from baseline b65f066, so nothing fixed it. No test asserts the cross (tests/test_navigation.py:42 covers only the camera reset). Two corrections downgrade the finding. (1) The origin-planes half of the title is wrong: planes are hidden identically in both paths and only shown during sketch placement (3222-3229), toggled by toggle_planes (3883-3890) and restored by cancel_placement (3238-3245); there is no new-vs-open difference there. (2) The Oct 4 model-workspace-1440.png is not an 'opened example': tools/design-surface-validation.FCMacro:326 creates that document with panel.operation("new", name="Design acceptance plate") (the screenshot shows 'Unsaved' and 'Extrude complete'), i.e. a created document that bypassed Panel.new. That actually widens the inconsistency: the typed/bridge 'new' (ui.py:1255-1263), the Part Studio fallback (ui.py:817) and create_assembly (ui.py:881) all call self.operation("new") directly and get neither the 100 mm camera reset nor the cross, so the real rule is 'toolbar New only' rather than 'new vs opened'. Startup-origin.png (Oct 6) does confirm the GUI-New cross. Severity stays low: the corner coordinate-system triad is present in both screenshots, so orientation is never fully lost; the cost is an empty-looking canvas for sketch-only or bridge-created documents.

### B-13 Constraint icons keep the Light pack's #E03131 while dimensions and constrained geometry are black

**opportunity · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: preference · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/theme.py` · `apply_native_light_preferences.view_names (ConstrainedIcoColor copied from the pack); SKETCH_STATE_COLORS` · lines src/kurtshape/theme.py 23-37 (SKETCH_STATE_COLORS lacks ConstrainedIcoColor), 239-248 (view_names copies it from the pack; key on line 241), 266 (override order); src/kurtshape/sketch_colors.py 289-302
- **Evidence:** view_names includes ConstrainedIcoColor and the pack value is 3761320447 = #E03131 (red); SKETCH_STATE_COLORS turns every dimension colour to #000000. The Oct 4 sketch-editor-1440.png shows red constraint ticks; sketch_colors.py:289-290 reads ConstrainedIcoColor at runtime so it adapts to any value.
- **Scenario:** Every sketch with coincident/horizontal/vertical constraints.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Red glyphs read as errors on the light canvas (InvalidSketchColor is also red #FF0000), fighting the black/blue/red solver scheme Kurt approved.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Immediately. Onshape draws constraint badges in grey/black and reserves red geometry for conflicts or over-definition; SolidWorks draws relations in green/teal and uses red/yellow for over-defined. A newcomer's first constrained sketch shows red marks on every coincident corner next to a "Fully constrained" label, and the glyph red (#E03131) is close enough to the conflict red (#FF0000) that they will ask whether something is wrong. They learn the convention within minutes, but it is a first-impression cost on the primary editing surface and it directly contradicts the Onshape semantic the audit plan commits to.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Open any constrained sketch; compare glyph colour with the dimension text.
- **Proposed change:** Set View/ConstrainedIcoColor to the muted ink token (#59636C) in SKETCH_STATE_COLORS, keep InvalidSketchColor red; sketch_colors.py needs no change (it reads the parameter).
- **Acceptance:** Glyphs grey, dimensions black, invalid state still red in a native sketch grab; sketch-colour recipe assertion updated.
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** Hovered/selected constraint glyphs keep native highlight colours.
- **Adversarial check:** Holds at HEAD f57d592. src/kurtshape/theme.py:239-248 copies the Light pack's View/ConstrainedIcoColor (3761320447 = #E03131FF; confirmed in runtime/.../PreferencePacks/FreeCAD Light/FreeCAD Light.cfg line 138) and SKETCH_STATE_COLORS (lines 23-37) overrides all four dimension colours to #000000 but has no ConstrainedIcoColor entry; the override runs after the pack copy (line 266), so the same mechanism would work for the icon colour exactly as it already does for ConstrainedDimColor, which appears in both sets. Working tree is clean for theme.py and sketch_colors.py, and no commit after 4681ddf touches the key, so it is not already solved. The bundled lib/SketcherGui.pyd reads ConstrainedIcoColor and has no separate coincident-point colour key; sketch_colors.py:289-302 reads the parameter at runtime as its 'coincident_color' candidate and already recolours coincident points (macro check 'Unconstrained coincident point is blue rather than native constraint red' in tools/sketch-dimension-point-colors-validation.FCMacro:240), so only the constraint icons keep the pack red. Evidence correction: the finder's Oct 4 sketch-editor-1440.png predates the Oct 5 solver-colour change (it shows red dimensions and green geometry too), so it does not demonstrate the mismatch on its own. The first post-change capture, validation/sketch-presentation/sketch-dimension-point-colors/native-mixed-point-colors.png (Oct 5 16:02), shows black dimension labels, #0000FF free geometry, and Block constraint glyphs whose pixels sample at exactly (224,49,49) = #E03131 (PIL probe under the bundled pythonw). Scenario correction: coincident constraints draw no glyph natively (their point colour is already managed), so the affected cases are horizontal/vertical/parallel/Block and other icon-bearing constraints. No doc records a deliberate choice for glyph colour (docs/M1_PROGRESS.md:64 describes blue/black/red for geometry and conflicts only), so this is an untouched pack default, consistent with 'opportunity'/low. Note for the fix: tools/sketch-edit-state-validation.FCMacro:141-142 iterates SKETCH_STATE_COLORS, so a new key is asserted automatically (only the label text 'blue, black and red' would be stale); and DeactivatedConstrDimColor is #000000, so grey (#59636C) active glyphs would be lighter than deactivated black ones; black for active glyphs would match the 'black = constrained' scheme and avoid that inversion.

## C. Everyday modeling and interaction

### C-01 add_line keeps line-to-line junctions as coordinate dimensions by documented contract; legacy add_line chains still open silently when a junction dimension is edited (add_polyline is the HEAD remedy)

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/core.py` · `Controller._add_line / _anchor_points` · lines src/kurtshape/core.py 2145-2166 (_add_line), 2174-2184 (_anchor_points), 2285+ (_add_polyline); docs/OPERATION_CONTRACT.md 42-44; tests/test_sketch_polylines.py 197-213
- **Evidence:** core.py:2152-2154 '# Line-to-line junctions keep their coordinate dimensions' with anchors = _anchor_points(sk, lambda curve: not isinstance(curve, Part.LineSegment)). Experiment: after add_line (0,0)->(10,0) and (10,0)->(10,5) the constraints were Coincident(origin), DistanceX 'EndX', PointOnObject, DistanceX 'StartX', PointOnObject, DistanceX 'EndX_2', DistanceY 'EndY'; set_parameter EndX=12 gave geo0 (0,0)->(12,0), geo1 (10,0)->(10,5): gap 2 mm, 2 open wires. A later add_line (10,5)->(0,5) onto a polyline's free end also received its own StartX/StartY instead of a Coincident.
- **Scenario:** Kurt or an agent builds a profile with add_line (the only typed line op in the running app; bone-mallet steps use it), finishes, then edits a corner dimension from Feature settings or the bridge to tweak the part.
- **What Kurt would notice:** The profile silently opens; the next recompute fails with 'Native feature rebuild failed' or an open-profile error, and the user has to re-add coincidences by hand or rebuild the sketch. Mixing add_polyline with a trailing add_line has the same hole.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Through the bridge only. In the GUI they draw with the native Sketcher, which creates coincidences itself, so they never hit it.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Headless Controller (isolated env): new → create_sketch S1 XY → add_line 0,0→10,0 → add_line 10,0→10,5 → set_parameter S1 EndX '12 mm' → sk.getPoint(1,1) still (10,0). Script: scratchpad/area-c-everyday/exp_continuity.py (§1, §2b).
- **Proposed change:** In core.py _add_line let _anchor_points accept Part.LineSegment too (as _add_polyline and _add_arc already do), so an endpoint within ENDPOINT_TOLERANCE of a fixed line endpoint gets Sketcher.Constraint('Coincident') and only the free endpoint is dimensioned; keep _require_fixed. Document in docs/SKETCH_PLANE_CONTRACT.md and AGENTS.md that add_line joins. Optionally deprecate add_line in favor of add_polyline in the contract.
- **Acceptance:** New native test in tests/test_sketch_polylines.py: add_line onto an add_line/add_polyline endpoint produces a Coincident, DoF stays 0, and set_parameter on the shared X moves both segments (gap < 1e-6). Regression gate: tests/test_sketch_polylines.py, tests/test_sketch_arcs.py, tests/test_sketch_planes.py unchanged.
- **Dependencies:** Restart of the running app to load any change (it predates 24bab02).
- **Risk:** Existing scripts that intentionally built overlapping separate lines could now join; the DoF/diagnostic guard in _require_fixed rejects redundant cases, so failures become explicit rather than silent.
- **Adversarial check:** The behaviour is real at HEAD: core.py _add_line (2145-2166) passes `lambda curve: not isinstance(curve, Part.LineSegment)` to _anchor_points (2174-2184) with the explicit comment 'Line-to-line junctions keep their coordinate dimensions', so two add_line calls sharing an endpoint get independent EndX/StartX dimensions and no Coincident; editing one does open the profile. But it is not a defect: docs/OPERATION_CONTRACT.md:42 documents exactly this ('line-to-line junctions keep coordinates'), the add_polyline row (:44) states 'lines included, unlike add_line', and tests/test_sketch_polylines.py:199-200 pins it ('Unlike add_line, a polyline vertex on a fixed line end joins it'). Commit 24bab02 (2026-10-17, at HEAD) added add_polyline precisely so 'editing a profile corner keeps it closed instead of opening a gap', and AGENTS.md line 44 tells bridge users to 'prefer it over chains of add_line'. The finder's 'only typed line op in the running app' is true only because Kurt's running build predates 24bab02; the source under review has the remedy. What survives is a design weakness: add_line still lays a silent trap for legacy sketches (every bone-mallet profile built before the restart keeps coordinate-only corners forever), mixing add_polyline with a trailing add_line is documented but easy to miss, and nothing in inspect or set_parameter warns when a dimension on a coordinate-matched junction is changed. The proposed one-line change (accept LineSegment anchors) would also silently change which parameter names exist (joined endpoints lose StartX/EndX), so it is a contract change, not a bug fix.

### C-02 New-result Studio operation rows render in the hidden-feature gray and offer 'Show', which reveals the neutral tool solid

**verified defect · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.refresh / Panel.history_menu` · lines src/kurtshape/ui.py 1683-1686, 1696-1704, 3800-3804, 3960-3961, 4009-4015; src/kurtshape/studio.py 526-529, 767-772, 1342-1359; src/kurtshape/core.py 886, 1357-1362
- **Evidence:** ui.py:1703-1704 'if failed or held or (obj and not obj.ViewObject.Visibility): row["color"] = ... COLORS["disabled_ink"]'. studio.py:1342-1359 records the tool itself (a Part::Extrusion for a Blind pad, extrusion_edit.py:98) as the representative of an operation "new"; studio.py:770-772 hide_internal hides every Part:: object carrying KurtShapeStudioOperation on every reconcile. ui.py:3961 builds the menu entry as 'Hide' if obj.ViewObject.Visibility else 'Show' with no Studio filter.
- **Scenario:** Any Part Studio with parts created by Extrude·New / Revolve·New (the first feature of every part).
- **What Kurt would notice:** The first feature of each part looks hidden/inactive like a held or hidden row; right-click shows 'Show'; choosing it makes a second, default-colored neutral solid appear on top of the part until the next operation re-hides it. Users cannot tell hidden from active history.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Immediately: Onshape never shows a feature as hidden, so the gray base feature looks like an error or suppression; right-click Show then produces an unexplained extra body.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** GUI: new Part Studio, sketch rectangle, Extrude with Operation New, Confirm. Inspect panel.items['Extrude'].foreground(0).color().name() (expect accent/ink, see disabled_ink #646f78) and right-click the row. Headless cannot show it (hide_internal needs App.GuiUp).
- **Proposed change:** In Panel.refresh derive the visual visibility of a Studio operation from its logical outputs (KurtShapeOutputParts → body.KurtShapePartVisible) rather than obj.ViewObject.Visibility when getattr(obj,'KurtShapeStudioOperation',''); in history_menu route Hide/Show for Studio rows to set_part_appearance(visible=...) of the output parts or omit the entry, and never toggle a KurtShapeInternal/neutral object directly.
- **Acceptance:** GUI recipe check: a New-result row's foreground equals the normal ink and its context menu offers Hide (acting on the part) — verified on the part-studio and history-lifecycle recipes; no row for a current, visible part is drawn in disabled_ink.
- **Dependencies:** studio.inspect part visibility (already in state['studio']['parts']).
- **Risk:** Low; presentation only. Add an assertion to tools/history-lifecycle-validation.FCMacro.
- **Adversarial check:** Chain verified in current source. studio.py _result_operation (1342-1359): for operation 'new' the representative passed to _record is `tool`, the top-level Part::Extrusion/Part::Revolution named after the feature (AGENTS.md line 44 says the same), and _record (526-529) stamps it KurtShapeStudioOperation with KurtShapeInternal=False. hide_internal (767-772, blame df26c91 2026-10-03) sets ViewObject.Visibility=False on every Part:: object carrying KurtShapeStudioOperation, and core.py:886 runs it inside every managed rebuild. Add/Remove representatives come from _native_boolean (1394-1407) inside output Bodies, so they are PartDesign:: tips that hide_internal keeps visible with the part; only 'new' rows are affected, as the title says. core _inspect (1357-1362) lists every non-origin object as a feature and Panel.refresh keeps `order` members (ui.py 1683-1686), so the hidden Part::Extrusion is the history row; 1703-1704 (blame 4681ddf 2026-10-13) then colours any row whose object is invisible with disabled_ink, identical to a held row, with a tooltip of only 'Extrusion · Extrude'. history_menu 3960-3961 adds 'Show' from obj.ViewObject.Visibility with no Studio check, and set_visibility (4009-4015) flips the Part::Extrusion directly, so a second default-coloured solid coincident with the part appears until the next rebuild re-hides it. Extra wrinkle the finder missed: accept_task (3800-3804) sets obj.ViewObject.Visibility=True on args['id'], which for Extrude·New is that same representative, so the neutral solid is briefly visible right after Confirm while the row is already gray; the next operation flips it back. No GUI run was possible under the rules, but every step is a deterministic read of current code with no Studio-aware branch in ui.py (grep KurtShapeStudioOperation in ui.py hits only the edit-intent reader at 2621). Confidence raised from medium to high on that basis.

### C-03 Controller error details (failing feature, conflicting constraints, ambiguous matches) are dropped before they reach the user

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value high · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel._operation` · lines src/kurtshape/ui.py 656-666 (notify), 702-716 (append_message), 772-787 (native Report mirror), 849, 900, 1328, 1349 (notify sites), 1696-1704 (row tooltip); src/kurtshape/core.py 1310-1332 (_build_status), 1397-1399 (inspect payload), 1856 (preview failure flattening), 2199-2206 (constraint_conflict details); src/kurtshape/general_actions.py 263
- **Evidence:** ui.py:1349 self.notify(response["error"]["message"], "error") and notify() appends only str(message) to Messages. Experiment 1 §5: a mutation on a document with an over-constrained sketch returned {"code":"build_failed","message":"Native feature rebuild failed","features":[{"feature":"S4","message":"Over-constrained sketch\nRemove at least one of the following conflicting constraints:1, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7"}]}; only 'Native feature rebuild failed' would be shown. core.py:2206 constraint_conflict carries constraints=added; selectors carry 'matches'; build_errors from _build_status (core.py:1327-1328) are never read by ui.py (grep: no build_errors use).
- **Scenario:** An upstream dimension edit breaks a downstream feature; a fillet query becomes ambiguous after a rebuild; a sketch gets a redundant constraint from a native tool.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Kurt sees a red one-liner and a red '!' with a tooltip naming only type·id; he has to open Messages and hope the native report mentions the object, then find it in history himself. An Onshape user expects the failing feature named and selected.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Strongly: Onshape and SolidWorks name the failing feature, select it and show the reason inline; here they have to guess which feature failed and why.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Headless: create two conflicting DistanceX constraints natively on a finished sketch, then dispatch any mutation; compare the error payload with what notify() would display. GUI: open a sketch, add a conflicting dimension with D, Finish — status shows the bare message.
- **Proposed change:** In Panel._operation, on failure build the status text from message plus the first detail (features[0].feature + ': ' + its message, or 'N matches' for ambiguous_reference) and append the full error dict (pretty JSON) to Messages; when the error names a feature that exists in history, select_feature() it and put the native status string in the row tooltip (refresh: add state['build_errors'] messages to the tooltip at ui.py:1699-1702). Keep the one-line status short; the detail lives in Messages and the tooltip.
- **Acceptance:** GUI recipe: after a forced build failure the status names the feature, the row tooltip contains the native status string, and Messages holds the JSON detail. Native test: notify receives message + detail for build_failed/constraint_conflict/ambiguous_reference.
- **Dependencies:** None; error payloads already exist.
- **Risk:** Longer status text; keep it elided with a tooltip.
- **Adversarial check:** Read every error surface in ui.py: the four failure sites (849, 900, 1328, 1349) all call self.notify(response['error']['message'], 'error'), and notify (656-666) sets the status label and append_message with str(message) only; grep for error.get/['error'][ shows no other reader of the error dict, and grep for build_errors in src finds it consumed only by general_actions.py:263 for the saved problem-report JSON. Panel.refresh (1696-1704) builds the tooltip from type·id plus reference_health entries; a native Invalid/Error state only appends '  !' and red colour, while the per-feature getStatusString that core _build_status gathers (core.py 1310-1332, as build_errors[{feature,message}]) is never shown. core.py 1856 flattens preview failures to {code,message} too. OperationError payloads such as constraints=added (core.py 2199-2206 _require_fixed), features=errors (1850) and selector 'matches' therefore never reach the status, the tooltip or Messages. Partial mitigation the finder under-weighted: ui.py 772-787 mirrors the native Report view into Messages, so FreeCAD's own recompute lines can land there, but they are unstructured, not tied to the history row, and absent for typed-only failures (constraint_conflict, ambiguous_reference, open_profile detail). Classification design_weakness is honest: nothing is wrong per se, the information is simply discarded.

### C-04 Enter confirms Extrude from its Depth field but does nothing in the Fillet/Chamfer/Revolve/Shell/Plane quantity fields

**verified defect · severity medium · confidence high · user value high · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.eventFilter / studio_tool` · lines src/kurtshape/ui.py 4257-4272, 2176-2181, 2462-2478, 3811-3817, 4203; src/kurtshape/shortcuts.py 225-227; src/kurtshape/feature_panel.py 29-70, 105-108
- **Evidence:** ui.py:4257-4272 handles Return only for self.depth.lineEdit(), self.value.lineEdit() and self.formula. studio_tool's QuantityField widgets (2176-2181) get no key filter; feature_panel.py and quantity_field.py define no keyPressEvent/returnPressed (grep). shortcuts.py:226 returns False for any text input except Escape and Ctrl+S/N/O, so the registry's general.accept (Enter) never fires while a QAbstractSpinBox has focus.
- **Scenario:** Kurt opens Fillet (Shift+F), picks edges, types 2 in Radius and presses Enter, as he would in Onshape or in KurtShape's own Extrude.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Nothing happens; he must reach for the header check mark. Shift+Enter (accept and repeat) also only works for Extrude (ui.py:3811-3817). The two owned editors feel like different products.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Immediately. Onshape and SolidWorks commit a feature on Enter from any numeric field; the dialog appearing to ignore Enter reads as a hang or a validation failure.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** GUI: Shift+F on a solid, pick an edge, type a radius, press Enter: dialog stays open. Compare Shift+E, type depth, Enter.
- **Proposed change:** In studio_tool install one event filter on every QuantityField/QDoubleSpinBox lineEdit (and the formula field) that maps Return→dialog.accept() when dialog.confirm_button.isEnabled() and Shift+Return→accept then reopen the same tool; or give FeaturePanel a keyPressEvent that any child without its own Return handling inherits. Extend accept_repeat to studio tools.
- **Acceptance:** GUI recipe (dress-up-edit or everyday-ui): typing a radius then Enter commits one Fillet in one Undo step; Enter with an invalid preview does nothing; Extrude behavior unchanged.
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** A QuantitySpinBox normalizes on focus loss; evaluate proposal() before accept (schedule+flush already does this at 2542-2544).
- **Adversarial check:** Read ui.py eventFilter 4257-4272: Return is handled only when watched is self.depth.lineEdit(), self.value.lineEdit() or self.formula; the filter is installed app-wide (ui.py:230) so it does see Studio spinbox keys but ignores them. studio_tool's quantity() (2176-2181) builds a QuantityField (Gui::QuantitySpinBox wrapper, quantity_field.py, no key handling) and the wiring loop (2462-2478) connects only textChanged/valueChanged. FeaturePanel (feature_panel.py:29-70) is a QDockWidget with QToolButton confirm/cancel, so there is no QDialog default-button Enter path, and QAbstractSpinBox ignores Return so it just bubbles to the dock and main window. The shortcut registry maps general.accept to accept_task(), which would call studio_dialog.accept() (ui.py:4203, 3767-3770), but shortcuts.py:225-227 returns False for any key other than Escape/Ctrl+S/N/O while the focus widget or any ancestor is a QAbstractSpinBox/QLineEdit, so Enter never reaches it from a field. accept_repeat (3811-3817) keys off self.task, which is None for Studio dialogs, so Shift+Enter cannot accept-and-repeat them even from a non-text widget. Tried to refute via a passing recipe: tools/dress-up-edit-validation.FCMacro types into radius.lineEdit() with keyClicks and then confirms with mouseClick(dialog.confirm) at lines 225/271/350/375/393, never Return, and test_shortcuts.py pins that text inputs swallow registry keys. docs/ONSHAPE_SHORTCUTS.md:35 advertises Enter = Accept tool and docs/EXTRUSION_EDIT.md:15 says 'Confirm / Enter' for Extrude, so the inconsistency is against the app's own stated behaviour. One nuance: Enter does accept a Studio dialog when focus sits on a non-text widget (the pick button or list), because the floating dock is not a QDialog; the title's 'in the quantity fields' scoping is therefore exact. No git commit after the finder's read touches this path.

### C-05 Sketch angles are shown and edited in radians (Feature settings and bridge); a bare '30' becomes 30 rad

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: reproduced · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/core.py` · `native_parameters / Panel.parameter_value` · lines 117-123 (core.py); 2687-2697 (ui.py)
- **Evidence:** core.py:117 '"unit": "rad" if c.Type == "Angle" else "mm"'; ui.py:2691 self.value.setSuffix(" " + info["unit"]). Experiment 2 §A: inspect reported Tilt (0.785398, 'rad'); set_parameter '30 deg' → 30°, '0.5 rad' → 28.65°, '30' → stored 30 rad = 1719°, '1' → 57.3°.
- **Scenario:** Editing a chamfer/draft/revolve angle or a sketch Angle dimension from Feature settings or through an agent.
- **What Kurt would notice:** The value field reads '0.7854 rad'; typing 30 without a unit yields a 1719° angle and a silently rotated sketch. Onshape and SolidWorks users think in degrees.
- **A newcomer would notice:** An Onshape/SolidWorks user sees a field reading "45.00°", types 30, hits Enter, and watches the sketch rotate wildly while the field now says "1718.87°". Nothing on screen explains it; the workaround (type "30°"/"30 deg", or commit with Apply/Tab rather than Enter) is undiscoverable. It reads as "the dimension editor is broken", which is a trust hit early in the first session. Recovery is one Undo, so no work is lost.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Headless script scratchpad/area-c-everyday/exp2_units_expr.py §A; GUI: select a sketch with an angle dimension, choose it in Feature settings.
- **Proposed change:** Report angles with unit 'deg' (value math.degrees(c.Value)) in native_parameters and accept both deg and rad strings in number(); keep set_parameter's internal conversion to radians (core.py:2705 Quantity with unit). For contract 3.0 compatibility add an additive 'display_unit':'deg' and 'value_deg' field first, then switch the GUI field to degrees; update docs/OPERATION_CONTRACT.md ('Driving angles use radians').
- **Acceptance:** Native test: inspect returns deg for Angle constraints and revolve/pattern angles; set_parameter '30' on an angle stores 30°; GUI value field shows '45 deg'. Regression gate: tests/test_core.py, test_pattern_tools.py, test_datum_planes.py (line-angle planes).
- **Dependencies:** Bridge clients (dodec/mallet scripts) that pass radians today; a one-release dual-accept keeps them working.
- **Risk:** Contract change; mitigate by accepting explicit units and documenting.

### C-06 Finished-sketch dimensions appear as 'Constraints[4]' in the Feature settings combobox with no viewport cue

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort M** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: source · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.parameters / parameter_value` · lines 2590-2632, 2687-2697
- **Evidence:** core.py:118 name = c.Name or f"Constraints[{i}]"; experiment 2 §B: a two-line sketch dimensioned the way native Sketcher does it lists {"Constraints[4]": 8 mm, "Constraints[5]": 3 mm}. ui.py:2606-2613 fills the combobox with these keys; nothing highlights the chosen constraint in the viewport, and native dimensions are only drawn in sketch edit mode. ui.py:1858-1864 pops the editor on every history single-click, including features with no parameters (2630-2632).
- **Scenario:** Kurt selects a hand-drawn sketch in history to change one length without entering the sketch; or an agent reads inspect to find 'the slot length'.
- **What Kurt would notice:** A list of anonymous indexes; the only safe path is Edit sketch and double-click the dimension natively, which makes the Apply dimension panel dead weight for GUI-drawn sketches. The editor also pops up empty for Boolean/Mirror/Transform rows.
