- **Reproduction / verification needed:** GUI: draw a rectangle with G, dimension with D, Finish, click the sketch row: Dimension combobox shows Constraints[n] entries.
- **Proposed change:** Describe each entry as '<type> <value> · <geometry>' (DistanceX on Line 1: 'Horizontal 8 mm · line 1→2'), and on combobox change briefly enter a read-only highlight: Gui.Selection.addSelection(sketch, 'Constraint'+str(i+1)) is only valid in edit mode, so instead draw a transient Coin overlay of the two constrained points (reuse sketch_references.show_hover line-set code) or auto-name dimensions at Finish ('Length1', 'Width2') through a KurtShape naming pass. Hide the editor for features with no parameters and no edit action.
- **Acceptance:** GUI recipe: selecting 'Horizontal 8 mm · line 1' highlights the two endpoints; Apply changes the right constraint; selecting a Boolean row opens no empty editor.
- **Dependencies:** Coin overlay helper from sketch_references.show_hover.
- **Risk:** Auto-naming changes expression paths; prefer description + overlay first.

### C-07 Formula whitelist rejects valid FreeCAD syntax (function argument separators, <<Label>> references, ^, %, ?:) and reports only a generic sentence

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/extrusion_edit.py` · `EXPRESSION_PATTERN / Controller set_expression` · lines extrusion_edit.py:14, 240-241; reference_tools.py:204, 375-376; core.py:2693-2694; GUI entry points ui.py:2332 (fillet/chamfer Formula), ui.py:2702 (Feature settings Formula)
- **Evidence:** EXPRESSION_PATTERN = r"[A-Za-z0-9_ .+*/()\[\]-]+". Experiment 2 §C: 'max(R.Constraints.Width, 5 mm)', '<<R>>.Constraints.Width / 2', 'R.Constraints.Width ^ 2 / 10 mm' and a trailing ';' were all rejected with 'Use a dimensional formula with named properties and arithmetic'; 'sqrt(R.Constraints.Width * 1 mm)' and '… * 0.5 + 1 mm' were accepted.
- **Scenario:** Kurt types a FreeCAD expression he knows (label references, max/min with two arguments, powers) into Formula in Feature settings or the Extrude dialog.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Rejected without saying which character is disallowed; label-based references are impossible even though FreeCAD's expression engine supports them, so renamed features must be referenced by internal Name.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Onshape/SolidWorks equations use max(a, b); typing that into Formula is rejected with no hint that the comma is the issue, and there is no way to tell a syntax slip from a disallowed feature.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** scratchpad/area-c-everyday/exp2_units_expr.py §C.
- **Proposed change:** Allow ',', '<', '>', '^', '%' and ';' in the whitelist (or validate by App.Units/Expression parse: doc.getObject(x).evalExpression / App.Expression.parse in a try) and report the offending character or the native parse error text instead of the generic sentence.
- **Acceptance:** Native test: the five expressions above are accepted or rejected with the native parser's reason; invalid references still fail with invalid_expression.
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** Widening the whitelist is still not code execution (FreeCAD expression language); keep length limit.
- **Adversarial check:** Read extrusion_edit.py:14 (EXPRESSION_PATTERN = r"[A-Za-z0-9_ .+*/()\[\]-]+"), core.py 2690-2700 (set_expression: re.fullmatch against that pattern, generic 'Use a dimensional formula with named properties and arithmetic'), extrusion_edit.py 240-241 and reference_tools.py 204, 375-376 (the same whitelist duplicated for edit_extrusion and edit_dress_up), and the GUI entry points ui.py:2702 (Feature settings Formula -> set_expression) and ui.py:2332 (fillet/chamfer Formula -> edit_dress_up), so every GUI formula field hits the whitelist. Re-read the finder's experiment output (scratchpad/area-c-everyday/exp2_units_expr.out.txt): comma and semicolon max(), <<R>>, ^ and trailing ';' rejected, sqrt() accepted, exactly as claimed. Ran a 20 s headless probe with the bundled FreeCAD 1.1.4 (isolated env): the native engine accepts 'max(Ref.Length, 5 mm)', 'max(Ref.Length; 5 mm)', '<<My Ref>>.Length / 2' (=10), 'Ref.Length ^ 2 / 10 mm' (=40), 'Ref.Length % 7 mm' (=6) and 'a > 5 mm ? 3 mm : 4 mm' (=3), and rejects a trailing ';' with ParserError 'Failed to parse expression'. So the whitelist blocks valid FreeCAD syntax (two-argument functions are impossible, label references impossible, no power/modulo/conditional), and the one example the finder listed that FreeCAD also rejects (trailing ';') shows the second half of the claim: the native parse error text is never surfaced, only the generic sentence. Git log: the pattern was introduced 2026-10-13 (7d1c56a) and copied in 659b5e7; unchanged at HEAD. Classification design_weakness and severity low are honest: nothing breaks, the author has to rewrite the formula.

### C-08 Escape with no active sketch tool most likely falls through to native Sketcher and silently commits the sketch

**hypothesis · severity medium · confidence medium · user value medium · effort S** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: source · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.cancel_task / sketch_lease_needs_finish / tick` · lines 3819-3845, 3376-3383, 1246-1247, 1267-1268
- **Evidence:** sketch_references.py:657-673 handles Escape only when a native tool or reference picking is active. Otherwise shortcuts.py:237-240 invokes general.cancel → cancel_task(); in a plain sketch every branch is false and it returns False (3845), so the KeyPress is not accepted and reaches the viewport. FreeCAD's ViewProviderSketch leaves edit mode on Escape when 'LeaveSketchWithEscape' is true (its default; nothing in src sets it, grep). After native resetEdit, tick() sees sketch_lease_needs_finish() and calls finish_sketch() (commit, not cancel).
- **Scenario:** An Onshape user presses Escape to 'deselect' or to abandon a half-drawn sketch.
- **What Kurt would notice:** The sketch closes and is committed as Finish (one Undo step), not cancelled; the README documents Escape only as 'cancels the current drawing tool or placement'. Surprising but recoverable with Ctrl+Z.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** GUI: Shift+S, Top, draw a rectangle, press Escape twice (first ends the tool). Expect: sketch still open. If it closes, check the Undo stack has 'KurtShape: new sketch contents'. Also read the Sketcher preference LeaveSketchWithEscape in the app's profile.
- **Proposed change:** Decide the semantics explicitly: either make cancel_task() return True inside a sketch (consume Escape, keep the sketch open, status 'Escape ends the tool; Finish or Cancel closes the sketch') or set Mod/Sketcher LeaveSketchWithEscape=False in the owned profile like sketch_snapping does for Snap. Document in README/ONSHAPE_SHORTCUTS.
- **Acceptance:** GUI recipe (sketch-behavior): Escape with no tool keeps panel.active_sketch() and the lease; the responsibility is documented. Regression gate: sketch-behavior recipe 'Escape ends Line without leaving sketch edit'.
- **Dependencies:** Confirm native default in FreeCAD 1.1.4 first.
- **Risk:** None if consumed; the native exit path remains via Finish/Cancel.

### C-10 Disabled tools give no reason on hover, and activating an 'Unavailable right now' search result closes the dialog without notifying or triggering

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/tool_search.py` · `availability / Panel.show_tool_search.activate / Panel.sync_edit_ui` · lines tool_search.py:125-131 (availability), 133-144 (_context_reason); ui.py:2764 (trigger = QAction.trigger), 2814-2827 (activate), 3395-3402 (sync_edit_ui disables for native task / unmanaged); toolbar_labels.py:122-127; ui.py:1753-1754 (reference-file status text already names 'adopt a copy')
- **Evidence:** tool_search.py:332-333 returns 'Unavailable right now' when entry.enabled is False; ui.py:2820-2822 notifies only for 'Switch to'/'Not available' and otherwise calls entry['trigger']() on a disabled QAction (a no-op under Qt 6). sync_edit_ui:3397-3402 disables file/model actions for an active native Tasks dialog and for unmanaged files with no hover reason; toolbar_labels shows only the name.
- **Scenario:** A native Hole/Draft Tasks dialog is open, or a reference FCStd is inspected: the whole model bar is gray.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Hovering gives a name but no reason; search closes and nothing happens. The newcomer does not know that the right-hand Tasks dialog must be closed or that 'Adopt copy' is the way in.
- **A newcomer would notice:** The whole toolbar going grey with no explanation reads as a broken app; the only cue is the right-hand Tasks panel. Search tells them 'Unavailable right now' in grey but pressing Enter just closes it, with no toast saying to finish or cancel the open feature first.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** GUI: More → Cut → Hole (native dialog opens), hover Extrude; Alt+C, type fillet, Enter.
- **Proposed change:** Add Panel.unavailability_reason() ('Finish or cancel the open Tasks dialog first', 'Adopt a copy before editing this reference file', 'Finish the sketch first') and (a) pass it to toolbar_labels.register as a second line when the button is disabled, (b) return it from tool_search.availability via a reason provider, (c) notify it on activation instead of triggering.
- **Acceptance:** test_tool_search: a disabled entry carries the provided reason; GUI recipe: hover label shows two lines while a native task is active; activation of an unavailable entry notifies.
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** None.
- **Adversarial check:** tool_search.py is 144 lines, so the cited 330-336 are wrong; availability() is at 125-131 and returns 'Unavailable right now' whenever entry['enabled'] is False, before any context reason. ui.py:2764 builds entries with 'enabled': action.isEnabled() and 'trigger': action.trigger (the raw QAction slot); activate() at 2814-2827 closes the dialog, notifies only for 'Switch to'/'Not available' reasons, and otherwise calls the trigger. sync_edit_ui (ui.py 3395-3402) disables model_actions when native_feature_task_active() (Gui.Control.activeDialog() with no active sketch, ui.py 1172) or when state['managed'] is False, and file/history actions likewise, without setting any reason text. toolbar_labels._show_pending (122-127) renders only label, shortcut and the optional kurtshapeHelp property (set only on Check geometry, ui.py 575), so a disabled button's hover gives the name alone. Settled the Qt claim with a headless probe: the bundle ships Qt6Core.dll and the probe reports Qt 6.8.3, where QAction.trigger() on a disabled action does not emit triggered (fired=False), so Enter on an 'Unavailable right now' row really does nothing after the dialog closes. tests/test_tool_search.py covers only the context reasons (sketch/assembly), none for enabled=False. Minor overstatement: for an unmanaged reference file the status label already reads 'Reference file · inspect or adopt a copy · ...' (ui.py 1753-1754), so the 'Adopt copy' way in is visible there; the native-Tasks case has no explanation anywhere except the Tasks panel itself being open. Classification design_weakness, severity low are honest. Not already solved at HEAD.

### C-11 Sweep path and Split face are captured once at dialog open; picking afterwards has no effect

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.studio_tool` · lines 2239-2247, 2260-2261, 2375-2388, 2435-2438, 2489-2519
- **Evidence:** ui.py:2239 path_references = self.geometry_references("Edge") evaluated once; 2244 hint text 'Select one path edge in the viewport before opening Sweep.'; 2260-2261 refs = self.geometry_references("Face") once for split_part; capture() (2489-2519) only updates ReferencePicker fields and the Part combo, so these two are never refreshed. Revolve axis, Mirror plane, Fillet edges and Plane support use live reference_field pickers.
- **Scenario:** Open Sweep from the toolbar, then click the path edge; or Split by 'Selected face' and then click the face.
- **What Kurt would notice:** The dialog keeps 'Select one path edge … before opening' and the preview never validates; the user must Cancel, select, and reopen. Inconsistent with the other dialogs' blue receiving fields.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Immediately in Split: choose 'Selected face', read 'Split face requires a native reference', click the face, nothing changes, Confirm stays disabled, no hint says why. In Sweep the hint explains, but a 'select before opening' rule does not exist in Onshape or SolidWorks, where the path field receives picks live.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** GUI: solid with a sketch profile; Sweep → Path source 'Selected edge' → now click an edge: hint unchanged, Confirm disabled.
- **Proposed change:** Replace the one-shot captures with reference_field('path', 'Path edge', 'Edge') and reference_field('face', 'Split face', 'Face') so capture() fills them, and make 'Selected edge/face' the active picker when that choice is current.
- **Acceptance:** GUI recipe: Sweep opened with nothing selected validates after clicking an edge; Split by face likewise. Native tests for sweep/split unchanged.
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** None; same selector construction via reference_from_geometry.
- **Adversarial check:** Read ui.py studio_tool 2120-2560, geometry_references 2066-2075, SelectionObserver 110-125, capture_feature_selection 2103-2118, studio.py sweep 1286-1313 and _plane 1459-1484, core.py 2551-2556, feature_panel.ReferencePicker 207-286, git -S on path_hint/values["face"], tools/*.FCMacro. Held: ui.py:2239 path_references = self.geometry_references("Edge") runs once; 2243 values["selected_path"] closes over it; 2244 hint text; 2260-2261 refs for split captured once. capture() (2489-2518) only updates pickers registered by reference_field (none exist for sweep or split) and fields["part"] (split has one, sweep does not), so every later pick reaches capture() through SelectionObserver.addSelection -> capture_feature_selection -> studio_selection_callback and is dropped. proposal() 2375-2378 then sends path=None -> studio.py:1295-1296 missing_reference 'Sweep path must name a native curve feature'; 2379-2388 sends face=None -> _plane 1474-1476 invalid_argument 'Split face requires a native reference'. Split has no hint row at all; only that error, which reads like a prompt to pick. Default path_by is 'feature' unless exactly one edge was preselected (2240), so the sweep dead end needs the user to switch to 'Selected edge'. git -S shows both captures introduced in df26c91 and untouched since; no test or validation macro drives the sweep/split dialogs (macros cover fillet, create_plane, mirror, move_face, revolve). Preselection does work: core.py:2552-2555 resolves a selector face before studio.handle, and sweep accepts dict paths via selectors.native_reference. The 'inconsistent with other dialogs' claim is accurate: revolve axis_reference (2234), mirror plane (2281), create_plane support (2296) and fillet/shell edges/faces (2301) all use live ReferencePicker fields. Nothing material failed to hold; not fixed at HEAD.

### C-12 Pattern direction/axis take typed vectors only (in the bridge too), the Plane dialog is offset-only, and include_source has no checkbox; the bridge's extra plane modes and include_source are unreachable from the GUI

**opportunity · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort M** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: preference · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.studio_tool (linear/circular pattern, create_plane)` · lines ui.py 2274-2297; studio.py 29-33, 1139-1144, 1652-1659; modeling_geometry.py 21-26, 267-273, 302-314
- **Evidence:** ui.py:2276-2284 Linear pattern = vector('direction') + spacing; Circular = vector('origin') + vector('axis_direction'); only Mirror has reference_field('plane'). 2292-2297 create_plane offers base_plane/support/offset only, while modeling_geometry.py:267-273 and 444-450 accept point_normal, three_points and line_angle (used for every tilted dodec-hub feature).
- **Scenario:** Pattern pin holes around an arm axis, or a plane through three vertices / at an angle to an edge, as in the dodec-hub README; in Onshape these are edge/face picks.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Kurt types world-coordinate vectors by hand (and must know the Body frame), or scripts it through the bridge. Patterns of a cutter also need include_source, which has no GUI checkbox (studio.ARGS line 29-32).
- **A newcomer would notice:** Onshape's circular pattern asks for an axis pick; typing an origin and [0,0,1] in world millimetres feels alien, and 'Plane through three points' or 'at an angle to an edge' is simply absent from the Plane dialog.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** GUI: Circular pattern dialog has no 'Axis' picker; Plane dialog has no 'Through three points'/'Angle to edge'.
- **Proposed change:** Add reference_field('axis_reference','Axis edge or cylindrical face','Edge') to circular pattern and reference_field('direction_reference','Direction edge','Edge') to linear pattern, mapping to selectors the way revolve does (bind_revolve_axis in modeling_geometry.py:106); add a 'Define by' choice to the Plane dialog with point-normal (picked vertex + face normal), three points and line-angle pickers; add an 'Include source part' checkbox mapping to include_source.
- **Acceptance:** GUI recipe: circular pattern from a picked cylindrical face edge produces the same solid as the typed axis; plane by three picked vertices matches create_plane three_points in a native test.
- **Dependencies:** Selector support for edge-as-axis already exists for revolve; add a direction-from-edge helper for patterns.
- **Risk:** Moderate: new selector consumers must rebind on rebuild (test_selector_consumers).
- **Adversarial check:** Read ui.py 2274-2297 and 2358-2374, studio.py ARGS 20-36, _axis 1139-1144, pattern handler 1625-1710, modeling_geometry.py PLANE_MODES 21-26, plane_mode 267-273, _line 302-314, _plane_frame 317-339, bind_revolve_axis 106-130, docs/OPERATION_CONTRACT.md 161-213, git log (24bab02 added include_source at HEAD~2). Held: linear pattern = vector('direction') + spacing (2277-2278); circular = vector('origin') + vector('axis_direction') (2282-2284); count 2286-2289; angle 2291; no include_source checkbox anywhere in 2274-2291 although studio.ARGS 29-33 accept it and the contract documents its semantics (lines 184-187). The Plane dialog (2292-2297) offers base_plane/support/offset only, while PLANE_MODES and plane_mode() accept point_normal, three_points and line_angle (contract 171, 193-213), used for the dodec-hub tilted features. Did not hold: (1) the title's 'bridge's richer modes are unreachable' is false for patterns: the bridge has no richer mode. studio.py:1652 takes direction only via _vector, and _axis() 1139-1144 raises 'Axis requires origin and direction' for anything but an origin/direction dict; there is no selector path like bind_revolve_axis for patterns. An edge-picked pattern axis is new backend work (schema, selector binding, rebuild re-evaluation), not a GUI mapping. (2) 'Plane definition ... number-only' is wrong: the Plane dialog is pick-based (reference_field support, base plane choice) but offset-only. (3) 'must know the Body frame' is wrong: create_plane takes world mm converted once to the Body frame (contract 193) and pattern placements are world; nothing asks the user for Body coordinates. Classification 'opportunity' is honest. Effort M holds only for the GUI-side work (plane modes with vertex/edge pickers, include_source checkbox, pattern axis converted to vectors at dialog time); a tracked selector axis for patterns is L.

### C-14 Active part is an unlabeled combobox; the history's Origin planes and the sketch placement help never say which part they belong to

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.make_history (body_choice) / part_selection / extrude` · lines 278-281, 1146-1147, 1335-1338, 1722-1727, 1872-1874, 1909-1918, 3205-3232, 3449-3455
- **Evidence:** validation/onshape-theme-repair/model-workspace-1440.png (Oct 4, current shell): a bare combobox 'Body' under 'Parts (1)' with no caption; ui.py:279 the only explanation is a tooltip 'Part for new sketches and features'. part_selection (1909-1918) makes a clicked part active, but viewport selection does not; extrude() derives Add/New and the merge list from the sketch's owner, not the combobox (3449-3455), while Shift+S on Top/Front/Right uses the combobox's Body origin (1146-1147, 3217-3229).
- **Scenario:** Two or more parts; Kurt clicks Part 2's face in the viewport, presses Shift+S, picks 'Top' from history.
- **What Kurt would notice:** The sketch lands in whichever part the combobox names (often Part 1), with no visual sign in the viewport or on the sketch header; later the extrude defaults to New/Add based on that owner. Experts learn the rule; newcomers get features in the wrong part.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Onshape and SolidWorks have no 'active part'; an unlabeled dropdown under Parts reads as a filter. With two parts they would sketch on 'Top' expecting Part 2, then find Extrude defaulting to Add on Part 1 and have to change the scope by hand, without understanding why.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** GUI: create two parts; select a face of Part 2 (do not click the Parts list); Shift+S then click Top; read the new sketch's Body in inspect.
- **Proposed change:** Caption the chooser ('New sketches and features go to') and echo the active part in the sketch placement help ('Top plane of Part 2 · change in Parts'); offer 'Make active' in the Parts context menu; when a viewport selection belongs to a different current part, offer the part in the placement editor as a second choice; show the active part's origin planes with the accent color during placement (PlaneHighlight already exists).
- **Acceptance:** GUI recipe part-studio: the placement editor names the target part; selecting a face of another part during placement creates the sketch in that part (already true) and the status says so.
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** None.
- **Adversarial check:** Read ui.py make_history 271-298, SelectionObserver 110-125, active_body/plane/change_body 1123-1157, _operation 1335-1338, refresh 1716-1747, history_click 1866-1884, part_selection 1909-1918, capture_feature_selection 2103-2118, start_sketch 3195-3236, choose_support/begin_sketch 3251-3268, extrude 3434-3474, core.py _body 906-928 and _new_sketch 2062-2078, validation/onshape-theme-repair/model-workspace-1440.png, docs/onshape-design-audit/PLAN.md 85-89, docs/HISTORY_LIFECYCLE.md 40-49. Held: 278-281 is a bare QComboBox whose only explanation is the tooltip 'Part for new sketches and features'; the Oct 4 screenshot shows 'Parts (1)' over an unlabeled 'Body' dropdown and a bold 'Body' row. 1722-1727 add only bold and a tooltip. 1909-1918 makes a Parts-list click activate the part; SelectionObserver.addSelection -> capture_feature_selection never touches body_choice, so viewport selection does not change the active part. 1146-1147 resolves the history's Top/Front/Right rows against active_body(); history_click 1872-1874 calls begin_sketch(plane=...) without a body, and _operation 1335-1338 injects owner(target) or self.active_body(), so an origin-plane sketch lands in the combobox's Body. 3449-3455 derive the Extrude default (New/Add) and merge list from the sketch's owner. Did not hold: the reproduction. start_sketch 3205-3210 inspects the current selection first and starts the sketch immediately on a selected Face with body=owner, so 'click Part 2's face, Shift+S, pick Top' never shows a Top choice; in placement mode a face click goes through choose_support 3251-3259 with body=owner too. The wrong-part outcome only arises when the user takes Top/Front/Right from the history (or create_plane 2293-2294) while the combobox names another part. Because Bodies normally sit at identity, Part 1's Top and Part 2's Top coincide geometrically, so the consequence is a defaults mismatch (Extrude opens on Add into Part 1 with Part 1 in the merge list) that the Extrude dialog's operation and scope controls can correct, not a feature in the wrong part. The sharper weakness is that the single 'Origin' group in the history and the placement help (3232 'Top, Front or Right in the history') never say whose planes they are. PLAN.md P2 (85-89) already records 'History, rollback and active part need clearer relationships', and HISTORY_LIFECYCLE.md:43 documents a prior bug from the same coupling.

### C-15 Feature suppression is absent; Hide/Show and rollback are the only ways to take a feature out of the build

**opportunity · severity medium · confidence high · user value high · effort L** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: preference · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.history_menu` · lines ui.py 3950-4003, 1789-1790; studio.py 257-302, 454-468, 1083-1096; core.py 1272
- **Evidence:** history_menu offers Hide/Show, Edit…, Rename, Delete, Feature settings and Roll back/forward only; grep for 'suppress' in ui.py/studio.py/core.py finds only the assembly joint 'suppressed' suffix (ui.py:1789-1790) with no toggle. Rollback is contiguous (studio rollback_index).
- **Scenario:** Kurt wants to compare the hub with and without the rim fillet, or temporarily drop a failing hole while fixing its sketch, without deleting it.
- **What Kurt would notice:** He must delete and redo (losing picks/queries) or roll back everything after it. Onshape/SolidWorks users reach for suppress constantly.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Right-click on a feature with no Suppress is the first thing an Onshape or SolidWorks user reaches for and misses; they would assume the app cannot do what-if comparisons.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Right-click any feature row.
- **Proposed change:** Add a studio operation 'set_feature_suppressed {feature, suppressed}' that marks the representative (KurtShapeSuppressed) and makes studio.order()/_state() skip it during rebuild as if it were beyond the bar (reuse the rollback machinery per feature), native PartDesign features via the native Suppressed property; show suppressed rows struck-through/gray with a 'suppressed' tooltip; expose Suppress/Unsuppress in history_menu and search.
- **Acceptance:** Native tests: suppress/unsuppress round-trips volumes and is one Undo step; downstream features rebuild; save/reopen keeps the flag; GUI recipe shows the row state.
- **Dependencies:** Studio rebuild semantics (consumers of a suppressed op must rebind to its inputs); auto merge scope reevaluation.
- **Risk:** High in studio part lineage; start with native-Body features (Pad/Pocket/Fillet/Hole) where FreeCAD already supports Suppressed.
- **Adversarial check:** Read ui.py history_menu 3950-4003 and refresh_assembly 1787-1790, grep -i suppress over src/, docs/, tests/, tools/, studio.py order/_cutoff/_state/held_part_ids 186-338, _snapshot 454-468, _inputs 1083-1096, core.py 1265-1272, docs/2026-10-03-claude-review/CODEX_PROMPT.md 1619-1693 and 1954-1975, AGENTS.md known gaps. Held: the context menu offers Hide/Show, Edit sketch/Extrude/dress-up/feature, Rename, Delete, Feature settings and the three Roll actions only. No feature-suppress operation exists: the only 'suppress' hits are assembly joints (assembly.py 535-551, 887; ui.py 1789-1790 shows a ' · suppressed' suffix with no toggle), core.py:1272 _expects_shape merely tolerating the native Suppressed flag, document_state tracking SuppressedShape, onshape.py 156-157 rejecting suppressed features on import, and hover/navigation internals. The October 3 plan scheduled a suppress_feature op (Increment 8, Suppressed property, build_failed rejection, undo) and the suppress UI with Shift+U (Increment 12b); neither was built, and the AGENTS.md gap list does not mention it. Rollback is a contiguous cutoff (_cutoff 257-270, _state 273-302), as claimed. Did not hold: the effort framing 'reuse the rollback machinery per feature'. Rollback leaves downstream native links intact and merely reads state up to a cutoff; studio features consume snapshot Bodies bound to a specific output tip (_inputs -> _snapshot with a ShapeBinder on that feature), so skipping feature N requires relinking every downstream consumer of N's outputs, which is the reorder/insert problem, not rollback. Native PartDesign features inside a Body (Pad, Pocket, Hole, native fillet/chamfer) already carry Suppressed and the build-status code tolerates it, so a native-only Suppress is a tractable M; covering studio features (Part::Revolution/Sweep/Loft, booleans, patterns, split) pushes it to L-XL. Kurt's compare use case is partly served today by preview (disposable clone) and rollback, but neither skips a single feature.

### C-16 Shift+H and Shift+Y reveal hidden hole-helper sketches and internal tool Bodies until the next operation (Isolate does not)

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.toggle_sketches / show_hidden / isolate` · lines 3892-3898 (toggle_sketches), 3918-3922 (show_hidden part-studio branch); isolate 4183-4187 iterates raw objects but does not expose helpers
- **Evidence:** toggle_sketches sets Visibility on every Sketcher::SketchObject; show_hidden on every sketch and Body/Tip; isolate on every Sketcher::/PartDesign:: object; none filters KurtShapeInternal, KurtShapeHoleHelper or KurtShapeUseOwner binders (hole_tools.py:217-223 marks hole bridge profiles internal).
- **Scenario:** Press Shift+H to see all sketches on a part that has a bridge-created Hole or cross-body Use references.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Hidden helper sketches/binders appear as extra geometry the user did not draw, can be selected, and stay visible until the next studio reconcile hides them again.
- **A newcomer would notice:** An Onshape/SolidWorks user expects Show hidden to reveal only things they hid; seeing geometry they never drew, selectable in the viewport but absent from the feature list, reads as a corrupt model and erodes trust in the history list.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Headless: build a hole via the typed op on a sketch in another Body; GUI: Shift+Y.
- **Proposed change:** Filter with a shared predicate (not getattr(obj,'KurtShapeInternal',False) and not KurtShapeHoleHelper and not studio neutral results) in all three callbacks; for isolate, operate on current parts instead of raw objects.
- **Acceptance:** Native/GUI check: after Shift+Y no object with KurtShapeInternal is visible.
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** None.
- **Adversarial check:** Read src/kurtshape/ui.py toggle_sketches (3892-3898), show_hidden (3910-3922), isolate (4172-4188), refresh (1630+), plus hole_tools.py _internal/_bridge_profile (217-243), studio.py hide_internal/_show_bodies/reconcile (711-781), bodies.py bodies() (6-10), sketch_tools.py unconsumed_sketches (7-14), studio.py _eligible/order (176-186), docs/ONSHAPE_SHORTCUTS.md (Shift+H row 64, Shift+I row 82, Shift+Y row 104). HELD: toggle_sketches sets Visibility on every Sketcher::SketchObject with no KurtShapeInternal/KurtShapeHoleHelper filter; the Hole op (commit 1f0001e, at HEAD) creates its bridge profile as a Sketcher::SketchObject inside the user's Body marked internal and hidden (hole_tools.py 242-243, 217-222), so Shift+H reveals it. show_hidden's part-studio branch shows every sketch plus every Body and Body.Tip from bodies(), and bodies() does not filter KurtShapeInternal, so Shift+Y also reveals internal tool/boolean Bodies and their Tips (studio.py 1037-1043, modeling_geometry.py 527) as phantom solids overlapping the part; this is worse than the finder stated. refresh() never calls hide_internal; hide_internal only runs from reconcile/present (studio.py 723, 764), so the exposure persists until the next operation, as claimed. studio.order/_eligible excludes KurtShapeInternal objects, so the revealed helpers have no history row ('geometry the user did not draw'). sketch_owners() (ui.py 1133) and unconsumed_sketches already filter KurtShapeInternal, so the fix has in-repo precedent, and the assembly branch of show_hidden already restricts itself to instances (tools/assembly-gui-validation.FCMacro:262 verifies Shift+Y exposes nothing embedded in assemblies; no equivalent part-studio check exists; no test references toggle_sketches/show_hidden/isolate). DID NOT HOLD: the isolate claim is overstated. isolate only sets Visibility = (name in selection) on unselected objects and later restores the recorded values, so hidden helpers stay hidden and are restored hidden; it never reveals them. The 'cross-body Use references' part of the scenario is also overstated: Use binders are PartDesign::ShapeBinders (sketch_tools.py 284-285), mirror drivers are PartDesign::Plane and direction lines Part::Line, none of which toggle_sketches or show_hidden touch. Not fixed at HEAD (git log -S shows these functions unchanged since baseline b65f066). Severity stays low: visual-only, transient, no data change; the extra Shift+Y phantom-solid case is more confusing than the sketch case but still self-corrects on the next operation.

### C-17 Export always proposes 'my-part.step' (and lists the export under Recent projects); closing with an open sketch auto-commits rather than asking

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.save / export / eventFilter (Close)` · lines 3185 (export default name), 2980-2981 (unsaved-doc save default), 4273-4276 (Close auto-commit); 2924 is context only
- **Evidence:** ui.py:3185 default export path 'my-part.step' regardless of a saved project; 2980 'my-part.FCStd' for an unsaved doc; new() always names the document 'Untitled part' (2924); eventFilter 4273-4276 on QEvent.Close calls finish_sketch() (commit) with no prompt.
- **Scenario:** Exporting the osteotome after saving it; closing KurtShape with a half-finished sketch.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Every export dialog starts from 'my-part.step' and the previous export is overwritten by habit; a draft sketch is committed into the document on close instead of being asked about (recovery would have kept the draft).
- **A newcomer would notice:** Onshape/SolidWorks users expect Export to default to the part's name; a fixed 'my-part' plus exports showing up in Recent projects looks unfinished. Closing with a sketch open and having it silently kept is less surprising to them than losing it would be.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Save a project as X.FCStd, then Export: filename field shows my-part.step. Open a sketch, close the main window.
- **Proposed change:** Default export name = Path(native_file).stem or state['name'] + '.step'; default save name from the document label; on Close with an active sketch ask Finish / Cancel sketch / Keep working (QMessageBox) before FreeCAD's own save prompt.
- **Acceptance:** GUI recipe: export dialog default equals the project stem; close with an open sketch shows the three-way prompt.
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** None.
- **Adversarial check:** Read ui.py new (2922-2927), save (2967-2984), export (3179-3188), eventFilter Close branch (4273-4276), finish_sketch (3309-3341), checkpoint_projects/fill_recovery (2843-2875), fill_recent (2707-2714); settings.py grant_destination/remember (33-42); recovery.py capture/close (25-70, 132-136); core.py checkpoint (848-858) and the recover op (1493-1546). All cited line numbers are correct. HELD: export always proposes 'my-part.step' regardless of a saved project (3185); save proposes 'my-part.FCStd' only for a never-saved non-imported document (2980-2981); the Close branch calls finish_sketch() without asking (4273-4276). PARTLY HELD / OVERSTATED: (1) 'overwritten by habit' omits that QFileDialog.getSaveFileName shows the native overwrite confirmation, so it takes an extra click-through; the export directory is remembered via grant_destination->remember so only the filename is wrong. (2) The save-name sub-claim is moot: once saved, Save writes straight to native_file and Save As defaults to the native path, and new() always labels the doc 'Untitled part', so 'default save name from the document label' would only yield 'Untitled part.FCStd'. (3) The close auto-commit is low-stakes: if finish fails the close is refused (event.ignore()), the committed sketch then goes through FreeCAD's normal save-on-close prompt, and the finder's own parenthetical holds: checkpoint_projects runs while a sketch is in edit (it only skips during a live native tool, busy, dialog or mouse-down), recovery.capture stores 'sketch' and 'accepted_sketch', records survive a normal close (recovery.close only drains its executor), and the recover op reopens the draft in edit mode (core.py 1530-1546). Asking Finish/Cancel/Keep working is a reasonable preference but auto-commit is a defensible safe default. Not fixed at HEAD (git log -S 'my-part.step' and 'QEvent.Close' show only baseline b65f066). Net: a real but small polish item; the export-name half is the part with daily friction.

### C-18 Clicking a history row during a Fillet/Chamfer session clears the picked edges' native highlight while the dialog keeps them

**hypothesis · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.history_click / studio_tool.capture` · lines ui.py 1879-1880 (history_click -> sync_native_selection), 2494-2516 (capture), 2511-2512 (skip already listed), 2571-2576 (edit-only re-add); history_selection.py 210-215 (not 470-475); feature_panel.py 298-322
- **Evidence:** history_click calls history_controller.sync_native_selection() which does Gui.Selection.clearSelection() (history_selection.py:470-475) whenever a history row is clicked outside a sketch; the ReferencePicker keeps references/native_inputs (feature_panel.py:298-323), so the edges stay in the dialog but their native highlight disappears; capture() skips re-adding them (2511-2512).
- **Scenario:** While picking fillet edges, Kurt clicks the Fillet's part or the sketch in history to orient himself.
- **What Kurt would notice:** The viewport no longer shows which edges are in the fillet while the dialog says three are; confirming still fillets the listed edges. Mild trust loss; preview overlay still shows the result.
- **A newcomer would notice:** SolidWorks keeps the feature tree live during a fillet and preserves the selection; seeing highlights disappear with no message makes them assume the edges were lost and they re-pick or cancel, which slows the first fillet they try.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** GUI: Shift+F, pick two edges, click a history row: highlight gone, rows remain.
- **Proposed change:** Skip sync_native_selection while self.studio_dialog is set, or re-add picker.native_inputs to Gui.Selection after the sync (as begin() does at 2575-2578).
- **Acceptance:** dress-up-edit recipe: after a history click during edge picking, Gui.Selection still contains the picker's native inputs.
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** None.
- **Adversarial check:** Read ui.py history_click (1866-1884), capture()/cleanup()/begin() in studio_tool (2489-2583), the fillet/chamfer field block (2298-2320), SelectionObserver (110-125), capture_feature_selection (2101-2107); history_selection.py sync_native_selection (210-215; the finder's 470-475 is impossible, the file has 257 lines); feature_panel.py ReferencePicker.add/_remove_rows (298-345); edge_picking.py EdgeGate.allow and ClickFeedback (39-58, 96-123). The mechanism is unconditional in code: history_click resolves the row and, whenever no sketch is in edit, calls sync_native_selection, which does Gui.Selection.clearSelection() then addSelection(obj) for each selected row; there is no self.studio_dialog or edge_picking.active guard. During a fillet/chamfer dialog the EdgeGate rejects anything whose subelement is not 'Edge…' of an eligible solid, so the whole-object re-add of the history row is refused (if FreeCAD applied the gate only to viewport picks, the row object would instead be selected and capture() could flip the dialog's Part combo via fields['part'], 2498-2501; either way the edge highlight is gone). ReferencePicker keeps references/native_inputs, capture() only adds from the current (now empty) selection and skips already-listed inputs (2511-2512), and only the edit path's begin() re-adds native_inputs once at start (2571-2576), so nothing restores the highlight. commit() builds the request from picker.references, so the fillet still applies to the listed edges, matching the finder's consequence. ClickFeedback only reports gate refusals for QOpenGLWidget clicks, so the history click gives no message: silent divergence between dialog and viewport. Re-picking an already-listed edge is a harmless no-op that restores its highlight. No test or GUI recipe covers a history click during a studio dialog (tools/ grep: only assembly-gui-validation.FCMacro calls history_click, in assembly mode). Not fixed at HEAD (f57d592 changed fillet query references, not this path). Upgraded from hypothesis: the path is deterministic from the source, though not exercised at runtime in this review; severity stays low (cosmetic trust loss, no wrong geometry).

### C-19 Typed Hole and query fillet groups are bridge-only; the GUI Hole opens the native Tasks panel and only Diameter/Depth/PointAngle are editable afterwards

**opportunity · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort L** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: preference · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/hole_tools.py` · `hole_tools.handle / Panel.primary_feature_tool` · lines hole_tools.py:420-500 (handle), 531-554 (set_parameter/set_expression); ui.py:2043-2051 (primary_feature_tool), 1886-1907 (history_double_click -> parameters for a Hole), 2298-2335 (fillet/chamfer edge field, picked references only); feature_tools.py:72; core.py:135-156 (native_parameters for PartDesign::Hole); reference_tools.py:60-111
- **Evidence:** hole_tools.handle implements a typed Hole (diameter, depth/through, 118° point, counterbore/countersink, reversed, cross-Body centre bridging) with validation; ui.py:2043-2051 maps only revolve/sweep/loft/patterns/mirror/fillet/chamfer/shell/boolean/plane/move_face to owned dialogs and sends 'hole' to native_command(PartDesign_Hole) (feature_tools.py:72, More → Cut). Multi edge references {selector, all:true} (reference_tools.py:60-111) have no GUI entry; fillet dialog takes picked edges only.
- **Scenario:** Drill holes on the dodec-hub arms or the mallet; fillet 'all rims between the cup cylinder and the tori' so that the group survives a rebuild.
- **What Kurt would notice:** In the GUI Kurt gets FreeCAD's 30-field Hole Tasks panel (threads, tolerances) with no KurtShape preview/verdict and no editable KurtShape parameters; query fillets are script-only, so GUI fillets degrade to picked edges that can go ambiguous after edits.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Hole is the one everyday feature whose panel looks nothing like Extrude/Fillet: ~30 native fields (threads, tolerances, standards), no KurtShape preview or verdict. Fillet has no 'all edges like this' option, so large edge groups are picked one by one and only Onshape-style per-edge associativity survives.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** More → Cut → Hole opens the native panel; Fillet dialog has no 'all edges like this' option.
- **Proposed change:** Add 'hole' to studio_tool: profile sketch choice, Diameter, End (Blind/Through), Depth, Point angle, Counterbore/Countersink group, Reverse, with the shared preview adapter (proposal() → {op:'hole',...}); make double-click on a Hole row open it with hole_tools._settings. In Fillet/Chamfer add a per-row 'Match all like this' action that converts a picked edge into {selector:{kind:'geometry', ... adjacent ...}, all:true} using resolve_selector to show the match count before Confirm.
- **Acceptance:** GUI recipe: Hole dialog previews and commits one PartDesign::Hole with KurtShape parameters editable in Feature settings; a fillet created with 'Match all like this' re-evaluates after a pattern count change (test_multi_edge_references as gate).
- **Dependencies:** hole_tools (1f0001e) and multi references (f57d592) are in the checkout but not in the running app.
- **Risk:** Low for Hole (controller validation exists); query authoring UI needs a small set of templates (same radius, adjacent to picked face).
- **Adversarial check:** Read hole_tools.py 1-80 and 380-628 (handle at 420: typed diameter/depth/through/point angle/counterbore/countersink/reversed with validation, cross-Body bridge profile), ui.py 2043-2051 (primary_feature_tool maps only revolve/sweep/loft/patterns/mirror/fillet/chamfer/shell/boolean/plane/move_face to studio_tool; 'hole' falls to native_command(PartDesign_Hole)), feature_tools.py:72 (hole under 'Remove material' = the More -> Cut menu, ui.py 562-570), reference_tools.py 1-140 (multi {selector, all:true} entries), ui.py 2298-2335 (fillet/chamfer dialog: reference_field of picked edges, no query/all authoring; f57d592 only taught the reopen path to tolerate a de-duplicated row), and grep: ui.py contains no 'hole' at all. Git log at HEAD f57d592: hole (1f0001e) and multi references (f57d592) landed 2026-10-17 with no GUI counterpart; docs/OPERATION_CONTRACT.md documents them as operations only. HELD: no owned Hole dialog, no preview/verdict, no GUI way to author a query fillet group. DID NOT HOLD: 'no editable KurtShape parameters'. history_double_click (ui.py 1886-1907) sends a Hole row to parameters(show=True), and core.native_parameters (core.py 135-156) exposes Diameter, Depth (blind) and PointAngle (angled blind) for any PartDesign::Hole, GUI-made or bridge-made, routed through hole_tools.set_parameter/set_expression with its clamp and plausibility checks. So the post-creation edit story is partial, not absent; what is missing is the end type, counterbore/countersink and reverse controls plus the creation dialog. Classification 'opportunity' is honest; the gap is a feature gap, not a defect, and the native Hole panel works (and does threads/standards the typed op cannot). Not already solved.

## D. Architecture and code quality

### D-01 Post-commit inspection failure is reported as a rejected request although the change was committed

**verified defect · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: reproduced · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/core.py` · `Controller._dispatch` · lines 1697-1701, 1731-1749, 1750-1798 (1784-1797)
- **Evidence:** After `doc.commitTransaction()` (1697) and `self._new_revision(doc)` (1701), `result = self.inspect(doc, measure)` (1731) still runs inside the mutation's try; the except (1750) maps any exception to `code = "operation_failed"` (1785) and writes `{"request": request, "error": error, "rejected": True}` to last_failures and `meta.LastFailure` (1795-1797). Headless reproduction: create_sketch with an injected summary failure returned ok:false/operation_failed while `doc.getObject("ExpSketch")` existed, the revision had advanced, UndoNames == ['KurtShape: create_sketch'], last_failures[...].rejected == True, LastFailure was set, ledger status 'failed', and an identical request_id replay returned the same failure.
- **Scenario:** Any exception raised while building the inspection payload (native_feature_summary over a native property that raises, a corrupt QueryHealth JSON, a FreeCAD wrapper error) after a successful typed mutation, from the bridge or from Panel.operation.
- **What Kurt would notice:** An agent sees ok:false with rejected:true, retries with the same request_id and gets the stored failure forever, then re-inspects and finds the feature already there; the document carries a persisted LastFailure that says the request was rejected. In the GUI, `_operation`'s error path (ui.py:1344-1345) calls the raw `self.core.inspect`, which raises again and escapes the Qt slot, so the panel keeps stale state while the model changed.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Run scratchpad/area-d-architecture/exp2_inspect_failure_after_commit.py under the bundled pythonw with the isolated env (output in exp2_out.txt). Verification in the app: patch `core.native_feature_summary` the same way in a GUI recipe, press Shift+S/plane, observe the status bar error and the feature in the viewport.
- **Proposed change:** Split `_dispatch` into three phases with separate exception scopes: admit(request) -> mutate(doc, request) under the transaction/rollback scope -> report(doc). A failure in report() must not abort/rollback anything (nothing is pending) and must not mark the request rejected: return `{"ok": false, "error": {"code": "inspection_failed", "applied": true, "revision": <new>}}`, leave LastFailure alone, and record the ledger status as `completed_unreported` so an identical replay returns the same honest answer. In ui.py `_operation`, replace the raw `self.core.inspect` in the error path with `self.core.dispatch({"op":"inspect",...})` and notify. Document `applied` in OPERATION_CONTRACT.md (contract 3.1).
- **Acceptance:** New native test (modelled on exp2) asserting: committed feature exists, response code inspection_failed with applied:true, LastFailure unchanged, replay identical; existing 426 native passes and bridge tests unchanged. Regression gate: test_core / test_core_safety / test_bridge_native plus the sketch-lifecycle GUI recipe.
- **Dependencies:** None; contract revision note.
- **Risk:** Agent scripts that branch on code strings see a new code; keep `operation_failed` for genuine mutation-phase unknown exceptions.

### D-02 Panel.tick has no backoff for a persistently failing inspect: 40 Hz silent failure loop with one traceback appended per attempt

**hypothesis · severity medium · confidence medium · user value medium · effort S** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: reproduced · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.tick, Controller._dispatch logging` · lines 1245-1269 (tick), 1344-1345 (_operation error path); src/kurtshape/core.py:1786-1793
- **Evidence:** tick: `result = self.core.dispatch({"op": "inspect", ...})` (1250) followed by `if result["ok"]:` (1251) with no else branch; `self.core.observer.state(doc)["refresh"] = False` (1266) is only reached on success, so `needs_refresh` stays True and the 25 ms timer (ui.py:223) re-runs the failing inspect every tick. core.py:1786-1793 appends `traceback.format_exc()` to kurtshape.log for every unknown exception with no deduplication. Headless half verified: five consecutive failing inspects appended five tracebacks (exp2).
- **Scenario:** Same trigger as the previous finding, or any object whose summary read raises persistently (foreign-document link, a script-owned object with a failing property getter).
- **What Kurt would notice:** Kurt sees the history panel freeze at its last good state with no message; CPU is spent re-raising at 40 Hz and runtime/kurtshape.log grows by one traceback every 25 ms (about 140 KB/s) until restart.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** GUI recipe: inject the same summary failure via a monkeypatch in a validation macro, let the app idle 2 s, assert kurtshape.log grew by about 80 tracebacks and no Messages entry/status text appeared. Headless half: exp2.
- **Proposed change:** In tick, on `not result["ok"]`: notify once per document ("Inspection failed: ... see Messages"), store `self.inspect_failure = (doc.Name, generation)` and skip re-inspection until the observer generation/change_serial moves or 2 s elapse; in `_dispatch`, rate-limit log writes (dedupe by the last traceback line, at most one per second per code) and route a one-line summary to Messages via App.Console.PrintError. Use `core.dispatch` in `_operation`'s error path.
- **Acceptance:** Unit test: a controller whose inspect raises logs one traceback per second at most; GUI recipe asserts exactly one Messages entry and a status-bar error, and that a successful inspect resumes refresh. Gate: test_responsiveness, gui-validation recipe.
- **Dependencies:** Pairs naturally with the previous finding.
- **Risk:** Low; only the failure path changes.

### D-03 Session retry ledger refuses every mutation after 4096 tracked requests per app session; evicted ids are never forgotten

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: reproduced · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/request_ledger.py` · `RequestLedger.register / RequestLedger.complete` · lines 15, 38-39, 43-52
- **Evidence:** `self.session_request_limit = limit * 8` (15); `if len(self.records) + len(self.expired) >= self.session_request_limit: return request_capacity_reached` (38-39); complete() moves evicted ids into `self.expired` (52) which is never pruned. Experiment 1: the 4097th distinct tracked request returns request_capacity_reached with records=512, expired=3584; a fresh id afterwards is still refused. Tested as intended behaviour in tests/test_bridge.py:443 and test_review_followup.py:309.
- **Scenario:** Kurt keeps one GUI instance running for days (today's started 01:28) while agents model through the bridge; every mutation (pad, fillet, set_parameter, save, export) is tracked. dodec_steps.py alone has 22 typed calls per run; iterative rebuilds, retries and multi-part sessions accumulate.
- **What Kurt would notice:** After the ceiling, every mutation, including `save`, is rejected with request_capacity_reached until Kurt restarts the app; inspect/preview keep working, so the agent appears connected but cannot change or save anything. The message tells the agent to 'restart', which it cannot do without Kurt.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** scratchpad/area-d-architecture/exp1_ledger_capacity.py (exp1_out.txt). Field sizing: count `request_id` mutations in a day of bridge traffic (capabilities exposes result_limit/session_request_limit).
- **Proposed change:** Make the ceiling a soft LRU: cap `expired` at, for example, 8*limit entries and drop the oldest (their status becomes not_recorded, which the contract already defines as non-proof of non-execution), and/or expire by age (24 h). Keep request_id_reused protection for live records. Expose `remaining_capacity` in capabilities and warn in Messages at 90 percent. Smaller alternative: raise limit to 4096 (cap 32768) and keep semantics.
- **Acceptance:** test_bridge capacity test updated: 100k tracked requests still accepted; len(records)+len(expired) bounded; request_expired still returned for ids inside the retention window; OPERATION_CONTRACT retry paragraph updated. Gate: bridge suite.
- **Dependencies:** Contract note.
- **Risk:** An id reused with a different payload after it has been forgotten is accepted as new instead of request_expired; mitigated by the retention window and by agents' existing inspect-before-mutate discipline.
- **Merged duplicate F-13:** Session retry cap (4096 IDs) is reachable in a day of iterative agent builds and the only remedy is an app restart — hypothesis, low, reproduced · not independently verified. Evidence: request_ledger.py:15 `session_request_limit = limit * 8` (4096); 38-39 rejects `request_capacity_reached` once records+expired reach it; `expired` never shrinks. A full dodec-hub bridge build is ~70 tracked mutations (projects/dodec-hub/build-bridge.log, 73 lines); bone-mallet rebuilds in place every run (build_mallet_kurtshape.py:52-56 delete_features then full rebuild, ~40 mutations); failed attempts consume IDs too. ~60-100 iterations in one app session exhaust it. Memory is not the issue: a 26-feature plate inspect result is 15 KB (probe B1), so 512 cached results are ~8 MB.

### D-04 Alt+C cannot find 'Move face': toolbar actions are merged by native command id and Move face shares PartDesign_Pad with Extrude

**verified defect · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: reproduced · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/tool_search.py` · `tool_search.build_entries, feature_tools.MODEL_TOOLS('move_face')` · lines 51-95 (build_entries, merge at 64); src/kurtshape/feature_tools.py:60,77,176-178; src/kurtshape/ui.py:548-555
- **Evidence:** feature_tools.py:77 `_tool('move_face', 'Move face', 'PartDesign_Pad', ...)` (icon reuse, introduced in 4681ddf) while 'pad' also uses PartDesign_Pad (:60). ui.make_toolbars registers Extrude with command=PartDesign_Pad (548-551) before the primary tools (553-555); build_entries keys by `action.get("command")` first (64), so Move face is folded into Extrude. Experiment 5 with the real catalog and shortcuts.json: 'Move face' absent from entries; Extrude groups ['Part Studio','Add material','Modify']; search 'move face' -> Shell, Extrude · Cut. The same run shows 'fillet' producing three rows (toolbar Fillet without a key, registry 'Edge fillet' and 'Sketch fillet' with Shift+F) because registry and toolbar labels differ.
- **Scenario:** Kurt types 'move face' (or 'fillet') in the search field or Alt+C.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Move face is only reachable from its toolbar button; the EVERYDAY_UI.md promise 'each tool appears once' is broken for Fillet, and the toolbar Fillet row shows no shortcut. Applies to the running app as well (code predates it).
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** scratchpad/area-d-architecture/exp5_move_face_search.py (exp5_out.txt) and exp4_tool_search_merge.py (exp4_out.txt).
- **Proposed change:** Give every toolbar/menu QAction a stable `kurtshapeId` property (feature_tools key, or shortcut id for registry-only actions) and merge in build_entries by that id, falling back to label only when no id exists; add a catalog invariant test that no two NativeTool entries share `command` unless they declare distinct ids; align registry labels with toolbar labels for merged pairs (feature.fillet label 'Edge fillet' vs toolbar 'Fillet') or merge via the id.
- **Acceptance:** test_tool_search gains a case built from the real feature_tools catalog in make_toolbars order asserting every PRIMARY_TOOLS label is its own entry, 'move face' returns Move face first, and 'fillet' yields one Part Studio row carrying Shift+F. Gate: test_tool_search, everyday-ui GUI recipe.
- **Dependencies:** None (can precede the descriptor work).
- **Risk:** Low.

### D-05 Command metadata lives in seven places and has drifted: registry status/native_command disagree with the wired callbacks, keys are embedded in tooltip strings, hover labels ignore rebinding

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort M** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: source · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.tool / Panel.make_toolbars / Panel.primary_feature_tool / ShortcutRouter / ToolbarLabels / tool_search` · lines 430-446 (tool, key parsed from tip at 445), 548-551 and 607-608 (keys as literals), 2043-2051 (primary_feature_tool typed-vs-native map), 4195-4228 (shortcut_callbacks); shortcuts.json:39-44,110; src/kurtshape/tool_search.py:12-45; src/kurtshape/toolbar_labels.py:39-53,66-83,110-123; docs/ONSHAPE_SHORTCUTS.md:63
- **Evidence:** shortcuts.json:40 `feature.fillet ... status:native, native_command:PartDesign_Fillet` and :44 `feature.revolve ... native PartDesign_Revolution`, but ui.py:4199-4200 wires `feature.fillet` to `studio_tool("fillet")` and `feature.revolve` to `studio_from_sketch("revolve")` (callbacks win in ShortcutRouter.invoke); ONSHAPE_SHORTCUTS.md:63 publishes 'Edge fillet | Shift+F | native | PartDesign_Fillet'. Shortcut keys are literals in tips ('Extrude · ... · Shift+E', ui.py:550) and in the sketch bar tuple list ('L','G','C','A','Shift+S','D','M','Q', ui.py:607), re-parsed by `tip.split(" · ")[-1]` (445) and `tool_search.shortcut_from_tip` (45). ToolbarLabels.register stores that static key (39-53) and `_scan_toolbar` falls back to it because the QActions carry no QKeySequence (66-83), so `_show_pending` (123) renders the default after a rebind. tool_search.ALIASES is keyed by display label (12-42). Op semantics are repeated in `_operation` (1296-1297, 1300, 1305-1308, 1312-1318, 1335-1339, 1366) and in `_dispatch` (1567, 1693).
- **Scenario:** Kurt rebinds Shift+E via Shift+/ Rebind; hovers Extrude; reads ONSHAPE_SHORTCUTS.md; a developer adds the typed `hole` op to the GUI.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Hover label shows Shift+E while help and search show the new key; documentation claims native backends that are not used; adding a command touches shortcuts.json, feature_tools, make_toolbars, shortcut_callbacks, primary_feature_tool, ALIASES, the `_operation` op lists and the contract, with no test catching a missed place (the Move face defect is one instance).
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Code reading above; rebind check: set shortcuts.user.json bindings {"feature.extrude":"Ctrl+E"}, start the app, hover Extrude (label) vs Shift+/ (help) vs Alt+C (search). Drift check: compare registry entries with status 'native' against ids present in Panel.shortcut_callbacks().
- **Proposed change:** Registry design: a `commands.py` table of CommandDescriptor{id, label, aliases, icon, tip, category, contexts, default_key, kind in {typed_op, native_command, ui_task, builtin}, native_command?, typed_ops?, availability predicate name, help}. shortcuts.json becomes the binding layer (id -> key, user overrides unchanged); toolbars, menus, search, help, ToolbarLabels and ONSHAPE_SHORTCUTS.md are generated from the table; ToolbarLabels looks up the current key by id at show time. Keep these distinctions separate in the schema: runtime context availability (predicate) vs native FreeCAD command id (may be absent on other installs, filtered by available_catalog) vs typed bridge op(s) vs UI task (Extrude dialog fans out to pad/pocket/studio ops/edit_extrusion; Sketch is create_sketch+begin_sketch_edit+native setEdit) vs key binding vs presentation. GUI commands with no bridge op (all Sketcher_* and native PartDesign dialogs, view/selection/measure/isolate, workspace switching, Recovery) and bridge ops with no GUI command (sketch_rectangle/sketch_circle/add_*, resolve_selector, preview, list_documents, capabilities, request_status, diagnostics, assembly_candidates, orphan_helpers) remain representable because kind is per descriptor. Smaller alternative (S): keep JSON + feature_tools, stop embedding keys in tips (pass shortcut_id to ToolbarLabels.register and resolve at show time), key ALIASES by id, and add a drift test asserting every registry entry with status 'native' has no Python callback and every 'callback' entry has one. Migration: descriptors for the Part Studio bar first, then sketch/assembly bars, menus, search and help; regenerate ONSHAPE_SHORTCUTS.md from the table. Compatibility: shortcuts.user.json format unchanged. Failure modes: duplicate ids, missing icon names; rollback is deleting the table and restoring the inline tuples. Evidence before promotion: test_shortcuts/test_tool_search extended, toolbar-validation and everyday-ui recipes green, generated doc diff reviewed.
- **Acceptance:** Drift test passes (registry backend == wiring); hover label equals the current binding after a rebind in a GUI recipe; ONSHAPE_SHORTCUTS.md regenerates without a diff; Move face/Fillet search cases from the previous finding pass; no toolbar icon or label changes in the theme-repair screenshots (validation/onshape-theme-repair/*.png, Oct 4 era) beyond intended ones.
- **Dependencies:** Previous finding (search merge id) is the first step.
- **Risk:** Medium: touches every toolbar; mitigate by generating the same QActions and comparing action lists before/after in a test.

### D-06 Error taxonomy: 122 emitted codes, about 26 documented, with synonyms and one code covering ten conditions

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort M** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: source · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/core.py` · `OperationError / _error helpers / AssemblyError` · lines 1437 (unsupported_operation) vs src/kurtshape/assembly.py:934 (unknown_operation); core.py:906-928 (_body translates studio missing_part to missing_body) vs modeling_geometry.py:506, hole_tools.py:429, reference_tools.py:130 (missing_body with 'part' messages); core.py:1470,1477,1481,1566,1573,1579,1820,1824 (sketch_edit_active); core.py:1785,1856 (operation_failed/preview_failed fallbacks); docs/OPERATION_CONTRACT.md (284 lines, no error table)
- **Evidence:** Inventory (grep of OperationError("…"), _error("…"), AssemblyError("…"), incompatible("…")): 122 distinct codes across core (65), assembly (26), selectors (11), studio (11), modeling_geometry (12), hole_tools, reference_tools, extrusion_preview, step/geometry workers, plus 8 transport-only codes in bridge.py/request_ledger.py (queue_full, timeout, outcome_unknown, request_pending, request_expired, request_id_reused, request_capacity_reached, invalid_request_id). Backticked identifiers in OPERATION_CONTRACT.md that are error codes: about 26; 96 emitted codes do not appear there at all (e.g. stale_revision, busy, unknown_document, unmanaged_document, sketch_history_changed, empty_history, save_failed, export_failed, path_outside_workspace, invalid_format). Families mix prefixes for the same meaning: no_solid/no_orphans/no_sketch_edit vs empty_cut/empty_profile/empty_history vs missing_body/missing_file/missing_directory.
- **Scenario:** An agent (or ks.py / build_bridge.py) branches on error codes to decide retry vs re-inspect vs abort; a developer adds an op in a satellite module and invents a code.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Clients cannot know the retryable set (stale_revision, busy, outcome_unknown) from the contract; identical situations surface as different codes depending on which module raised; sketch_edit_active hides whether the blocker is a managed lease, a native Task dialog or an assembly edit, so the agent cannot pick the right remedy.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Re-run the inventory: grep -oh 'OperationError("[a-z_]*"\|_error("[a-z_]*"\|AssemblyError("[a-z_]*"' src/kurtshape/*.py | sort -u, then compare against docs/OPERATION_CONTRACT.md backticks.
- **Proposed change:** Add `errors.py` with a frozen CODES table (code -> category in {admission, validation, reference, geometry, history, io, transport}, retryable bool, remedy hint, contract anchor). OperationError asserts membership in dev/test mode. Collapse synonyms behind aliases for one contract revision (unknown_operation -> unsupported_operation; missing_part accepted but documented as missing_body), split sketch_edit_active into lease_active / native_editor_active / assembly_edit_active with `blocker` detail, and append an error table to OPERATION_CONTRACT.md. Add a unit test that scans src for emitted codes and fails on any code absent from CODES or from the contract table.
- **Acceptance:** The scanning test passes; capabilities reports `error_codes` version; AGENTS.md/OPERATION_CONTRACT list retryable codes; bridge and native suites green. Gate: the scanning test itself plus test_bridge/test_core.
- **Dependencies:** Coordinate with Area (bridge/observability) if another finder owns capabilities.
- **Risk:** Agent scripts matching old codes; keep aliases for one revision and list them.

### D-07 Two disposable-clone evaluators with different acceptance rules: Controller._preview vs ExtrusionEvaluator.evaluate

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort M** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: source · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/core.py` · `Controller._preview, ExtrusionEvaluator.evaluate` · lines 1812-1884 (_preview; valid rule 1864-1867, one-solid rule 1851-1854); src/kurtshape/extrusion_preview.py:340-524 (evaluate; aggregate rule 398, acceptance 431-433, cleanup 505-524)
- **Evidence:** Both: saveCopy -> hidden openDocument -> openTransaction + getActiveTransaction id -> _model_operation -> studio.reconcile -> _recompute -> _build_status -> commit/closeActiveTransaction -> closeDocument + geometry.drop_document + observer.states.pop + setActiveDocument + _restore_gui, each with its own busy handling. Rules differ: `_preview` accepts `build_status in {"valid","sketch_only"}` (1865) and applies the one-solid rule only when `not studio.enabled(clone)` (1851-1854); `evaluate` requires 'valid' except create_plane (431) and skips the one-solid rule whenever `"operation" in normalized or op not in {pad,pocket}` (398, 432). `_preview` uses tempfile per call; evaluate caches eight results and reuses a snapshot per generation.
- **Scenario:** An agent runs `preview` for a pad with an explicit `operation` on a non-studio Body, then the same proposal through the GUI Extrude dialog; or a fix lands in one path (for example the closeActiveTransaction(True) cleanup) and not the other.
- **What Kurt would notice:** The bridge preview and the GUI preview can disagree on validity for the same proposal; two cleanup paths to keep correct; the worker process (preview_worker.run_worker) depends on the evaluate variant only, so bridge previews never exercise the worker-proven path.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Native test: build a two-Body non-studio document, propose `pad` with operation:'add' on one Body producing two solids; compare `_preview` verdict with `ExtrusionEvaluator(core, doc, rev).evaluate(proposal).valid`. Code reading above establishes the rule difference.
- **Proposed change:** Extract `preview_clone.py` with a `DisposableClone(controller, source_doc, snapshot_path, borrowed)` context manager owning busy flag, GUI capture/restore, transaction id, close/drop/states.pop, and one `evaluate_proposal(controller, clone, proposal, *, rules)` where the acceptance rule set is a function of op and studio state. `_preview` becomes a thin wrapper that returns the bridge payload; ExtrusionEvaluator keeps caching/snapshot reuse but calls the shared function.
- **Acceptance:** Parity test over a proposal matrix (pad/pocket/create_plane/fillet x studio/non-studio x valid/invalid) asserting identical valid/invalid/error.code from both entry points; test_extrusion_preview, test_borrowed_preview, test_preview_worker_leases and test_core preview cases green; preview GUI recipe unchanged.
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** Medium: the evaluate path carries S16 borrowed-snapshot validation calls at three points (362, 395, 483) that must stay in order.

### D-08 Geometry-check and STEP-export background jobs are two hand-duplicated state machines in Panel; their controller halves live in core.py but are only used by ui.py

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort M** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: source · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.check_geometry/_poll_geometry_check/_cancel_geometry_check/..., Panel.start_step_export/_poll_step_export/..., Controller.prepare_*/accept_*` · lines 137-154 (12 fields, 2 timers), 1412-1628 (check family), 2999-3177 (STEP family), 3819-3823 (cancel_task returns None vs True); src/kurtshape/core.py:338-847 (prepare/accept pairs, _replace_export_pair)
- **Evidence:** The two families mirror each other method for method (`_context_current`, `_current`, `_retire`, `_cancel`, `_restore_status`, `_poll`, fallback) with the same generation/ownership fences and the same magic status strings duplicated into set literals (1505-1506 vs 3102). Already diverged in small ways: `cancel_task` does `return` for STEP (3821) and `return True` for check (3823); `_poll_step_export` carries an `invalid_state` branch (3151-3164) the check twin lacks. core.py:338-847 (~510 lines) implements prepare_geometry_check/accept_geometry_check/prepare_step_export/accept_step_export used by no bridge op (bridge check_geometry/export stay synchronous).
- **Scenario:** Adding a third job (for example a background Rebuild or an STL export worker), or fixing an ownership-fence bug found in one twin.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Fixes must be applied twice; the S17/S18 guard ordering ('context first, native reads last, pure ownership') is encoded by position in two 200-line methods and cannot be unit-tested without a QApplication and FreeCAD.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Read the two families side by side; diff after normalising the kind name.
- **Proposed change:** Introduce `jobs.py` (move the controller prepare/accept pairs and `_replace_export_pair` out of core.py; Controller keeps thin delegating methods) and `job_controller.py` with a `WorkerJobController(kind, prepare, accept, present, status_texts)` holding generation/job/preparing/closed and one QTimer; Panel owns two instances. Keep the exact fence order as named helper methods so a headless test with a fake worker can assert the sequence (prepare -> context recheck -> submit -> poll -> accept(is_current, owns_job) -> present).
- **Acceptance:** Headless unit tests for WorkerJobController phases with fake workers (cancel during prepare, document switch during poll, stale generation at accept); tests/test_geometry_check_jobs.py and test_step_export_jobs.py unchanged and green; geometry-check-lifecycle and step-export GUI recipes (91 checks) green; core.py shrinks by about 500 lines with no behaviour change.
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** Medium: the STEP recipe's exact exception-cause assertions depend on message text; keep messages verbatim.

### D-09 Sketch session and modal interaction state are implicit: seven Panel flags, a tick auto-finisher, a hand-ordered Escape chain, and an untyped lease dict in the controller

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence medium · user value medium · effort L** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: source · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.start_sketch/edit_sketch/finish_sketch/leave_native_sketch_edit/sketch_lease_needs_finish/cancel_task; Controller.sketch_edits` · lines 155-165 (flags), 3190-3421 (start_sketch .. sync_edit_ui), 1236-1248 and 1267-1268 (tick forcing document and auto-finish), 3819-3845 (cancel_task chain), 4273-4276 (close guard only for main window); src/kurtshape/core.py:1106-1214, 1601-1646 (lease dict with optional keys created/use_sources/grouping_started)
- **Evidence:** States are encoded in `pending_sketch`, `selecting_support`, `placement_document`, `plane_visibility`, `finishing_sketch`, `entering_sketch`, `native_edit_failure` plus `state["active_sketch_edit"]` and `Gui.getInEdit()`; `sketch_lease_needs_finish` (3376-3383) derives 'lease open but editor closed' from four of them; tick (1246-1247, 1267-1268) auto-finishes. `cancel_task` (3819-3845) is a seven-way priority chain over unrelated subsystems (STEP job, check job, plane editor, reference picking, studio dialog, placement, extrusion task). In core, `sketch_edits[id]` is a dict whose phase is implied by `editing.pop("grouping_started", False)` (1196) and `editing.get("created")` (1169). tool_session.py already shows the explicit alternative.
- **Scenario:** Any new modal surface (a typed Hole dialog, a Measure tool, the plane editor) or any change to Finish/Cancel behaviour, like the Oct 6 'right-click fully ends continuous drawing' repair.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Escape semantics depend on insertion order with no test enumerating them; invariants (a lease exists iff the editor is open or a teardown failure is recorded) are enforced in six methods; a headless state-machine test is impossible today, so regressions are only caught by GUI recipes.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Code reading; try to write a unit test for 'Escape during plane-pick while a STEP job is pending' without a GUI.
- **Proposed change:** UI: a `SketchSession` object with explicit phases {idle, placing, entering, editing, finishing, teardown_failed} owning the current flags and exposing `needs_finish()`, `can_switch_document()`, `on_escape()`; a small `ModalStack` where each subsystem pushes/pops an interrupt handler so `cancel_task` becomes `stack.cancel_top()`. Core: `SketchLease` dataclass (feature, initial, undo_before, signature, undo/redo deques, created: bool, phase: Enum{open, grouping, restoring}) replacing the dict; `_commit_sketch_edit`/`_preserve_sketch_draft`/`_rollback_sketch_edit` become methods. No behaviour change intended.
- **Acceptance:** Headless tests for SketchSession/ModalStack transitions and for SketchLease phases (begin, native commit, finish grouping failure -> draft preserved, cancel after external history change -> sketch_history_changed); sketch-lifecycle, sketch-edit-state, sketch-behavior and line-endpoint-freeze GUI recipes green; test_sketch_planes/test_history_lifecycle unchanged.
- **Dependencies:** Do after the job-controller extraction so cancel_task's first two branches become stack entries.
- **Risk:** High if rushed: this code embodies the Oct 5-6 interaction repairs; land as pure refactor with recipe gates per step.

### D-10 studio_tool is a 470-line method with 15 closures building 14 different dialogs; the GUI Hole is the native dialog, not the typed op

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort M** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: source · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.studio_tool, Panel.primary_feature_tool` · lines 2120-2590 (studio_tool), 2043-2051 (primary_feature_tool); src/kurtshape/hole_tools.py:3-33, 234-242, 473-474
- **Evidence:** `studio_tool` spans 2120-2590 with nested defs field/choice/quantity/vector/checklist/reference_field/activate/proposal/status/schedule/capture/cleanup/commit/eligible/begin capturing shared mutable dicts (fields, values, pickers, active_picker). `primary_feature_tool` maps 12 catalog keys to typed ops and sends everything else (including 'hole') to `native_command`; the typed `hole` builds a Source binder, Centres binder and private Profile sketch (hole_tools.py:26-33, 234-242) while the native PartDesign_Hole dialog creates a Hole whose Profile is the user sketch, so the two Holes are structurally different features.
- **Scenario:** Giving the GUI the typed Hole (editable Diameter/Depth/PointAngle, stable cross-Body centres) or adding a form field to Transform.
