- **What Kurt would notice:** Every dialog change edits one function; proposal construction cannot be unit-tested without a QApplication and a document; GUI-made and bridge-made Holes behave differently on edit/delete/inspection.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Read 2120-2590; count closures (15).
- **Proposed change:** Per-operation form specs as data (fields, pickers, defaults, proposal builder) in a `studio_forms.py`; one `StudioDialog` that renders a spec and owns picker/preview lifecycle; `primary_feature_tool` reads the spec table. Then add a `hole` spec so the toolbar Hole uses the typed op (keep the native dialog in More for parity).
- **Acceptance:** Headless tests build proposals from specs for each op and compare with today's dialogs' proposals (captured once as fixtures); dress-up-edit, u2-part-model and everyday-ui GUI recipes green; the Hole from the toolbar inspects with the same `parameters` as a bridge hole.
- **Dependencies:** Command descriptor work benefits from the same spec table.
- **Risk:** Medium: edit mode (describe_dress_up) threads `edit` through the closures.

### D-11 Per-document UI/controller state that is not keyed or reset: isolate visibility leaks across documents, stale last_failed_attempt after same-UUID reopen, workspace memory keyed by reusable document Name

**verified defect · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: source · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.isolate, Panel.set_workspace_mode, Controller.attach` · lines 4171-4188 (isolate), 810-811 and 1641 (workspace_documents by Name); src/kurtshape/core.py:1021-1048 (attach), 1402 (inspect reports last_failed_attempt)
- **Evidence:** `isolate`: `self.isolated_visibility = {}` is a flat dict of object names (4183) restored onto `self.document().getObject(key)` at the next call (4176-4181) regardless of which document is active. `attach` only sets `self.last_failures[id]` when `meta.LastFailure` is truthy (1045-1048) and never pops it, while every failure also stores the in-memory entry (1796); `_inspect` reports `self.last_failures.get(meta.DocumentId)` (1402). `workspace_documents[mode] = doc.Name` (1641) and lookup by Name (810-811); FreeCAD reuses freed document names.
- **Scenario:** Isolate a feature in part A (Shift+I), switch to part B (Ctrl+Tab), press Shift+I: B's Sketch/Pad/Body visibility is set from A's saved values and A stays isolated. Open A, hit a rejected op, close A without saving, reopen A from disk: inspect still reports the old last_failed_attempt though the file carries none.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Visibility corruption Kurt has to repair by hand; agents see a phantom failure after reopen; workspace switching may jump to an unrelated document that inherited a name.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Isolate case: GUI recipe with two documents as above. Reopen case: native test: new -> save -> pad with bad length -> close -> open same path -> inspect()["last_failed_attempt"] should be None.
- **Proposed change:** Key `isolated_visibility` by document Name and clear it in tick's document-change branch; in `attach`, `self.last_failures.pop(meta.DocumentId, None)` before loading from meta; key `workspace_documents` by DocumentId (or the observer instance_id).
- **Acceptance:** The two tests above plus a Ctrl+Tab/workspace recipe assertion; no change elsewhere.
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** Low.

### D-12 Resource leftovers without sweep or rotation: stale snapshot archives survive sessions, kurtshape.log is append-only, write grants accumulate

**design weakness · severity low · confidence medium · user value medium · effort S** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: source · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/snapshots.py` · `SnapshotStore, NativePreviewWorker.close, Controller._dispatch logging, Settings.grant_destination` · lines 46-53 (__init__ no sweep), 55-60 (_delete), 135-147 (retire/close); src/kurtshape/preview_worker.py:141-196 (daemon reaper releases pins); src/kurtshape/core.py:1786-1793 (log append); src/kurtshape/settings.py:33-37 (grant_destination)
- **Evidence:** runtime/snapshots holds snapshot-ae96b27f...FCStd dated 2026-10-15 19:37 alongside two files from today's 01:28 session (names/dates only were listed); SnapshotStore never scans its directory on construction and deletes only records it created this process; a pinned record is deleted on lease release, which happens on the daemon 'KurtShape-preview-cleanup' thread after process kill/wait, so interpreter exit can skip it. kurtshape.log is opened in append mode with no cap. `grant_destination` adds every Save/Export parent folder to `project_roots` permanently with no UI to review or prune.
- **Scenario:** Many sessions over weeks; a crash or kill during a preview; a Save As into an odd folder once.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Snapshot archives (one full FCStd copy per document per session, 600 KB here, larger for real parts) accumulate silently; a failure storm (see tick finding) grows the log unbounded; bridge write scope widens permanently after any GUI Save As.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** ls runtime/snapshots (dates older than the current process start); read SnapshotStore.__init__; read settings.json project_roots after a Save As to a new folder.
- **Proposed change:** SnapshotStore.__init__: remove `snapshot-*.FCStd` not referenced by this process (keys are per-process instance ids, so all pre-existing files are stale). Reuse responsiveness.BoundedJsonlWriter's rotation pattern for kurtshape.log (cap size, keep two generations). Show grants in Settings with a Remove button and record grant time; optionally expire grants not used for 30 days.
- **Acceptance:** Unit test: constructing a store over a directory containing foreign snapshot files deletes them; log rotation test; Settings dialog lists and prunes grants (GUI recipe). Gate: test_snapshots, theme/settings recipes.
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** Deleting a snapshot pinned by another live KurtShape instance sharing the same session dir; the README says one normal instance per default session, and private sessions use their own dirs.

### D-13 FreeCAD's own AutoSaver (every 60 s, GUI thread) runs alongside KurtShape recovery; a second writer that bypasses the idle gate and the responsiveness evidence

**hypothesis · severity low · confidence medium · user value medium · effort S** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: source · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `ui.start, Panel.checkpoint_projects` · lines 4313-4317 (start sets RecoveryEnabled/AutoSaveEnabled/AutoSaveTimeout=1); docs/OPERATION_CONTRACT.md:118 ('Native recovery/autosave remains enabled at one minute in the app profile'); src/kurtshape/ui.py:2854-2877 (checkpoint_projects idle gate)
- **Evidence:** start() forces AutoSaveEnabled=True with a 1-minute timeout and saves the parameter; KurtShape's own recovery is idle-gated, mouse-button-gated and bounded to one capture per pass (2859-2877), while FreeCAD's AutoSaver serializes every modified document on its own timer on the GUI thread; M1_PROGRESS.md:19 lists 'native recomputation/autosave' as unreproduced pause sources.
- **Scenario:** Dragging a sketch endpoint or orbiting a large part exactly when the minute timer fires.
- **What Kurt would notice:** A periodic stall whose size scales with the document, not attributable through KurtShape's monitor (it is not a KurtShape command), and two recovery mechanisms with different retention semantics; FreeCAD's recovery dialog can also appear at startup before KurtShape hides the Start page.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Open the 1000-solid fixture, enable the responsiveness stall log, idle-drag for 3 minutes with AutoSaveEnabled on, then off; compare stalls at minute boundaries. Not measured here (concurrent CPU load).
- **Proposed change:** Decide explicitly: either disable native AutoSave while KurtShape recovery is active (keep RecoveryEnabled false too, since its dialog depends on autosave files) and document that KurtShape Recovery is the only path, or raise the timeout and gate it behind the same idle predicate by toggling AutoSaveEnabled from checkpoint_projects. Record the decision in OPERATION_CONTRACT.md:118.
- **Acceptance:** Stall-log comparison shows no minute-boundary stalls; review-recovery-crash recipe still recovers drafts; documentation updated.
- **Dependencies:** Area on responsiveness may own the measurement.
- **Risk:** Losing FreeCAD-native crash recovery for unmanaged documents (setting is global).

### D-14 Runtime assumptions are implicit: no fail-fast version check, deprecated exec_ alongside exec, direct shiboken6 imports, nine independent session-dir computations

**opportunity · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: preference · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `ui.start, Settings, ShortcutRouter` · lines 4311-4341 (start); exec_ in ui.py (8 sites), feature_panel.py, general_actions.py (2) vs .exec in shortcuts.py; shiboken6 imported in hover_outline.py, sketch_references.py, theme.py, toolbar_labels.py, ui.py; KURTSHAPE_SESSION_DIR read in core.py:1787, settings.py:10, shortcuts.py:44,91, ui.py:218, responsiveness.py:200 and the three workers
- **Evidence:** Bundled runtime is PySide6 6.8.3 / Python 3.11 / FreeCAD 1.1.4 (runtime/.../site-packages); code relies on Python 3.10+ (`TemporaryDirectory(ignore_cleanup_errors=True)`, `removesuffix`, `is_relative_to`), FreeCAD 1.1 element-map APIs (`UseHasher`, `_ElementMapVersion`, `getElementMapVersion`, core.py:1029-1044, 1250-1261), the Assembly workbench's JointObject module (assembly.py:49-53 already guards its path) and `App.Version()[3]` build strings (1282-1283). Nothing asserts these at startup; a different FreeCAD on PATH would fail somewhere inside a tick with a traceback into the hidden Report view.
- **Scenario:** Kurt launches with a system FreeCAD or a future 1.2 runtime; a contributor runs the FCMacro outside launch-kurtshape.ps1.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Late, confusing failures instead of one readable message; `exec_` removal in a PySide upgrade would break menus/dialogs in eleven places.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Code reading; grep counts above.
- **Proposed change:** Add `runtime_check.py` called first in start(): assert FreeCAD (1,1), OCCT 7.8, PySide6 6.8.x, Python 3.11, Assembly module path; on mismatch show one Messages/status error and refuse to install the panel. Add `session.py: session_dir()` used by all nine readers. Replace `exec_` with `exec`.
- **Acceptance:** Unit test for runtime_check with faked versions; grep shows one session-dir computation and zero exec_; startup recipe passes.
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** Low.

### D-15 Numeric and path validation is implemented four times with different limits and error codes

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: source · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/core.py` · `core.number, assembly._number, selectors._measure, ExtrusionEvaluator._proposal` · lines 53-72 (number: unit-aware, 1e6 limit, invalid_argument), 75-85 (write_path), 1922 (import path check); src/kurtshape/assembly.py:512-526 (_number: 1e7 limit, invalid_assembly_motion), 174 (source path check); src/kurtshape/selectors.py:714-719 (_measure: numbers only, invalid_selector); src/kurtshape/extrusion_preview.py:284-311 (_proposal per-key normalisation); src/kurtshape/modeling_geometry.py:39-41 (wrapper dropping unit)
- **Evidence:** `number('30 deg', ..., unit='rad')` is accepted by pad/revolve/hole paths and by move_assembly_joint (assembly accepts quantity strings too) but a selector `radius:'22 mm'` is rejected by `_measure` (714-719, int/float only); limits differ (1e6 vs 1e7); error codes differ for the same mistake (invalid_argument vs invalid_assembly_motion vs invalid_selector). `_proposal` re-lists which keys are lengths/angles per op (300-310), duplicating knowledge that the op modules own.
- **Scenario:** An agent sends quantity strings uniformly ('22 mm') across pad, selector filters and assembly moves.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Inconsistent acceptance and error codes; new ops copy whichever helper is nearest; the preview normaliser must be updated by hand when an op gains a numeric field.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Headless: dispatch resolve_selector with radius '22 mm' (invalid_selector) vs pad length '22 mm' (accepted).
- **Proposed change:** `values.py` with length(value, name, *, positive, limit), angle(...), count(...), ratio(...), read_path(...), write_path(...); every module imports these; ARGS tables gain field types so `_proposal` (and a future bridge schema) normalise generically.
- **Acceptance:** Unit matrix of inputs x validators; selector parameter tests extended with quantity strings; contract notes the shared rules.
- **Dependencies:** Pairs with the error-taxonomy table.
- **Risk:** Accepting quantity strings in selectors changes replay digests only for new requests.

### D-16 Exception-handling conventions: 48 broad catches, 26 silent passes, no logging layer, and two semantic fallbacks that silently change intent

**design weakness · severity low · confidence medium · user value medium · effort S** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: source · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.reference_from_geometry, core.native_intent, Panel.make_messages` · lines 2082-2097 (reference_from_geometry falls back to a literal reference), 772-787 (Messages mirrors the hidden Report view); src/kurtshape/core.py:192-197 (native_intent drops unreadable properties from the revision digest); grep: 48 `except Exception` sites, 26 `except ...: pass` across src
- **Evidence:** `reference_from_geometry` wraps selector authoring in `except Exception: pass` (2096-2097) and returns `{feature, subelement}`, so a fillet picked in the GUI may be stored as a literal reference that breaks on regeneration while the user believes a query was authored; `native_intent` skips a property whose getter raises (192-197), so an intermittently failing getter flips the digest (spurious revision rotation) or stable failure hides edits to that property (the preview worker explicitly refuses this tolerance, preview_worker.py:215-228). No `logging`/App.Console wrapper exists; uncaught Qt-slot exceptions reach Messages only through the Report-view mirror, with the unread counter as the sole cue.
- **Scenario:** Picking a face whose Surface type is unmapped (torus) in a studio dialog; a third-party object with a failing property.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Silent downgrade of reference quality; hard-to-diagnose revision churn; developers cannot grep one log API.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Pick a toroidal face for shell in the GUI and inspect the stored `selectors` of the feature: literal reference rather than a query.
- **Proposed change:** Add `log.py` (debug/info/warning/error -> App.Console with dedupe and optional file sink); turn semantic fallbacks into explicit outcomes: `reference_from_geometry` notifies 'stored as literal reference' and records the exception; `native_intent` records the failing property name in the digest instead of skipping it (stable but visible). Keep genuinely benign catches (icon lookup, Windows permission retries) but comment why.
- **Acceptance:** Grep shows no bare `except Exception: pass` without a comment; test that a failing property appears in native_intent output as {"<unreadable>": name}; dress-up GUI recipe asserts the notification when a literal fallback happens.
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** Changing native_intent output rotates revisions once for affected documents; acceptable since revisions are session-scoped.

## E. Responsiveness and scalability

### E-01 Native saveCopy is the single largest avoidable-feeling GUI stall and is taken by five callers; SaveBinaryBrep/CompressionLevel are untested levers

**design weakness · severity high · confidence high · user value medium · effort M** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: reproduced · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/recovery.py` · `RecoveryStore.capture / Controller.prepare_geometry_check / Controller.prepare_step_export / DebouncedExtrusionPreview._start_exact / Controller._save` · lines recovery.py:40-46; core.py:390, 632, 1839, 1898, 2798; extrusion_preview.py:371, 876; ui.py:2854-2877
- **Evidence:** recovery.py:46 `doc.saveCopy(str(stage))` on the GUI thread after the 2 s idle gate (ui.py:2861 `not self.responsiveness.is_idle()`) and 30 s/revision gate (recovery.py:40). extrusion_preview.py:876 `evaluator.document.saveCopy(str(worker.snapshot))` for the first exact preview; core.py:390/632 for check/STEP capture; core.py:2798 for Save. Only GUI measurement: 1.30 s median / 1.69 s max on Plate60, whole-run largest timer gap 1.74 s (docs/responsive-modeling-upgrade/IMPLEMENTATION.md table); 250 ms on 20 pockets; probe: 48-68 ms on 12 pockets. FreeCADApp.dll contains the preference strings `SaveBinaryBrep` and `CompressionLevel`; probe saveCopy on the 12-hole plate: default 68.0 ms, CompressionLevel=1 61.6, =0 63.1, SaveBinaryBrep 48.2 ms (reopen volume exact in all variants).
- **Scenario:** Edit a feature on a deep-history part, stop moving for ~2-3 s (recovery), or start Extrude on a big part (first exact snapshot), or press Check geometry / Export / Save.
- **What Kurt would notice:** A 1-2 s freeze that arrives shortly after Kurt pauses, and again when the Extrude dialog is opened; on larger models proportionally longer (PartDesign stores every feature's full solid, so archive size grows roughly quadratically with history depth).
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** tools/run_gui_recipe.ps1 -Recipe bench-gui -Holes 60 -Repeat 5 (reports 'Native recovery snapshot in the GUI'); -Recipe responsiveness-preview (preview_snapshot_gui). Then repeat with the isolated profile's Document preferences SaveBinaryBrep=1 and CompressionLevel in {1,3} and compare recovery_snapshot_gui/preview_snapshot_gui medians; verify archives with tools/native_validation_snapshot.py and the STEP/geometry-check child equivalence checks.
- **Proposed change:** (1) Measure and, if material, enable SaveBinaryBrep (and a lower CompressionLevel) in the app profile at start() (ui.py:4313-4317 already sets Document preferences); prove archive equivalence for `_snapshot_equivalence_state`, `canonical_brep` fingerprints and native_validation_snapshot on binary archives before adoption. (2) Paint feedback before the stall: adopt the S15 paint-marker pattern so 'Saving recovery…'/'Preparing preview…' is painted before saveCopy, and defer idle recovery while the pointer is over the 3D view and a wheel/orbit happened within the last 2 s. (3) Share own-captures: when the exact preview or a check/export captures its own archive, publish it to SnapshotStore on the file executor so the other two paths can lease it within the same accepted key. What cannot be done: moving saveCopy off the GUI thread (live document, GIL) or chunking it (single native call).
- **Acceptance:** recovery_snapshot_gui and preview_snapshot_gui medians on Plate60 reduced by a measured factor with identical strict snapshots; new native test: save with SaveBinaryBrep, reopen, strict native_validation_snapshot equality; regression gate: bench-gui 'Native recovery snapshot' median and preview 'Native snapshot on the GUI thread'.
- **Dependencies:** Measurement on Plate60/deep-history fixture; S15 private pending helper for painted feedback; native_validation_snapshot compatibility with binary BRep.
- **Risk:** Binary BRep archives are unreadable by FreeCAD < 1.0 and change byte-level fingerprints (SHA of archive) but not shape-level canonical_brep; verify selector/element-map persistence with UseHasher=false.

### E-02 Finish sketch runs the managed recompute three times; with two or more native sketch commands the dependent chain is natively recomputed twice

**verified defect · severity high · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: reproduced · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/core.py` · `Controller._dispatch (finish_sketch_edit) / Controller._commit_sketch_edit` · lines 1633-1646, 1160-1191
- **Evidence:** core.py:1638 `self._recompute(doc)` + `_build_status`, then 1642 `self._commit_sketch_edit(doc)` which, when `doc.UndoCount > editing['undo_before'] + 1 or created` (1170), undoes the native commands (1177-1178), `restore_sketch_content(sketch, final)` (1184) and calls `self._recompute(doc)` again (1186) + `_build_status`; back in the branch 1645 `self._recompute(doc)` a third time and `_new_revision` (1803-1810) runs `_build_status` once more. Restoring content re-touches the sketch, so every dependent Pad/Pocket/Boolean re-executes in the second pass.
- **Scenario:** Open the base sketch of a deep-history part (e.g., the Plate60 plate rectangle), draw or dimension two or more things, press Finish.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Finish takes roughly twice the dependent-chain recompute (Plate60 upstream recompute is 6.5-8.8 s per pass in S10/REVIEW), plus three O(objects) `_build_status` passes and selector/auto-scope traversals per `_recompute`.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** GUI recipe (sketch-lifecycle or sketch-behavior) on Plate60: enter the plate sketch, add two constraints/lines via native commands, Finish; count `native_recompute` timing events and `observer.state(doc)['recomputed_objects']` sizes per pass; expect two passes each touching all pockets. Headless: same via Controller begin_sketch_edit + two native addGeometry commands in separate transactions + finish_sketch_edit.
- **Proposed change:** Make `_commit_sketch_edit` the single recompute site when grouping will run: compute `grouping = created or doc.UndoCount > undo_before + 1` first; if grouping, skip the 1638 recompute/validation and validate once after the grouped restore (the `grouping_started` draft-preservation path already handles failure); skip the trailing 1645 `_recompute` when the previous pass left nothing touched (check `recomputed_objects`/Touched flags) or fold it into `_new_revision`. Cancel path (1143-1151) similarly runs undo+restore+recompute then 1645 again.
- **Acceptance:** Exactly one `native_recompute` event per Finish with grouped commands; Plate60 base-sketch Finish wall time about halves; all sketch history/lifecycle tests unchanged; add a native test asserting recompute count and identical final BRep/history to the current three-pass result.
- **Dependencies:** Existing sketch lifecycle tests (tests for SKETCH_CREATE_CONTENT grouping, draft preservation).
- **Risk:** Build-failed detection must still restore the draft; keep the validation semantics identical (one _build_status after the grouped recompute).

### E-03 STEP acceptance re-exports the full world BRep of every target about six times (and preparation 2-3 times) on the GUI thread

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort M** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: source · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/core.py` · `Controller.prepare_step_export / Controller._step_export_current / Controller.accept_step_export` · lines 594-646 (prepare), 669-693 (_step_export_current), 695-846 (accept), 2855-2881 (_replace_export_pair guards)
- **Evidence:** prepare: 594 `roundtrip_digest` (exportBrepToString + importBrepFromString per target), 612 `initial_world_fingerprints` (export again unless the single-target ticket), 646 `_step_export_current` -> 685 `world_fingerprints` (export again) plus 684/688 two provenance builds. accept: `current()` (700-702 -> _step_export_current) is called at 703, 806 and inside `commit_current` (809-817) which `_replace_export_pair` calls before and after each of the two renames (2876, 2880): six full exports of every target plus twelve provenance builds and six target rescans. S15 measured 522-555 ms per fingerprint pass and 929 ms roundtrip on 1000 solids; S09B measured 2.34 s launch and 3.42 s completion polling. Geometry checks, by contrast, guard with `check_key` hashCodes only (core.py:411-430).
- **Scenario:** Export STEP from a multi-part Part Studio or an imported model with many solids.
- **What Kurt would notice:** The GUI freezes for ~2 s before the child starts and ~3 s when it finishes, even though the child only needed ~1 s of real export; multi-target exports get none of the S16 reuse.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** tools/run_gui_recipe.ps1 -Recipe step-export on the retained 1000-solid S09B fixture (runtime/architecture-upgrade-working) with `world_fingerprints` wrapped by a counting patch; observe 2-3 calls in prepare and 6 in accept; time step_export_snapshot_gui and the accept call.
- **Proposed change:** Compute world fingerprints once per phase under `busy`, then guard the later checks with the pure observer fence already used by step_capture._pure_current/_require_current (states identity, instance_id/generation/change_serial, snapshot_key, document identity, `check_key` hashCode per target) instead of re-exporting BRep; extend the one-use digest ticket to multi-target (one canonical hash per target carried from roundtrip admission). Keep the child's independent archive comparison and the final independent fingerprint for the single eventful acceptance read. Cannot be threaded (native shape reads).
- **Acceptance:** Counted `world_fingerprints` calls: prepare <= 1 per target, accept 0 BRep exports when change_serial is unchanged; step_export_snapshot_gui and acceptance wall time on the 1000-solid fixture drop by the measured fingerprint phases; S17/S18 recipes pass unchanged; destination/provenance bytes identical.
- **Dependencies:** S16/S17/S18 focused tests and STEP GUI recipe as regression oracle.
- **Risk:** Reentrancy argument: native reads can pump events; mitigated because the fence compares change_serial captured before and after the single fingerprint read, which is exactly the S16 ticket discipline.

### E-04 Sketch region fill rebuild is unbounded per sketch and runs on the GUI thread after every geometry change: 1.1 s per rebuild on a 300-circle sketch

**verified defect · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: reproduced · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/sketch_regions.py` · `region_faces / SketchRegionFill._rebuild` · lines 30-75 (region_faces), 179-190 (slotChangedObject), 346-347 (timer), 371-432 (_rebuild)
- **Evidence:** sketch_regions.py:63 `Part.makeCompound(closed_edges).makeWires(...)` and 72 `Part.makeFace(wires, 'Part::FaceMakerBullseye')` over every closed wire of the sketch; 184-188 marks the entry dirty on each `Geometry`/`Shape` change; 346-347 starts the 80 ms timer; 371-432 runs the whole-sketch rebuild synchronously ('bound each turn to one sketch', not to time). Probe (headless, noisy): `region_faces` on a 300-circle sketch = 1145 ms median (300 faces), while inspect/signature/dumpContent on the same sketch were 2-7 ms. Sibling path: ProvisionalExtrusion.evaluate runs the same FaceMakerBullseye on the profile (extrusion_preview.py:175).
- **Scenario:** Drawing or dragging in a sketch with a few hundred closed entities (converted or hole-pattern sketches), or selecting a part whose dense sketches are visible.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Each placed or dragged entity is followed ~80 ms later by a freeze that grows superlinearly with region count (~1 s at 300 circles), on top of the native solver.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Build a sketch with 300 circles + 300 diameter constraints (probe_sync_paths.py section 1), make it visible/active in the GUI, add one circle, watch `sketch_region_build` in the diagnostics op or event_loop_delay; compare with 50 and 100 circles.
- **Proposed change:** (a) Do not rebuild while a native drawing tool is active (`panel.sketch_references.native_tool`) or while `panel.core.busy`; rebuild on tool end/commit and on Finish. (b) Budget: skip the fill above N closed wires or when the last build exceeded ~100 ms for that sketch (record `build_ms`, already stored per entry). (c) Incremental: group closed wires into connected components and rebuild only components whose geometry ids changed. Process isolation is possible here (pure geometry Content strings -> faces/triangles in a child) but (a)+(b) remove most of the pain. Cannot be threaded (Part calls hold the GIL).
- **Acceptance:** `sketch_region_build` p95 < 50 ms while a tool is active on the 300-circle fixture; no build events during an active Sketcher_Create* command in the presentation-scheduling recipe; existing sketch-regions checks pass.
- **Dependencies:** sketch-regions GUI recipe; SketchRegionFill counters (builds, discarded_builds, build_ms).
- **Risk:** Fill briefly absent during drawing (acceptable; it is a hint) and above the cap (document it).

### E-05 GUI-committed actions run three inspections (bridge mutations two) plus a refresh; the two warm inspections are avoidable bookkeeping

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel._operation / Panel.tick / Controller._dispatch` · lines ui.py:1326 (pre-op inspect), 1342 (dispatch), 1359 (refresh), 1245-1250 and 1266 (tick re-inspect and the only clear of the refresh flag); core.py:1731 (post-op inspect), 1558-1560 (expected_revision check); document_state.py:86
- **Evidence:** ui.py:1326 `self.core.dispatch({'op': 'inspect', ...})` before every non-create op; core.py:1731 `result = self.inspect(doc, measure)` at the end of `_dispatch`; the mutation sets the observer `refresh` flag (document_state.py:86) which only `tick` clears (ui.py:1266), so the next tick fails the early return at 1245 and dispatches another inspect (1250). S10 medians: inspect warm 14.7 ms (Plate60) / 35.2 ms (Studio), changed 38.3 / 222 ms; S11: a warm inspect still deep-copies 124 summaries and reruns `_build_status` (O(objects)).
- **Scenario:** Any set_parameter, pad confirm, undo, bridge mutation.
- **What Kurt would notice:** 30-70 ms of pure bookkeeping per action on the benchmark fixtures, scaling with object count (two warm inspections are avoidable; the changed one is needed); on the Studio fixture this is a visible share of the 963 ms non-native overhead.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Nothing attributable; the edit feels as fast or slow as the native rebuild.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** bench-gui with a counter on `core.timings.record('inspection', ...)` per `Panel.operation`: expect 3 samples per set_parameter today.
- **Proposed change:** In `_operation`, skip the pre-op inspect when `self.state['revision'] == core._meta(doc).Revision` and not `core.needs_refresh(doc)` (the controller re-validates `expected_revision`); after the op's own `refresh()`, set `observer.state(doc)['refresh'] = False` when `self.state['revision']` equals the document revision so `tick` does not re-inspect; keep the tick path for external native edits.
- **Acceptance:** One `inspection` event per committed GUI action in bench-gui (two when the op itself needs the pre-read); tip_edit.inspection_ms in bench_core unchanged; interface/part-studio recipes pass.
- **Dependencies:** None; GUI recipes assert state freshness.
- **Risk:** Missing an external native edit between op and tick — mitigated because native edits bump change_serial/refresh after the flag is cleared.
- **Adversarial check:** Read src/kurtshape/ui.py 1175-1300 (tick) and 1291-1400 (_operation), core.py 1424-1500 and 1690-1810 (_dispatch), document_state.py 80-92 (invalidate), core.py 997 (needs_refresh), core.py 1084-1104 (sync). The chain holds exactly as described for GUI-committed actions: _operation dispatches a warm inspect at ui.py:1326 for every non-create op, _dispatch ends with inspect at core.py:1731, the mutation's slotChangedObject -> invalidate sets state['refresh']=True (document_state.py:86), and the only place that clears it is Panel.tick ui.py:1266, so the next tick fails the early return at 1245 and inspects again at 1250 (it normally does not refresh again because revision/status match). git blame: lines 1245/1250/1326 date from 5f91ba2e (2026-10-02), 259a61bc (2026-10-03), 559e034d (2026-10-04); untouched by 24bab02/1f0001e/f57d592, so not fixed at HEAD. Two corrections: (1) bridge mutations bypass _operation (bridge.tick -> controller.dispatch directly), so they run two inspections, not three; (2) the S10 numbers the finder quotes are real (S10-MEASUREMENTS.md: warm 14.69/35.22 ms, changed 38.27/222.17 ms; S11: 124 summary copies and _build_status 4.3-5.8 ms per warm inspect) but the avoidable part is two warm inspections = ~29 ms of a 403 ms Plate tip edit and ~70 ms of a 1156 ms Studio tip edit (6-7%), which is not perceptible as a distinct symptom. Severity medium is inflated; the impact is a single-digit-percent bookkeeping tax that scales O(objects). The proposal is sound in principle: when needs_refresh is False, intent_dirty and native_recomputed are also False (both are only set via invalidate, which sets refresh), and _dispatch re-validates expected_revision at core.py:1558-1560, so skipping the pre-op sync/inspect cannot let a stale revision through.

### E-06 Escape during a long native recompute may abort FreeCAD's sequencer and make the error path run a second full recompute

**hypothesis · severity medium · confidence medium · user value medium · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/core.py` · `Controller._dispatch error path / Controller._recompute` · lines core.py:1750 (except Exception), 1775 (abortTransaction), 1780 (unguarded second _recompute), 1783 (_build_status), 1800 (busy released in finally); selectors.py:1624-1636 (re-raises); ui.py:184 (native status bar shown); ARCHITECTURE.md:101 (not 230)
- **Evidence:** core.py:1775 `doc.abortTransaction()` then 1780 `self._recompute(doc)` synchronously inside the exception handler, for any exception including a native abort. FreeCADApp.dll contains the 'Recompute...' SequencerLauncher strings and FreeCADGui.dll contains 'Press ESC to cancel' and `processEvents`; the extrusion_preview.py:354-355 and 737 comments confirm native loading/recompute/saveCopy pump Qt events inside KurtShape. ARCHITECTURE.md:230 states queued Escape cannot interrupt a non-yielding native call but does not cover a sequencer-aborted one.
- **Scenario:** Kurt changes an upstream dimension on a deep part (8+ s), regrets it, presses Escape.
- **What Kurt would notice:** If the abort reaches the kernel, the 'cancel' triggers abortTransaction plus another full recompute (same duration again); a second Escape inside that recompute would raise out of the except block, leaving `self.state` stale until the next tick.
- **A newcomer would notice:** An Onshape/SolidWorks user expects Escape to either do nothing or cancel cleanly; a doubled freeze followed by a cryptic error would read as a crash-like bug.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** GUI recipe on Plate60: `set_parameter` Pad Length (6-9 s), QTest keyClick Escape after 500 ms on the main window; record outcome (ok/error code), number and duration of `native_recompute` events, and whether an exception escapes `Panel._operation`.
- **Proposed change:** If confirmed: detect the native abort (exception text/type) and treat it as a deliberate cancel: abort the transaction, mark `needs_rebuild` with reason 'cancelled' instead of recomputing synchronously, notify 'Canceled — Rebuild to recompute'. If not confirmed (progress bar filters Escape), document that cancellation is impossible during commit and provide painted 'Recomputing… n features' feedback instead.
- **Acceptance:** Escape during a long edit returns control within one progress step without a second recompute; model status is needs_rebuild; Rebuild restores valid; no unhandled exception in the FreeCAD console.
- **Dependencies:** GUI recipe; FreeCAD sequencer behavior under KurtShape's app-level event filters.
- **Risk:** A half-recomputed document after abort must be left consistently Touched; the existing needs_rebuild/Rebuild path already handles that state.
- **Adversarial check:** Read core.py 1690-1810 and 862-895 (_recompute), selectors.py 1624-1676 (recompute wrapper), ui.py 170-230 (shell setup) and 4230-4275 (app event filter), ARCHITECTURE.md (126 lines; the cited line 230 does not exist, the relevant sentence is line 101). The structural half is verified from source: the handler at core.py:1750 catches every Exception; for a non-sketch transaction it calls doc.abortTransaction() (1775), closes the native transaction (1776-1779), then runs self._recompute(doc) synchronously (1780) and _build_status (1783), with no try/except around that second recompute, so a second abort propagates out of _dispatch (busy is released in finally at 1800, but dispatch's ledger.complete and Bridge.tick's reply.put are skipped). selectors.recompute (1624-1636) only times doc.recompute and re-raises. Nothing in src/kurtshape names FreeCADAbort, AbortException or Sequencer, so FreeCAD's default behaviour applies. The trigger half remains a hypothesis I could not exercise (no GUI runs allowed), but it is better grounded than the finder stated: ui.py:184 shows the native status bar, which hosts FreeCAD's progress bar; FreeCADGui.dll contains 'Press ESC to cancel' (4 occurrences) and FreeCADApp.dll 'Recompute...' plus the FreeCADAbort Python exception type (3 occurrences; 1 in FreeCADBase.dll); no 'Do you really want to abort the operation?' string exists, so a cancel would not be confirmed by a dialog. The sequencer pumps Qt events and grabs the keyboard while running on the GUI thread, which is exactly why extrusion_preview.py:354-355 and 737 warn that native recompute pumps events. Severity medium is fair if true: the user pays the full rebuild time twice, gets an 'operation_failed' error with a FreeCAD exception text, and a second Escape leaves an unhandled exception in a Qt slot (state stale for one tick, bridge caller left waiting until its 30 s timeout). Classification 'hypothesis' is honest and should stay until the GUI recipe in the finding is run.

### E-07 Switching the active document cancels an in-flight STEP export or geometry check by design, but without any notification

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.tick / Panel._cancel_step_export / Panel._cancel_geometry_check` · lines ui.py:1180-1196 (tick cancels on active-document change), 3088-3092 and 1509-1515 (context predicates require App.activeDocument() == doc), 3111-3123 and 1531-1543 (notify=False default -> restore_status only); core.py:669-694 and 411-430 (Controller predicates do not need the active document)
- **Evidence:** ui.py:1181-1183 `if geometry_document is not self._step_export_document: ... self._cancel_step_export()` and 1191-1193 for geometry checks, on every tick where `App.activeDocument()` changed; `_cancel_step_export(notify=False)` (3111) only calls `restore_status()` (3122), so no message is shown; the child is killed (3109 -> worker.close()). The acceptance itself only needs the source document open and the destination unchanged.
- **Scenario:** Kurt exports a large part (tens of seconds), clicks another tab to keep working, returns.
- **What Kurt would notice:** The export never appears and nothing says why; the same for a long geometry check. Also the exact-preview editor is cleared on switch (extrusion_preview.py:979), which is correct for an editor but confusing when combined.
- **A newcomer would notice:** An Onshape user expects an export to finish regardless of which tab is active; the file silently not appearing would look like a broken export.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** step-export GUI recipe: start an eligible export, call Gui.activateDocument on another open document before completion; observe status text, absence of the file and absence of any notification.
- **Proposed change:** Keep the job bound to its document, not to the active document: let `_step_export_context_current` accept 'document still open' and defer only the `self.state`/refresh publication until that document is active again (files and provenance do not depend on the view); at minimum notify 'STEP export canceled: switched documents' (notify=True). Same for geometry checks.
- **Acceptance:** Recipe: switching documents mid-export yields either the completed files plus a notification on return, or an explicit cancellation message; no silent restore_status.
- **Dependencies:** S17/S18 recipe cases that currently assert cancellation on switch would change to assert continuation or an explicit notification.
- **Risk:** Acceptance while another document is active must not touch `self.state` of the wrong document — publish files, store the result per document, refresh when active.
- **Adversarial check:** Read ui.py 1175-1200 (tick), 1120-1121 (Panel.document is App.activeDocument()), 1505-1545 (geometry check context/cancel), 2998-3060 (start_step_export), 3088-3160 (export context/cancel/poll), 655-690 (notify/restore_status); core.py 411-470 and 669-740 (Controller currency and acceptance predicates); tests/test_step_export_jobs.py 597-608; docs/architecture-responsiveness-upgrade/EXECUTION.md 469, 687 and STATUS_REPORT.md 48. The mechanics hold: on every tick where the active document differs from the one the job was started on, tick calls _cancel_step_export() (1181-1183) and _cancel_geometry_check() (1191-1193) with the default notify=False, which retires the job, closes the child worker (_retire_* -> worker.close()) and only calls restore_status(), so the 'Exporting STEP... Escape to cancel' text is replaced by the ordinary status and no 'canceled' message is ever shown; returning to the source document shows nothing. The Controller-level acceptance (accept_step_export core.py:695-702, _step_export_current 669-694; accept_geometry_check 432-470, _geometry_check_current 411-430) indeed does not require the active document, only identity, open state, snapshot key, topology/provenance and no active transaction, so the finder's claim about what acceptance needs is right. What does not hold is 'verified_defect': binding the UI job to the active document and cancelling on 'document switch/close' is the documented, deliberately tested lifecycle (EXECUTION.md:469 for geometry checks, :687 'Capture/document switch ... execute and pass' for STEP, S18 OSError cases after 'allocation/document switch'), and the UI poll path assumes the active document when it publishes (ui.py ~3150: self.state = invalid_state; self.refresh()). The defect-like part is the missing notification, not the cancellation. Blame: lines 1180-1196 come from 4681ddf (2026-10-13), unchanged by the three later commits, so not fixed at HEAD. Severity stays medium: a multi-second export lost with no message is a real workflow hit for a user who routinely Ctrl+Space's between a part studio and an assembly, though recovery is a single re-export.

### E-08 Every dequeued bridge request, including reads and already-cancelled ones, triggers a GUI inspect and full refresh; bridge throughput is capped at one request per 25 ms tick

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.tick / Bridge.tick` · lines ui.py:1216-1218 (processed), 1245 (early return requires not processed), 1250 (inspect), 1255-1256 (refresh), 221-223 (25 ms timer), 1178 (no dequeue while busy); bridge.py:16 (timeout), 18 (maxsize 32), 56-64 (request_status bypasses the queue), 76-79 and 90-91 (queue_full -> HTTP 400), 102-118 (one dequeue per tick, True even when cancelled)
- **Evidence:** ui.py:1218 `processed = self.bridge.tick()` is True for any request; 1245 `if self.state is not None and not processed and ...: return` so a read request falls through to 1250 `dispatch(inspect)` and 1255 `if processed or ...: self.refresh()`. bridge.py:18 queue maxsize 32 (bursts above 32 get HTTP 400 queue_full), 16 request_timeout 30 s; one `pending.get_nowait()` per tick (104).
- **Scenario:** An agent polls `inspect`/`capabilities`/`list_documents` while Kurt models, or scripts a long sequence of small operations.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Each read costs the GUI thread a warm inspect (15-35 ms on the fixtures) plus a refresh (~10 ms, icons, parameters) in the same 25 ms tick that handles Kurt's input; a 10 Hz poller keeps the GUI 30-50% busy; scripted modeling is capped at 40 requests/s even for trivial reads; a bridge mutation on a big model blocks the GUI exactly like a user action (same `dispatch`).
- **A newcomer would notice:** Nothing; the bridge is not part of the Onshape/SolidWorks-style GUI workflow.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Run tools/assistant_client.py in a loop of 200 `inspect` requests against a private session (projects/dodec-hub/start-session.ps1) while the GUI orbits via QTest; count `Panel.refresh` calls and `event_loop_delay` p95; measure requests/s.
- **Proposed change:** Refresh only when the processed request's op is not in READ_OPS or when revision/build_status/document changed (the existing predicate already covers the latter); drain up to N queued READ_OPS per tick within an ~8 ms budget and leave mutations at one per tick; report `queue_wait_ms` already present.
- **Acceptance:** 100 `inspect` requests cause 0 `Panel.refresh` calls and no `inspection` events beyond the requests themselves; read throughput > 100 req/s; mutation ordering unchanged.
- **Dependencies:** Bridge tests (tests for queue/ledger).
- **Risk:** A read that changes derived state (check_geometry is in READ_OPS and does change geometry_check) must still refresh — keep check_geometry/preview in the refresh set.
- **Adversarial check:** Read bridge.py in full (128 lines), ui.py 1175-1270 (tick), 215-230 (25 ms QTimer), core.py 247 (READ_OPS) and 1446-1476 (capabilities/request_status/list_documents early returns), tools/assistant_client.py:17, tests/test_bridge.py 205-225 and 445-461, docs/OPERATION_CONTRACT.md 104-118, EXECUTION.md 525/557 and STATUS_REPORT.md 56 (refresh timings). The mechanics hold exactly: Bridge.tick dequeues at most one request per call (bridge.py:104) and returns True whenever it dequeued anything, including a request already cancelled by its 30 s timeout (107-118; tests/test_bridge.py:459-461 asserts tick() is True with zero controller calls); Panel.tick sets processed from it (ui.py:1218), the early return at 1245 requires 'not processed', so a capabilities/inspect/list_documents/diagnostics read falls through to a GUI-thread inspect at 1250 and 'if processed or ...: self.refresh()' at 1255-1256. Queue maxsize 32 (bridge.py:18) with queue.Full -> HTTP 400 (76-79, 90-91) and request_timeout=30 (16) are as stated. Throughput: one dequeue per 25 ms tick, and zero while core.busy/preparing (1178). What is inflated is the consequence: the '10 Hz poller keeps the GUI 30-50% busy' scenario is hypothetical; tools/assistant_client.py is one-shot, request_status reads are answered on the HTTP thread without entering the queue (bridge.py:56-64, contract line 104), and Kurt's documented workflow is sequential scripted steps, where each read costs one warm inspect (15-35 ms on the fixtures) plus a refresh whose cost is unmeasured on these fixtures (S08 measured 103 ms median per refresh on a 1000-solid fixture after the icon fix, so it can exceed the finder's ~10 ms on big models). The 25 ms floor per request is invisible next to native recompute time. So the design weakness is real and cheap to fix, but its impact on actual use is low; it becomes medium only for a client that polls reads continuously. Not changed by any commit since 2026-10-03 (blame 259a61bc for line 1245).

### E-09 Exact preview reloads the clone and runs a whole-model display boolean per proposal; Confirm waits on it (7.4 s first / 6.2 s update on Plate60)

**opportunity · severity medium · confidence medium · user value high · effort L** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/extrusion_preview.py` · `ExtrusionEvaluator.evaluate` · lines extrusion_preview.py:383 (clone load), 401 and 441 (before/after whole-body shape copies), 462 and 472-473 (display boolean), 191-196 (_world_shape copy); preview_worker.py:315; ui.py:2403, 3743 (Confirm gating)
- **Evidence:** extrusion_preview.py:383 `_load_preview_clone(...)` per uncached proposal; 401/441 `_aggregate_shape(clone)`/`_world_shape(body)` before and after; 472-473 `previous.cut(shape)` or `shape.cut(previous)` to derive the displayed material. Probe phases on a 12-hole plate (noisy): first 296 ms = snapshot 66 + load 57 + model 58 + material 102; update 235 ms = load 62 + model 59 + material 102 (recompute 1 ms). S10 Plate60: preview_first 7.4 s, preview_update 6.2 s (headless evaluator). The worker reuses one process but still reloads the clone per evaluation (preview_worker.py:315 -> evaluate).
- **Scenario:** Typing depths in Extrude on a deep part; Confirm stays disabled until the exact result arrives.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Whole-action latency to an enabled Confirm of several seconds per depth change on Plate60-class parts even though the GUI stays responsive; the material boolean alone is a full-model cut done purely for display.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Compared with Onshape's near-instant extrude preview they see a 'Provisional preview · checking exact result…' banner and a greyed Confirm after every edit on anything beyond a simple part; on small parts (~250 ms) they will not notice.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** bench_core.py --fixture plate --holes 60 --sections preview, then read `preview_phase_*` medians from `core.timings` (bench_core currently drops them, see the harness finding) to attribute load vs model vs material.
- **Proposed change:** (1) For pad/pocket (and edit_extrusion) take the displayed material from the created PartDesign feature's `AddSubShape` (world-placed) instead of `previous.cut(shape)`/`shape.cut(previous)`; keep the boolean path for studio-scoped operations where the aggregate matters. (2) Retain the loaded clone between proposals of the same (profile, op, body) and apply the new depth as a forward parameter edit on the preview feature followed by recompute — a forward edit, not the transaction-abort reset that S05 rejected; fall back to a fresh clone whenever proposal structure or the source generation changes. Use the existing fresh-clone evaluation as the oracle in tests.
- **Acceptance:** `preview_worker_phase_material` near 0 for pad/pocket with identical material volume (1e-7) to the boolean path; preview_update_ms on Plate60 reduced by load+material phases; exact-preview tests and recipe pass.
- **Dependencies:** Preview native tests (11 focused) and extrusion-preview GUI recipe; S05/S06 counterexamples as regression inputs.
- **Risk:** Retained-clone drift (Touched flags, element maps) — mitigate by comparing material volume/BRep against a fresh-clone evaluation in tests and by resetting on any mismatch.
- **Adversarial check:** Read src/kurtshape/extrusion_preview.py:340-520 (ExtrusionEvaluator.evaluate). Per uncached proposal it really does: `_load_preview_clone` at 383 (App.openDocument / clone.load of the FCStd snapshot), `_world_shape(body)` (a full Body.Shape.copy, lines 191-196) before the model at 401 and after it at 441, then the display boolean `previous.cut(shape)` / `shape.cut(previous)` at 472-473 (462 for edit_dress_up), with an 8-entry LRU keyed by the proposal JSON and the clone closed in `finally`. preview_worker.py:315 calls `evaluator.evaluate` per request inside the resident worker process, so process reuse without clone reuse holds. Confirm gating holds: ui.py:2403 `dialog.set_accept_enabled(valid)` and ui.py:3743 `set_accept_enabled(bool(result and result.valid))`; provisional results are built with provisional=True and are not valid (extrusion_preview.py:186, ui.py:3756), so Confirm stays disabled until the exact worker answers. The cited S10 numbers are real: docs/architecture-responsiveness-upgrade/S10-MEASUREMENTS.md rows 75-76 give plate60 preview_first 7377.72 ms and preview_update 6226.69 ms (final medians); the review's own HEAD benchmark (docs/fable-deep-review/evidence/controller-benchmarks-2026-10-17.md:17-18) shows 8657-8715 / 7186-7192 ms under noise. The 12-hole phase split (snapshot 66 + load 57 + model 58 + material 102 of 296 ms) is the finder's own probe and was not reproduced here, but the `_preview_phase` labels it reads from exist in the code. Nothing at HEAD solves this: EXECUTION.md:331-366 records S05 assessing transaction-abort as a resident-clone reset and closing it as no-go (native Touched state persists after abort); a forward parameter edit was never assessed and 'resident-clone reset assessment' is still listed as deferred (EXECUTION.md:184, 217, 292, 329). What did not fully hold: proposal (1) is not semantically free. AddSubShape is the tool prism, so for a pad or pocket whose prism overlaps existing material it differs from the current net material (`shape.cut(previous)` / `previous.cut(shape)`); the after-shape copy is also still needed for the facts and the outline mesh, so only the before-copy and the boolean disappear. Proposal (2) carries the real risk and cost given the strict equivalence guards this codebase already demands (S01/S05 history), so effort M is understated.

### E-10 Exact-preview mesh transport is JSON parsed and flattened on the GUI thread at a fixed 0.15 mm deflection; the 8-entry exact cache is bounded by count, so large curved parts cost 0.4-1.8 s per exact result and up to ~2 GB resident

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort M** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/preview_worker.py` · `NativePreviewWorker.poll / render_mesh / CoinExtrusionOverlay.show / DebouncedExtrusionPreview._poll` · lines preview_worker.py:113-137 (json.loads at 119); extrusion_preview.py:127-139, 592-594, 664-665, 715-728, 957-960, 1014; hover_outline.py:37-39
- **Evidence:** preview_worker.py:119 `json.loads(path.read_text(...))` in the GUI poll; render_mesh caps at 500,000 vertices / 1,000,000 triangles (132-133) with absolute deflection 0.15 mm regardless of model size; overlay builds Python index lists (593); `exact_cache` keeps up to 8 full meshes (957-960). Probe: 3,032 vertices -> 287 KB JSON, json.loads 4.3 ms; linear scaling implies ~50 MB and ~0.7-1 s per result at the cap, and ~0.5 GB for eight cached meshes.
- **Scenario:** Exact preview on a large or curved part (fillets, patterns) where 0.15 mm tessellation produces hundreds of thousands of triangles.
- **What Kurt would notice:** A sub-second GUI stall when each exact result arrives and large resident memory in the GUI process; the Coin upload of a 1M-triangle overlay also stalls.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Onshape/SolidWorks previews do not hitch on each parameter edit; on larger parts the exact-ghost stall reads as sluggishness, on small parts nothing.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** responsiveness-preview recipe on a part with a filleted pattern; read `preview_ipc` and `preview_overlay` timings and GUI process working set across 8 proposals.
- **Proposed change:** Scale display deflection with model diagonal (e.g., 0.0005 x diagonal, min 0.05 mm) as hover_outline already does (hover_outline.py:38-39); transport vertices/triangles as binary float32/int32 files (struct/array) and feed `SoCoordinate3.point.setValues`/`coordIndex.setValues` from flat sequences; bound `exact_cache` by total bytes.
- **Acceptance:** `preview_ipc` p95 < 20 ms and overlay < 50 ms on a 200k-triangle fixture; cache memory bound test; existing mesh-reuse checks pass.
- **Dependencies:** Preview tests (mesh reuse checks).
- **Risk:** Coarser display mesh is visibly different on small features — keep a minimum deflection.
- **Adversarial check:** Read at HEAD f57d592. src/kurtshape/preview_worker.py:113-137 NativePreviewWorker.poll does json.loads(path.read_text()) and it is driven from DebouncedExtrusionPreview._poll on a 20 ms QTimer (extrusion_preview.py:664-665, 905), i.e. the GUI thread. render_mesh (extrusion_preview.py:127-139, not 127-141) tessellates at an absolute 0.15 mm, discretizes outline edges at 0.15 mm and only refuses above 500,000 vertices / 1,000,000 triangles; it serializes vertices as 3-float tuples. CoinExtrusionOverlay.show flattens the triangle list in Python (592-594) and feeds pivy setValues from Python lists; the exact mesh replaces the already-visible provisional on arrival (_display 715-728). exact_cache keeps up to 8 ExtrusionResult objects each holding the parsed mesh (957-960) and survives idle() for the whole feature-dialog session (clear() at 1014 drops it). hover_outline.py:37-39 already scales deflection by max(diagonal*0.0005, 0.01), so the proposed precedent is real. What held: every cited mechanism. What did not hold: the finder's numbers are underestimates. A pure-Python probe under the bundled pythonw 3.11 (no FreeCAD, scratch only) reproduced the 3k-vertex figure (4.5 ms json.loads vs the finder's 4.3 ms) and showed superlinear growth: 50k verts/100k tris = 5.4 MB JSON, 92 ms loads + 20 ms index flatten, 27 MB resident; 150k/300k = 16.8 MB, 401 + 67 ms, 81 MB; at the cap 500k/1M = 57 MB JSON, 1553 + 214 ms, 269 MB resident per parsed mesh. So a cap-size result freezes the GUI ~1.8 s before the pivy upload (not 0.7-1 s), and eight cached meshes can reach ~2 GB of GUI-process memory (not ~0.5 GB). What is unverified: how often real models reach 100k+ triangles at 0.15 mm absolute deflection; prismatic parts with holes stay well under 50k (<~120 ms stall), so this bites on filleted/patterned/large curved parts only. No test under tests/ exercises preview_too_large or mesh size, and no doc records the JSON transport as a deliberate decision. Not fixed at HEAD (git log for both files: last touched 3df4faa/659b5e7, transport unchanged since cb96ddf).

### E-11 Every refresh rasterizes a new grayscale Part icon per body into a never-evicted cache (~217 KB and ~1.5 ms per body per refresh) and re-sets every feature-row icon

**hypothesis · severity medium · confidence high · user value high · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/presentation_icons.py` · `grayscale_icon / icon_for / Panel.refresh / HistoryModel.reconcile` · lines presentation_icons.py:20-48 (no 'part' label), 72-73 (unbounded dicts), 107-118 (cacheKey keying + 36 rasterizations), 132-150, 153-156 (clear_cache has no callers); ui.py:1698, 1726, 1737, 1796, 1813; history_model.py:49-51
- **Evidence:** ui.py:1737 `icon_for('Part', native=body.ViewObject.Icon)`; 'part' is not in LABEL_ICONS (20-48) so icon_for falls to `grayscale_icon(native)` (147) which caches by `int(native.cacheKey())` (107) into the unbounded `_native_cache` (117) after rasterizing 9 sizes x 4 modes (RASTER_SIZES, 110-116). Probe under the bundled Qt: two QIcons built from the same QPixmap have different cacheKeys; a copy-constructed QIcon shares the key. FreeCAD's `ViewObject.Icon` wraps a fresh QIcon per call, so (if confirmed) every refresh rasterizes anew and adds one entry per body. Feature rows use `obj.ViewObject.Icon` directly (1698) and reconcile compares cacheKeys (history_model.py:50-51), so `setIcon` runs on every row every refresh. S08 measured ~1.7 ms per icon evaluation (1003 evaluations = 1.8 s).
- **Scenario:** Any refresh (every operation, status change, bridge request, document switch) over a session of hours; multi-part studios multiply it.
- **What Kurt would notice:** A few ms of rasterization per body per refresh, QTreeWidget relayout from per-row setIcon, and a slow unbounded memory leak of QIcon pixmap sets (tens to hundreds of KB per body per refresh) in a process Kurt keeps open for days.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Nothing in a short session; the leak is invisible until memory pressure in a long one.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** In the bench-gui or parts-refresh recipe: record `len(presentation_icons._native_cache)` and count `QTreeWidgetItem.setIcon` calls before/after 50 unchanged `panel.refresh()` calls on a 4-part studio; also check `body.ViewObject.Icon.cacheKey()` twice in a row.
- **Proposed change:** Key the native grayscale cache by a stable signature (object TypeId + view-provider icon pixmap image hash, or `icon.pixmap(20).toImage()` bytes) and bound it; in refresh pass a row 'icon signature' (type, failed, held, visible) and only call setIcon when it changes; prefer original art for 'Part' via LABEL_ICONS to skip the native path entirely.
- **Acceptance:** `_native_cache` length constant across refreshes; zero setIcon calls on an unchanged refresh; refresh median in bench-gui equal or lower; icons visually unchanged in the recipe screenshots.
- **Dependencies:** parts-refresh GUI recipe (S08) can host the assertions.
- **Risk:** Icon staleness if a view provider legitimately changes its icon (e.g., error overlay) — include the native State flags in the signature.
- **Adversarial check:** Code reading confirmed every cited anchor: presentation_icons.py:17 RASTER_SIZES, 20-48 LABEL_ICONS has no 'part' entry (only 'new part'), 72-73 `_original_cache`/`_native_cache` are plain unbounded dicts, 103-118 grayscale_icon keys by `int(native.cacheKey())` and rasterizes 9 sizes x 4 modes, 132-150 icon_for falls through to grayscale_icon for an unmapped label, and `clear_cache()` (153-156) has no caller anywhere in src/. ui.py:1726 and 1737 call `icon_for('Part', native=body.ViewObject.Icon)` per studio part / per non-empty native body on every Panel.refresh (also 1813 for assembly instances); feature rows pass `obj.ViewObject.Icon` straight through at 1698 and 1796, and history_model.py:49-51 compares `cacheKey()` before `setIcon`. The finder's open question ('if confirmed') was settled with an isolated offscreen probe (bundled pythonw, QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen, private FREECAD_USER_HOME/APPDATA/TEMP under the scratch folder, Gui.showMainWindow, 3.2 s total): `body.ViewObject.Icon.cacheKey()` returned 5 distinct keys in 5 calls, `pad.ViewObject.Icon` 3 of 3 distinct, `Gui.getIcon('PartDesign_Body')` 3 of 3 distinct, while a copy-constructed QIcon shares its key and the same Python object is stable. `original_icon_name('Part')` is None. Fifty `icon_for('Part', native=vo.Icon)` calls took 77 ms and left 50 entries in `_native_cache` (1.5 ms and 1 entry when the same QIcon is reused); each entry holds 28 pixmaps from 20 to 64 px, about 217 KB. A QTreeWidgetItem's stored icon key never equals a freshly read `ViewObject.Icon` key, so `setIcon` runs for every feature row every refresh (the compare+setIcon itself is cheap, ~9 us per row detached; the 'relayout' wording is an overstatement, it is a per-row dataChanged/update). Refresh frequency is one per model-changing event, not per bridge read: ui.py:1359 `_operation` refreshes after every GUI operation and `tick` (1250-1256) refreshes when the revision, document or build status changes; read-only bridge requests do not trigger it. S08 (EXECUTION.md:551-562) already cut the per-solid icon calls to one per body, so the remaining growth is one ~217 KB entry per body/part/instance per refresh, unbounded for the life of the process. Not fixed at HEAD: presentation_icons.py last changed in 4681ddf.

### E-12 Stall diagnostics are scoped to Sketcher_Create* tools by design; outside sketching the app retains only ~13 s of event-loop samples and no record of what was running during a stall

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort M** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/responsiveness.py` · `SketchStallDiagnostics.command / SketchStallDiagnostics.pulse / Timings / Panel.feedback_complete` · lines responsiveness.py:99-101, 117-143 (124 gate), 160-165, 200-202, 212-222, 302-308; timing.py:10; core.py:1461-1463; ui.py:2879-2888; tests/test_responsiveness.py:56-74; docs/sketch-interaction-reliability/IMPLEMENTATION.md:27
- **Evidence:** responsiveness.py:101 `re.fullmatch(r'Sketcher_Create[A-Za-z0-9_]{1,64}', value)` and 124 `if delay >= self.threshold_ms and stalled_command:` — a 3 s stall during an extrude confirm or recovery capture writes nothing; `event_loop_delay` lands in a 512-sample deque (timing.py:10) = the last 12.8 s at 25 ms; ui.py:2887 `input_to_feedback` is recorded when `refresh()` finishes, not at paint/swap (the Monitor marks `paint_event`/`frame_swapped` separately but only for the active QOpenGLWidget). Native progress-bar event pumping can keep the 25 ms pulse alive during a recompute, so a long native stall may not even appear as event-loop delay. On Kurt's machine `runtime/diagnostics/` does not exist (no record ever written).
- **Scenario:** Kurt reports 'it froze for a few seconds' after the fact.
- **What Kurt would notice:** The lead cannot attribute real-session stalls to recovery capture, commit recompute, STEP admission or sketch fill from the running app; measurement must be reconstructed in recipes.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Nothing; diagnostics are invisible to them.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Call `{'op':'diagnostics'}` on a session after a known 2 s stall: event_loop_delay worst shows it only if within the last ~13 s; no stall record exists unless a sketch tool was active.
- **Proposed change:** Record any pulse delay >= 100 ms with the current command (InteractionTrace.current kind/name), active document object count, phase and the last Timings label started (saveCopy/recompute/fingerprint wrappers), into the bounded JSONL writer for all contexts; replace the fixed deque with bounded per-label histograms plus a retained 'worst 32 stalls' list; make `input_to_frame_swapped` the headline latency; expose counts in `diagnostics`.
- **Acceptance:** A GUI recipe injecting a 300 ms sleep inside `Controller._recompute` produces one stall record naming the operation; diagnostics report shows the stall after 60 s of idle ticks.
- **Dependencies:** BoundedJsonlWriter already handles disk bounds; tests/test_responsiveness.py.
- **Risk:** Privacy of record contents (keep plain values, no paths/labels) as the existing writer does.
- **Adversarial check:** All factual claims hold at HEAD. responsiveness.py:99-101 `command()` accepts only `Sketcher_Create[A-Za-z0-9_]{1,64}`; pulse() at 117-143 writes a record only when `delay >= threshold_ms and stalled_command` (124), so a 3 s stall during an extrude confirm, recovery capture or STEP admission writes nothing, and the test suite pins that as intended (tests/test_responsiveness.py:56 `..._nonsketch_stalls_create_no_file`). The design doc confirms the deliberate scope: docs/sketch-interaction-reliability/IMPLEMENTATION.md:27 ('Sketch event-loop pauses over 500 ms now produce bounded local diagnostics at runtime/diagnostics/sketch-stalls.jsonl'). timing.py:10 keeps a 512-sample deque per label; the Monitor records `event_loop_delay` every 25 ms pulse (responsiveness.py:212, 222) so the window is ~12.8 s, and the `diagnostics` op (core.py:1461-1463) returns only `timings.report()` medians/p95/worst plus engine and recovery facts, nothing about which command or phase was active. ui.py:2879-2888 records `input_to_feedback` when refresh() finishes and its own comment calls it a legacy metric that is not a presented frame; paint_event/frame_swapped are marked only for the active QOpenGLWidget (responsiveness.py:302-308, 329-334). The live session dir is the repo runtime folder (launch-kurtshape.ps1:5,11,46) and `ls runtime` shows no `diagnostics` directory, so no stall record has ever been written there. One nuance: `input_to_frame_swapped` already exists as a Timings label via InteractionTrace.mark (responsiveness.py:160-165, 308), so making it the headline is a reporting change, not new instrumentation. The native progress-bar event-pumping caveat is plausible (FreeCAD's sequencer processes events during long recomputes) but was not verified here. Classification design_weakness is honest: this is a deliberate, tested scope decision whose cost is diagnosability, not a defect. Severity is lower than medium because nothing the user does is affected; the impact is on how long each 'it froze' report takes to attribute.

### E-13 Save verifies the staged archive (testzip) and copies the recovery file synchronously on the GUI thread; STL and conservative STEP exports run BRepCheck, meshing, sha256 and full measurements synchronously too, a documented and deliberately deferred limitation

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value low · effort M** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/core.py` · `Controller._save / Controller._export` · lines core.py:2793-2811, 2813-2853, 1731-1744; ui.py:2968-2984, 3041-3078 (fallback with WaitCursor), 1218-1222 + bridge.py:102-116 (GUI-thread dispatch); recovery.py:64-84 (existing background pattern)
- **Evidence:** core.py:2798 `doc.saveCopy(stage)` then 2801-2803 `zipfile.ZipFile(stage).testzip()` (decompresses the whole archive), 2806 `shutil.copy2(path, .recovery.FCStd)` and 2807 `os.replace`; `_export` 2845 `hashlib.sha256(stage.read_bytes())`, 2823 BRepCheck per target (`check(obj, compute=True)`), 2846 `_geometry(..., 'full')` — all before `inspect` (1731) and `Modified=False` (1744).
- **Scenario:** Ctrl+S on a large project; STL export; STEP export that falls to the synchronous path (clean metadata, Link sources, assemblies).
- **What Kurt would notice:** The freeze after Save is saveCopy plus a full decompress-and-CRC pass plus a file copy; for a 50 MB archive that is hundreds of ms of avoidable GUI time; STL export adds a full-model BRepCheck and tessellation synchronously with no painted feedback.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Nothing beyond ordinary desktop-CAD Save/Export hitching; Onshape users miss continuous saving but that is a different feature.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Time `command_save` minus `recovery_snapshot_gui`-class saveCopy on Plate60 via bench-gui; patch `zipfile.ZipFile.testzip` with a timer.
- **Proposed change:** Paint 'Saving…' first (S15 pattern); run testzip/sha/copy on the recovery file executor and report completion via the existing poll (keeping saveCopy synchronous); for STL export reuse the STEP child pattern (the STL writer only needs the detached world shapes already serialized in the snapshot).
- **Acceptance:** `command_save` - saveCopy duration <= 5 ms on Plate60; save/export recipes pass; failure injection into testzip leaves the previous file and shows the error.
- **Dependencies:** Save contract (success reported only after verification) must remain: report 'Saved' only when the executor finishes; the GUI Modified flag reset moves to that completion.
- **Risk:** A failed verification after the user has continued editing — keep the staged file and show an error; the previous file is untouched until replace.
- **Adversarial check:** Read at HEAD. src/kurtshape/core.py:2793-2811 Controller._save: saveCopy to a .saving.FCStd stage (2798), zipfile.testzip over the whole archive (2800-2803), shutil.copy2 of the previous file to .recovery.FCStd (2804-2806), os.replace (2807); all synchronous, no feedback. _export 2813-2853: BRepCheck per target with compute=True (2823), world-shape compound, STL meshing at 0.05 mm / 0.1 rad or exportStep, sha256 over stage.read_bytes() (2845), _geometry(..., 'full') (2846; volume/area/bbox/COM, memoized at core.py:962-979), then the sidecar and pair replacement. inspect runs at 1731 and Modified=False at 1744 only afterwards. Dispatch happens on the GUI thread for both the UI (ui.py:2984 operation('save'), no task_feedback and no wait cursor) and for bridge requests (bridge.py:102-116 tick runs controller.dispatch inside panel.tick, ui.py:1218-1222). The STEP child path exists (prepare_step_export, core.py:531-605) but falls back to the synchronous _export for STL, busy, unmanaged, active transaction, assemblies, no local dependencies, non-valid build, clean (non-Touched) metadata, no targets, App::Link sources and unstable roundtrips (ui.py:3041-3043 _step_export_fallback), matching the finder's list. One inaccuracy: the fallback does show feedback: QApplication.setOverrideCursor(WaitCursor) (ui.py:3061) is applied immediately even though the 'Exporting…' label text is set without a repaint; Save has no feedback at all. Magnitude is an extrapolation, not a measurement: testzip + copy2 on the typical few-MB project is tens of ms and saveCopy dominates; the 'hundreds of ms at 50 MB' claim is plausible but unmeasured, and geometry.check is memoized so the BRepCheck may be a cache hit. Already documented as a deliberately deferred gap: docs/engine-responsiveness-review/REVIEW.md Finding 6 ('Save, import and export also remain synchronous'); docs/architecture-responsiveness-upgrade/EXECUTION.md lines 33, 150, 213, 469, 589-597 ('Keep Save unchanged', 'Explicit Save was not modified', 'STL and conservative contexts retain synchronous fallback'). The recovery module already runs testzip/sha/copy/replace on a one-thread executor (recovery.py:64-84 _publish_cached), so the proposed pattern exists in-tree. tests/test_core.py:60-62 pins the .recovery.FCStd copy but not synchronous timing.

### E-14 Managed rebuild re-resolves every selector consumer and recomputes object-by-object across the whole document per edit; studio tip-edit overhead (~1.0-1.3 s) is 5-6x the kernel time and grew with HEAD's query features

**opportunity · severity medium · confidence high · user value high · effort L** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/selectors.py` · `selectors.recompute / Controller._recompute / studio._scope_parts_full / studio.sync_auto_scopes` · lines selectors.py:1580-1600 (_refresh_feature_impl re-resolves every input), 1624-1672 (recompute: 1666-1668 per-object refresh + doc.recompute([obj]), 1670-1671 whole-document walk); core.py:867-895 (879-885 scope loop, worst case len(order)+1); studio.py:839-851, 854-925 (859-860 all-domain token key), 928-1057; ARCHITECTURE.md:53
- **Evidence:** selectors.py:1656-1671 rebuilds every object (`for obj in doc.Objects: rebuild(obj)`) with `_refresh_feature` for each consumer and `doc.recompute([obj])` per object when consumers exist; core.py:879-885 loops `sync_auto_scopes` + `prepare_recompute` + another native pass up to len(order)+1 times; `_scope_parts_full` (839-851) does pairwise `_pair_matches` (bbox then exact common volume) for every part x tool. S10 Studio: tip_edit total 1156 ms of which native 193 ms, overhead 963 ms; selector evaluation 367 ms in Q00b. Since 5e9824f selectors.py grew by 497 lines (parameter filters, multi-edge fillet/chamfer references re-evaluated at each managed rebuild) and d95cc2e made studio booleans exact.
- **Scenario:** Editing a dimension in a multi-part studio with query-backed fillets, patterns and auto-scoped cuts.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Edit latency dominated by bookkeeping rather than the kernel on multi-part models; growth with (consumers x objects) and (parts x tools).
- **A newcomer would notice:** A visible pause after each dimension edit in a multi-part studio that Onshape or SolidWorks would do nearly instantly for an unrelated feature; on single-body parts the overhead is ~50-90 ms and they would not notice.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** bench_core.py --fixture studio --repeat 5 at HEAD (lead); compare tip_edit.overhead_ms, selector_evaluation_ms, auto_scope_sync_ms with S10; tools/measure_selector_rebuild.py.
- **Proposed change:** Affected-consumer scheduling: refresh only consumers whose dependency versions (observer per-object versions already maintained in document_state.py:40-48) changed since their last refresh, and keep the full traversal as an oracle under a flag; cache auto-scope membership per (operation, part-version, tool-version) instead of the all-domain token so an unrelated edit does not evict it. ARCHITECTURE.md explicitly records affected-consumer scheduling as not implemented.
- **Acceptance:** Studio tip_edit.overhead_ms reduced with byte-identical selector resolutions versus the oracle across the U2 fixtures; reference_errors unchanged; S06 counterexamples still rejected.
- **Dependencies:** S06 ordering counterexamples; `_resolve_full` oracle; U2 selector tests.
- **Risk:** Missed dependency through global/creator/owner selectors (ARCHITECTURE notes QuerySources/OutList cannot prove complete dependencies) — keep fail-closed full refresh when any query kind lacks a provable dependency set.
- **Adversarial check:** selectors.py:1624-1672 `recompute(doc)` is exactly as described: inside a transaction it walks `for obj in doc.Objects: rebuild(obj)` (1670-1671), calls `_refresh_feature` for every active consumer (1666-1667), and `native([obj])` i.e. `doc.recompute([obj])` for every object, consumer or not (1668), so the per-object native calls are O(objects) per managed rebuild regardless of what changed; `_refresh_feature_impl` (1580-1600) re-resolves every selector input of every consumer each time. core.py:867-895 `_recompute` runs prepare_recompute + a native pass and then loops sync_auto_scopes/prepare_recompute/native pass; the loop breaks as soon as sync_auto_scopes returns False, so steady state is one extra scope check and `len(order)+1` is the worst-case bound, not the typical count. studio.py:839-851 `_scope_parts_full` does `_pair_matches` for every part x tool, and `_scope_parts` (854-925) memoizes only under a token over every document_index domain (859-860), so any edit evicts every cached membership. The measurements are real and in-repo: S10-MEASUREMENTS.md rows 85-87 give the studio tip_edit final medians total 1155.78 / native 192.98 / overhead 962.80 ms (the finder's 1156/193/963); Q24b and STATUS.md record Q00b selector evaluation 367.172 ms. ARCHITECTURE.md:53 states verbatim that 'affected-consumer scheduling is not implemented' and ARCHITECTURE.md:122 that the S12 summary-reuse probe never reached production. `git diff --stat 5e9824f..HEAD -- src/kurtshape/selectors.py` is +497 lines (parameter filters, group/multi-edge references re-evaluated via `_refresh_group` each rebuild) and d95cc2e made studio booleans exact; the three HEAD commit messages cite test counts only, no benchmark numbers, so 'without new measurements' is true of the commits.
