One correction: the review itself measured HEAD (docs/fable-deep-review/evidence/controller-benchmarks-2026-10-17.md rows 7-8, 24-25): studio tip_edit total 1343-1463 ms, overhead 1190-1298 ms, tip_edit.selector_evaluation 652-711 ms and auto_scope_sync 444-491 ms at f57d592, noisy but consistent with growth over S10's 963 ms overhead, and showing auto-scope sync is a larger share than the Q24b 20 ms figure suggests. The finding is better classified as a design weakness: a documented architectural gap with a measured user-facing cost (overhead 5-6x the kernel time on studio edits), not merely a possible improvement.

### E-15 Monitor.active_view_key walks the active subwindow's widget subtree per frame swap/paint; sketch fills and sketch history re-read placements/visibility on a 0.5 s reconciliation, not per 25 ms tick

**opportunity · severity low · confidence high · user value low · effort S** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: preference · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/responsiveness.py` · `Monitor.active_view_key / Monitor.frame_swapped / SketchRegionFill.apply / HistoricalSketchView.apply` · lines responsiveness.py:232-240, 242-250, 302-307, 329-334; sketch_regions.py:304-311 (early return), 331-343 (placement read on presentation passes and the 0.5 s reconcile); sketch_history.py:118-139; ui.py:222-223 (25 ms tick)
- **Evidence:** responsiveness.py:246 `for widget in window.findChildren(QtGui.QWidget)` runs in `active_view_key()` which is called from `ui_updated`, `frame_swapped` (306) and every QOpenGLWidget paint event (332) — a widget-tree walk per frame during orbit; sketch_regions.py:333 `sketch.getGlobalPlacement()` for every visible sketch on every 25 ms tick; sketch_history.py:129-139 iterates all objects reading ViewObject.Visibility every 0.5 s while a sketch is edited.
- **Scenario:** Orbiting at 60 fps; models with many visible sketches.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Small but constant input-thread cost (sub-ms per frame today; grows with widget count and visible sketches); not a measured frame drop.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Nothing.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** bench-gui 'Unchanged idle tick' (0.020 ms for 20 pockets) with 100 visible sketches; time `Monitor.eventFilter` for Paint events during a QTest orbit.
- **Proposed change:** Cache the active view key per QMdiSubWindow (invalidate on subWindowActivated/destroyed) instead of findChildren per event; in sketch_regions compare a cheap placement token only when the observer reported Placement/Group/Tip changes (already tracked via slotChangedObject 189) rather than per tick.
- **Acceptance:** Paint-event filter cost < 20 µs; unchanged idle tick stays <= 0.05 ms with 100 visible sketches.
- **Dependencies:** presentation-scheduling recipe counters.
- **Risk:** None material.
- **Adversarial check:** Read at HEAD. responsiveness.py:242-250 Monitor.active_view_key does window.findChildren(QtGui.QWidget) on the active QMdiSubWindow and returns at the first visible QOpenGLWidget; it is called from ui_updated (232-240, only on feedback_complete/notify), frame_swapped (302-307, once per swap) and the eventFilter Paint/Show branch (329-334, only when the watched widget is itself a QOpenGLWidget). That part holds, but the walk covers only the active 3D subwindow's handful of widgets and stops early, so it is sub-ms and does not grow with model size; the finder itself says it is not a measured frame drop. The sketch_regions claim is wrong on frequency: SketchRegionFill.apply (sketch_regions.py:304-311) returns before any sketch loop unless presentation_dirty or membership_dirty, and those are set by observer slots (slotChangedObject 179-189) or by the 0.5 s safety reconciliation (305-310, which also rescans doc.Objects for sketches). sketch.getGlobalPlacement() (331-343) therefore runs for each visible sketch about twice a second and on changes, not on every 25 ms tick (ui.py:222-223 ticks at 25 ms). sketch_history.apply (118-139) does iterate self.desired reading ViewObject.Visibility every 0.5 s while a sketch is edited, as stated. docs/responsive-modeling-upgrade/IMPLEMENTATION.md:55 records the unchanged idle tick at 0.020 ms for 20 pockets; no test or report shows any of this reaching frame time. No change since 4681ddf/a558615. Net: a true but minor micro-optimization with one overstated sub-claim; honest classification is opportunity, not a design weakness.

### E-16 Benchmark harness gaps (all already documented): headless preview phases not exported, studio budget inert under measure=false, regression gate never fails on absolute misses, inert 50 ms synchronous preview target, and no dense/assembly/imported/cold fixtures or input-to-paint budget

**opportunity · severity low · confidence high · user value low · effort M** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: preference · verified

- **Source:** `tools/perf/bench_core.py` · `Benchmark.preview / evaluate_budgets` · lines bench_core.py:138-161, 335-358, 417-427; report.py:22-32, 44-56; budgets.json:6-39, 41-50; bench_gui.FCMacro:133 (controller_timings dump); responsiveness.py:164-178 (existing input_to_* metrics)
- **Evidence:** bench_core.py:335-358 records only first/update totals although `ExtrusionEvaluator.evaluate` records `preview_phase_*` into core.timings (extrusion_preview.py:523-524); report.py:49-52 fails only when `current > target` AND `> best*1.2+5` on two independent runs (an unmet absolute target alone never fails, as REVIEW.md Finding 7 noted); budgets.json studio budget requires measurement_level true and a Q00b-era 160-object baseline, so `--measure false` (the S10 policy) returns 'needs a recorded Q03 median' (report.py:28-32); phase1 `preview_first_ms: 50` targets the synchronous clone evaluator, which the architecture made irrelevant (the async adapter's provisional is the user-facing latency).
- **Scenario:** The lead reruns benchmarks on a quiet machine to decide priorities.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Results cannot attribute preview time to load/model/material, studio budgets silently do not apply, and the only user-facing latency (input to first painted provisional/exact) is not in any budget.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Nothing.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Run bench_core.py --fixture studio --measure false and inspect `budgets.reason`; inspect a report's timings keys for preview_phase_*.
- **Proposed change:** Export per-phase medians from captured `preview_phase_*`/`preview_worker_phase_*` events in Benchmark.capture; add fixtures: dense sketch (300 circles), 120-hole plate (archive growth), imported STEP (examples/acceptance-plate.step), assembly (assembly recipe fixture); add a bench-gui metric for `input_to_frame_swapped` and provisional-to-paint; re-baseline budgets at HEAD with `measure=false`; keep 'absolute open' reporting but add an opt-in absolute gate for the user-facing provisional and tick metrics.
- **Acceptance:** Reports contain phase medians and the new fixtures; budgets evaluate for both fixtures at measure=false; a documented absolute gate exists for provisional paint latency.
- **Dependencies:** Lead's quiet-machine run.
- **Risk:** None.
- **Adversarial check:** Read at HEAD. tools/perf/bench_core.py:335-358 Benchmark.preview returns only preview_first_ms/preview_update_ms and does not use self.capture() (138-161), so the preview_phase_* samples ExtrusionEvaluator records (extrusion_preview.py:523-524) never reach the headless report's timings; bench_gui.FCMacro:133 does dump panel.core.timings.report() as controller_timings, so a GUI run would carry per-phase medians there (partial mitigation the finder missed; budgets still ignore them). report.py:49-52: suspected requires current > target AND current > best*1.2+5, and failed additionally requires an independent confirmation run; an unmet absolute target alone is reported as 'open target' and can never fail, exactly as docs/engine-responsiveness-review/REVIEW.md:83 (Finding 7) already states. budgets.json:6-39 studio policy has measurement_level true and a 160-object Q00b baseline; report.py:22-32 returns 'Studio budget needs a recorded Q03 median for this measurement level' whenever the report's measurement_level differs, so the S10 policy of measure=false (docs/architecture-responsiveness-upgrade/EXECUTION.md:711-717) never evaluates it. phase1 preview_first_ms: 50 targets the synchronous ExtrusionEvaluator; docs/responsive-modeling-upgrade/IMPLEMENTATION.md:57-58 says those diagnostics 'do not measure the new asynchronous adapter's provisional feedback', and measured values are 800-8700 ms (S10-MEASUREMENTS.md:75,94; fable-deep-review/evidence/controller-benchmarks-2026-10-17.md:17), so the budget is inert. The fixture gaps are likewise documented (S10-MEASUREMENTS.md:39: dense-sketch, assemblies, private projects, memory, cold processes, rendering and input-to-paint remain unmeasured). One correction to the proposal: input_to_frame_swapped already exists as a Timings metric from InteractionTrace (responsiveness.py:164-178; tests/test_responsiveness.py:223-230); what is missing is a benchmark that drives real input and a budget for it. Everything holds; it is a correct consolidation of known, documented gaps rather than a new discovery. No change to tools/perf since 559e034.

### E-17 Closed-document entries are never pruned from Controller.documents/signatures/last_failures and orphan snapshot-*.FCStd files from crashed sessions are never swept; selector contexts are already capped at 16 (not a gap)

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value low · effort S** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/core.py` · `Controller.attach / Controller._prune_closed_sketch_edits / SnapshotStore / selectors._Context.world_shape` · lines core.py:304-307, 1021-1033, 1050, 1078-1082, 1796, 1911, 1991-1992; document_state.py:188-200; snapshots.py:45-52, 142-147; settings.py:10; selectors.py:49, 290, 366-371, 1083-1090; document_index.py:17
- **Evidence:** core.py:1033 `self.documents[meta.DocumentId] = doc`, 1050 `self.signatures[...]`, 1796 `self.last_failures[...]` are only popped on failure paths; `_prune_closed_sketch_edits` (1078-1082) prunes sketch_edits only, so closed documents' wrappers/strings accumulate per session (small). SnapshotStore has no startup sweep: runtime/snapshots on Kurt's machine holds a 5.7 KB snapshot from Oct 15 19:37 left by an earlier session alongside the current session's files. selectors.py:290/366-371 each `_Context` copies world shapes (`_world_shape` -> `.copy()`), up to 16 contexts per document (49, 1087), invalidated on any domain change but retained while idle — up to 16 copies of every result solid in memory on multi-boundary selector use.
- **Scenario:** Multi-day session opening/closing many documents; selector-heavy studio left idle.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Slow growth of small Python state and leftover snapshot files; transient multi-x geometry memory while idle (not a leak, but unmeasured).
- **A newcomer would notice:** Nothing. No UI surface, message, timing or memory effect is visible; an Onshape/SolidWorks user closing and opening projects through the GUI sees normal behaviour.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Open/close 200 documents via bridge and check `len(core.documents)`; list runtime/snapshots after killing a session; measure working set after resolving selectors at 16 history boundaries.
- **Proposed change:** Pop `documents/signatures/last_failures` in `DocumentState.slotDeletedDocument` (core already receives it, document_state.py:188-200); sweep `snapshots/snapshot-*.FCStd` and worker folders older than the session at Controller init when no live PID owns them; cap `_Context._world_shapes` by total shape count or drop contexts after an idle timeout.
- **Acceptance:** `len(core.documents)` equals open documents after close; no stale snapshot files after a simulated crash + restart; working set bounded in the selector idle test.
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** Reopening a document with the same DocumentId after close relies on `existing is None or not _is_open(existing)` (core.py:1024) — popping on close keeps that logic valid.
- **Adversarial check:** Read core.py 295-330, 975-1000, 1020-1095, 1460-1510, 1780-1810, 1812-1880, 1900-1996; document_state.py 150-206; snapshots.py (all); selectors.py 40-60, 218-240, 274-300, 300-405, 1070-1100, 1681-1684; document_index.py 10-60; settings.py:10; general_actions.py 180-197; ui.py 804-812, 1222-1248, 2862-2874; tests (test_part_studio.py 118-136, test_selector_preparation.py 181/225/261/278, test_snapshots.py, test_step_import.py:130); git log -S for _CONTEXT_LIMIT, retire_document, _prune_closed_sketch_edits, slotDeletedDocument; AGENTS.md; and a directory listing of runtime/snapshots and the two worker folders. HELD (part 1): self.documents / self.signatures / self.last_failures are populated in attach (core.py:1033, 1050) and the dispatch error path (1796) and popped only on the two failure paths (1911; 1991-1992). DocumentState.slotDeletedDocument (document_state.py:188-200) clears assembly/selectors/geometry/unmanaged/observer state and retires snapshots but never touches those three dicts; _prune_closed_sketch_edits (1078-1082) handles sketch_edits only. Nothing at HEAD fixes this (last touch b65f066 / 5f91ba2). HELD (part 2): SnapshotStore (snapshots.py:45-52) deletes only records it created; close() (142-147) retires only _current; no constructor or Controller-init sweep exists. runtime/snapshots on disk holds snapshot-ae96b27f...FCStd (5,746 bytes, 2026-10-15 19:37) beside two files from the current session (Oct 17 01:28/01:29). The directory is shared across sessions in the normal launch because settings.py:10 defaults KURTSHAPE_SESSION_DIR to ROOT/runtime. recovery.py:157 already globs its own directory, so the pattern exists. DID NOT HOLD / OVERSTATED: (a) the proposed reproduction 'open/close 200 documents via bridge' cannot run: AGENTS.md lists 'no close-document operation' among known gaps, and the only App.closeDocument sites (core.py:1506, 1876, 1912, 1993) are failure/rollback paths or the disposable preview clone, which is never registered in self.documents (1812-1880 pops only geometry/observer state). Closed entries therefore come only from GUI File-Close of distinct projects; reopening the same project ID overwrites its entry (1021-1033). (b) Every reader of self.documents guards with _is_open (core.py:1465, 1476; ui.py:808, 1244, 2867; general_actions.py:183; step_capture.py:41), so a stale entry cannot raise and costs one App.listDocuments() per iteration; the retained objects are a dead DocumentPy wrapper (the C++ document is freed on close), a sha256 hex signature (incremental_intent.signature) and a small last_failure dict (requests carry file paths, never payloads: _import_step requires an absolute path). Growth is bytes per GUI-closed document. (c) The 'worker folders older than the session' half of the proposed sweep has no observed problem: runtime/exact-preview-workers and runtime/geometry-check-jobs are both empty. (d) The selector-context part is an existing deliberate bound, not a gap: _CONTEXT_LIMIT = 16 (selectors.py:49) with LRU eviction (1083-1090), dropped on any structure/history/topology/placement change (document_index.py:17), and covered by test_part_studio.py:134-136 ('Distinct history consumers cannot retain unbounded native shapes') and test_selector_preparation.py:181/225. _world_shapes is populated lazily: only objects that reach world_shape() through a literal seed (399) or the full-enumeration fallback (359, via rows) are deep-copied (obj.Shape.copy(), 225). '16 copies of every result solid' is the worst case, reached only when the full path runs at 16 distinct boundaries, and the finder's own text concedes it is unmeasured. Net: two small, real housekeeping gaps (no dict pruning on close, no orphan-snapshot sweep) with negligible observable impact, plus one accepted, tested design bound. The classification design_weakness and severity low already fit; the title and reproduction overstate scope, so downgraded with a narrowed title.

## F. Reliability, bridge and data safety

### F-01 Session token file inherits a Modify ACL that includes the sandboxed-agent accounts and six orphaned SIDs; threat model does not match the machine

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: source · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/bridge.py` · `Bridge.__init__ (session file write)` · lines 97-98
- **Evidence:** bridge.py:98 `self.path.write_text(json.dumps({"url":...,"token":self.token,"pid":...}))` with no ACL handling. icacls runtime\assistant-session.json: every ACE is inherited (I): the local sandbox-agent group:(I)(M), six unresolved S-1-5-21-*:(I)(M), Kurtz/SYSTEM/Administrators:(F). icacls C:\Users\Kurtz\Documents\KURT shows those ACEs set explicitly (non-inherited) at that level. Get-LocalGroupMember the local sandbox-agent group = two sandbox-agent accounts (both Enabled). Seven enabled local users exist.
- **Scenario:** Any process running as a Codex sandbox account (or under one of the six unresolved SIDs if they ever map to an account) reads the token and can drive Kurt's live app: mutate open documents, save/export anywhere under CADkz\Codex or CADkz\projects, open any FCStd. The loopback port itself is reachable from every local account.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Kurt would notice nothing until a sandboxed agent (or a bug in one) edits or saves over an open project. Today the ACL is probably intentional so sandboxed agents can use the bridge, but nothing states that trust decision; the docs say only "loopback" and "do not publish session files".
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** PowerShell: icacls runtime\assistant-session.json ; Get-LocalGroupMember the local sandbox-agent group ; Get-LocalUser | ? Enabled. A `runas /user:a sandbox-agent account` shell could read the file (not attempted).
- **Proposed change:** 1) Write the session file with an explicit DACL: after write_text, run `icacls <file> /inheritance:r /grant:r "%USERNAME%:F" SYSTEM:F` (or win32security/ctypes SetNamedSecurityInfo), with an opt-in `KURTSHAPE_SESSION_READERS` list (e.g. the local sandbox-agent group) when Kurt wants sandboxed agents to connect. 2) Add a "Threat model" section to docs/OPERATION_CONTRACT.md stating: loopback reachable by all local accounts; token readable by X; bridge writes bounded by write roots; browser CSRF blocked. 3) Optionally bind the token to the client PID via a second header? Not worth it; the ACL is the lever.
- **Acceptance:** icacls shows no (I) ACEs on the session file and only the allowed principals; new tests/test_bridge.py case on win32 asserts the DACL via icacls output; AGENTS.md documents who can connect. Regression gate: existing bridge suite unchanged.
- **Dependencies:** Kurt's decision whether the sandbox accounts are meant to reach the bridge.
- **Risk:** Restricting the ACL silently breaks sandboxed-agent bridge use; hence the opt-in list and an explicit error message in assistant_client when the file is unreadable.

### F-02 Handler threads have no socket timeout and no concurrency cap; a local client can pin unlimited threads; some disconnects print tracebacks

**verified defect · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: reproduced · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/bridge.py` · `Handler (BaseHTTPRequestHandler subclass), ThreadingHTTPServer` · lines 27-29, 48-53, 90-93, 95-96
- **Evidence:** Handler defines only log_message/send_json/do_POST (27-39): no `timeout`, so `rfile.read(size)` (53) and header parsing block forever; `ThreadingHTTPServer` with `daemon_threads=True` (95-96) spawns one thread per connection with no bound. except clause (90-93) catches ValueError, queue.Full, BrokenPipeError, ConnectionResetError only. Probe A1-A3: 200 idle sockets -> active threads 2 -> 202; 50 sockets with `Content-Length: 1000` and a 6-byte body (valid token) -> 252; still 252 after 15 s. A8: a 60 KB `[[[[...` body raised RecursionError inside json.loads, escaped do_POST, client saw RemoteDisconnected and socketserver printed a traceback. A10: closing the 50 partial-body sockets produced 50 `ConnectionAbortedError: [WinError 10053]` tracebacks (send_error(400) on a dead socket is not in the except tuple).
- **Scenario:** A buggy or hostile local process (any account; no token needed for the header stage) keeps connections open; each costs a thread (1 MB reserved stack) for the life of the app. Partial-body disconnects and pathological JSON spam the native log/Report view with tracebacks.
- **What Kurt would notice:** In the probe the app kept answering legitimate requests (A4), so this is resource exhaustion and log noise, not a lockout. With thousands of connections the FreeCAD process would bloat and eventually fail to spawn threads while Kurt has unsaved work.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Run scratch bridge_probe.py (transport-only, DummyController): sections A1-A3, A8, A10. Output: ...\scratchpad\area-f-reliability\bridge_probe.out.txt.
- **Proposed change:** In bridge.py Handler: set `timeout = 15` (socket timeout; BaseHTTPRequestHandler then ends the request with 408 on `socket.timeout`); override `ThreadingHTTPServer.process_request` with a `threading.BoundedSemaphore(64)` that answers 503 JSON when exhausted; widen the except to `ConnectionError` (covers ConnectionAbortedError) and add `RecursionError` -> 400; keep protocol HTTP/1.0 (no keep-alive, verified) so threads end after one response.
- **Acceptance:** tests/test_bridge.py: idle socket and lying-Content-Length socket both end within timeout+1 s and thread count returns to baseline; 65th concurrent connection gets 503 JSON {code:"too_many_connections"}; partial-body disconnect leaves no traceback on stderr (capture with contextlib.redirect_stderr). Gate: existing 7 bridge tests unchanged.
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** A 15 s socket timeout must not cut legitimate slow writers: bodies are <= 64 KiB over loopback, so it cannot.

### F-03 Queue-full and malformed-body both answer a bare HTTP 400 HTML page; assistant_client raises HTTPError instead of returning a typed error

**verified defect · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: reproduced · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/bridge.py` · `Handler.do_POST (queue.Full path), assistant_client.send` · lines 74-79, 90-91; tools/assistant_client.py:25-26
- **Evidence:** bridge.py:76-79 on queue.Full completes the ledger with code queue_full then re-raises; 90-91 `except (ValueError,queue.Full): self.send_error(400)`. Probe A5 with 32 blocked requests: 33rd request -> (400, 'text/html;charset=utf-8', '<!DOCTYPE HTML>...Error response'); a tracked mutation likewise 400, while ledger.status reports {status:'cancelled', error.code:'queue_full'}. assistant_client.send does `with opener.open(message,timeout=35) as response: return json.loads(response.read())` so urllib raises `HTTPError: 400`.
- **Scenario:** An agent runs a burst (parallel inspects, or a retry storm after outcome_unknown) while the GUI thread is inside a long recompute; the 33rd request fails with a Python exception indistinguishable from a malformed request, and the build script aborts.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Kurt sees an agent script crash with 'HTTP Error 400: Bad Request' and no CAD error; the ledger knows the real reason but nobody reads it.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** bridge_probe.py section A5; or against a running app: send 33 `inspect` requests concurrently while a fillet runs.
- **Proposed change:** bridge.py: on queue.Full send 503 with JSON body {ok:false,error:{code:'queue_full',retry_after_ms:250}} and a Retry-After header; on JSON/ValueError send 400 with JSON {code:'invalid_request'}; add a `send_json(result, status)` parameter. assistant_client.send: catch HTTPError, parse a JSON body when Content-Type is application/json and return it as the dict; optional bounded retry on 503.
- **Acceptance:** tests/test_bridge.py: full queue -> status 503, JSON code queue_full; invalid JSON -> 400 JSON code invalid_request; assistant_client.send returns dicts for both. Gate: A5 oracle tests still see 32 timeouts.
- **Dependencies:** None (wire additive; contract_revision bump 0->1).
- **Risk:** Clients that relied on HTTPError must adapt; ks.py/bone-mallet scripts only look at res['ok'].

### F-04 30 s request timeout is within 2x of measured dodec-hub operation times, and no client helper reconciles outcome_unknown

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: source · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/bridge.py` · `Bridge.__init__ request_timeout, do_POST timeout branch; assistant_client.send` · lines 16, 80-88; tools/assistant_client.py:16-26; projects/dodec-hub/ks.py:33-44
- **Evidence:** bridge.py:16 `request_timeout=30`; 81-88 after `queue.Empty` returns outcome_unknown when started. assistant_client.py:25 `timeout=35`, no handling of outcome_unknown. ks.py:39-41 `if not res.get('ok'): ... raise BridgeError`. projects/dodec-hub/build-bridge.log: `ok fillet RimAndNotches (15.1s)`, `ok fillet SocketBreakouts (9.8s)`, `ok transform_part PlaceHub (7.9s)`, `ok set_part_appearance (5.0s)`, `ok save (3.0s)`; the saved file is 9.7 MB (dodec-hub/Dodec Hub Conformal.FCStd).
- **Scenario:** A part twice the hub's complexity, or the same hub while a geometry check or STEP export runs, pushes one fillet past 30 s. The app completes the fillet; the client gets outcome_unknown, raises, and the next run finds a half-built document it must delete_features and rebuild (or hits stale_revision).
- **What Kurt would notice:** Kurt watches the model finish in the viewport while the agent reports failure and starts over; wasted minutes per iteration and a confusing history.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** tests/test_bridge.py already shows the mechanics (test_started_timeout_reports_unknown...). Live: run dodec build_bridge.py with Bridge(request_timeout=10) in a private session; RimAndNotches returns outcome_unknown while the feature appears.
- **Proposed change:** tools/assistant_client.send: on error.code == 'outcome_unknown', poll {op:'request_status',request_id} (canonical fast path, bridge.py:56-64) every 0.5 s until status in {completed,failed,cancelled} (cap e.g. 10 min) and return the stored response; document this as the standard client behavior in OPERATION_CONTRACT.md. Server side, optionally accept `timeout_s` (<= 600) per request so a known-long op can wait in-band.
- **Acceptance:** tests/test_bridge.py: DummyController delay 0.5 s with request_timeout 0.1 -> send() returns the completed result once; project helpers (ks.py) drop their BridgeError on outcome_unknown. Gate: existing timeout tests unchanged.
- **Dependencies:** None; AGENTS.md should tell agents to use send() rather than raw urllib.
- **Risk:** A client that polls forever on a crashed app: bound by the cap and a pid liveness check.

### F-05 A rejected bridge request writes LastFailure into the document without the Modified guard used by every other metadata write

**verified defect · severity low · confidence medium · user value medium · effort S** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: reproduced · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/core.py` · `Controller._dispatch except branch; compare _build_status, _rebuild_reason_on_open, _restore_native_map_versions` · lines 1794-1797 vs 1343-1347, 1276-1294, 1252-1261
- **Evidence:** core.py:1796-1797 `self.last_failures[...] = {"request": request, "error": error, "rejected": True}; self._meta(doc).LastFailure=json.dumps(...)` with no `_modified`/`_restore_modified` around it, whereas 1343-1347 wraps `meta.BuildStatus = status` in exactly that guard and SKETCH_PLANE_CONTRACT.md:43 explains why (property writes set the GUI Modified flag). Probe B3/B4: after a save, `{"op":"save","path":5}` and a bad set_parameter both left meta.LastFailure = the full rejected request JSON (262 bytes) while the revision stayed unchanged.
- **Scenario:** Kurt saves, an agent sends one wrong feature name (missing_reference) or a path typo; the title flips to '· Modified', closing the app prompts to save, and the next save persists the rejected request text (including absolute paths) into the FCStd.
- **What Kurt would notice:** False unsaved marker and a 'did I lose something?' moment; also a minor privacy wart (rejected requests with paths end up inside shared FCStd files).
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** GUI needed: save a part; `python -B tools/assistant_client.py --json '{"op":"set_parameter","feature":"Nope","parameter":"Length","value":1,"document_id":...,"expected_revision":...}'`; observe the title suffix (ui.py:1770-1772). Headless proof of the write: scratch controller_probe.py B3/B4.
- **Proposed change:** Wrap the LastFailure write in `modified = self._modified(doc)` / `self._restore_modified(doc, modified)`; or keep last_failures in memory only and persist LastFailure in `_save` just before saveCopy; set the property status NoModify via `meta.setPropertyStatus('LastFailure','NoModify')` at creation (core.py:88-100).
- **Acceptance:** GUI recipe (tools/run_gui_recipe.ps1 interface or a new small one): save -> rejected bridge request -> Gui document Modified is False and the title has no suffix. Native test: LastFailure write leaves revision and intent signature unchanged (already true, keep as gate).
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** None; inspect's last_failed_attempt still comes from memory.

### F-06 Save/export over a destination another process holds open fails with a raw WinError and leaves stage-file orphans beside the project

**verified defect · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: reproduced · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/core.py` · `Controller._save, Controller._export, _replace_export_pair` · lines 2793-2811, 2813-2853, 2855-2897
- **Evidence:** _save (2797-2811): on any exception only `doc.FileName=old_name; raise` — the `.saving.FCStd` stage is never removed and the `.recovery.FCStd` copy (2804-2806) was already made. _export writes `stage`/`temp` (2817, 2851-2852) and `_replace_export_pair` rolls back targets (2883-2896) but never unlinks the stages. Probe B5: hold probe.FCStd open with a plain read handle, bridge save -> `operation_failed | [WinError 5] Access is denied`, folder afterwards: probe.FCStd, probe.recovery.FCStd, probe.saving.FCStd; a traceback was appended to kurtshape.log. B6: export over an open probe.step -> `export_failed` (pair restored correctly) but probe.exporting.step and probe.step.json.saving remain. Real folders already show the pattern: projects/bone-mallet holds four *.recovery files; nothing cleans stages.
- **Scenario:** An agent (or BumpMesh step) reads the STEP/FCStd the app just wrote while the next save/export runs; OneDrive, Defender or a FreeCAD preview handler holds the file; Kurt's own Save gets 'Access is denied' with no hint what holds it, and the project folder slowly fills with .saving/.exporting litter.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Confusing error text in Messages, orphan files that look like real outputs (an agent could pick up probe.exporting.step), no retry.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** scratch controller_probe.py sections B5/B6 (isolated session); or GUI: open the project's .step in a Python `open()` and press Export to the same path.
- **Proposed change:** _save: `try/finally` unlink of the stage on failure; map PermissionError/OSError from os.replace to `OperationError('destination_locked', 'Another program holds <name> open; close it and retry', path=...)`; retry os.replace up to 5x with 100 ms backoff for transient AV locks before failing; keep the recovery copy only after success or document that it is intentionally kept. _export/_replace_export_pair: unlink `stage`/`temp` in a finally when not committed; same locked-destination code. Also report the GUI error with the file name, not the WinError text.
- **Acceptance:** tests/test_core_safety.py: new cases hold the destination open, assert error code destination_locked, original bytes unchanged, no *.saving/*.exporting files, FileName restored; a success-path assertion that no stage remains (extend test_build_status.absent_export). Gate: existing readonly-sidecar test unchanged.
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** Retrying replace while the user genuinely has the file open adds up to 0.5 s to a failing save; acceptable.

### F-07 Bridge save can overwrite another project's FCStd (different DocumentId) and re-point the document at it; adopt has the check, save does not

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: source · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/core.py` · `Controller._save vs Controller._adopt` · lines 2793-2811 vs 1887-1889
- **Evidence:** _save only checks write_path and `if path.exists(): shutil.copy2(path, path.with_suffix('.recovery.FCStd'))` then os.replace (2804-2808). _adopt refuses an existing destination (1888-1889 `adoption_destination_exists`). The GUI Save As path relies on the QFileDialog overwrite prompt (ui.py:2975-2984); the bridge has no equivalent. projects/ folders already contain stray files (`Untitled part.FCStd` in bone-mallet and dodec-hub, `Dodec Hub Conformal2.FCStd`), i.e. path mistakes happen.
- **Scenario:** An agent builds a destination from a part name and lands on Kurt's other project file (or a second agent session saves its dry-run document over the live project); the file is replaced, one generation survives as .recovery.FCStd, and the open document's FileName now points at the victim path.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Kurt discovers his osteotome file now holds a drill; one undo of the mistake exists (.recovery) and is itself overwritten by the next save.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Headless: save doc A to a.FCStd, new doc B, bridge `save` B to a.FCStd -> succeeds, a.recovery.FCStd holds A (not run today; logic is unambiguous at 2804-2808).
- **Proposed change:** Before replacing an existing FCStd, read its `Document.xml` from the ZIP (cheap, stdlib) and extract KurtShapeProject.DocumentId; if present and != this document's DocumentId, raise `destination_is_another_project` (details: existing_document_id, existing_name) unless the request has `overwrite:true`. The GUI passes overwrite:true after its dialog confirmation. Unmanaged FCStd destinations (no metadata) also require overwrite:true from the bridge.
- **Acceptance:** tests/test_core_safety.py: save B over A's file rejects with the code and leaves A's bytes and A's FileName intact; with overwrite:true behaves as today. GUI Save As over another project still works after confirmation.
- **Dependencies:** ARGS['save'] gains 'overwrite'; contract note.
- **Risk:** Reading Document.xml of a 10 MB archive adds ~tens of ms; acceptable. Scripts that intentionally overwrite must add the flag.

### F-09 Missing required fields and wrong-typed identifiers surface as operation_failed with a Python KeyError/TypeError message, a traceback in kurtshape.log and a persisted LastFailure

**verified defect · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: reproduced · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/core.py` · `Controller._dispatch (open/recover/save/export branches), Controller._doc` · lines 1493-1499, 1727-1730, 1057-1061
- **Evidence:** core.py:1499 `path = Path(request["path"]).resolve()`, 1728 `self._save(doc, request["path"])`, 1730 `self._export(doc, request["path"], ...)`: KeyError when absent. `_doc` 1058-1059 `self.documents.get(identifier)` raises TypeError for an unhashable document_id. Probe B3: {op:save} / {op:export} / {op:open} / {op:recover} all -> `operation_failed | 'path'`, kurtshape.log gained tracebacks, and LastFailure recorded the request. ARGS (core.py:253-300) lists allowed fields but there is no required-field table; unknown fields are rejected cleanly (1439-1441).
- **Scenario:** An agent omits `path` or sends document_id as a list; it gets 'operation_failed: 'path'' with no hint, the app's log grows a traceback per attempt, and the document's LastFailure changes.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Opaque errors for the most common scripting mistake; log noise that hides real failures.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** controller_probe.py B3; or `python -B tools/assistant_client.py --json '{"op":"save","document_id":"...","expected_revision":"..."}'`.
- **Proposed change:** Add `REQUIRED = {'open':{'path'},'recover':{'path'},'save':{'path'},'export':{'path'},'import_step':{'path'},'adopt':{'path'},...}` next to ARGS and check it right after the unknown-field check (1439-1441) raising `invalid_argument` with `missing:[...]`; validate `document_id`/`expected_revision`/`request_id` are strings in `_doc`/dispatch; expose both tables through capabilities (see build-identity finding).
- **Acceptance:** Native tests: each op without its required field -> invalid_argument with missing list, no kurtshape.log growth, LastFailure unchanged; unhashable document_id -> unknown_document.
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** None.

### F-11 inspect has no modified/unsaved flag although the controller already reads it and the GUI title shows it

**opportunity · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: preference · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/core.py` · `Controller._inspect, Controller._modified` · lines 1353-1408, 1236-1243; src/kurtshape/ui.py:1770-1772
- **Evidence:** _inspect returns native_file but no modified key (probe B10 key list); `_modified(doc)` (1236-1243) already returns `Gui.getDocument(doc.Name).Modified` (None headless) and ui.py:1770-1771 uses it for the '· Unsaved'/'· Modified' suffix. AGENTS.md:42: 'Ask Kurt before restarting KurtShape... (the bridge does not report the modified flag)'.
- **Scenario:** An agent wants to restart the app to load a fix, or to close a document, and cannot tell whether Kurt has unsaved work.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Agents interrupt Kurt with questions, or (worse) assume saved state.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** list_documents / inspect output has no such key.
- **Proposed change:** Add `modified: self._modified(doc)` and `saved_revision` (revision recorded in _save after success; None for unsaved) to _inspect; include in list_documents; mention in OPERATION_CONTRACT inspect row.
- **Acceptance:** GUI recipe: edit -> inspect.modified true; save -> false; headless -> null documented.
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** None.

### F-12 No close_document (or feature-visibility) operation; with duplicate_document_id this blocks reopening relocated copies and cleaning up dry-run documents

**opportunity · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort M** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: preference · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/core.py` · `Controller.OPS, Controller.attach duplicate check` · lines 247-252, 1020-1023
- **Evidence:** OPS (252) has new/open/recover/import_step/adopt but no close; attach raises duplicate_document_id when the same UUID is open (1021-1023). AGENTS.md:22 'Never write an FCStd that is open in the app: the app rejects a second copy... the open tab goes stale' and :44 'no feature-visibility or close-document operation'. set_part_appearance only toggles parts (OPERATION_CONTRACT.md:173).
- **Scenario:** Agent rebuilds a part headless, wants the live app to show the new file: it cannot close the stale tab, so Kurt must click; agents that `new` per run leave tabs (and recovery pairs) behind.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Manual steps and stale tabs; Kurt is asked to close documents the agent opened.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** capabilities.operations contains no close op.
- **Proposed change:** `close_document {document_id, expected_revision, discard_unsaved:false}`: reject `sketch_edit_active`/`busy` as usual, reject `unsaved_changes` when `_modified(doc)` is True and discard_unsaved is false, then release sketch_edits, snapshots.retire_document(instance_id), geometry.drop_document, App.closeDocument; GUI tick already handles a vanished document (ui.py:1270-1275). `set_feature_visibility {feature, visible}` mapped to ViewObject.Visibility with the existing presentation guard (studio.present).
- **Acceptance:** Native tests: close unsaved without flag rejects; with flag closes and a later open of the same UUID succeeds; GUI recipe shows the tab disappearing without prompts.
- **Dependencies:** modified flag finding; recovery retire hook for pruning.
- **Risk:** Closing while a worker borrows a snapshot: retire_document already handles pinned leases (snapshots.py:135-140).

### F-14 An agent mutation cancels the feature editor Kurt has open, and bridge requests are dispatched while modal GUI dialogs are up

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence medium · user value medium · effort S** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: source · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.tick (bridge dispatch and studio_dialog revision check); Controller._dispatch native-edit guard` · lines 1216-1218, 1231-1235; src/kurtshape/feature_panel.py:39; src/kurtshape/core.py:1573-1580
- **Evidence:** ui.py:1216-1218 dispatches a queued bridge request every 25 ms tick unconditionally; 1233-1235 `elif self.studio_dialog and ... Revision != self.studio_dialog.base_revision: self.studio_dialog.reject(); self.notify('Feature canceled because the project changed...')`. The KurtShape feature editor is NonModal (feature_panel.py:39), so core.py:1573-1580 (which blocks only native Gui.Control dialogs and getInEdit) does not stop a bridge pad while Kurt has Extrude/Fillet open. Modal `dialog.exec_()` sites (ui.py:930, 1032, 1091) run nested event loops in which the 25 ms timer still fires.
- **Scenario:** Kurt is adjusting a fillet radius in the editor; an agent sends its next step; Kurt's editor closes with 'Feature canceled because the project changed'; he re-picks edges. In a modal assembly dialog the agent's edit lands under the dialog (staged_revision then rejects Kurt's OK with stale_revision).
- **What Kurt would notice:** Lost dialog input and a feeling that the app fights him; the agent gets no signal that a human is mid-edit.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** GUI: open Extrude on a sketch, then from a shell send any mutation through tools/assistant_client.py; observe the editor close and the notification. Not run today (no GUI control allowed).
- **Proposed change:** Expose a human-edit lease to the bridge: in Panel.tick, when `self.studio_dialog`, `self.task`, a placement task or `QApplication.activeModalWidget()` is set, answer queued mutations (not READ_OPS) with `{code:'user_editing', retry_after_ms:1000}` instead of dispatching (do it in Bridge.tick via a controller/panel predicate so HTTP threads stay out of Qt); keep inspect/preview flowing. Agents retry on that code (same helper as queue_full).
- **Acceptance:** GUI recipe: with the Extrude editor open, a bridge pad returns user_editing and the editor stays; after Cancel the same request succeeds. Native: predicate False keeps today's behavior (bridge suite unchanged).
- **Dependencies:** JSON error finding (clients must see the code).
- **Risk:** An agent build stalls while Kurt dawdles in a dialog: that is the desired precedence; document it.

### F-15 Default write roots include the whole CADkz\Codex tree, i.e. the checkout itself (tracked fixtures are writable by the bridge)

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: source · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/settings.py` · `Settings.__init__ roots; core.WRITE_ROOT` · lines 17-18; src/kurtshape/core.py:37-38
- **Evidence:** settings.py:18 `self.roots = {root.parent.resolve(), self.project_directory.resolve()}` where root is the kurtshape checkout, so CADkz\Codex (containing the repo, examples/acceptance-plate.FCStd, validation fixtures) is a default root; probe B7 lists it. docs/2026-10-03-claude-review/CODEX_PROMPT.md:164 records the proposal (P1-12: narrow to projects + explicit grants with Revoke) as 'unchanged until Kurt decides'.
- **Scenario:** An agent `save`/`export` with a path under Codex\kurtshape (e.g. examples/) replaces a tracked file; git shows a modified fixture later.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Surprising repo diffs; no model loss (recovery copy exists).
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** write_path('C:\\...\\Codex\\kurtshape\\examples\\x.FCStd', {'.fcstd'}, settings.roots) is accepted.
- **Proposed change:** Implement the already-proposed P1-12: default roots = projects only; GUI grants per folder with a Settings list and Revoke; keep tests' temporary grants via settings.json.
- **Acceptance:** test_review_followup grant test extended: save under Codex\kurtshape without a grant rejects path_outside_workspace.
- **Dependencies:** Kurt's pending decision (documented Oct 3).
- **Risk:** Scripts that save under Codex must be granted once from the GUI.

### F-16 Capability gaps ranked by the real projects they unblock: helix path, spline, typed sketch constraints, nth/created_by-through-Hole selectors

**opportunity · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort L** · verification: not independently verified · evidence basis: preference · not independently verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/core.py` · `Controller.MODEL_OPS / studio.OPS / reference_tools.OPS / modeling_geometry.OPS` · lines 248-252; src/kurtshape/studio.py:16; src/kurtshape/reference_tools.py:8; src/kurtshape/modeling_geometry.py:18
- **Evidence:** No op creates a Part::Helix or a sketch BSpline (grep: only core.py:2177-2178 recognizes BSplineCurve endpoints); typed constraints are limited to pierce and project_geometry (OPERATION_CONTRACT.md:99,177); 'No nth-element selector exists' (243). AGENTS.md:22 routes helix/spline work to the headless Controller, which is why zimmer-8mm-drill (helical flute sweeps, build_drill.py docstring) and smith-peterson-osteotome were built headless, not in the live app. dodec-hub/README.md:72 'created_by selectors cannot trace through the Hole features yet'. Hole, add_polyline, add_arc, arbitrary planes and multi-edge references all exist at HEAD (commits 3df4faa..f57d592) but not in the running app.
- **Scenario:** Any threaded/fluted part (drill, screw, knurl base) cannot be modeled live; bridge-made sketches have independent endpoint dimensions so hand edits open gaps (PRELIMINARY_REVIEW lead 4; add_polyline fixed this for polylines only); fillets on faces downstream of a Hole must be picked by adjacency.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Kurt watches the osteotome/drill appear only as finished files instead of live, editable history.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** capabilities.operations lacks create_helix/add_spline/add_constraint.
- **Proposed change:** In priority order: (1) `create_helix {pitch,height,radius,angle,left_handed,placement}` as a Part::Helix in the Body with a stable id usable as `sweep.path` (S-M; unblocks the drill live); (2) `set_feature_visibility` (S, see close op); (3) `add_constraint {sketch, kind: coincident|tangent|equal|horizontal|vertical|symmetric|point_on_object, refs}` validated with the same DoF/conflict rules as add_polyline (M); (4) `add_spline {sketch, poles|points, periodic}` with pole dimensions optional (M); (5) selector `nth {query, index, order_by}` and Hole-aware created_by element-name parsing (M).
- **Acceptance:** zimmer drill rebuilt through the live bridge with a helix sweep and valid check_geometry; dodec notch fillets selected by created_by through Hole; native tests per op.
- **Dependencies:** App restart to load; AGENTS.md known-gaps paragraph.
- **Risk:** Constraint ops must keep the 'fully constrained, unmoved by solve' contract or agents will produce drifting sketches.

## G. Testing, packaging and maintenance

### G-01 No KurtShape build identity: window, bridge capabilities/diagnostics, Messages and STEP provenance cannot name the running source revision

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value high · effort S** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.make_toolbars (Help menu), start()` · lines ui.py 528-531, 1756, 1828, 4311-4341; core.py 520-530, 1446-1463; bridge.py 97-98; contract.py 3-4; __init__.py 1
- **Evidence:** Help menu offers only "Shortcuts" and "Example" (ui.py 528-531); start() (4311-4341) logs nothing about the source tree; src/kurtshape/__init__.py is a one-line docstring with no __version__; capabilities returns contract/session_id/engine/operations only (core.py 1446-1458, confirmed by headless probe: keys = assembly, contract, contract_revision, engine, geometry_check, import_formats, measurement, operations, preview_operations, request_size_limit_bytes, retry, session_id); diagnostics returns timings/engine/recovery_directory/checkpoint_interval_seconds (core.py 1461-1463, probe); launch-kurtshape.ps1 writes no identity file.
- **Scenario:** Kurt launched freecad.exe at 01:28 from a build that predates 24bab02/1f0001e/f57d592; later he or an agent reads docs/OPERATION_CONTRACT.md (which documents hole/add_polyline) and sends those ops to the bridge, or reports a bug that is already fixed.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Kurt cannot tell from the window, Messages, a Copy-all paste or an exported STEP sidecar which KurtShape code produced it; agents only discover a stale build by diffing capabilities.operations by hand (this is exactly how the review had to date the running app).
- **A newcomer would notice:** No Help > About, no version in the title bar or Settings, nothing in the export sidecar; SolidWorks users expect a version/SP in About and Onshape users expect the build to be implicitly current. They would not know whether a documented operation exists in the build they launched.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Open the app; look at the title bar, Help menu, Messages and the Settings dialog: no version or commit. Send {"op":"capabilities"} and {"op":"diagnostics"} via the bridge: engine git hash is present (App.Version()[7] = 4fd3bf320...), KurtShape revision is absent. Export a STEP and read the .json sidecar: provenance has engine/settings/document revision, not a source commit.
- **Proposed change:** Add src/kurtshape/build_identity.py: at import compute {git_head (via `git -c safe.directory=<root> rev-parse HEAD`, falling back to a runtime/build-id.json the launcher writes), dirty flag from `git status --porcelain -- src`, sha256 over src/kurtshape/*.py + shortcuts.json, engine git hash App.Version()[7]}. Surface it in: main window title suffix (QMainWindow.setWindowTitle in start()), the first retained Messages line, `capabilities.build` and `diagnostics.build` (core.py 1446/1461), export provenance `program` field (core.py ~523), the Copy-all header (ui.py 731), and `launch-kurtshape.ps1 -PrintOnly`. Have launch-kurtshape.ps1 warn (Write-Warning + Messages line via env KURTSHAPE_LAUNCH_NOTE) when another freecad.exe already runs from a different checkout against the same SessionRoot (Get-CimInstance Win32_Process command lines, as update-green-copy.ps1 already does).
- **Acceptance:** tests/test_runtime_layout.py asserts capabilities.build has a 40-hex git_head or a 'launcher' source, a boolean dirty flag and a src sha256; a GUI recipe asserts the window title and first Messages line contain the same short SHA; export sidecar JSON contains program.git_head. Regression gate: the capabilities snapshot test fails if the field disappears.
- **Dependencies:** None; the AGENTS.md workflow (check capabilities before modeling) benefits immediately.
- **Risk:** git may be unavailable or blocked by ownership checks at runtime; the fallback file must be written by the launcher and marked 'unverified'.
- **Adversarial check:** Read: ui.py 500-560 (Help menu has only Shortcuts/Example at 528-531; the only setWindowTitle calls at 1756/1828 write '<name> — KurtShape'), ui.py 4311-4341 (start() logs nothing about the tree), src/kurtshape/__init__.py (one docstring line, unchanged since b65f066), core.py 1446-1463 (capabilities carries contract/contract_revision/session_id/engine; diagnostics carries timings/engine/recovery dir), core.py 520-530 (_step_export_provenance has engine/settings/document revision, no program field), ui.py 731-734 (copy_messages copies raw text, no header), launch-kurtshape.ps1 (writes no identity file), bridge.py 97-98 (session file carries contract/contract_revision/pid only). Headless probe with the bundled pythonw confirmed App.Version()[7] = 4fd3bf320d95... and [3] = '20260928 (Git shallow)'. contract.py pins CONTRACT_VERSION=3, CONTRACT_REVISION=0 and git log shows it untouched since 559e034, i.e. not bumped for 24bab02/1f0001e/f57d592, so contract_revision cannot stand in for a build id. No docs mention a build/source identity. What did not hold: the consequence 'agents only discover a stale build by diffing capabilities.operations by hand' overstates it; AGENTS.md line 7 already prescribes capabilities as the op inventory, and an unsupported op fails loudly with unsupported_operation, so work is not silently wrong. The remaining real cost is attribution: bug reports, Messages pastes and STEP sidecars cannot name the KurtShape revision, and Kurt must remember when he last restarted (AGENTS.md line 44 tracks 'needs an app restart' by hand). Design weakness stands; severity high is inflated for an observability gap with no geometry or data impact.
- **Merged duplicate F-10:** Agents cannot tell which KurtShape build is running: capabilities and the session file carry engine and contract only, no source revision; argument tables are not exposed — opportunity, medium, preference · not independently verified. Evidence: capabilities returns contract, contract_revision, session_id, measurement, geometry_check, operations (names only), preview_operations, import_formats, assembly, retry, request_size_limit_bytes, engine (probe B1 key list); no build/git field and no per-op argument lists although `Controller.ARGS` has 73 entries (B10). Session file keys: contract, contract_revision, pid, session_id, token, url (probe A9). AGENTS.md:44 keeps a hand-written list of which commits need an app restart; Kurt's running app (started 01:28) lacks hole/add_polyline/describe_dress_up and only op-name probing reveals that. The assistant_client never checks that the session `pid` is alive (bridge.close unlinks the file only on clean shutdown, 120-128), so a crashed app leaves a stale file that AGENTS.md step 1 treats as 'running'.

### G-02 Launcher and worktree sprawl: five launchers and five checkouts; KurtShape-green.cmd and KurtShape-sweep.cmd run other checkouts' code against main's live session folder and silently replace its bridge token

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `tools/update-green-copy.ps1` · `launcher generation` · lines tools/update-green-copy.ps1 7-11, 31-53, 147-153; bridge.py 97-98, 121-128; launch-kurtshape.ps1 4-5; recovery.py 15
- **Evidence:** Codex/KurtShape-green.cmd and Codex/KurtShape-sweep.cmd run `kurtshape-green\launch-kurtshape.ps1` / `kurtshape-sweep\launch-kurtshape.ps1` with `-RuntimeRoot "%~dp0kurtshape\runtime" -SessionRoot "%~dp0kurtshape\runtime"` (file contents read); `git worktree list` shows kurtshape (f57d592), kurtshape-green (660acc5, Oct 3), kurtshape-opus-review (7d1c56a), kurtshape-sweep (f607984), extrude-review (30f16b2); README 31: 'Earlier local workflow/green-copy launchers ... are historical'; docs/long-run/STATUS.md 12: 'Green copy: still at longrun/Q24a ... updater refused'.
- **Scenario:** Kurt double-clicks whichever shortcut is on the desktop (CADkz/KurtShape.lnk per AGENTS.md); an agent starts a second instance from kurtshape-sweep for a test.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Two processes from different checkouts share runtime/settings.json, shortcuts.user.json, project-recovery and runtime/assistant-session.json (the second overwrites the bridge token), and the 'green' rollback path is 35 commits and two weeks behind the features Kurt uses daily, so it is not a rollback of anything current.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Five launchers with similar names and no indication which is current; picking the wrong one loads two-week-old code against the live profile with no warning.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** cat Codex/KurtShape-green.cmd and Codex/KurtShape-sweep.cmd; `git -c safe.directory=... worktree list`; compare green HEAD 660acc5 to main f57d592 (git log 660acc5..HEAD --oneline | wc -l).
- **Proposed change:** Decide one supported launcher (KurtShape.cmd) and delete or move the other .cmd files into docs/archive with a note; make launch-kurtshape.ps1 default SessionRoot to `<checkout>\runtime` only for the main checkout and require an explicit `-SessionRoot` otherwise; refuse to start when `runtime/assistant-session.json` belongs to a live pid from another checkout (reuse Get-KurtShapeGreenGuard logic from update-green-copy.ps1 31-53). Either retire the green copy or make `tools/update-green-copy.ps1 <tag>` part of the release step (see maintenance process finding) so 'green' means 'last tagged release'.
- **Acceptance:** Only one top-level launcher remains; launching a second instance against a session dir that already has a live bridge prints a clear refusal; a PowerShell-level test (tools/ci) runs launch-kurtshape.ps1 -PrintOnly for the main checkout and asserts the session root; docs/long-run/STATUS.md 'Green copy' line is either current or removed.
- **Dependencies:** Build identity finding (the guard needs to name checkouts).
- **Risk:** Kurt may rely on the sweep/review launchers for side-by-side comparison; keep an explicit `-SessionRoot` path for that.
- **Adversarial check:** Read: Codex/KurtShape-green.cmd and Codex/KurtShape-sweep.cmd (both run the sibling checkout's launch-kurtshape.ps1 with -RuntimeRoot and -SessionRoot pointing at kurtshape\runtime, exactly as cited), Codex/KurtShape-design-update.cmd (main code, separate SessionRoot runtime\design-workspace, harmless) and Codex/KurtShape-workflow-update.cmd (main code, defaults, harmless duplicate of the in-repo KurtShape.cmd); tools/update-green-copy.ps1 7-11 and 147-153 (generates the green launcher with both roots on main/runtime, as cited; guard logic at 31-53); git worktree list (five worktrees exactly as listed; green 660acc5 Oct 3, sweep f607984 Oct 16 on claude/sweep-tool, opus-review 7d1c56a, extrude-review 30f16b2; all clean); README.md 31 and 41 (as cited); docs/long-run/STATUS.md 12 (as cited); docs/long-run/Q01-green-copy.md (green copy was designed to share main's runtime/profile deliberately); bridge.py 97-98 and 121-128 (assistant-session.json is written unconditionally with the new pid, so a second instance on the same SessionRoot replaces the first instance's url/token; close() only unlinks when the pid matches, confirming the overwrite is known); recovery.py 15 (project-recovery keyed by DocumentId under the shared session dir, no instance lock); settings.py 27-31 (atomic but last-writer-wins). The CADkz/KurtShape.lnk target (read via WScript.Shell, no desktop control) is main's launch-kurtshape.ps1, so Kurt's daily path is main; the hazard needs someone to use the green/sweep .cmd while main runs. AGENTS.md line 33 documents the safe alternative (projects/dodec-hub/start-session.ps1 with its own session dir and port), which mitigates but does not prevent. Corrections: green is 24 commits behind (git log 660acc5..HEAD | wc -l), not 35; the two-week age is right (Oct 3 -> Oct 17). The sibling launchers' launch-kurtshape.ps1 copies are byte-identical to main's modulo CRLF, so the only difference is the Python source they load. Finding stands as a design weakness at medium: a stale bridge token mid-session breaks Kurt's agents, and two instances can interleave checkpoints for the same DocumentId into one project-recovery folder; the saved FCStd itself is not at risk.

### G-03 Launcher only checks that freecad.exe exists; runtime layout and versions are verified at install (archive hash) and in CI, not at launch, and engine_stamp omits the engine build hash

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value low · effort S** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `launch-kurtshape.ps1` · `exe existence check` · lines launch-kurtshape.ps1 96; install-runtime.ps1 21, 26-28; tools/ci/check-runtime-layout.ps1 7-23; run-checks.ps1 12; core.py 102-103, 1279-1289
- **Evidence:** launch-kurtshape.ps1 96: `if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $exePath)) { throw 'Run install-runtime.ps1 first.' }` is the only runtime check; install-runtime.ps1 21 verifies the archive SHA256 then 27 runs `7z x ... -y` over any existing folder, leaving no manifest; tools/ci/check-runtime-layout.ps1 checks five files plus versions but is wired only into CI and tools/ci/run-local.ps1; core.py 102-103 `engine_stamp()` returns ('1.1.4','7.8.1') only, while App.Version() also carries the git hash 4fd3bf320... (headless probe) that is not part of the document stamp.
- **Scenario:** A future 'compatible native runtime' with the deferred Sketcher point-color patch (docs/M1_PROGRESS.md 37-44) is extracted over runtime/freecad-1.1.4, or an extraction is interrupted, or a DLL is replaced by an antivirus quarantine.
- **What Kurt would notice:** The app starts with mixed binaries; documents opened get EngineFreeCAD='1.1.4' as before so `engine_changed` never fires even though the kernel build differs; failures surface as unexplained native crashes rather than a clear 'runtime mismatch' at launch.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Nothing; install-runtime.ps1 plus the launcher work as expected on a clean machine.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Source logic is unambiguous (launch-kurtshape.ps1 96; install-runtime.ps1 27). To demonstrate: in a scratch RuntimeRoot copy, delete bin/FreeCAD.pyd and run `launch-kurtshape.ps1 -RuntimeRoot <copy> -PrintOnly` then without -PrintOnly: the launcher proceeds to Start-Process.
- **Proposed change:** install-runtime.ps1: extract into `freecad-1.1.4.extracting-<guid>`, then rename atomically, and write `runtime/freecad-1.1.4/.kurtshape-runtime.json` {archive_sha256, extracted_utc, file_count, total_bytes, freecad_version, git_hash}. launch-kurtshape.ps1: refuse when the manifest is missing or the archive hash differs from the pinned constant, and (cheaply) verify the five layout files plus bin/python311.dll sizes; expose `-SkipRuntimeCheck` for experiments. core.py engine_stamp(): include App.Version()[7] in a new `EngineBuild` property used only for `rebuild_reason` reporting (not for blocking), and show it in capabilities.engine_build.
- **Acceptance:** tools/ci/check_runtime_layout.py reads and validates the manifest; tests/test_runtime_layout.py asserts the manifest's git_hash equals App.Version()[7]; a deliberately corrupted scratch runtime makes the launcher exit non-zero with a one-line reason.
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** Adding a hard launch gate could block Kurt if the manifest is absent on the existing install; first release must write the manifest for an already-extracted runtime (a `-Adopt` switch).
- **Adversarial check:** Read: launch-kurtshape.ps1 96 (Test-Path on freecad.exe is the only runtime check; confirmed), install-runtime.ps1 16-28 (archive SHA256 pinned and verified at 21, then 7z x -y into runtime/freecad-1.1.4 over whatever exists at 27, no manifest written; confirmed), tools/ci/check-runtime-layout.ps1 (five bin files plus a python probe of versions and Mod/Assembly; wired only in .github/workflows/windows-ci.yml 58 and tools/ci/run-local.ps1 17; run-checks.ps1 12 checks only python.exe exists), core.py 102-103 (engine_stamp = version[:3] + OCC version; confirmed), tests/test_runtime_layout.py (pins 1.1.4/7.8.1 and rejects another version for Assembly only). Probe: App.Version()[3]='20260928 (Git shallow)', [7]=engine git hash. What did not hold: the consequence 'engine_changed never fires even though the kernel build differs' ignores core.py 1281-1289, where _check_engine compares doc.getProgramVersion() with f'{v[0]}.{v[1]}R{v[3]}' and sets RebuildReason='saved_by_other_build' for any document saved by a differently stamped FreeCAD build; OPERATION_CONTRACT.md 29 documents this. A patched runtime built on a different date would therefore be detected on open for existing documents; only a build that reproduces the identical revision string would slip through. The scenarios are speculative: nothing in the repo currently replaces runtime files, docs/M1_PROGRESS.md 37-44 explicitly plans to keep the current portable runtime as a fallback and to 'build and test a separate compatible native runtime before switching the launcher', and an interrupted extraction or AV quarantine of a core DLL would make freecad.exe fail at load rather than run with mixed binaries. The absence of a launch-time integrity check is real and cheap to close, but the stated impact is mostly hypothetical and partly already mitigated, so severity drops to low. Classification remains design_weakness because the missing check is verified; the mixed-binary consequence is a hypothesis.

### G-04 Hosted CI has failed on all 13 runs since the first push (Oct 13): 19 ruff errors at HEAD, native-tests cannot find runtime/test-tmp on a fresh checkout, 12/15 GUI recipes crash on the hosted runner, and README says CI is not running

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value high · effort M** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `.github/workflows/windows-ci.yml` · `jobs.lint, jobs.perf, jobs.gui-recipes` · lines .github/workflows/windows-ci.yml 36-39, 59-67, 78-81, 143-150; tests/support.py 102-104; README.md 119, 121
- **Evidence:** windows-ci.yml 150 matrix: `[interface, ui-refinement, extrusion-preview, sketch-behavior, review-interaction, review-lifecycle, review-copy, messages, assembly, part-studio, step-import, assembly-step, review-recovery, review-startup, bench-gui]` (15) vs tools/run_gui_recipe.ps1 2-4 ValidateSet (45 recipes, none of the Oct 13-15 ones: extrusion-edit, history-lifecycle, everyday-ui, dress-up-edit, headless-open, rollback-history, sketch-regions); lines 81 and 146 `continue-on-error: true`; the workflow file last changed Oct 3 (git log d2e1b35); every packaged evidence file carries `"ci": {"status": "not_run", "reason": "push not authorized", "run_url": null}` (tools/record_evidence.py 61; 20 files under validation/2026-10-03-upgrade); README 119 'S19 does not run native suites or hosted CI', 121 'Hosted GUI rendering remains unverified'; `ruff --version` on this machine: command not found, so run-checks.ps1 36 only warns.
- **Scenario:** A commit breaks the everyday-ui or dress-up-edit GUI flow or introduces an undefined name in a FCMacro; Kurt pushes main.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Nothing hosted catches it: the GUI job runs only on schedule/dispatch, skips the new recipes and cannot fail the workflow; lint has never run on Kurt's machine; no artefact anywhere records a hosted result, so 'CI' is a file, not a gate.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Opens the repo, sees every commit marked red, runs `ruff check .` and gets 19 errors, reads README 119 saying hosted CI does not run, and concludes the project's checks are broken or abandoned.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Compare the matrix list with the ValidateSet; grep -r run_url validation docs (all null); `ruff --version` (absent); origin/main == HEAD (f57d592) so a run may exist on GitHub, but nothing local shows it.
- **Proposed change:** (1) Generate the GUI matrix from run_gui_recipe.ps1's recipe table (a tiny script `tools/ci/list-recipes.ps1` + a CI step that fails when the committed matrix differs) or run `-Recipe all`; (2) make `native-tests` + `lint` required and keep `perf`/`gui-recipes` non-blocking but visible, and have tools/record_evidence.py accept `--run-url` so published evidence can record the hosted run; (3) `tools/ci/ensure-ruff.ps1` that installs ruff==0.16.10 into the bundled Python (`python.exe -m pip install --target runtime/tools ruff==0.16.10`) so run-checks -Lint works offline; (4) add the stage-2 recipes to the matrix now.
- **Acceptance:** A hosted run URL is recorded in validation/ evidence for HEAD; the matrix equals the runner's recipe set (CI step asserts); run-checks.ps1 -Lint runs ruff locally with exit 0; README's 'Hosted GUI rendering remains unverified' sentence is replaced by a dated run link.
- **Dependencies:** Kurt confirming GitHub Actions is enabled on kazzurt/KurtShape and whether a Windows runner budget exists.
- **Risk:** Hosted FreeCAD GUI under software OpenGL may be flaky; keep gui-recipes non-blocking until two consecutive scheduled green runs are recorded.
- **Adversarial check:** Read .github/workflows/windows-ci.yml (last changed d2e1b35, 2026-10-03), tools/run_gui_recipe.ps1 1-4 and its $recipes table, run-checks.ps1 32-37, tools/record_evidence.py 56-61, README 119/121, and then checked the real hosted history read-only with `gh run list -R kazzurt/KurtShape` (origin is github.com/kazzurt/KurtShape; HEAD f57d592 == origin/main). The finder's 'unverified' framing is wrong in letter and understated in substance: the workflow has run 13 times since the first push on 2026-10-13 (every push plus the daily 09:23 UTC schedule) and every run failed. Why: (1) `lint` fails with 19 real ruff F401/F841 hits at HEAD (src/kurtshape/core.py:535, reference_tools.py:4,6, selectors.py:1651, ui.py:622, four tests, nine FCMacros); ruff is not on this machine and run-checks.ps1 only warns, so nobody ever saw them. (2) `native-tests` fails on both OS images in ~1 min because tests/support.py 102-104 (isolated_session, added 77af50c 2026-10-04, one day after the workflow) hard-requires ROOT/runtime/test-tmp to exist; a fresh checkout has no such folder (install-runtime.ps1 does not create it and the CI step points KURTSHAPE_TEST_TMP at runtime/ci-local/github-tests/test-tmp instead), so ~25 fixture tests ERROR with 'Native fixtures require the runner's test-tmp directory'. (3) `perf` is skipped on every run (needs native-tests). (4) `gui-recipes` does run on the schedule: on 2026-10-17 3 of 15 passed (review-startup, review-copy, review-recovery) and 12 exited -1073740940 (0xC0000374 heap corruption) on windows-2025 with QT_OPENGL=software; continue-on-error hides this. Matrix facts hold: 15 entries vs 46 ValidateSet entries (finder said 45), none of extrusion-edit, history-lifecycle, everyday-ui, dress-up-edit, headless-open, rollback-history, sketch-regions; 23 packaged evidence files carry ci.status not_run. README 119 'S19 does not run native suites or hosted CI' (blame 4681ddf, 2026-10-13, the day CI started) and 121 'Hosted GUI rendering remains unverified' are therefore false: it runs, and it is red. Classification raised to verified_defect because two concrete, reproducible breakages (lint content, test-tmp precondition) make the gate non-functional, not merely unproven.

### G-05 Validation evidence is local-only and unstamped: newest tracked content is Oct 5, 300 screenshots are gitignored, the Oct 15 stage-2 GUI receipts live in another worktree on an unmerged branch, and no runner except bench_core records the commit

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort M** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `.gitignore` · `validation/** ignore rules` · lines .gitignore 13-15; tools/record_evidence.py 46-61; docs/EVERYDAY_UI.md 61-65; tools/perf/bench_core.py 103-107
- **Evidence:** .gitignore 13-15: `validation/**`, `!validation/**/`, `!validation/**/*.json` so only JSON is tracked: `git ls-files validation | grep -c png` = 0 (305 PNGs exist locally, dated Oct 1-6; 5 PNGs tracked under docs/); newest tracked validation JSON is validation/sketch-presentation/acceptance-summary.json 2026-10-05 16:06; acceptance-summary.json keys contain regression counts, engine, source_sha256 but no git field (checked validation/sketch-presentation and docs/sketch-interaction-reliability/evidence); only validation/2026-10-03-upgrade/* carry `base_commit`; the Oct 15 Windows validation exists only as bullet prose in docs/EVERYDAY_UI.md 61-65 (no validation/everyday-ui, extrusion-edit, history-lifecycle, dress-up-edit or headless-open folder exists); commits 24bab02/1f0001e/f57d592 (Oct 17) add 1,500+ test lines with no GUI evidence and no README change.
- **Scenario:** Kurt (or a reviewer) wants to know whether the current light shell, toolbar or dress-up editor was validated on the code he is running, or wants to see what 'passed' looked like.
- **What Kurt would notice:** The images under validation/ are the only visual record and live on one disk; docs cite receipts that cannot be matched to a commit; the newest features are supported by headless tests only, and README still describes the Oct 8 checkpoint (see docs finding).
- **A newcomer would notice:** Clones the repo, finds validation/ full of JSON that references screenshots which are not there, docs that cite 46/46 and 43/43 with no artefact, and no way to tell which commit any receipt belongs to.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** `git -c safe.directory=... ls-files validation | grep png` → nothing; `find validation -name '*.json' -printf '%TY-%Tm-%Td %p\n' | sort | tail -1` → Oct 5; `ls validation | grep -E 'everyday|extrusion-edit|history-lifecycle|dress-up'` → nothing.
- **Proposed change:** Add a shared `stamp()` in tools/kurtshape_evidence.py returning {git_head, git_dirty, src_sha256, engine (App.Version incl. hash), platform, utc} and call it from run_native_tests.py child reports, validate_modeling_workflows.py, bench_core.py (replacing its own base_commit) and every FCMacro's report writer (a helper `tools/recipe_support.py` imported by the bootstrap macro so recipes do not each reimplement it); make run_gui_recipe.ps1 write `receipt.json` next to the report with the same fields plus pid/exit code. Decide an image policy: track one downscaled (≤200 KB) reference PNG per recipe under validation/<recipe>/reference/ and keep the rest local, or publish a zip per tag. Add `tools/evidence_index.py` that scans validation/**/receipt.json and writes docs/EVIDENCE.md (recipe, date, commit, result, checks).
- **Acceptance:** Every report produced by the runners contains git_head and src_sha256; docs/EVIDENCE.md is regenerated and lists every recipe with a last-run commit ≥ the commit that last touched its macro; a CI step fails when a recipe's receipt is older than its macro.
- **Dependencies:** Build identity finding; maintenance-process finding.
- **Risk:** Repo growth from images; mitigated by the size cap and one-per-recipe rule.
- **Adversarial check:** Read .gitignore 13-19 (validation/** ignored, only folders and *.json re-included), tools/record_evidence.py 46-61, tools/kurtshape_evidence.py, tools/run_native_tests.py, tools/run_gui_recipe.ps1, docs/EVERYDAY_UI.md 55-70 and the git history. Everything checks: `git ls-files validation` = 173 files, 0 PNG (the only tracked PNGs are 5 under docs/ and the icon); 300 PNGs exist locally, dated Oct 1-5 (finder: 305, Oct 1-6). The Oct 16 snapshot 5e9824f committed 28 more validation JSON files, but their content dates are Oct 5 (onshape-workflows accepted_utc 2026-10-05T02:50Z, sketch-references date 2026-10-05), so the newest tracked evidence content is still Oct 5. validation/sketch-presentation/acceptance-summary.json keys are passed, source_unchanged_at_collection, engine, regression, gui_checks, gui, reproduction, limits, superseded_run: no git field; only the 23 files under validation/2026-10-03-upgrade carry base_commit (record_evidence.py 58), and no tracked validation JSON has any git/commit key. tools/perf/bench_core.py 103 is the only runner that stamps rev-parse HEAD. No validation/everyday-ui, extrusion-edit, history-lifecycle, dress-up-edit or headless-open folder exists; the Oct 15 Windows results are prose only (EVERYDAY_UI.md 61-65, commit b2415ff 10:57 PDT). Stronger than claimed: the actual stage-2 GUI runs are not even in this checkout. They sit in the sibling worktree C:\Users\Kurtz\Documents\KURT\CADkz\extrude-review\runtime\ci-gui (gitignored), generated 14:10-14:14 PDT Oct 15 on branch claude/pattern-mirror-references whose commits (44ba9f8, 63cca89, 30f16b2) are not ancestors of main; their recipe-result.json has recipe/passed/pid/exit_code/paths but no commit, so they cannot be tied to b2415ff, 6a32445 or anything else. The three Oct 17 commits add 2,182 test lines (tests/test_pattern_tools.py, test_selector_parameters.py, test_sketch_polylines.py, test_holes.py, test_multi_edge_references.py) with no evidence folder and no README change; f57d592's '554 passed' has no local receipt either (newest runtime/test-tmp report, 02:11 PDT Oct 17, is 536 tests / 531 passed, before the last two commits). Partial mitigations the finder did not weigh: suite-result.json already records source_sha256 for every src/tests/tools file plus engine, and recipe-result.json already records pid and exit code, so the missing piece is only the git identity and an index. Classification and severity stand.

### G-06 Documentation contradicts current code or state in at least six verifiable places (426 vs 558 native tests, Hole as a primary button, --timeout 210, 'publication pending', 'no hosted CI', 'hosted GUI unverified')

**verified defect · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `README.md` · `status paragraphs` · lines README.md 15, 71, 117, 119, 121; ARCHITECTURE.md 5; docs/INTERFACE_PASS.md 43; docs/architecture-responsiveness-upgrade/STATUS_REPORT.md 3, 87, 108
- **Evidence:** (a) README 15 and 117, ARCHITECTURE.md 5: '426 native passes/four opt-in skips' vs static inventory 558 native methods (554 + 4 perf) and f57d592's message '554 passed, 4 opt-in skips'.
