(b) docs/INTERFACE_PASS.md 43: 'Sweep, Loft, Hole, Shell, Draft and Boolean are directly visible' vs README 71 'Hole is omitted from the default buttons' vs src/kurtshape/feature_tools.py 176-178 PRIMARY_TOOLS = revolve, sweep, loft, fillet, chamfer, shell, draft, move_face, linear_pattern, circular_pattern, mirror, boolean, datum_plane (no hole): README is right, INTERFACE_PASS is stale, and README 71 omits Revolve/Fillet/Chamfer/Move face. (c) docs/architecture-responsiveness-upgrade/STATUS_REPORT.md 87 recommends `--timeout 210`; the native suite took 321 s for 536 tests (runtime/test-tmp/regression-run-20261017-021118 suite-result.json) and 177 s for 430 on Oct 8, so the documented command now times out. (d) STATUS_REPORT 3/108 and README 15: 'publication remains pending' / 'publication is pending' while `git rev-parse HEAD origin/main` are both f57d592. (e) docs/M1_PROGRESS.md 3 'Updated October 6' though the file changed Oct 16 (5e9824f) and none of the Oct 13-17 features appear in it. (f) docs/OPERATION_CONTRACT.md 9 'Contract 3.0' and capabilities report contract 3 / revision 0 (headless probe), yet 73 operations now include hole, add_polyline, add_arc, create_plane modes and edit_dress_up added after 3.0 with no revision bump. (g) docs/EXTRUSION_EDIT.md 109 and docs/HISTORY_LIFECYCLE.md 142 record Linux runs with 5 'environmental' test_native_files failures (tests/test_native_files.py 39 asserts the Windows runtime path) next to Windows numbers without a platform column. (h) README never mentions hole/add_polyline/add_arc/create_plane although OPERATION_CONTRACT documents them. Consistent claims checked: README 89 '126 ... plus Ctrl+S/N/O' = 129 entries in shortcuts.json; README 51 three external + four opt-in skips = static count; README 115 four suites = SUITES; ARCHITECTURE 37 'no unified registry' still true (tool_search.py 1-7 builds entries from live QActions).
- **Scenario:** A contributor or agent reads README/INTERFACE_PASS to decide whether Hole is a button, how many tests should pass, or which command to run for a native check.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Wrong expectations (a 'missing' Hole button, a 'failed' 426 vs 558 comparison, a timed-out command), and the review had to reconstruct current truth from commit messages and runtime folders.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Reads README/INTERFACE_PASS, expects a Hole button that is not there and Revolve/Fillet/Chamfer buttons that are not listed, compares a 554-pass run against '426' and thinks the suite changed under them, and is told hosted CI does not exist.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** grep -n '426 native' README.md ARCHITECTURE.md; sed -n 176,178p src/kurtshape/feature_tools.py; sed -n 43p docs/INTERFACE_PASS.md; sed -n 87p docs/architecture-responsiveness-upgrade/STATUS_REPORT.md; git rev-parse HEAD origin/main; run the AST counter in the scratch folder (suite_counts.json).
- **Proposed change:** One documentation pass fixing (a)-(h), then prevent recurrence with `tools/check_docs.py` (run by run-checks.ps1): assert every `N native` figure in README/ARCHITECTURE equals the static inventory or is inside a sentence containing 'historical'; assert every `tools/*.FCMacro`, `validation/...` and `docs/...` path mentioned in docs exists; assert OPERATION_CONTRACT's op table equals Controller.OPS (parse the markdown table + backticked op names, compare with the AST-literal of OPS sets); bump CONTRACT_REVISION whenever OPS changes (a test asserting a committed `ops-fingerprint.json` matches, so adding an op forces the bump).
- **Acceptance:** check_docs.py passes on HEAD; INTERFACE_PASS.md either corrected or moved to archive; README quotes 558/19/33 (or whatever the fresh run reports) with the commit SHA; CONTRACT_REVISION is 1 or higher with a changelog entry listing hole/add_polyline/add_arc/create_plane/edit_dress_up.
- **Dependencies:** Maintenance-process finding for where the single current page lives.
- **Risk:** None beyond doc churn.
- **Adversarial check:** Checked each sub-claim against the files and HEAD. (a) CONFIRMED: README 15 and 117 and ARCHITECTURE.md 5 say '426 native passes/four opt-in skips'; an AST count of every class in tools/run_native_tests.py SUITES gives 558 native test methods (554 + 4 StatusPerformanceTests), 610 across all suites, matching f57d592's '554 passed, 4 opt-in skips'; README 15 presents this under 'Current accepted architecture checkpoint'. (b) CONFIRMED: docs/INTERFACE_PASS.md 43 (last changed 259a61b, Oct 3) says 'Sweep, Loft, Hole, Shell, Draft and Boolean are directly visible'; src/kurtshape/feature_tools.py 176-178 PRIMARY_TOOLS has no hole and does have revolve, fillet, chamfer, move_face, which README 71 (unchanged since cb96ddf, Oct 2) omits while listing the others. (c) CONFIRMED: STATUS_REPORT.md 87 recommends `--suite native ... --timeout 210`; run_native_tests.py 324/456 applies --timeout per suite process (default 1800); the native suite took 321 s on Oct 17 (536 tests) and 287 s on Oct 16 versus 177 s on Oct 8 (430 tests), so the documented command now times out. (d) CONFIRMED and stronger: STATUS_REPORT 3/108 and README 15 say publication is pending, yet HEAD == origin/main and 13 hosted runs were triggered by pushes since Oct 13; README 119 ('S19 does not run ... hosted CI') and 121 ('Hosted GUI rendering remains unverified') are also false, CI runs and fails (see G-04). (e) WEAK: docs/M1_PROGRESS.md 3 'Updated October 6' is true of its content: 5e9824f (Oct 16) only committed Oct-6-dated sections; this is stale coverage, not a contradiction. (f) WEAK: CONTRACT_REVISION has been 0 since 559e034 (Oct 4) while OPERATION_CONTRACT.md changed in 8 commits since Oct 13 and the op set grew to 73 (52 Controller.ARGS keys + studio 12 + reference_tools 6 + modeling_geometry 3, matching the finder's number); hole, add_polyline, add_arc, create_plane and edit_dress_up are documented in the contract, so the doc does not contradict the code; no written policy requires a revision bump and `capabilities` is the discovery mechanism, so this is a design weakness, not a doc defect. (g)/(h) were truncated in the batch file and could not be verified; README 51 (four opt-in perf methods, three external-fixture skips) and 89 (126 + 3 = 129 records in shortcuts.json) check out. Net: six verifiable contradictions, two of the eight weak; classification and severity stand because the false CI/publication statements actively hide a red pipeline and the stale totals/button list mislead the agents that work this repo.

### G-07 Build/test/evidence truth is fragmented across 76 tracked docs and a changelog-shaped README with no contributor entry point; only the bridge side has a current-state page (CADkz/AGENTS.md, outside the repo)

**opportunity · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort M** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: preference · verified

- **Source:** `README.md` · `document structure` · lines README.md 1-25, 104-129
- **Evidence:** README has no heading before 'Launch' at line 25; lines 3-23 are dated status paragraphs (M1, S18, Oct 6, Oct 5, Q03, Oct 2 review); verification instructions are spread over 104-129 with pointers to STATUS_REPORT (Oct 8), INTERFACE_PASS (Oct 2-3), M1_PROGRESS ('Updated October 6'), long-run/STATUS ('PAUSED', Oct 4); `grep -rhoE '[0-9]{2,3} native( passes| tests| methods| cases| regressions)' --include=*.md` finds 18 distinct totals across ≥15 files (426, 407, 141, 421, 159, 403, 187, 164, 150, 250, 170, 430 ...); docs/ holds 78 .md files and 8.3 MB; there is no CONTRIBUTING.md and no docs index.
- **Scenario:** Kurt returns after a week, or a new agent session starts, and needs: what is the current build, what works, how to run checks, where evidence is.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Hours of reconstruction (this review needed git log, runtime/test-tmp and commit messages to date the state); stale pages are indistinguishable from current ones; every milestone report re-states totals that immediately go stale.
- **A newcomer would notice:** README opens with eleven paragraphs of milestone status before saying how to launch; there is nothing that says what is current versus historical, no CONTRIBUTING, and four candidate 'status' pages (M1_PROGRESS, STATUS_REPORT, INTERFACE_PASS, long-run/STATUS) with no way to pick one.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Open README.md and try to find 'how do I run the tests' and 'what is the latest validated commit' within one screen; open docs/ and pick which of M1_PROGRESS.md, STATUS_REPORT.md, long-run/STATUS.md, INTERFACE_PASS.md is current.
- **Proposed change:** Adopt a small maintenance process: (1) docs/CURRENT.md generated by `tools/evidence_index.py` + `tools/check_docs.py` (never hand-edited): HEAD SHA/date, engine hash, suite totals from the last run report, GUI recipe table with last-run commit/date/result, open limitations list (hand-maintained block), launcher name; README links to it in its first paragraph. (2) README reorganised to What / Launch / Model / Assistant / Verify / Contribute (≤ 80 lines), with the dated paragraphs moved to CHANGELOG.md (one line per commit group) and milestone reports moved under docs/archive/<date>-<name>/ with a one-line index in docs/README.md. (3) 'Keep current' set = README, ARCHITECTURE, OPERATION_CONTRACT, DESIGN, docs/CURRENT.md, docs/EVIDENCE.md, the per-feature guides (EXTRUSION_EDIT, FILLET_CHAMFER_EDIT, EVERYDAY_UI, HISTORY_LIFECYCLE, STEP_IMPORT, SOLIDWORKS_NAVIGATION, ONSHAPE_SHORTCUTS); everything else is archive and gets a top banner 'Historical (<date>, commit <sha>)'. (4) Release ritual per tag: run-checks -Full, the recipe set, evidence_index, update green copy to the tag, bump CONTRACT_REVISION if OPS changed.
- **Acceptance:** A newcomer can answer 'current commit, test totals, how to run checks, where evidence is' from docs/CURRENT.md alone; README under 80 lines with headings; check_docs.py enforces that no non-archive doc quotes a native total other than the inventory; every archived page carries the banner.
- **Dependencies:** Evidence stamping finding (CURRENT.md reads receipts).
- **Risk:** Archiving may hide context agents rely on; keep the archive index searchable and keep links working.
- **Adversarial check:** Read README.md in full and counted: no heading before '## Launch' at line 25; lines 3-23 are seven dated status paragraphs (U2, extrusion edit, fillet/chamfer, everyday UI, history lifecycle, M1, S18/S19, Oct 6, Oct 5, Q03, Oct 2 review); verification instructions are spread over 104-129 and point to STATUS_REPORT (prepared Oct 8), INTERFACE_PASS (Oct 2-3) and M1_PROGRESS (content Oct 6). Confirmed there is no CONTRIBUTING.md, CHANGELOG.md, docs/README.md or docs/INDEX.md. Corrected figures: 76 tracked .md files under docs (82 tracked .md in the repo), 6.9 MB tracked; the finder's 78 files / 8.3 MB included the untracked docs/fable-deep-review output of this very review. `grep -hoE '[0-9]{2,3} native( passes| tests| methods| cases| regressions)'` over tracked markdown finds 13 distinct totals (15, 61, 141, 150, 159, 164, 187, 250, 403, 407, 421, 426, 430) across 16 files, not 18. The scenario ('new agent session needs current build/what works/how to run checks/where evidence is') is real: this verification also needed git log, gh run list and runtime/test-tmp to date the state. What the finder missed: C:\Users\Kurtz\Documents\KURT\CADkz\AGENTS.md (pulled in by CADkz/CLAUDE.md) is a maintained, dated current-state page for the bridge and modeling side ('Known gaps, as of 2026-10-16' plus 2026-10-17 additions, restart requirements, worked examples), and it is the entry point Kurt's agents actually use, so 'no single current state page' is only true for build/test/evidence state. It is itself starting to grow the same changelog-shaped paragraph, which supports the proposed process rather than refuting it. Opportunity classification is honest; severity stays medium because every agent session Kurt runs pays the reconstruction tax and this fragmentation is the mechanism that produced the G-06 contradictions; title corrected for the numbers and the AGENTS.md scope.

### G-08 Eleven GUI macros (navigation, named cameras, viewport actions, view selection, select-other, native-task guards, theme, layout) are unreachable from run_gui_recipe.ps1; their receipts are Oct 2 and toolbar-validation.FCMacro now KeyErrors on the Oct 13-14 toolbar layout

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort M** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `tools/run_gui_recipe.ps1` · `$recipes table` · lines tools/run_gui_recipe.ps1 2-4, 22-69; tools/toolbar-validation.FCMacro 116, 400; src/kurtshape/ui.py 501-504, 541-546
- **Evidence:** 55 FCMacros tracked; 45 referenced by run_gui_recipe.ps1; orphaned: appearance-validation, theme-validation, toolbar-validation, navigation-validation, viewport-actions-validation, view-selection-validation, q03-status-validation (own wrapper tools/validate-q03-status.ps1), line-endpoint-freeze-validation, general-actions-select-other-validation/-probe, shortcut-command-probe, bench_gui (referenced only as `perf\bench_gui.FCMacro`). docs/INTERFACE_PASS.md 21-33 and 47 and docs/M1_PROGRESS.md 213-225 still cite their reports (72 theme checks, 133 layout checks / 21 screenshots, 40 viewport, 20 view-selection, 16 navigation); seven legacy tools/validate-*.ps1 and three root validate-*.ps1 wrappers (Oct 3) predate the runner; the toolbar changed on Oct 14 (a558615: Undo/Redo removed, Extrude unified) and the theme on Oct 4.
- **Scenario:** Kurt asks whether the light theme, grayscale toolbar, SolidWorks navigation or named-camera round trip still pass on the build he runs.
- **What Kurt would notice:** No runnable path answers it: the only receipts are Oct 2-3 images that predate the theme repair and toolbar change (historical era per the review brief), and the macros probably no longer match the toolbar (hypothesis: toolbar-validation.FCMacro expects Undo/Redo buttons).
- **A newcomer would notice:** Nothing in the app itself. A SolidWorks user would feel a navigation or camera regression immediately, but this finding is about why such a regression would not be caught before it reached them.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** comm -23 <(ls tools/*.FCMacro | xargs -n1 basename | sort) <(grep -oE '[a-z0-9_-]+\.FCMacro' tools/run_gui_recipe.ps1 | sort -u); then `tools/run_gui_recipe.ps1 -Recipe toolbar` → ValidateSet rejects it.
- **Proposed change:** Register each still-relevant macro in the $recipes table with a report path (appearance → validation/appearance-pass/layout-test-result.json, theme → theme-result.json, toolbar → validation/toolbar-pass/toolbar-test-result.json, navigation → validation/navigation-test-result.json, viewport-actions, view-selection, q03-status, line-endpoint-freeze, select-other), run them once on HEAD, fix or retire failures, delete the root validate-*.ps1 and tools/validate-*.ps1 wrappers (keep validate-q03-status only if it does something the runner cannot), and move probe-only macros (shortcut-command-probe, select-other-probe) under tools/probes/. Add `-Recipe all` (or `tools/run_all_recipes.ps1`) that iterates the table and writes a summary.
- **Acceptance:** Every tracked FCMacro is either in the runner table, under tools/probes/, or deleted; `run_gui_recipe.ps1 -Recipe all` completes with per-recipe results; docs/EVIDENCE.md shows a HEAD-dated pass for theme, toolbar and navigation.
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** Some macros may depend on removed UI (Undo/Redo buttons, 'Feature settings' dock) and need edits; budget half a day.
- **Adversarial check:** Read tools/run_gui_recipe.ps1 2-4 and 22-69, the eleven unreferenced macros, ui.py 490-560, docs/INTERFACE_PASS.md, docs/M1_PROGRESS.md, docs/NATIVE_TOOL_CATALOG.md and the git log. HELD: comm of tools/*.FCMacro (54 files, not 55) against the runner's references gives 11 orphans (appearance, theme, toolbar, navigation, viewport-actions, view-selection, q03-status, line-endpoint-freeze, select-other validation+probe, shortcut-command-probe). Their tracked receipts (toolbar-pass/toolbar-test-result.json, appearance-pass/theme-result.json, navigation-test-result.json, viewport-actions-test-result.json, view-selection-result.json) all date from b65f066 on Oct 2. The finder's hypothesis about toolbar-validation is now verified by reading, not guessed: line 116 builds custom_controls from document_bar actions whose text is in {New,Open,Save,Undo,Redo}, and line 400 indexes controls['New']..controls['Redo']; since 4681ddf (Oct 13) New/Open live in the Document menu (ui.py 501-504) and since a558615 (Oct 14) Undo/Redo do too (ui.py 541-546), only 'Save' is collected and the guard section raises KeyError. Seven tools/validate-*.ps1 plus three root validate-*.ps1 wrappers are Oct 3 pre-runner scripts (validate-view-interaction.ps1 is a one-line shim to the runner; validate-q03-status.ps1 is a working Oct 4 isolated runner with its own 17-check gate). DID NOT HOLD: (1) bench_gui is registered ('bench-gui' -> perf\bench_gui.FCMacro, present at tools/perf/bench_gui.FCMacro), so the count is eleven, not twelve. (2) 'No runnable path answers it' is overstated: registered recipes already cover parts of the question - 'theme-startup' (18 checks on light-theme recovery and the Restore light appearance button), 'everyday-ui' (checks 'Toolbars no longer carry Undo/Redo buttons', 'Only one primary Extrude tool', search), 'view-interaction' (hover pixels, face selection, deterministic orbit); and INTERFACE_PASS.md 51 / NATIVE_TOOL_CATALOG.md 38-40 document the manual Start-Process invocation for the legacy macros, while INTERFACE_PASS.md 15 already labels that section 'Superseded October 3' with historical counts. What truly has no registered recipe is the SolidWorks gesture table and named-camera round trip (navigation-validation), clipping/construction layers/tessellation (viewport-actions), normal-view-on-datum/flip/hide (view-selection), select-other ray hits, and the native-task guard matrix in toolbar-validation. (3) line-endpoint-freeze-validation was touched on Oct 16 (5e9824f), so it is a recent ad-hoc macro rather than a stale one. Net: the design weakness is real and the toolbar macro is provably broken at HEAD, but the title's count and the consequence are inflated, and the effort is M rather than S once fixing or retiring eleven macros after the Oct 13-14 UI changes is included.

### G-09 Coverage gaps: real window-close path, extrusion-preview worker on shutdown, DPI matrix, settings schema and contract inventory have no automated tests (worker shutdown for geometry check and STEP export is already covered by GUI recipes)

**opportunity · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort M** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: preference · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.eventFilter (QEvent.Close), Panel.shutdown, start()` · lines ui.py 4273-4276, 4279-4309, 4328; settings.py 10-16; tools/run_gui_recipe.ps1 93-95; tools/geometry-check-lifecycle-validation.FCMacro 318-333; tools/step-export-validation.FCMacro 872-881
- **Evidence:** No test imports kurtshape.ui (`grep -lE 'kurtshape\.ui' tests/*.py` → none) although ui.py is 4,341 lines; shutdown() (4279-4309) cancels STEP/geometry workers, stops three timers, closes 12 helpers and the bridge; the Close guard (4271-4277) runs finish_sketch; `main.destroyed.connect(panel.shutdown)` (4328) is the only trigger; GUI recipes end with `QApplication.quit` (gui-validation.FCMacro 27/311, history-lifecycle-validation.FCMacro 261) and check exit code, not that workers exited or logs are clean; DPI: tests/test_sketch_cursor.py 97-125 cover cursor DPR only, startup.py has no test, docs/INTERFACE_PASS.md 5 'The tested display scale is 150%'; preferences: settings.py 10-16 has no schema version; headless coverage of topology/parameters/rollback/cancellation/workers/recovery/focus is strong (see architecture_notes).
- **Scenario:** Kurt closes the window with an active sketch while an exact preview child and a STEP export child are running, or opens the app on a 100%/200% monitor, or a future build renames a settings key.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Regressions in the close path show up as orphaned freecad python children, lost recovery pairs or a hung exit; DPI regressions show up only on Kurt's 150% screen; preference breakage silently resets state.
- **A newcomer would notice:** On a 100% or 200% monitor they might hit cursor/snap/toolbar layout glitches that no recipe has ever run at; otherwise nothing visible.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Verification needed: write the recipes below; today there is nothing to run.
- **Proposed change:** Prioritised missing tests: (1) GUI recipe `shutdown-lifecycle`: open the plate, start a sketch, start an Extrude preview, trigger an eligible STEP export, then close the main window; assert the finish-sketch path, that every child pid recorded by the worker modules has exited within 10 s, no 'Traceback' in freecad.log, recovery pair published, exit code 0. (2) GUI recipe `tab-close-during-worker`: close the document tab while preview/check/export children run; assert no stale publication (pairs with test_step_export_jobs.py 634 at GUI level). (3) DPI matrix: add `-ScaleFactor` to run_gui_recipe.ps1 setting QT_SCALE_FACTOR (already in the restored env list) and run startup-view, theme-startup and sketch-origin-snap at 1.0/1.5/2.0 with DPR-relative pixel assertions. (4) tests/test_preferences.py (headless): settings.json with unknown keys, wrong types and a legacy shape loads without data loss and persists a schema_version; shortcuts.user.json with one stale id keeps the others (see shortcut finding). (5) tests/test_contract_inventory.py: capabilities.operations equals the op set parsed from OPERATION_CONTRACT.md and equals the union of core/studio/reference_tools/modeling_geometry OPS. (6) A headless `Panel` construction smoke test using FreeCADGui offscreen (`QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen`, `FreeCADGui.showMainWindow()`) that builds Panel(Controller()), calls shutdown(), and asserts timers inactive and event filters removed; if FreeCADGui cannot start offscreen, make it a hidden-GUI recipe instead.
- **Acceptance:** Recipes (1)-(3) pass on HEAD and are in the runner table and CI matrix; tests (4)-(5) are in SUITES; a deliberate regression (comment out `self.extrusion_preview.close()` in shutdown) makes recipe (1) fail.
- **Dependencies:** Orphaned-recipe finding (runner changes), build identity (for log headers).
- **Risk:** Hidden-GUI recipes are slower (minutes); keep them out of run-checks default and in the scheduled set.
- **Adversarial check:** Read: ui.py 4255-4341 (Close guard is actually 4273-4276, shutdown 4279-4309, main.destroyed.connect at 4328 as cited), tests/ listing (49 files; grep confirms none imports kurtshape.ui), tests/test_sketch_cursor.py 90-130 (DPR cursor coverage as cited), settings.py 1-45 (no schema version; invalid JSON/non-dict silently resets to {}), startup.py (no unit test), docs/INTERFACE_PASS.md line 5 ('tested display scale is 150%'), tools/run_gui_recipe.ps1 1-115 and 159-185. What did not hold: (1) 'ui.py has no automated protection' is wrong in spirit: 13 GUI recipe macros under tools/ import kurtshape.ui and run through run_gui_recipe.ps1, and 20 of the 52 recipes are devicePixelRatio-aware; (2) 'recipes end with QApplication.quit and check exit code, not that workers exited' is false for the two worker kinds the scenario names: tools/geometry-check-lifecycle-validation.FCMacro 318-333 starts a geometry check child, calls panel.shutdown(), asserts the job is invalidated, polling stops, the child exits and releases its archive, then asserts 'Every owned child has confirmed cleanup' and records worker_pids; tools/step-export-validation.FCMacro 872-881 does the same for a STEP export child (shutdown invalidates export without destination writes; cleanup_complete and pid recorded); closing the source document mid-job is also covered (geometry-check-lifecycle 311-316, step-export 861, test_step_export_jobs.py 634, test_geometry_check_jobs.py 529); (3) 'QT_SCALE_FACTOR already in the restored env list' is false: run_gui_recipe.ps1 line 95 restores QT_QPA_PLATFORM, not QT_SCALE_FACTOR; (4) startup-view-validation.FCMacro 78-104 already asserts maximize on the primary screen. What held: nothing exercises the real exit path (QEvent.Close guard with an active sketch, then main.destroyed -> shutdown, whose Python slot may or may not run before interpreter teardown), the extrusion preview worker is not covered on shutdown (extrusion-preview-validation only closes documents), every recipe runs at the host's DPI only, settings.json has no schema version and a corrupt file silently drops project_roots/recent_projects, and no test compares capabilities.operations to OPERATION_CONTRACT.md (test_runtime_layout 44-50 checks only the contract constants). The classification 'opportunity' is honest; severity high is not, since the named worker-orphan scenario is already guarded and the uncovered paths are annoyance-level.

### G-10 One unknown id in shortcuts.user.json discards every user rebinding, the message does not name it, and the help dialog's Rebind/Reset cannot clear it

**verified defect · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/shortcuts.py` · `load_registry, ShortcutRouter.__init__` · lines src/kurtshape/shortcuts.py 49-51, 92-96, 375-389
- **Evidence:** shortcuts.py 50: `if set(overrides["bindings"]) - known: raise ValueError("Unknown shortcut override")`; 91-96: `except (ValueError, OSError) as exc: self.status_callback("User shortcuts rejected: " + str(exc)); self.registry = load_registry(registry_path, ROOT / "runtime" / "no-user-shortcuts")`. Headless probe (scratch probe_shortcut_override.py): a file with one unknown id raises ValueError; constructing ShortcutRouter with it yields 129 default entries and the message 'User shortcuts rejected: Unknown shortcut override'; a valid override in the same file is not applied.
- **Scenario:** Kurt rebinds a few keys; a later build renames or removes one shortcut id in shortcuts.json (the registry has changed in 617c537/a558615 era commits), or Kurt hand-edits the file.
- **What Kurt would notice:** All his rebindings stop working at once with a single Messages line that does not say which id was unknown; the help dialog's Rebind path then rewrites the file from scratch.
- **A newcomer would notice:** A SolidWorks user who rebinds a handful of keys to their habits is the most likely rebinder; the same all-or-nothing loss and dead-end dialog would read as the app forgetting their settings.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Write <session>/shortcuts.user.json = {"schema_version":1,"bindings":{"sketch.pierce":"Ctrl+Alt+G","feature.gone":"Ctrl+Alt+H"}}; start the app (or run the probe): pierce stays Shift+G and Messages shows the rejection.
- **Proposed change:** In load_registry, collect unknown ids into `registry["ignored_overrides"]` instead of raising, apply the known ones, and have ShortcutRouter report 'Ignored unknown shortcut overrides: feature.gone' once; keep raising for schema/JSON errors; show ignored entries greyed in the help dialog with a 'Remove' button. Add a RouterTests case.
- **Acceptance:** tests/test_shortcuts.py RouterTests: stale + valid overrides → valid applied, stale listed in ignored_overrides, one status message naming it; existing conflict test still raises.
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** None.
- **Adversarial check:** Read src/kurtshape/shortcuts.py 40-96 and 366-390, tests/test_shortcuts.py 23-36 and 122-128, docs/ONSHAPE_SHORTCUTS.md 7, and the git history of shortcuts.json and shortcuts.py; ran a headless probe under the bundled pythonw.exe with an isolated KURTSHAPE_SESSION_DIR under the scratch folder. HELD exactly as stated: with {sketch.pierce: Ctrl+Alt+G, feature.gone: Ctrl+Alt+H} the router emitted 'User shortcuts rejected: Unknown shortcut override', loaded 129 default entries and left sketch.pierce at Shift+G (overridden None); the same file with only the valid id applied Ctrl+Alt+G (overridden True). The code path is lines 49-51 (set difference, raise without naming the id) and 92-96 (whole-file fallback to runtime/no-user-shortcuts). No test exercises partial application; test_shortcuts.py 23-36 asserts only the conflict ValueError. STRONGER than stated: the help dialog cannot repair the file. change() at 375 re-reads the existing file so the stale id stays in data, stages the edit, then validates the stage with load_registry (383), which raises the same error, so every Rebind and Reset fails with 'Shortcut unchanged: Unknown shortcut override' and os.replace never runs; the finder's 'rewrites the file from scratch' is wrong - the file is untouched and only a hand edit recovers. WEAKER than stated: the scenario's premise that the registry changed in the 617c537/a558615 era is wrong; shortcuts.json last changed in df26c91 (Oct 3) and a558615 only added a search_provider hook to shortcuts.py, so no id rename has happened in this repo's history. The realistic triggers today are a typo in a hand edit or a future id rename. ONSHAPE_SHORTCUTS.md 7 documents 'invalid startup files fall back to usable defaults', so the fail-closed fallback is intentional design; the defect is that one unknown id poisons the known ones, the message names nothing, and the UI is locked out of fixing it. Classification verified_defect and severity medium stand: when it bites, all rebindings vanish at once, the message gives no clue which id, and the built-in Rebind/Reset path cannot clear it; the narrow trigger lowers priority, not impact.

### G-11 Support diagnostics are thin: no crash handler, kurtshape.log only sees unknown bridge exceptions, Copy all and the diagnostics op carry no identity or log context

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value high · effort M** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/core.py` · `Controller.dispatch (diagnostics op, operation_failed logging)` · lines src/kurtshape/core.py 1461-1463, 1786-1793, 1402; src/kurtshape/ui.py 437, 731-734; src/kurtshape/general_actions.py 268-270; launch-kurtshape.ps1 41
- **Evidence:** core.py 1785-1794 writes a traceback to <session>/kurtshape.log only when `not known_error` inside dispatch; the live runtime/kurtshape.log is 2,168 bytes dated Oct 2 (size/date only read); ui.py has no sys.excepthook or faulthandler (`grep -nE 'excepthook|faulthandler' src/kurtshape/ui.py` → none); diagnostics op (1461-1463, probe) returns timings, engine, recovery_directory, checkpoint_interval_seconds; Copy all (ui.py 731, 747-748) copies retained messages only; problem reports go to validation/user-reports (general_actions.py 268-270), which is gitignored and outside any session dir.
- **Scenario:** Kurt hits a native crash or a Qt-callback exception during a sketch and pastes 'Copy all' into chat.
- **What Kurt would notice:** The paste lacks build/engine identity, document ids/revisions and the log tail; a hard crash leaves only FreeCAD's own log; an agent cannot correlate the report with a commit or session.
- **A newcomer would notice:** After a crash or a silent toolbar failure they would have nothing to attach to a bug report beyond the Messages text; Report a problem writes a JSON they cannot easily find (validation/user-reports) and that is not bundled with logs.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Trigger any exception in a toolbar callback (e.g. a failing `self.operation` path): it appears in FreeCAD's Report view / freecad-launch.log, not in kurtshape.log; run {"op":"diagnostics"} and note the four keys.
- **Proposed change:** At start(): `faulthandler.enable(open(session/'crash-<pid>.log','w'))` and a sys.excepthook that appends to kurtshape.log and calls panel.notify; prefix Copy all output with a header block (build identity, engine incl. git hash, session dir, open documents with ids/revisions, active sketch, worker pids, UTC time); extend diagnostics with build, session_dir, log_tail (last 50 lines), retained_messages (last 50), workers (pids/states), last_failures; add a Document-menu 'Save diagnostic bundle…' that zips kurtshape.log, freecad-launch.log, settings.json, shortcuts.user.json, the recovery manifest list and the Copy-all text into the chosen folder.
- **Acceptance:** A test asserts diagnostics contains build/session_dir/log_tail; a GUI recipe injects an exception in a callback and asserts it lands in kurtshape.log and Messages; the Copy-all text starts with the identity header.
- **Dependencies:** Build identity finding.
- **Risk:** Privacy: the bundle contains paths; keep it a user-chosen explicit action and say so in the dialog.
- **Adversarial check:** Read src/kurtshape/core.py 1458-1463 and 1784-1798, ui.py 430-446, 656, 720-760 and 1287-1312, general_actions.py 255-272, .gitignore 19, launch-kurtshape.ps1 36-47, README.md 75 and docs/OPERATION_CONTRACT.md 95; grepped src/kurtshape, tools/*.py, launch-kurtshape.ps1 and KurtShape.FCMacro for excepthook/faulthandler. HELD: the diagnostics op returns exactly timings, engine, recovery_directory and checkpoint_interval_seconds (1461-1463), and OPERATION_CONTRACT.md 95 defines it that narrowly ('bounded timing samples and checkpoint location'), so the thin payload is by contract. kurtshape.log is appended only at 1786-1793 for exceptions that are not OperationError/AssemblyError inside dispatch; nothing else writes it. No sys.excepthook or faulthandler exists anywhere in the shell or launcher; ui.tool() at 437 connects callbacks straight to QAction.triggered with no try/except, so an exception in a toolbar callback propagates to Python's default hook (FreeCAD Report view / the --log-file freecad-launch.log that launch-kurtshape.ps1 41 passes), never to Messages or kurtshape.log. copy_messages (731-734) copies messages.toPlainText() only, and no startup message carries App.Version(), a commit hash or the session dir (grep found none in ui.py). Problem reports write to validation/user-reports (268-270), gitignored at .gitignore 19 and outside the session dir. PARTLY REDUNDANT in the proposal: per-document last failures are already exposed by inspect as last_failed_attempt (core.py 1402), and a checkpoint/recovery system plus the review-recovery-crash recipe already handle crash data recovery; what is missing is crash diagnostics and identity, not recovery. UNVERIFIED: the 2,168-byte Oct 2 size of the live runtime/kurtshape.log (I did not stat the live runtime). Classification design_weakness and severity medium are right: nothing is broken, but the gap has a recurring cost in this project's agent-driven workflow - AGENTS.md warns the running app keeps the code it started with, and this very review notes Kurt's app is on an older build, so a Copy all paste without build identity routinely costs a round trip. Effort is S for faulthandler/excepthook/header and diagnostics keys, M if the diagnostic-bundle menu item is included.

### G-12 Test harness gaps: source snapshot omits validate_modeling_workflows.py and shortcuts.json, the workflows suite is a fixed six-case Oct 4 validator, the native-command check reads an Oct 3 probe JSON, and the ungranted scratch deliberately lands in CADkz

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value low · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `tools/run_native_tests.py` · `SUITES['workflows'], source_snapshot` · lines tools/run_native_tests.py 67, 73-83, 168, 268-279; tools/validate_modeling_workflows.py 639-641; tests/test_shortcuts.py 122-128; tests/support.py 183; src/kurtshape/settings.py 17-18; tools/ci/run-local.ps1 12-14
- **Evidence:** source_snapshot() hashes src/kurtshape/*.py, tests/*.py and seven tool files but not tools/validate_modeling_workflows.py, tools/*.FCMacro, shortcuts.json or resources/; SUITES['workflows'] = () (67) and the suite runs validate_modeling_workflows.main() (268-279) whose six cases (639-641: linear/circular pattern, offset/angled plane, sweep, loft) were last edited Oct 4 and exercise none of the ops added since; tests/test_shortcuts.py 122-128 validates native command ids against validation/onshape-shortcuts-command-probe.json (Oct 2) instead of Gui.listCommands(); tests/support.py 183 falls back to ROOT.parents[1] (CADkz) for the 'ungranted' scratch, creating temporary folders in Kurt's CADkz directory during ordinary runs (acknowledged in tools/ci/run-local.ps1 comments).
- **Scenario:** An edit to shortcuts.json or the workflows validator during a run; a runtime command rename; a reviewer wondering why a folder appears in CADkz while tests run.
- **What Kurt would notice:** The source-unchanged guard can report true while inputs changed; the workflows suite gives a fixed 415-check number that never grows with new ops; the command probe can drift silently.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Nothing.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Read source_snapshot (73-83); `grep -n 'def case_\|for name, function' tools/validate_modeling_workflows.py`; `grep -n ROOT.parents tests/support.py`.
- **Proposed change:** Add shortcuts.json, resources/**, tools/validate_modeling_workflows.py and tools/*.FCMacro to source_snapshot; make test_shortcuts assert against `Gui.listCommands()` when FreeCADGui is importable and fall back to the probe JSON otherwise; either fold the six workflow cases into a unittest class (so they count and get receipts) or add cases for hole/polyline/arc/create_plane/dress-up edits; create the ungranted scratch under `%TEMP%\kurtshape-ungranted` (system temp outside the session) instead of CADkz.
- **Acceptance:** Changing shortcuts.json mid-run flips source_unchanged to false; workflows suite reports ≥ 10 cases including one per newer op; no folder is created under CADkz during run-checks.
- **Dependencies:** None.
- **Risk:** Hashing resources/ adds a few ms per run.
- **Adversarial check:** Read tools/run_native_tests.py 55-83, 162-172, 255-295 and 440, tools/validate_modeling_workflows.py 637-660, tests/test_shortcuts.py 122-128, tests/support.py 181-190, tests/test_review_followup.py 393-400, src/kurtshape/settings.py 17-21, tools/ci/run-local.ps1 1-30 and run-checks.ps1 26. HELD: source_snapshot hashes src/kurtshape/**/*.py, tests/**/*.py, the runner, validate_offline.py, kurtshape_evidence.py, native_validation_snapshot.py and three perf files, but not tools/validate_modeling_workflows.py (which the workflows suite imports and executes at 268-270) nor shortcuts.json (which load_registry reads), so source_unchanged can be true while those inputs changed mid-run. SUITES['workflows'] = () (67) and main() runs the same six cases (639-641: linear/circular pattern, offset/angled plane, sweep, loft), last edited in 559e034 on Oct 4. test_all_native_ids_exist_in_pinned_runtime_probe checks only SketcherWorkbench commands from validation/onshape-shortcuts-command-probe.json (Oct 3). ungranted_scratch tries KURTSHAPE_TEST_OUTSIDE, tempfile.gettempdir(), then ROOT.parents[1]; default roots are {CADkz/Codex, CADkz/projects} (settings.py 18) and the runner overrides TEMP to runtime/test-tmp/.../temporary inside CADkz/Codex, so under run-checks.ps1 (which does not set KURTSHAPE_TEST_OUTSIDE) the fallback lands in CADkz, and run-local.ps1 14 sets KURTSHAPE_TEST_OUTSIDE to CADkz explicitly with the comment at 12-13 - deliberate, one test (test_review_followup.py 394), short-lived prefix review-project-folder-. CORRECTIONS: tools/*.FCMacro are GUI recipes this runner never executes, so their absence from the snapshot is not a guard weakness; the ops added since Oct 4 do have unittest coverage in other suites (hole 1 file, add_polyline 2, add_arc 2, create_plane 5, edit_dress_up 2), so 'exercise none of the ops added since' is true of the workflows suite only and the regression exposure is small; the '415-check number' is not supported by what I read (the tracked validation/onshape-workflows/native-regression.json carries per-case check counts summing to 1245 across its entries); and the proposed Gui.listCommands() assertion will not work in the headless unittest process because workbench commands register only when a workbench activates in a GUI session, which is what the orphaned shortcut-command-probe.FCMacro (G-08) exists for. The proposed %TEMP% scratch must read the real system temp before the runner overrides TEMP. Classification design_weakness and severity low are right; this is maintenance hygiene with no user-facing behavior.

### G-13 Linux probe runs show five Windows-path test_native_files setUp failures that must be explained in prose each time; runner report.json has no platform field

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value low · effort S** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `tests/test_native_files.py` · `NativeFileBehaviorTests.setUp runtime path assertion` · lines tests/test_native_files.py 39-40; tools/run_native_tests.py 286-289, 461-466, 473-474; tests/support.py 190
- **Evidence:** test_native_files.py 39-40 asserts `Path(App.getHomePath()).resolve() == support.ROOT / 'runtime' / 'freecad-1.1.4' / 'FreeCAD_1.1.4-Windows-x86_64-py311'`; docs/EXTRUSION_EDIT.md 109 '5 fail ... environmental on Linux', docs/HISTORY_LIFECYCLE.md 142 '5 environmental test_native_files Windows-path failures', docs/EVERYDAY_UI.md 58 and docs/HISTORY_LIFECYCLE.md 116 record recipes that 'segfault during setup in this harness'; run_native_tests.py reports carry engine but no platform field.
- **Scenario:** A cloud agent validates a branch on Linux and reports numbers; Kurt later compares them with his Windows run.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Numbers that are not comparable are read as regressions or passes; 'environmental' failures must be explained in prose each time.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Nothing; this is contributor/maintainer tooling with no user-visible effect.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Read the cited lines; run the native suite on any non-Windows host.
- **Proposed change:** Mark Windows-specific assertions with `@unittest.skipUnless(sys.platform == 'win32', 'platform: Windows runtime layout')` and teach run_native_tests.py a 'platform:' skip prefix that --require-native rejects on Windows only; add `platform` (sys.platform, OS version) to child reports and receipts; docs/EVIDENCE.md shows a platform column.
- **Acceptance:** A Linux run reports 0 failures with N 'platform:' skips; a Windows run with --require-native still rejects any such skip; receipts contain platform.
- **Dependencies:** Evidence stamping finding.
- **Risk:** None.
- **Adversarial check:** What held: tests/test_native_files.py:39-40 (blame 77af50c, 2026-10-04, unchanged at HEAD f57d592) asserts in setUp that App.getHomePath() resolves to runtime/freecad-1.1.4/FreeCAD_1.1.4-Windows-x86_64-py311, so all 5 methods of NativeFileBehaviorTests fail before their real checks on any other host; the docs record exactly those 5 'environmental' failures on four Linux cloud runs (docs/EXTRUSION_EDIT.md 109 and 131, docs/HISTORY_LIFECYCLE.md 112 and 142) and Linux recipe segfaults (HISTORY_LIFECYCLE.md 116, EVERYDAY_UI.md 58). tools/run_native_tests.py child_suite (286-289) and main (473-474) put only engine={FreeCAD, OCCT} in report.json; no sys.platform anywhere in the runner. There is no platform skip prefix: --require-native rejects every skip reason not starting with 'external fixture:' or 'perf:' (run_native_tests.py 461-466), and support.py:190 already emits an ad-hoc 'environment:' skip the runner does not recognise. No commit after 77af50c touched this. What did not hold: 'Windows-only by construction' is overstated. The Python suites are portable (os.name branches for the interpreter name in test_borrowed_preview 345, test_geometry_check_jobs 251, test_history_lifecycle 402, test_step_export_jobs 241; 427-442 native passes on Linux per the docs); only these 5 path assertions and the PowerShell recipe runner pin Windows. Platform is not entirely absent from evidence either: per-test receipts carry engine.home (test_native_files 65-67, perf_fixture.py 116) and every timing report carries hardware.platform = platform.platform() (perf_fixture.py 117-118, tools/perf/bench_core.py 109-110, tools/record_evidence.py 64-65, tools/validate_review_performance.py 21), so the numbers that would actually be compared are already tagged. Every Linux table in docs is captioned 'Linux cloud session' / 'Windows not run', so the consequence 'read as regressions' has not occurred; the project policy is explicitly Windows-only with Linux as a probe tool (docs/2026-10-03-claude-review/REVIEW.md 249, CODEX_PROMPT.md 75, 105). The one real cost the finder understates: because setUp fails first, Linux runs give zero coverage of the five native-file checks, and a genuine sixth-cause failure could hide behind the habitual '5 environmental' caption.

### G-14 Performance checks are opt-in and record-only; the planned switch to --enforce never happened, and the existing rule cannot fail without a target miss confirmed by a second run

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort M** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `tests/test_build_status.py` · `StatusPerformanceTests.setUp (support.perf_only)` · lines tests/test_build_status.py 761-775; tests/perf_fixture.py 106-126; tests/support.py 30-32; run-checks.ps1 27; .github/workflows/windows-ci.yml 78-112; tools/perf/report.py 39-52, 80-81, 116; tools/ci/run-local.ps1 26-28; tools/perf/budgets.json
- **Evidence:** test_build_status.py 775 `support.perf_only(self)` skips the four methods unless KURTSHAPE_PERF=1 (support.py 30-32); windows-ci.yml 81 perf job `continue-on-error: true`; tools/perf/budgets.json keeps phase0/phase1 targets with 'open' misses; README 119 'enforcement is opt-in'; docs/long-run/STATUS.md 116-130 lists open targets (changed inspect 41 ms vs 20, first preview 8.1 s vs 50 ms).
- **Scenario:** A change doubles inspection cost; run-checks.ps1 passes.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Performance regressions are discovered by Kurt's hands, not by the gate; the documented targets are honest but unenforced.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Nothing directly; only the downstream effect if a regression ships and the app feels slower than it did.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** run-checks.ps1 without -Full: four perf skips; CI perf job cannot fail the workflow.
- **Proposed change:** Keep correctness and timing separate as documented, but add a cheap relative gate: bench_core `--baseline runtime/bench/local-baseline.json --enforce` in run-checks -Full with the existing 1.2x+5 ms rule, and a scheduled CI trend artifact; print the four perf results in the GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY table even when non-blocking.
- **Acceptance:** run-checks -Full fails when a metric exceeds baseline by the configured factor on two consecutive runs; the summary table shows the four timings.
- **Dependencies:** CI finding.
- **Risk:** Noisy machines (this review's runs overlapped with a regression run) produce false alarms; the second-run confirmation rule in budgets.json already mitigates.
- **Adversarial check:** Every cited fact holds at HEAD, and the situation is slightly stronger than stated. tests/support.py 30-32 perf_only skips unless KURTSHAPE_PERF=1; tests/test_build_status.py 761-775 (class at 761, setUpClass gate at 765, support.perf_only at 775) applies it to the four methods; run-checks.ps1 27 sets KURTSHAPE_PERF=1 only with -Full. Even under -Full the four methods only record: tests/perf_fixture.py write_report 106-126 stores budget_met/open per metric and asserts nothing on timing (docstring 'Keep absolute misses open; correctness and final paired gates are separate'); test_idle_and_post_change_inspection_budget asserts only correctness, so a doubled inspect cost passes. .github/workflows/windows-ci.yml 78-81 perf job is continue-on-error and its report.py call (110-112) omits --enforce, so the step can only throw on unavailable sections (108); it writes nothing to GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY (only run_native_tests.step_summary 405-417 does). tools/ci/run-local.ps1 26-28 runs bench_core at 20 holes with no --enforce, and report.py 39-43 / budgets.json minimum_plate_holes=60 means plate budgets are not even evaluated there. README 119 'enforcement is opt-in'; docs/long-run/STATUS.md 114-131 lists open targets (changed inspect 41.166 vs 20 at line 119, first preview 8116.546 vs 50 at line 121). Two corrections to the finder's framing: (1) this was a documented, deliberate staging decision, not an oversight: docs/2026-10-03-claude-review/CODEX_PROMPT.md 653, 943, 2366, 2413 say the perf job is non-blocking 'at first' and --enforce --phase phase0 should be switched on after 5 stable nightly samples, and STATUS.md 131 says 'Use --enforce for future default studio benchmarks'; neither was ever wired into a runner. (2) The proposed fix is weaker than described: report.py 49-52 and 80-81 fail a metric only when it exceeds BOTH its absolute target AND best x1.2+5 ms on two independent runs (--confirm; budgets.json requires_second_run), so 'bench_core --baseline local-baseline.json --enforce' in run-checks -Full still cannot fail on a single run, and a metric inside its absolute target (tip overhead 62 ms vs 150) can double without being flagged at all. A gate that catches the finder's own scenario needs a new relative-only rule, a maintained local baseline on Kurt's machine and a noise policy (STATUS.md 131: 'Initial timings varied broadly'). Also: the manual paired-benchmark process the docs rely on has actually been exercised (Q03 confirmation run, S10 in docs/architecture-responsiveness-upgrade/EXECUTION.md 713-721), so regressions are detected late rather than never, which keeps severity low.

## H. Assembly, import and document lifecycle

### H-01 Stale per-document workspace mode: a modeled Part Studio renders as an empty Assembly workspace after the canonical Create Assembly path

**verified defect · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.set_workspace_mode / Panel.create_assembly / Panel.refresh` · lines ui.py:821-823 (stale write), 879-886 (create_assembly new-document branch), 1636-1639 and 1645-1650 (refresh honours remembered mode), 1818-1819 (empty-assembly status), 3405-3407 (Create Assembly enabled when no assembly), 1244-1256 (tick refresh on document switch), general_actions.py:198-210 (_activate -> tick); workspace_modes resets only at ui.py:813
- **Evidence:** Choosing 'Assembly' in the combo on a modeled Part Studio P with no assembly document open falls through to line 821-823: `self.workspace_mode = mode` / `self.workspace_modes[self.state['document_id']] = mode`. Create Assembly then creates a NEW document A (879-882) and only sets workspace_modes[A]. Nothing ever resets workspace_modes[P]. Returning to P by clicking its tab or Ctrl+Space runs tick() -> refresh(); P has no assembly, so line 1639 returns the remembered 'assembly' and line 1649 calls refresh_assembly, which shows 'Instances (0) / Joints (0)', the Assembly toolbar with Create Assembly enabled, and the status 'Create Assembly to begin. Insert saved FCStd parts...' (1818-1819). The feature history and model toolbar are hidden.
- **Scenario:** Kurt models a bracket, picks Assembly, clicks Create Assembly, names it, inserts parts, then clicks the bracket's tab to tweak a dimension.
- **What Kurt would notice:** His part appears to have lost its features and tools; the combo says 'Assembly' on a document that has none. Clicking Create Assembly here (enabled, since P has no assembly) spawns a second assembly document. Recovery requires choosing 'Part Studio' in the combo, which is not obvious.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Immediately and alarmingly: the part looks converted into an empty assembly, the modeling toolbar is gone, and the enabled Create Assembly button invites creating a second assembly document. Recovery (choose Part Studio in the combo) is not discoverable.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** GUI: New part -> sketch+extrude -> combo Assembly -> Create Assembly 'Asm' -> Insert any FCStd -> click the first tab (or Ctrl+Space). Expect: Part Studio toolbar and features. Observe: assembly chrome with empty Instances/Joints. (Source trace above; a Panel-level test can stub `state` and `workspace_modes`.)
- **Proposed change:** In Panel.create_assembly, after `self.operation('new', ...)` succeeds, set `self.workspace_modes[tokens['_document_id']] = 'part_studio'` for the source document. Harden Panel.refresh: treat a remembered 'assembly' mode as valid only when the document actually contains an Assembly object or is the document currently being prepared (`self.workspace_documents.get('assembly') == doc.Name`); otherwise fall back to 'part_studio'. Optionally drop the 'empty assembly workspace' state entirely by making Create Assembly available from the combo/Document menu in Part Studio mode.
- **Acceptance:** GUI recipe (validate-review-gui assembly scenario) gains a check: after Create Assembly from a modeled Part Studio, activating the original Part Studio tab shows the model toolbar, Features list and combo 'Part Studio'. Unit test on Panel.refresh with workspace_modes pre-seeded to 'assembly' and no assembly in state asserts workspace_mode == 'part_studio'.
- **Dependencies:** none
- **Risk:** low; only the mode dictionary changes. Keep the bridge-created-assembly auto-switch (ui.py:1637) intact.
- **Adversarial check:** Source trace holds at HEAD f57d592. Picking 'Assembly' on a modeled Part Studio P with no assembly document open falls through set_workspace_mode to ui.py:821-823 and writes workspace_modes[P]='assembly'. create_assembly (ui.py:879-886) then runs operation('new') because P is modeled, creates the assembly in the new document A and only ever writes workspace_modes[A] (via refresh at 1636-1637 and set_workspace_mode at 821-823). Nothing resets P: grep shows workspace_modes is written only at 813, 823, 1637 and 1639. Returning to P by MDI tab or Ctrl+Space goes through general_actions._activate (198-210) -> tick -> refresh (ui.py:1244-1256), where assembly_state() is empty so 1639 returns the remembered 'assembly' and 1645-1650 calls refresh_assembly: 'Instances (0) / Joints (0)', status 'Create Assembly to begin...' (1818-1819), model bar hidden (3408-3409) and Create Assembly enabled because P has no assembly (3405-3407). A second Create Assembly there spawns another assembly document because P is modeled. The only reset path is choosing 'Part Studio' in the combo while in A, which hits line 813 (workspace_modes[target]='part_studio') before activating P; the GUI recipe tools/assembly-gui-validation.FCMacro:239-247 and :363-365 returns to the original project only that way, so the tab/Ctrl+Space path is untested. No fix in the git log (workspace_modes unchanged since 10c333f). Verified by deterministic code reading, not a runtime probe; no Panel-level test exists.

### H-02 Assembly failures reach the user as one sentence; residuals, feature names and native reasons in the error payload are discarded

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel._operation (failure branch) / Panel.notify; core.Controller.dispatch error dict; assembly.solve / assembly._reference` · lines ui.py:1343-1352 (failure branch), 656-666 (notify), 1220-1229 (bridge failure notification, also message-only); core.py:1794-1797 (error dict with details, last_failures); assembly.py:443 (reason), 826-827 (features), 849-856 (residuals)
- **Evidence:** EXP1 payload from the real controller: message 'Native Assembly did not satisfy every requested joint' plus residuals naming Base ('The grounded occurrence moved'), FixedJoint (rotation 2.22 rad), SecondFixed (1.41 mm, 3.93 rad) and Conflict. EXP1b: 'This occurrence does not have a supported selected subelement' with reason 'Shape index 99 out of bound 6'. The GUI path shows and retains only the first sentence; `last_failed_attempt` (core.py:1402) is never rendered by ui.py (no reference to it).
- **Scenario:** Kurt adds a third fixed joint that over-constrains two blocks, or types 'Face12' when the part has six faces.
- **What Kurt would notice:** He sees a red one-liner and has no idea which joint conflicts or that the face index is out of range; the only diagnostic is a generic sentence in Messages. Compared with SolidWorks' over-defined mate highlighting this is a dead end.
- **A newcomer would notice:** A red one-liner with no joint name and no hint that 'Face12' is out of range; compared with SolidWorks' over-defined mate flags or Onshape's mate error list it reads as a dead end, and the Messages panel adds nothing beyond the same sentence.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** Run scratchpad\area-h-assembly\exp_assembly.py (EXP1/EXP1b) and compare the payload with what Panel._operation forwards.
- **Proposed change:** In Panel._operation failure branch, after notify, append structured detail lines to Messages: for each entry in error.get('features', []) + error.get('residuals', []) write `<label>: <message> (<residual values>)` using the history labels; append `Reason: <reason>` when present; set the status text to '<message> — see Messages'. Select/scroll the offending joint in the history (`self.select_feature(entry['feature'])`) when exactly one non-grounded joint is named. Same path serves STEP import failures (`step_import_failed` reason) and `invalid_assembly_source` reasons.
- **Acceptance:** GUI assembly recipe: after the deliberate conflicting joint, Messages contains the lines 'FixedJoint: Native solution does not satisfy the joint' and 'Conflict: ...'; the status ends with 'see Messages'. tests/test_assembly.py already asserts residual names; add a Panel-level unit test for the formatter.
- **Dependencies:** none (payload already exists)
- **Risk:** low
- **Adversarial check:** Panel._operation's failure branch (ui.py:1343-1352) forwards only response['error']['message'] to notify, and notify (656-666) writes that same string to the status label, tooltip and Messages. The bridge-failure notification in tick (1220-1229) is likewise message-only. core.py:1794 builds the error dict as {'code','message', **details} so features/residuals/reason/native_code reach the GUI and are dropped; last_failed_attempt (core.py:1402) is never referenced in ui.py (grep: no matches for last_failed_attempt, residuals, 'reason'). assembly.py:443 attaches reason (EXP1b: 'Shape index 99 out of bound 6'), 826-827 attaches features, 849-856 attaches residuals per joint with mm/rad values. The finder's probe log (exp_assembly.log lines 11-14) shows the real payloads. The GUI recipe (assembly-gui-validation.FCMacro:308) only asserts that 'did not satisfy' lands in Messages, consistent with the one-liner. Not fixed in the git log. WORKFLOW.md:19 documents 'Native solver errors appear in red status text and are retained in Messages', so the one-liner is the current design, which makes design_weakness the honest class.

### H-03 Joint and Move dialogs are modal and validate only after closing; a rejected joint or a constrained-instance Move discards the form, with references recoverable only from the surviving viewport selection

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort L** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.assembly_joint / Panel.move_assembly_selection (vs feature_panel.FeaturePanel)` · lines ui.py:980-1043 (assembly_joint: modal QDialog, exec_() at 1032, dispatch at 1040), 1045-1100 (move dialog, type-only pre-check at 1056-1058, instance branch 1074-1084); assembly.py:426-444 (_reference validation inside the mutation), 580-587 (move_instance rejects constrained occurrences); feature_panel.py:1-46 (modeless FeaturePanel)
- **Evidence:** assembly_joint builds a QDialog, calls exec_() at line 1032, and only then dispatches create/edit (1040). Any rejection (EXP1b typo, EXP1 conflict) arrives after the dialog is gone; the typed name, type and references are lost. move_assembly_selection does not consult `connected_to_ground` or joint references before showing six fields (1074-1084); EXP1c shows the core rejects afterwards with 'Use joint movement for constrained occurrences'. The help text itself admits the workaround: 'Ctrl-select a reference on each instance before opening this tool' (998). Extrude/Fillet already use the modeless FeaturePanel with viewport picking.
- **Scenario:** Kurt opens Fixed, realises he wants a different face, cannot click the viewport (modal), types 'Face7' wrongly, presses Enter, gets a red status line and an empty dialog on retry.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Joint creation is slower and more error-prone than in any mainstream CAD; mistakes cost the whole form. Keyboard access is fine (Enter/Escape via QDialogButtonBox), but the interaction model is the problem.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Immediately: in Onshape or SolidWorks a mate dialog stays open while you click faces; here the viewport is blocked, references are typed as 'Face7', and a typo or over-constraint closes the dialog with a red one-liner. Move on a jointed part opening six fields and then failing reads as a bug.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** GUI: Insert two parts, click Fixed without pre-selection, try to click a face (blocked), type Face99, Create joint -> status error, dialog closed.
- **Proposed change:** Rebuild the joint editor on FeaturePanel (modeless dock, Confirm/Cancel header): a Gui.Selection observer fills First/Second from viewport clicks (reuse Panel.assembly_references), each reference row shows the picked element and validity, Confirm is enabled only when both references pass a read-only check (new READ op `assembly_reference_check` wrapping assembly._reference without mutation, or run the proposal through the existing `preview` clone). On operation failure keep the panel open and show the error inline (ties to finding 2). For Move: disable 'Move free instance' for instances referenced by any joint/ground (inspect already returns joints[*].first/second and instances[*].grounded) and offer 'Move <joint>' instead; keep the numeric dialog for typed coordinates.
- **Acceptance:** GUI recipe: open Fixed with nothing selected, click a face on each of two instances, see both rows populate, Confirm creates the joint; type an invalid element -> inline error, panel stays open, previous values retained. Existing 58 assembly GUI checks still pass.
- **Dependencies:** finding 2 for inline error text; FeaturePanel reuse
- **Risk:** medium: selection observers must not conflict with sketch/reference pickers (sketch_references.py, edge_picking.py); gate on workspace_mode == 'assembly'.
- **Adversarial check:** The mechanics are confirmed: assembly_joint builds a QDialog and calls exec_() (ui.py:994-1032), reads the fields after the dialog is gone (1034-1035) and only then dispatches create/edit (1040); any core rejection (assembly.py:426-444 reference checks, 849-856 solver residuals) arrives with the dialog closed and no retry. move_assembly_selection (1045-1100) screens only joint type (1056-1058) and opens six placement fields for any instance; assembly.py:580-587 rejects grounded or jointed instances afterwards (EXP1c), although inspect already returns instances[*].grounded, connected_to_ground and joints[*].first/second. The FeaturePanel (feature_panel.py:1-46) is a modeless dock that Extrude/Fillet already use, so the better pattern exists in the codebase. Severity is overstated, though: the dialog pre-fills both references from the current viewport selection (ui.py:990, 1013-1019), that workflow is documented in the dialog help (998) and WORKFLOW.md:46, and a rejected operation does not clear Gui.Selection (the failure branch at 1343-1352 does not call refresh or clear selection), so reopening re-populates the references; only the typed name and type are lost. The cost is friction and lost typing, not blocked work or data loss, which is medium impact. The GUI recipe drives the dialogs by object name (assembly-gui-validation.FCMacro:154-178), so a rebuild must update it.

### H-04 No 'update from source' and no staleness signal: edited parts never reach the assembly, and re-insert silently creates a second snapshot

**opportunity · severity high · confidence high · user value high · effort L** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/assembly.py` · `assembly.insert / assembly.inspect / Panel.refresh_assembly` · lines assembly.py:377-395 (snapshot keyed by sha+object), 899-911 (inspect source/policy fields), 603-619 (cascade removal of joints on delete), 738-741 and 894-896 (in-document tamper signal only), 745-757 (validation checks references on suppressed joints too); ui.py:911 (only policy hint), 1787-1793 (tooltip id only); docs/assembly-workflow/WORKFLOW.md:9, 48
- **Evidence:** EXP3: after rewriting block.FCStd (sha 7e64… -> 8c47…), inspect still reports solver_status 'solved' and every instance keeps sha 7e64…; inserting again created EmbeddedPart001 and a new instance 'Fresh' while Base/Fastened/Rotor kept the old geometry and joints. No ui.py code reads `source`/`sha256`; the words 'source', 'snapshot' or 'update' never appear in the assembly history or status.
- **Scenario:** Kurt changes a hole position in the bracket Part Studio, saves, switches to the assembly and expects the bracket to update (SolidWorks/Onshape behaviour). Nothing changes and nothing says why. To update he must delete the occurrence (which cascades its joints), re-insert, re-ground and re-create joints.
- **What Kurt would notice:** The documented policy is defensible for portability, but it is invisible in the UI, and the manual update path destroys joints. This is the biggest expectation gap for a mainstream-CAD user.
- **A newcomer would notice:** The biggest expectation gap: Onshape and SolidWorks propagate part edits into assemblies; here nothing updates, nothing says why, and the fix destroys every joint on the occurrence. They would assume the assembly is broken.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** exp_assembly.py EXP3; or GUI: insert a part, edit and save its source, observe no change and no indicator.
- **Proposed change:** Phase 1 (S): surface the policy. Instance tooltip/row: 'from bracket.FCStd · Body · snapshot'; add a READ op `assembly_source_status` that hashes SourcePath on explicit command/open only (keeps the 'no idle source reads' invariant, assembly.py:702-712) and marks rows ' · source changed' with a status line 'N occurrences have newer sources · Update'. Phase 2 (M-L): `replace_assembly_source` ASSEMBLY op: read the source via _source_document, build the new EmbeddedPart, `setLink` every App::Link that used the old snapshot, keep placements, remove the orphaned snapshot, `solve`; joints referencing FaceN/EdgeN that no longer resolve are reported by validation_errors (assembly.py:751-755 'Broken or unsupported joint reference') and left suppressed for re-pick rather than deleted. Expose both in the Assembly bar ('Update') and the history context menu.
- **Acceptance:** tests/test_assembly.py: insert, modify source, `replace_assembly_source` -> occurrences report new sha, placements equal, origin-referenced joints still solve, FaceN joint reported by name; GUI check that an instance row shows ' · source changed' after the source is rewritten and that Update refreshes the viewport.
- **Dependencies:** finding 3 (re-picking broken references), finding 2 (reporting)
- **Risk:** medium-high: OCCT topological naming across source edits; keep origin-referenced joints stable and document FaceN fragility.
- **Adversarial check:** Confirmed at HEAD. assembly.insert (assembly.py:377-395) keys the embedded snapshot on SourceSHA256+SourceObject, so a changed source file yields a new EmbeddedPart and a new instance while existing instances keep the old geometry; inspect (899-911) reports source path/sha and the literal policy string 'embedded solid snapshots; no external dependencies or automatic updates', but no GUI code reads instance['source'] (grep: SourceSHA256/SourcePath/sha256 appear only in assembly.py). refresh_assembly tooltips (ui.py:1791-1793) show only the id; the only wording about the policy is the Insert dialog's 'Its geometry is stored in this assembly' (911), which does not say edits will not propagate. The finder's probe (exp_assembly.log EXP3, lines 20-25) shows solver_status 'solved' with the old sha after the source was rewritten and a second snapshot EmbeddedPart001 after re-insert. The existing 'embedded source geometry changed' status (assembly.py:894-896, 738-741) detects in-document tampering with the snapshot, not a changed source file, so there is no staleness signal. WORKFLOW.md:9 documents the policy and :48 lists 'automatic source updates' as deferred, so this is a known design choice, which makes 'opportunity' the honest classification. Impact if a user relies on it stays high: the manual update path is delete occurrence with cascade (assembly.py:603-619, joints removed), re-insert, re-ground, re-create joints.

### H-05 Assembly documents opened with an engine/build rebuild reason stay 'Rebuild required' (Solve never clears it, export is blocked) and the Assembly workspace offers no Rebuild

**verified defect · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/core.py` · `Controller._rebuild_reason_on_open / Controller._build_status / dispatch ASSEMBLY_OPS branch; Panel.make_toolbars (assembly_bar); tool_search._context_reason` · lines core.py:1281-1292 (reason only for ProgramVersion or FreeCAD/OCCT stamp change), 1339-1341, 1515-1517, 1647-1665, 1686-1687, 2815-2816; ui.py:584-598 (assembly_bar, no Rebuild), 183 (native menu bar hidden), 807-820, 1820-1821, 2765-2768 (tool search contexts), 2820-2822 (activate refuses 'Switch to' entries), 3408; tool_search.py:140-141
- **Evidence:** EXP2 (headless, real Controller): with RebuildReason='saved_by_other_build' on an assembly document, build_status 'needs_rebuild'; `solve_assembly` returns ok but build_status stays 'needs_rebuild'; dispatching `rebuild` sets it to 'valid'. The GUI offers no way to dispatch `rebuild` in assembly mode: the model bar is hidden (ui.py:3408), tool search refuses ('Switch to Part Studio'), and the combo switch activates another document.
- **Scenario:** After any KurtShape/FreeCAD update (the running app is already an older build than HEAD), Kurt opens last week's assembly: status 'Rebuild required · saved by another application build', Export refuses, Solve 'succeeds' but the status never clears.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Assembly looks broken with no visible remedy; only the bridge `rebuild` op (or editing in Part Studio mode, which is a different document) clears it.
- **A newcomer would notice:** The same status with no Rebuild button anywhere in Assembly; typing 'rebuild' in tool search says 'Switch to Part Studio', which jumps to a different document; the assembly cannot be exported from the app at all. In Onshape/SolidWorks a rebuild is always one click away in any workspace.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** exp_assembly.py EXP2; GUI: open an assembly saved by a different build, press Solve, try Export.
- **Proposed change:** Add `self.tool(self.assembly_bar, 'Rebuild', lambda: self.operation('rebuild'), 'Rebuild the assembly after an engine or build change', 'Std_Refresh', command='Std_Refresh')` to Panel.make_toolbars and add it to assembly_actions so it follows the managed/enabled rules; and/or in the ASSEMBLY_OPS branch (core.py:1657-1665) call `self._mark_rebuilt(doc)` after a successful solve when `meta.RebuildReason` is an engine/build reason and no object is Touched/missing. Mirror the 'Rebuild required · …' toast (ui.py:1373-1374) with the hint 'Assembly → Rebuild'.
- **Acceptance:** tests/test_assembly.py: set RebuildReason, dispatch `rebuild` -> build_status valid and export succeeds; GUI assembly recipe: Rebuild action discoverable/enabled in Assembly mode and clears the status. Regression gate: any new rebuild reason must keep a GUI path in both workspaces (test asserts assembly_bar contains 'Rebuild').
- **Dependencies:** none
- **Risk:** low; `rebuild` already works on assembly documents (EXP2).
- **Adversarial check:** The mechanism holds exactly as the finder's EXP2 log shows (exp_assembly.log lines 15-18): with RebuildReason set on an assembly document, build_status is needs_rebuild, solve_assembly returns ok but leaves it needs_rebuild, and only `rebuild` flips it to valid. Code confirms it: the ASSEMBLY_OPS branch (core.py:1647-1665) recomputes and calls _build_status but never _mark_rebuilt; only op == 'rebuild' does (core.py:1686-1687); _build_status keeps needs_rebuild while meta.RebuildReason is non-empty (core.py:1339-1341); _export refuses anything but 'valid' (core.py:2815-2816). The GUI gap is also real: the assembly bar (ui.py:584-598) has Solve but no Rebuild; the model bar with Rebuild is hidden in assembly mode (ui.py:3408); tool search tags model-bar tools context 'model' (ui.py:2765) so in the assembly workspace Rebuild reports 'Switch to Part Studio' (tool_search.py:140-141) and activate() refuses any 'Switch to' entry (ui.py:2820-2822); the workspace combo leaves an assembly document for another one or creates 'Untitled part' (ui.py:807-820); the native menu bar is hidden (ui.py:183) and shortcuts.py has no rebuild binding. Files are identical to HEAD f57d592, so this is not already solved. What is inflated is the trigger: _rebuild_reason_on_open (core.py:1281-1290) only sets a sticky reason when the file's FreeCAD ProgramVersion differs from the running build or the recorded FreeCAD/OCCT stamp differs; a KurtShape git update (what 'the running app is an older build than HEAD' refers to) does not change ProgramVersion, and engine stamps (5f91ba2) predate assembly support (10c333f), so 'engine_unrecorded' cannot occur for a KurtShape assembly. Touched objects and missing_shapes reasons are cleared by Solve's recompute. So the scenario needs a FreeCAD runtime/OCCT change, which is rare but inevitable for a pinned-runtime app, and Kurt has the bridge `rebuild` op as a workaround. Severity high -> medium: a hard export block with no in-GUI remedy when it happens, but narrow trigger and a bridge workaround.

### H-06 Recovered documents are labelled with their UUID and Save proposes a generic 'my-part.FCStd' instead of the checkpoint's original name

**verified defect · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: reproduced · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/core.py` · `Controller.dispatch (op == 'recover') / Panel.save / Panel.fill_recovery` · lines core.py:1496-1502 (opens <uuid>.FCStd), 1528-1529 (FileName cleared; record name/source_file unused), 1397-1398 (name/native_file in inspect); ui.py:1756, 1772, 1828 (title/identity from state['name']), 2976-2981 (Save default); recovery.py:54-55
- **Evidence:** EXP6: after `recover`, `doc.Label == '6913e47a-c655-479e-baeb-647cec6be3e5'`, state['name'] is the UUID, native_file None. The tab, window title and document identity label will all show the UUID; Ctrl+S opens a Save dialog proposing my-part.FCStd in last_directory rather than the original bracket.FCStd.
- **Scenario:** KurtShape crashes while Kurt edits bracket.FCStd. He picks 'bracket · checkpoint' from Recovery, gets a tab called '6913e47a-…', presses Ctrl+S and saves 'my-part.FCStd' next to whatever he last opened — now two diverging copies exist.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Recovery works technically but produces an unrecognisable document and invites duplicate files; the one moment the user is stressed is the moment the naming breaks.
- **A newcomer would notice:** SolidWorks auto-recover and Onshape keep the document's name; a UUID title plus a generic save name during crash recovery reads as 'the wrong file came back', and saving as proposed leaves two diverging files.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** exp_assembly.py EXP6 (headless, real recover op).
- **Proposed change:** In the recover branch after attach: `doc.Label = recovery_record.get('name') or doc.Label`; store `recovery_record.get('source_file')` in `self.observer.state(doc)['recovered_from']` and expose it in _inspect as `recovered_from`. In Panel.save, when native_file is None and state.get('recovered_from') exists, default the dialog to that path (the native dialog handles overwrite confirmation) and add a toast 'Recovered from checkpoint of bracket.FCStd · Save to replace it or choose a new name'.
- **Acceptance:** New test in tests/test_core.py or test_assembly.py: checkpoint, close, recover -> state['name'] equals the pre-close label and state['recovered_from'] equals the original path; GUI recipe asserts the tab title after recovery.
- **Dependencies:** none
- **Risk:** low
- **Adversarial check:** Both halves are verified. EXP6 (exp_assembly.log lines 31-32) shows that after `recover` doc.Label is the document UUID and FileName is ''. The recover branch opens '<uuid>.FCStd' from project-recovery (core.py:1497, 1502), FreeCAD names the document after the file stem, and recovery_record['name'] (stored at recovery.py:54-55) is never applied; FileName is cleared deliberately (core.py:1529). inspect reports name=doc.Label and native_file=None (core.py:1397-1398), which feed the window title (ui.py:1756/1828) and identity label (ui.py:1772). Panel.save then falls to the generic default 'my-part.FCStd' / 'my-assembly.FCStd' in last_directory (ui.py:2976-2981). Nothing in ui.py's post-recover handling renames (grep 'recover'). Files are identical to HEAD, so not fixed. Where the finding overstates: clearing FileName is documented intent ('recovered documents require choosing a Save destination', docs/2026-10-02-claude-review-follow-up/IMPLEMENTATION.md:16; test_assembly.py:290 asserts native_file is None), so Ctrl+S does not silently write anywhere; it opens a Save dialog where the user can type the real name and folder. The original bracket.FCStd is untouched. The UUID label is a real defect; the generic default name is a design weakness. Severity high -> medium: confusing and duplicate-inviting at a stressful moment, but no data loss and the user sees and controls the dialog.

### H-07 Recovery menu lists both checkpoint generations with identical undated labels, never prunes abandoned checkpoints, and offers entries for documents that are still open

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value medium · effort M** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: reproduced · verified
