- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/recovery.py` · `RecoveryStore.available / RecoveryStore._publish / Panel.fill_recovery / Controller.attach` · lines recovery.py:96-102 (previous pair written on every replacement), 154-166 (glob '*.json', source-mtime filter only, no open-document check, no pruning); ui.py:2843-2852 (label/tooltip, no date, no forget); core.py:1021-1023 (duplicate_document_id on recover of an open document)
- **Evidence:** EXP5: after two forced checkpoints and closing without saving, available() returned two entries both named 'Exp' (…json and ….previous.json) with no source_file; four files stayed in project-recovery. In the GUI they render as two identical 'Exp · checkpoint' rows. Untitled documents (source_file None) never satisfy the mtime filter, so every 'Untitled part' edited for >30 s accumulates two rows for the life of the profile. Picking an entry for a document that is still open fails with the duplicate_document_id error.
- **Scenario:** After a few weeks the Recovery submenu is a wall of 'Untitled part · checkpoint' rows in unknown order with no dates; Kurt cannot tell today's crash from last month's discarded scratch part, and picking the entry for the tab he has open errors.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Recovery loses credibility exactly when needed; disk usage grows by two full FCStd copies per abandoned document.
- **A newcomer would notice:** SolidWorks' auto-recover list is dated and clears after use; here identical rows pile up with no way to tell today's crash from last month's scratch part and no way to forget any of them.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** exp_assembly.py EXP5; inspect fill_recovery with that store.
- **Proposed change:** Add `RecoveryStore.listing()` that groups manifests by document_id, returns the newest with `saved_at`, `previous` manifest, `source_file`, and `open` (core.documents has a live doc with that id). Panel.fill_recovery renders 'bracket · unfinished sketch · 5 min ago' with the previous checkpoint in a submenu, disables entries for open documents ('already open'), and adds 'Forget checkpoint' plus automatic pruning of pairs older than N days whose source_file is newer (or whose document was explicitly discarded—record a 'discarded' flag when our own close prompt from finding 9 is answered Discard).
- **Acceptance:** Unit tests on listing(): two checkpoints of one document -> one row with a previous entry; open document -> flagged; GUI: Recovery menu shows relative times and no duplicate labels.
- **Dependencies:** finding 9 for the discard signal (optional)
- **Risk:** low-medium: pruning must never delete the last checkpoint of a document with no saved file.
- **Adversarial check:** Every factual claim checks out, but the store behaves as designed and the gap is in presentation and lifecycle, so this is a design weakness rather than a defect. available() globs '*.json' (recovery.py:157), which includes the '.previous.json' manifest that every replacement writes (recovery.py:98-102) and, transiently, '.saving.json'; EXP5 (exp_assembly.log lines 29-30) lists two 'Exp' entries with no source_file and four files on disk. The previous pair is retained on purpose as the corruption fallback (read() error 'inspect the previous checkpoint', recovery.py:151; tests test_review_followup.py:454-467 and test_responsiveness.py:359-360 read it directly), so listing it is not wrong, but fill_recovery (ui.py:2843-2852) labels both rows record['name'] + ' · checkpoint' with no date, and the tooltip is source_file or path, so for saved documents the two rows are indistinguishable. No code unlinks or prunes project-recovery (grep: the only unlinks are STEP-export stages at core.py:844, bridge token, snapshots, workers); untitled documents (source_file None) never satisfy the mtime filter at recovery.py:160-162, so they accumulate for the life of the profile. An entry for a document that is still open is listed and `recover` fails at attach with duplicate_document_id (core.py:1021-1023), which is at least a clear message rather than a crash. Disk growth is real: two FCStd copies per abandoned document, including embedded assembly sources. Files identical to HEAD. Severity medium stands (recovery menu degrades to noise over weeks; wasted disk), classification corrected to design_weakness.
- **Merged duplicate F-08:** Recovery checkpoints are never pruned: every new or recovered document leaves a permanent pair and unsaved ones stay in the Recovery menu forever — verified defect, low, reproduced · not independently verified. Evidence: recovery.py contains no unlink/remove/rmtree (grep); `_publish` only replaces the same DocumentId's pair and keeps `.previous.*` (98-104); `available` hides a pair only when its source_file exists and is newer (160-162), so documents never saved are listed indefinitely. Probe B8: three `new` documents + forced checkpoint, documents closed -> directory holds 6 files and available() lists 'Throwaway 0/1/2' with source_file None. ui.py:2854-2877 checkpoints every open document on idle after 30 s.

### H-08 GUI Insert always re-frames the assembly in isometric fit-all, discarding the current view orientation

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value low · effort S** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.insert_assembly_part / Panel.frame_import / Panel.isometric` · lines ui.py:933-939 (fit + deferred frame_import after insert), 963-966 (frame_import: isometric + fit), 3857-3858 (isometric -> view_isometric_keep_sketch); navigation.py:200-207
- **Evidence:** insert_assembly_part reuses the STEP-import helper, which was written to fit a freshly imported document; for an assembly it runs after every occurrence insertion, resetting orientation to isometric and fitting all.
- **Scenario:** Kurt orients the assembly to Front to line up a bracket, inserts the next part, and the view jumps back to isometric fit-all; with ten parts this happens ten times.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Friction on the most repeated assembly action; the deferred fit also fires after the user may already have started orbiting.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Onshape and SolidWorks keep the camera when inserting components; lining the assembly up in Front view and having it jump to isometric after every one of ten inserts is visible, repeated friction.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** GUI: Assembly -> Insert twice while in Front view.
- **Proposed change:** In insert_assembly_part drop isometric(): keep the camera, and only `fit()` when the new occurrence's bounding box lies outside the current view (compare `obj.Shape.BoundBox` with the viewer's visible volume) or offer 'zoom to new instance' via general_actions.zoom_to_selection after `select_feature(result['created_feature'])`. Keep frame_import for open_path only.
- **Acceptance:** GUI check: set Front view, Insert -> camera orientation unchanged; a part inserted far outside the view is still visible (fit triggered). Existing STEP-import fit check unchanged.
- **Dependencies:** none
- **Risk:** low
- **Adversarial check:** The code does exactly what the finder says: after a successful insert, Panel.insert_assembly_part calls self.fit() and then defers frame_import (ui.py:933-939); frame_import calls isometric() then fit() (ui.py:963-966); isometric() is navigation.view_isometric_keep_sketch (ui.py:3857-3858), which applies a fixed isometric rotation (navigation.py:200-207). So every GUI Insert reorients to isometric and fits all. git blame puts these lines in 10c333f (2026-10-02, the original assembly commit) and they are unchanged at HEAD, so this was a deliberate reuse of the STEP-import framing rather than a regression. Two qualifications: the finder verified this by reading code only (no runtime run), though the code is unambiguous; and the clause about the deferred fit firing 'after the user may already have started orbiting' is overstated, since QTimer.singleShot(0) runs on the next event-loop pass. Inserts sent over the bridge go through core.dispatch and never touch Panel.insert_assembly_part, so scripted inserts keep the camera. Severity low is right; classification corrected to design_weakness because it is intended behaviour that is wrong for repeated inserts, not a broken code path.

### H-09 Closing a document tab is FreeCAD-native: the native Unsaved-document prompt's Save bypasses KurtShape's staged save, `.recovery.FCStd` sibling, Recent list and project-root grant

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort M** · verification: downgraded · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.eventFilter / Panel.tick / Controller._save / GeneralActions._can_switch` · lines ui.py:4273-4276 (only main-window close, sketch leases only); ui.py:496-516 (Document menu has no Close); shortcuts.json (no close binding); ui.py:1231-1243 and 1270-1285 (tick teardown / reset after the document is gone, 25 ms timer at ui.py:223); ui.py:2976-2984 + settings.py:33-43 (Save -> grant_destination -> remember); core.py:2793-2811 (staged save, ZIP check, .recovery.FCStd); general_actions.py:180-191 (_can_switch guards switching only)
- **Evidence:** The MDI tab 'x' (visible in every workspace screenshot) and Ctrl+F4 go to FreeCAD, which shows its own 'Unsaved document … Save/Discard/Cancel' box; 'Save' runs the native Document.save/saveAs: no ZIP verification, no .recovery.FCStd, no Recent entry, native default directory, and KurtShape's `Gui…Modified` bookkeeping is skipped. A KurtShape Extrude editor or sketch-placement state open on that document is torn down by tick() only after the document has already been deleted (1242-1243).
- **Scenario:** Kurt closes the bracket tab with unsaved changes, answers Save in the FreeCAD prompt, saves to the FreeCAD default folder; later 'Recent projects' does not list it and no .recovery.FCStd exists.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Two save paths with different guarantees; the one reached from the most natural gesture is the weaker one. Also the only place where FreeCAD's own wording/dialog chrome appears in the everyday journey.
- **A newcomer would notice:** The only FreeCAD-branded dialog in the everyday journey appears on the most natural gesture (closing a tab); its Save opens a native Save As in a different default folder and the project then does not show up under Recent projects. Discard works as expected; no data loss.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** GUI: modify a part, click the tab 'x', observe the native prompt; choose Save on an unsaved document and compare with Ctrl+S behaviour (dialog folder, Recent, .recovery.FCStd).
- **Proposed change:** Install an event filter on each QMdiSubWindow (hook `maximize_document_view`/tick where subwindows are enumerated, ui.py:1159-1175) for QEvent.Close: if the document is modified show a KurtShape QMessageBox 'Save changes to <name>?' with Save (-> Panel.save(), then proceed), Don't save, Cancel; cancel KurtShape tasks explicitly before proceeding (`cancel_task`, `studio_dialog.reject`); after our save set `Gui.getDocument(name).Modified = False` so FreeCAD does not prompt again. Alternatively register a Std_CloseActiveDoc override via the shortcut router. Record 'discarded' for finding 7.
- **Acceptance:** GUI recipe: close a modified tab -> KurtShape prompt; Save writes via staged save (file + .recovery.FCStd when overwriting, Recent updated); Don't save closes without prompt from FreeCAD; closing while an Extrude editor is open cancels the editor before the document disappears.
- **Dependencies:** none; coordinate with finding 7
- **Risk:** medium: must not double-prompt or block FreeCAD's own close while a native task is active; verify with the hidden GUI recipe.
- **Adversarial check:** The structural claim holds at HEAD f57d592: Panel.eventFilter intercepts only `watched is self.main and QEvent.Close`, and only when a sketch lease is live (ui.py:4273-4276, blame b65f066/5f91ba2e, Oct 2, untouched since); grep of src finds no QMdiSubWindow close filter and no Std_CloseActiveDoc override; the KurtShape Document menu offers New/Open/Export/Recent/Recovery/Adopt/Repair but no Close (ui.py:496-516); shortcuts.json has no close binding, so the tab 'x' (visible in failure.png and reopened-native-assembly.png) and FreeCAD's own Ctrl+F4 reach FreeCAD's Unsaved-document prompt. KurtShape's Save goes through Panel.save -> settings.grant_destination -> remember (ui.py:2976-2984, settings.py:33-43) and Controller._save's staged write + ZIP check + `.recovery.FCStd` sibling (core.py:2793-2811), none of which a native Document.save touches, so the Recent and staged-save deltas are real. Three parts are overstated, which is why this is downgraded rather than confirmed: (1) 'no backups' ignores that FreeCAD's own save keeps .FCBak backups by default and KurtShape never disables CreateBackupFiles (start() only sets RecoveryEnabled/AutoSave, ui.py:4315-4318), so only the passive `.recovery.FCStd` sibling is missing, and KurtShape's Recovery menu does not list those siblings anyway (fill_recovery reads core.recovery.available(), ui.py:2843-2845); (2) 'Gui...Modified bookkeeping is skipped' is moot because a native save clears Modified itself; (3) the 'next tick' teardown is 25 ms (ui.py:223), tick rejects the studio dialog / cancels the task on document change (ui.py:1231-1243) and resets the panel when no document remains (ui.py:1270-1285), and the preview code guards with core._is_open (extrusion_preview.py:265, 979), so no crash or stuck state is evidenced. FreeCAD itself refuses to close a document while a Task dialog (sketch edit) is active, so the sketch-lease case is not exposed either (FreeCAD behaviour, not verified in this repo). Net impact if hit: FreeCAD-worded dialog chrome, a native Save As defaulting to FreeCAD's folder rather than the project directory, no Recent entry, no `.recovery.FCStd` sibling and no project-root grant; nothing is lost. That is a low-severity design weakness, not medium.

### H-10 The 'Part Studio | Assembly' combo reads as a mode of the current document but is a document switcher, and the assembly's name is not the document's name

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value high · effort M** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.make_toolbars (mode_choice) / Panel.set_workspace_mode / Panel.create_assembly / Panel.save / GeneralActions._activate / GeneralActions.tab_manager` · lines ui.py:523-526 (combo + tooltip), 796-838 (set_workspace_mode; switch/create at 807-820), 860-887 (create_assembly; unmodeled branch 879-884 keeps the document label, modeled branch 880-883 names a new document after the assembly), 1804 (features_title = assembly name), 1828 (window title = state name = doc.Label via core.py:1397), 2976-2980 (Save default my-assembly.FCStd regardless of label); core.py:1482-1485 (`new` sets doc.Label from name); general_actions.py:209 ('Active part studio:'), 232 ('Open part studios')
- **Evidence:** validation/assembly-step-import/failure.png (Oct 2, dialog structure unchanged since: ui.py:860-887 last touched 10c333ff/eb3baf1 on 2026-10-02) shows document label 'Untitled part' with history title 'Direct STEP assembly'. EXP4 headless: state['name'] 'Exp' vs assembly name 'Exp assembly'. reopened-native-assembly.png shows the status 'Active part studio: gui-solved-assembly-65028' on an assembly.
- **Scenario:** Kurt names his assembly 'Hub mount', the tab says 'Untitled part : 1*', Ctrl+S proposes my-assembly.FCStd, and choosing 'Part Studio' in the combo unexpectedly jumps to a different tab (or creates a new empty one) instead of changing anything about the assembly.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Document identity is inconsistent across title, tab, history header and save name; the combo's behaviour contradicts its affordance, which is the root of finding 1.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Coming from Onshape, the Part Studio | Assembly chooser looks like Onshape's tab kinds; selecting Part Studio silently switches to another tab or opens an empty 'Untitled part', and the assembly they named 'Hub mount' is still 'Untitled part : 1*' in the tab and proposes my-assembly.FCStd on Ctrl+S.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** GUI: New part (empty) -> Assembly -> Create Assembly 'Hub mount' -> look at tab/title -> Ctrl+S.
- **Proposed change:** (a) In create_assembly set `doc.Label`/state name to the assembly name in both branches (send `name` to the 'new' op as today, and for the unmodeled branch dispatch `rename_feature` on the document or extend create_assembly to relabel) and use that label for the Save default. (b) Relabel the combo as a document switcher: 'Hub mount (Assembly) ▾' listing open documents grouped Part Studios/Assemblies plus 'New Part Studio', or remove the combo and let the tab strip plus a 'Create Assembly' entry in the Document menu carry the workflow. (c) Change _activate's toast to 'Active document: …' and the tab manager title to 'Open documents'.
- **Acceptance:** GUI check: after Create Assembly 'Hub mount' the tab, window title, identity label and Save default all read 'Hub mount'; switching documents via the combo shows the document names; shortcut/tool-search tests unchanged.
- **Dependencies:** finding 1
- **Risk:** low-medium; the bridge auto-switch and tool_search contexts depend on workspace_mode, which stays derived from the document.
- **Adversarial check:** Every cited behaviour is present at HEAD. The combo's tooltip says 'Return to an open Part Studio or Assembly; a new Part Studio opens if none is available' (ui.py:523-526) and set_workspace_mode (ui.py:796-838) does exactly that: choosing the other mode activates a different open document matching that kind or, for Part Studio, dispatches `new` with name 'Untitled part' and toasts 'Part Studio ready · your assembly stays open in its document tab' (ui.py:807-820); only when no target exists for Assembly does it fall through and merely flip the toolbars on the current document. create_assembly (ui.py:860-887, blame 10c333ff 2026-10-02, line 875 touched by 559e034 on Oct 4 without changing the logic) names a new document after the assembly only in the modeled branch (`operation('new', name=...)`, core.py:1482-1485 sets doc.Label); in the unmodeled branch, which is the New -> Create Assembly path, the document keeps 'Untitled part' while the assembly object gets the typed name (assembly.py:107-114). state['name'] is doc.Label (core.py:1397), so the identity label, the window title (ui.py:1828) and the MDI tab say 'Untitled part' while the history header says the assembly name (ui.py:1804); Panel.save ignores the label entirely and defaults to 'my-assembly.FCStd' (ui.py:2976-2980, assembly-save-dialog.png). _activate toasts 'Active part studio: ...' for any document and the tab manager is titled 'Open part studios' (general_actions.py:209, 232). The finder's probe log line 7 reads 'doc label after create_assembly: Exp | assembly name: Exp assembly'; failure.png (Oct 2) shows identity 'Untitled part', tab 'Untitled part : 1*' and history header 'Direct STEP assembly'; reopened-native-assembly.png shows 'Active part studio: gui-solved-assembly-65028' on an assembly document. Classification and severity are honest: a pervasive identity/affordance inconsistency with no data loss.

### H-11 Insert picker leaks internal type names, offers no way to tell solids apart, and has no 'insert all'

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: captures · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.insert_assembly_part / assembly.candidates` · lines ui.py:906-932 (dialog; row text 917-921; single selection, one insert per exec); assembly.py:356-361 (candidates fields), 320-322 (STEP solid labels)
- **Evidence:** validation/assembly-step-import/insert-step-solid-picker.png shows 'Dodec pipe mount · Solid 2 · Part::Feature · 1 solid(s)' three times with nothing to distinguish the solids; for FCStd sources the row shows 'PartDesign::Body'. A six-solid STEP requires six Insert → file dialog → picker cycles.
- **Scenario:** Kurt inserts the hub STEP and must guess which 'Solid N' is the pipe bar.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Trial-and-error inserts; FreeCAD type identifiers in a user-facing list.
- **A newcomer would notice:** FreeCAD type identifiers in a user-facing list, three identical-looking 'Solid N' rows, and no multi-select or Insert all, unlike Onshape's insert dialog which previews and inserts several parts at once.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** GUI: Assembly -> Insert -> any multi-solid STEP.
- **Proposed change:** assembly.candidates adds `bbox_mm` (BoundBox lengths) and `volume_mm3` from the already-loaded temporary document; the dialog shows 'Solid 2 · 40 × 60 × 80 mm · 73.5 cm³' with 'Body'/'Solid' instead of TypeId; allow multi-selection and an 'Insert all N' button that dispatches sequential insert operations (or one new `insert_assembly_parts` op in a single transaction) and keeps a single undo step.
- **Acceptance:** Unit test on candidates() payload; GUI check that the picker shows dimensions and no '::'; inserting all three hub solids yields three instances in one go.
- **Dependencies:** none
- **Risk:** low
- **Adversarial check:** assembly.candidates returns only id/name/type(TypeId)/solid_count (assembly.py:356-361), STEP solids are labelled '<stem> · Solid N' (assembly.py:320-322), and the picker renders `name · TypeId · N solid(s)` in a default single-selection QListWidget with one insert per dialog (ui.py:906-932; row text at 917-921, blame 10c333ff Oct 2, unchanged). insert-step-solid-picker.png shows 'Dodec pipe mount · Solid 1/2/3 · Part::Feature · 1 solid(s)' with nothing else to distinguish them, and assembly-body-choice-dialog.png shows 'PartDesign::Body' for FCStd sources. The help text explicitly says 'Repeat Insert for another independent instance', so a six-solid STEP is six Insert -> file dialog -> picker cycles. Severity low is right: it is friction and trial-and-error, not incorrect output.

### H-12 Insert reads the saved file; unsaved changes in an open tab for that part are silently ignored

**design weakness · severity medium · confidence high · user value high · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.insert_assembly_part / assembly._source_document` · lines ui.py:889-937 (no open-document/modified check before assembly_candidates); ui.py:910 (dialog text omits 'saved'); ui.py:1819 (hint only before an assembly exists); assembly.py:169-220 (_preflight_source), 304-343 (_source_document copies the on-disk file); core.py:1236-1242 (_modified available); docs/assembly-workflow/WORKFLOW.md:7, 9; NATIVE_API.md:29, 33
- **Evidence:** The documented policy snapshots the saved file (WORKFLOW.md:7). The GUI offers the file dialog with the part Kurt is editing in another tab and embeds whatever is on disk; `core._modified(doc)` (core.py:1236) is available but unused here.
- **Scenario:** Kurt edits the bracket, forgets Ctrl+S, inserts bracket.FCStd into the assembly and gets the previous geometry; the discrepancy only shows when a hole does not line up.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Silent stale data in the assembly; compounds finding 4 because there is no later update path.
- **A newcomer would notice:** SolidWorks inserts the in-memory component and Onshape has no files at all, so a newcomer who edits a bracket tab and inserts bracket.FCStd expects the current geometry; they get the last saved one and discover it only when a hole does not line up, then have to remove the instance (cascading its joints) and re-insert.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** GUI: edit a saved part without saving, Assembly -> Insert that file.
- **Proposed change:** In insert_assembly_part before dispatching assembly_candidates: find an open managed document with `Path(doc.FileName).resolve() == Path(path).resolve()`; if `self.core._modified(doc)` show a QMessageBox 'bracket has unsaved changes. The assembly stores the saved geometry.' with 'Save and insert' (dispatch save on that document via core), 'Insert saved version', 'Cancel'.
- **Acceptance:** GUI check: modified open source -> prompt appears; 'Save and insert' results in the inserted sha equal to the new file hash; unmodified source -> no prompt.
- **Dependencies:** none
- **Risk:** low; saving another document from the assembly panel must respect busy/sketch-lease guards (reuse Panel.save logic with an explicit document).
- **Adversarial check:** insert_assembly_part (ui.py:889-937) goes straight from the file dialog to `assembly_candidates` and `insert_assembly_part` with the chosen path; neither it nor _preflight_source (assembly.py:169-220) nor _source_document (assembly.py:304-343, which sha256-hashes and copies the on-disk file into a temporary document) consults core.documents for an open managed document whose FileName is that path, and core._modified (core.py:1236-1242) is unused here. The policy is documented (WORKFLOW.md:7 'Original files are read'; NATIVE_API.md:29 'Insertion reads a saved FCStd'; NATIVE_API.md:33 'insert the revised saved source explicitly'), but at the moment of insertion the dialog only says 'Its geometry is stored in this assembly' (ui.py:910) and the 'Insert saved FCStd parts' hint shows only before an assembly exists (ui.py:1819). The result is a silent snapshot of stale geometry, and because accepted occurrences never update (WORKFLOW.md:9) the fix is delete-with-cascade and re-insert. Medium severity for a silent wrong-geometry outcome is honest; design_weakness is right since the behaviour is the documented policy, not a bug.

### H-13 Reference-file (unmanaged FCStd) state is explained only by the muted status line; disabled modeling tools give no reason, Ctrl+S silently becomes Adopt, and Adopt proposes a generic filename

**design weakness · severity low · confidence high · user value medium · effort S** · verification: confirmed · evidence basis: source · verified

- **Source:** `src/kurtshape/ui.py` · `Panel.refresh (status) / Panel.sync_edit_ui / Panel.adopt / Panel._operation (open toast)` · lines ui.py:1753-1754 (status text, only explanation; muted via theme.py:71), 1766-1772 (identity label has no reference marker), 1366-1367 ('Open complete' toast; 1368-1375 add nothing for unmanaged), 3401-3407 (model/assembly actions disabled with ordinary tips; toolbar_labels.py:124-127 shows a reason only via 'kurtshapeHelp'), 2971-2972 + 4197 + shortcuts.json:137 (Ctrl+S routes to adopt), 2994 (default 'adopted-part.FCStd'; core.py:1398 exposes native_file, core.py:1888-1889 rejects the source path), 844-846 / 2132-2133 / 3191-3192 / 3527-3528 / 4037-4038 / 4108-4109 (click-time guards only)
- **Evidence:** Opening a plain FreeCAD file yields a document whose entire toolbar is greyed; the only explanation is the bottom-left status text, and the Document menu's 'Adopt copy' tip ('Create an editable copy of the inspected file') is the sole pointer. Save (Ctrl+S) silently becomes Adopt (2971-2972) with a dialog proposing adopted-part.FCStd.
- **Scenario:** Kurt opens an FCStd a friend made in FreeCAD; everything is disabled and he does not know why until he reads the status line.
- **What Kurt would notice:** Mild confusion; the Adopt copy name discards the source name.
- **A newcomer would notice:** Immediately on opening a plain FreeCAD file: sketch/extrude/assembly tools go grey with no banner or hover reason, the only explanation is a muted status line at the bottom of the left panel, and 'Open complete' suggests everything worked. In Onshape or SolidWorks every opened file is editable, so this reads as a broken install until they find Document → Adopt copy or press Ctrl+S and are surprised by an 'Adopt a copy' dialog proposing a generic name.
- **Reproduction / verification needed:** GUI: Open any FCStd without a KurtShapeProject object.
- **Proposed change:** After an unmanaged open, toast 'Reference file · tools are read-only · Document → Adopt copy to edit' (ui.py:1368 block); set a 'kurtshapeHelp' property/tooltip on disabled modeling actions ('Adopt a copy to edit this reference file'); in Panel.adopt default the filename to `Path(doc.FileName).stem + '-copy.FCStd'` beside the source.
- **Acceptance:** GUI check: opening an unmanaged file shows the toast and the Adopt dialog proposes '<source>-copy.FCStd'.
- **Dependencies:** none
- **Risk:** low
- **Adversarial check:** Every cited line holds at HEAD f57d592 and none of the three newest commits (24bab02, 1f0001e, f57d592) touch this code; git -S dates the 'adopted-part.FCStd' default to 5f91ba2 and the 'Reference file · inspect or adopt a copy' status text to cb96ddf. Verified: (1) ui.py:1753-1754 is the only standing explanation; the label is QLabel#KurtShapeDocumentStatus, muted colour (theme.py:71), word-wrapped, bottom of the left panel, no severity property ever set on it; update_document_identity (ui.py:1766-1772) shows only name + ' · Unsaved/Modified', no reference marker. (2) ui.py:1366-1367 emits 'Open complete' for op='open'; the 1368-1375 follow-ups cover only orphan helpers, rebuild_reason and import_step, so there is no unmanaged-specific toast. (3) ui.py:3401-3407 disables model_actions and assembly_actions when state.managed is false; their tooltips stay the ordinary ones, and the hover label (toolbar_labels.py:124-127) appends a reason only when a 'kurtshapeHelp' property is set, which happens today for one action (ui.py:575). (4) save() at ui.py:2971-2972 returns adopt() for unmanaged documents; Ctrl+S is bound to local.save (shortcuts.json:137, tests/test_shortcuts.py:110) and mapped to self.save (ui.py:4197), so Ctrl+S silently opens the 'Adopt a copy' dialog defaulting to last_directory/'adopted-part.FCStd' (ui.py:2994) although state['native_file'] carries the source path (core.py:1398) and core._adopt already refuses the source path itself (core.py:1888-1889), so a stem+'-copy' default is safe. (5) The click-time guards 'Adopt a copy before editing this reference file.' exist at ui.py:844-846, 2132-2133, 3191-3192, 3527-3528, 4037-4038 and 4108-4109 and fire only from still-enabled entry points (double-click sketch, native commands, context menu). Design intent is documented (README.md:61 'Unmanaged FCStd opens for inspection; Adopt copy creates a managed copy'; docs/2026-10-02-claude-review-follow-up/UPDATE_PLAN.md:117 says to offer Adopt 'after showing supported/unsupported objects', which the GUI only does as an error after clicking Adopt, ui.py:2989-2991). Core adoption semantics are tested (tests/test_review_followup.py:355-391, tests/test_build_status.py:661-704, tests/test_assembly.py:635-647) but no test pins the GUI wording or the default filename. The finder's probe (area-h-assembly/exp_assembly.py, env/exp_assembly.log) never exercised the unmanaged state, so the finding is code-derived, but the code paths are unambiguous. One overstatement: 'entire toolbar greyed' is wrong; Fit/Normal/3D/Show planes are not modeling actions and New/Open/Save/Export stay enabled, so only the modeling tools, Rebuild, Check geometry, More and the assembly bar go grey. Classification design_weakness / low / high is honest as filed.

## Merged duplicates (folded into their primary finding; not separate backlog items)

- **B-14 → B-05:** Face hover outline colour is hard-coded instead of following View/HighlightColor; matches the live profile's blue preselection only by coincidence (design weakness, low; source · verified).
- **C-09 → A-11:** Read-only search field and selectable status labels take keyboard focus and swallow single-key tools (hypothesis, medium; source · not independently verified).
- **C-13 → A-10:** 'Rebuild required · pending model changes' is shown in the feature panel while a sketch is being edited (the model bar, with Rebuild, is hidden at that point) (design weakness, low; captures · verified).
- **F-08 → H-07:** Recovery checkpoints are never pruned: every new or recovered document leaves a permanent pair and unsaved ones stay in the Recovery menu forever (verified defect, low; reproduced · not independently verified).
- **F-10 → G-01:** Agents cannot tell which KurtShape build is running: capabilities and the session file carry engine and contract only, no source revision; argument tables are not exposed (opportunity, medium; preference · not independently verified).
- **F-13 → D-03:** Session retry cap (4096 IDs) is reachable in a day of iterative agent builds and the only remedy is an app restart (hypothesis, low; reproduced · not independently verified).

## Refuted during verification (not carried into the backlog)

- **A-15 Raster grayscale fallbacks may render soft on mixed-DPI desktops while SVG originals stay crisp** — The cited lines are correct (RASTER_SIZES at src/kurtshape/presentation_icons.py:17, grayscale_icon at 103-118; only one commit, 4681ddf, ever touched the file; no tests under tests/ cover it). The mechanism is only half right and the consequence does not materialise. (1) The bundled runtime is Qt 6.8.3/PySide6 (the finder's own native_gray.log prints "6.8.3"). QIcon.pixmap(QSize, mode, state) without a DPR argument uses qApp.devicePixelRatio(), which Qt documents as the HIGHEST DPR among all connected screens, not the screen the window is on. So the cache is not "built at first use on whatever screen"; its DPR is the desktop maximum regardless of where the window sits, and the "first use on a 125% screen then moving to 100%" scenario cannot occur as described. (2) The real native sources are 64 px rasters at DPR 1 (Gui.getIcon wraps a BitmapFactory pixmap; the finder's native_gray_metrics.json shows every gray icon's availableSizes capped at [20,24,32,40,48,60,64] even though RASTER_SIZES asks for 80/96, and one icon, PartDesign_CompPrimitiveAdditive, is a 16 px raster). (3) An offscreen PySide6 probe with QT_SCALE_FACTOR=1.5 running the real grayscale_icon on a 64 px raster native confirms the cache holds 30/36/48/60/64 px entries tagged DPR 1.5. Requesting the icon at DPR 1.5 returns the 30 px entry bit-exactly (mean/max diff 0.0 vs a direct render). Requesting at DPR 1.0 (the "other monitor" case) returns a 30→20 downscale whose pixels differ from a single-step 64→20 render by mean 0.00-0.07 and max 1.3-1.7 on a 0-255 scale, with identical edge-sharpness (mean gradient 17.04 vs 17.06, 23.65 vs 23.66, 16.79 vs 16.79 for pierce/fillet/dimension). That is below any visible threshold; the finder's own acceptance criterion ("pixel diff at DPR 1.0 and 1.5 shows no resampling blur") already holds at HEAD, and the proposed per-DPR cache key would change nothing visible. (4) Gray fallbacks are somewhat softer than SVG neighbours (e.g. fillet 23.65 vs 26.00, dimension 16.79 vs 20.10), but that gap comes from downscaling a 64 px (or 16 px) raster source and is the same on every screen; it is finding 1's domain, not a DPR-cache effect. Net: the cross-screen softness claim is refuted; what remains is at most a cosmetic tidy-up (pass an explicit DPR or use a QIconEngine) with no measurable benefit.
